## 2. The 12 canonical round-1 concepts (three per track)


### Track: Fire TV

#### FTV-1: Story Night: a family story circle on the TV

**Round:** 1. **Track:** Fire TV (primary). Mini challenges: AWS Builder, Open Source.

**Pitch:** A Vega OS app for one evening activity. Each phone in the room joins by scanning a QR code. One phone photographs an old print, and the photo fills the TV. The elder telling the story speaks into their own phone. Everyone else sends follow-up questions privately from their phones. The person holding the remote picks the next question with the D-pad. The session becomes a 'family episode' that keeps the teller's voice, adds captions and replays on the TV. When two tellers remember the same photo differently, the app records both versions with attribution and shows the conflict as a question for the family. The model never decides which version is right.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Primary user | Households where three to eight people of different generations share one living room: grandparents, parents and children. An adult child usually organizes. Indian joint families and visiting diaspora families are the first segment (assumption). |
| Frequency | Episodic: gatherings, festivals and visits. Estimated at monthly or less. This is an assumption and has not been measured. |
| Severity | Low urgency but irreversible. A story is lost when the teller dies. Emotional value is high and functional value is low. This is inference, not measured. |
| Current workaround | Asynchronous services: Remento ($99/yr; voice prompts sent by email or SMS, made into a printed book) and Storyworth ($99/yr; weekly written prompts). Two-person recording apps: Willow and StoryCorps (free; archive at the Library of Congress). Families also cast photos to the TV or send WhatsApp voice notes. No tool reviewed runs a group session around a shared screen. |
| Amazon integration and its indispensable role | Fire TV (Vega OS, React Native for Vega) is the shared stage. It shows the photo full-screen, the queue of incoming questions and the timeline, and it plays finished episodes. The remote, through TVEventHandler and focus management, gives the host control of the session. Captions follow the system caption style through KeplerA11ySettingsInterfaceTurbo. The phones only capture input. |
| Removal test (honest) | This is a partial weakness, flagged honestly. Without the TV, two people can get most of the value from Willow or StoryCorps on a phone. The TV is indispensable only for the group form: three or more people looking at one large photo together, a host who controls turn-taking from the couch, and replaying the archive at the next gathering. If falsification shows that families of three or more are as happy with a single phone, the concept fails. |
| Wow moment under 45 s | 0:00 Grandma taps 'Snap' on her phone and a 1978 print fills the TV within about 3 s. 0:05–0:25 She talks into her phone, and live caption text appears under the photo. 0:25 Two grandchildren's questions arrive as cards on the TV. The host presses D-pad right and OK on 'Who took this photo?'. 0:35 A 'Grandpa says 1979?' conflict chip appears beside the '1978 · Pune' chip on the timeline. 0:40 The TV replays the chapter in her own voice with word-synced captions. |
| Original mechanism | (1) A question relay: questions are submitted privately on phones and released publicly by the D-pad host, so the elder never sees a wall of questions and the host controls pacing. (2) A multi-teller claim ledger: Nova Lite extracts claims about each photo as structured data (who, where, when, event) with the speaker attached. Conflicts are detected deterministically by comparing year and place fields across speakers. They are shown on the TV for the family to resolve and are never merged by the model. (3) Episode compiler: photo, audio segments, word-timed captions, chapter questions and a timeline, replayable offline on the TV. |
| Simulator or real-data route (data route) | The phone PWA (HTTPS) requests a presigned PUT and uploads a JPEG (2 MB or less) and an audio clip (WebM/Opus) to S3 in us-east-1. An S3 event triggers Lambda, which starts a Transcribe job. On completion, Lambda calls Nova Lite (Converse) with the transcript and photo ID and gets back questions and claims as JSON. Lambda writes to DynamoDB, then sends to the TV and phones over the WebSocket. The TV fetches episode audio and images through presigned GET URLs or CloudFront and caches them for offline replay. All TV traffic is HTTPS/WSS, because Vega WebView blocks cleartext by default and the same policy is assumed for native code (assumption). |
| Authentication | No accounts. The TV creates a room and shows a QR code carrying a short-lived HMAC-signed join token (4 h). Each phone must accept a consent screen before its microphone or camera turns on. Roles are host (the TV), teller and asker. Every speaker can delete their own clips. Rooms expire after 30 days unless the family saves the episode. API keys stay server-side. |
| Preview constraints | The VVD has no microphone, but all audio is captured on phones, so this does not matter. There is no TV camera, and the phone camera is used. The demo needs at least 3 phones or browsers on HTTPS (getUserMedia requires a secure context). Vega WebSocket support must be confirmed on day 1. The video may contain only the family's own photos and voices, with consent from everyone on camera, and no copyrighted media. |
| Hardware | Apple Silicon Mac (M4 Pro, 24 GB) running the Vega Virtual Device, plus 2–3 phones of any brand. An optional Fire TV Stick 4K Select (₹5,499) for a real-device take. |
| SDK versions | Vega SDK 0.24.x (manifest [os.version] 1.2), RN 0.83 early access (0.72 as fallback), Node 20 through nvm (samples pin 18–20; the machine has v25), @amazon-devices/react-native-qrcode-svg 3.0.0, @amazon-devices/react-native-w3cmedia 2.3.2, @amazon-devices/kepler-a11y-settings-interface-turbo 1.0.0, AWS SDK for JavaScript v3. Optional: @strands-agents/sdk 1.19.0. |
| Deployment prerequisites | Install Rosetta (softwareupdate --install-rosetta), Vega SDK 0.24 and the VVD (20 GB disk; 48 GB free). Register the VVD only if Amazon services are needed (they are not here). Use a Paid-plan AWS account so promotional credits apply, and check Bedrock Nova quota in us-east-1 on day 1. The AWS budget alarm has to be enabled in us-east-1. HTTPS endpoints come from API Gateway defaults. Keep the backend live through 20 Nov 12:00 PT if judges are pointed at it, and provide a local-run path as well. |
| Firecrawl: research role | Used once during ideation to map rivals in story recording (Remento, Storyworth, Willow, StoryCorps), saved to .firecrawl/idea-ftv-family-stories.json. Optional later use: monitor rival feature pages before submission. |
| Firecrawl: runtime role | none |
| AWS Builder path | The AWS services do real work: API Gateway WebSocket provides managed fan-out with no server to keep warm through judging; S3 presigned uploads keep media off the TV; Transcribe gives word timestamps for caption sync; Nova Lite generates questions and extracts claims in structured form; DynamoDB stores room state. Optional: a Strands agent ('interviewer') with deterministic tools (list_open_conflicts, unanswered_people) chooses which three questions to propose. Development workflow uses Claude Code on Bedrock or Kiro Crew, which counts per forum 45321. Nova keeps spend within the promotional credits. |
| Open Source path | Publish 'vega-room-kit' under MIT. It contains: a React Native for Vega hook (useRoom) for QR pairing, presence, roles and reconnect; the phone PWA client; a reference Lambda/WebSocket backend; and a signed join-token spec, with vitest tests and protocol docs. Value: visible entrants (CommonScene, HomeBoard, Cinemastamps) each rebuilt phone pairing, and Amazon's documented Matter Casting route needs a Device Attestation Certificate, which is heavy for small apps. This is a new integration pattern, in line with the rules' Open Source creative example. |
| Fallback | If WebSocket fails on Vega, use HTTP long-polling with fetch at 1 s. If Transcribe latency hurts the live demo, show live captions from the phone's Web Speech API, label them as such, and use Transcribe for the archive. If the Vega toolchain blocks, build Fire OS react-native-tvos and record on a real Fire TV Stick over adb. The Android TV emulator is avoided because the rules name only a device or the Fire TV/Vega simulator. |
| Falsification test (≤1 day) | One day, two families of three or more people each. Run a 20-minute story session twice with matched prompts: once with a clickable prototype on the TV plus phones, once with a single phone recording (Willow or StoryCorps style). Measure the number of distinct speakers, minutes of story captured and stated preference. If the single phone matches or beats the TV on two of the three measures, the TV is not indispensable and the concept is dropped. Technical kill check on the same day: a phone event must reach the VVD app over WSS in under 500 ms median. |
| Risk profile | Medium-high execution risk: realtime choreography across several devices and a demo with live people. Low TV-platform risk, since the TV only renders, takes D-pad input and plays audio. Medium privacy risk (family voices). Low compute cost. |
| Estimate (unmeasured) | Solo 140 h; two-person 85 h per person; cloud $10–30 through 20 Nov 2026 (estimate). Assumptions: All estimates. hours_duo is per person when split (TV app vs backend and PWA), including about 15% coordination overhead. Includes 14 h for Vega toolchain, Rosetta and VVD setup, 10 h for the demo video and 8 h for submission writing. Assumes a developer fluent in React and TypeScript and new to Vega. Cloud usage assumes about 300 min of audio in dev and demo. Transcribe batch is $0.006–0.024/min (the digest lists $0.006/min at medium confidence; $0.024 is a conservative assumption), so ≤$7.20.… |

**Demand evidence** (quality labelled by the researcher):

- A review written by a competitor says people want to 'sit together and have the conversation in person' and criticizes Storyworth because it is 'designed for one person responding alone'. — [https://willow-stories.com/blog/best-storyworth-alternatives-2026/](https://willow-stories.com/blog/best-storyworth-alternatives-2026/) — *Low to medium. Vendor-authored and conflicted (Willow promotes its own product). It shows a stated preference, not measured demand.*
- Remento claims '1M+ stories recorded' and sells a $99/yr voice-first service with photo-based prompts and family prompt voting. This shows people pay for capturing stories by voice. — [https://www.remento.co/remento-vs-storyworth](https://www.remento.co/remento-vs-storyworth) — *Low. A vendor claim we cannot verify, but it does show a paid market exists.*
- A Storyworth blog post (June 2026) cites AARP's 2026 report: smartphone ownership among adults 50+ grew from 55% (2016) to 90% (2025). That supports using phones as companions for older tellers. — [https://welcome.storyworth.com/blog/remento---how-it-works-pricing-reviews-top-alternatives](https://welcome.storyworth.com/blog/remento---how-it-works-pricing-reviews-top-alternatives) — *Medium-low. A secondary citation inside a vendor blog. The AARP original was not read.*
- Using phones as controllers for a shared TV screen is now a mainstream pattern: Netflix Party Games (28 Jul 2026) and Amazon Luna GameNight on Fire TV (updated 29 Sep 2026). Families already know the 'scan a QR code on the TV' step. — [https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/entertainment/amazon-luna-redesign-gamenight-prime](https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/entertainment/amazon-luna-redesign-gamenight-prime) — *Supply evidence only. It shows familiarity with the pattern, not demand for storytelling.*

| [Willow / StoryCorps](https://willow-stories.com/blog/best-storyworth-alternatives-2026/) | Willow offers a 'together experience' with guided prompts for two people and AI-written biographies. StoryCorps is a free app for two-person conversations with a Library of Congress archive. | Supports 3–8 participants, shows the photo on a shared screen and lets participants submit questions privately from their phones. Whether either product supports group or TV sessions is not verified in reviewed sources. | Described by Willow, a conflicted source. The StoryCorps page itself was not read. |

Free with Prime on Fire TV. | The goal is not play. The output is a lasting family archive. No storytelling entrant was seen among about 37 visible Fire TV repos. | Read from the Devpost project page and repo descriptions. A lower bound, since private entries are invisible. |

**Smallest credible MVP:**

- Vega app (RN 0.83, or 0.72 as fallback) running on the VVD with four screens: room lobby with QR, photo stage with a question queue, timeline view, and episode player
- Phone PWA: join by QR, consent screen, photo capture, push-to-talk recording, question submission, delete my recordings
- Realtime room: API Gateway WebSocket, Lambda and DynamoDB (presence, roles, events), with reconnect
- Pipeline: upload to S3 through presigned URLs, then Amazon Transcribe batch with word timestamps, then Nova Lite structured output (three follow-up questions plus a claims JSON)
- Deterministic conflict detection on year and place claims, with a conflict chip on the TV that only the family can resolve
- Episode player: Ken Burns-style pan over the photo, the teller's audio, word-synced captions styled from KeplerA11ySettingsInterfaceTurbo, and VoiceView labels on every focusable element
- A table in the README separating verified from simulated items, plus friction log entries

**Deliberately excluded stretch:**

- Remote relatives joining over the internet
- Printed book or PDF export
- Languages other than English for captions (Hindi/Tamil transcription not verified)
- Face recognition or auto-tagging of people (deliberately excluded for privacy)
- Alexa+ voice control
- Amazon Photos import

**Blockers:** WebSocket support on React Native for Vega is documented only through a WebFetch summary. The CommonScene entrant corroborates it, but it needs a day-1 spike.; Rosetta is missing on macOS 27, so the Vega tools will not run until it is installed.; A new AWS account may have Bedrock quota set to 0 (two forum reports, no escalation path).; Demo logistics: at least three consenting real people, plus authentic old photos owned by the team's family.; Phone microphone permission and HTTPS on a local network during development (use a tunnel or deployed endpoints).

**Reasons judges might reject it:** It can look like another Jackbox or GameNight QR-join app, and QR pairing is common among entrants.; It may read as 'transcription plus slideshow', and question generation is a latent LLM capability.; Remento already turns photos into prompts, so novelty depends on the group mechanism and conflict tracking.; Impact is emotional rather than functional and is hard to quantify. Potential Impact may score lower than for health or accessibility entries.; Privacy and consent concerns for recordings of older relatives and minors.; The demo depends on staged family footage, so authenticity may be questioned.

**Gate result:** overall **conditional** (eligibility pass, feasibility conditional; native overlap low, visible crowding medium).

- Eligibility: Meets the Fire TV rule: a Vega OS app shown on the Vega Virtual Device, which the Official Rules name as 'the Fire TV/Vega simulator' (evt-rules-live.md L152). Fire TV is exempt from the runtime-hook rule. The TV only renders, takes D-pad input (TVEventHandler/FocusManager, verified in the RN-for-Vega overview, firetv.md §5) and plays audio. It needs no TV camera, no TV microphone (the VVD has none; all capture is on phones) and no arbitrary Alexa voice. Content is the family's own photos and voices, so it complies with forum 45329 (no third-party clips). It does not depend on the Android TV emulator.
- Feasibility: (1) The 140 h solo estimate exceeds solo capacity of about 117 h. That capacity is an estimate (6.5 focused h/day × 18 build days), the baseline the other concepts use. (2) It is the heaviest choreography in the set: a realtime room across 3+ phones plus the TV, and a shoot with live consenting people. (3) Vega WebSocket support is documented only through a WebFetch summary (firetv.md §5, medium confidence). The 1 s fetch-polling fallback is rules-safe. (4) Transcribe and Bedrock depend on a Paid-plan account with non-zero quota (forum 45416/45384 report quota 0 or suspension). (5) The stated fallback, 'Fire OS react-native-tvos on a real Fire TV Stick over adb', does not work with the stic…
- Conditions: (1) A 2-person split (about 85 h each, the concept's estimate) or a solo scope cut to about 110 h or less. For example, make the multi-teller claim ledger a stretch goal and use labelled Web Speech captions live, with Transcribe only for the archive. (2) At least 3 consenting participants are available for the shoot, including an elder; minors need guardian consent. Old photos must be genuinely family-owned. (3) Phone-to-TV events reach the Vega app on the VVD over WSS, or over 1 s polling, by day 2. (4) Bedrock Nova Lite and Transcribe are usable in us-east-1 on a Paid-plan AWS account (quota is not 0). (5) For a real-device take: the Vega path needs the 4K Select confirmed on OS 1.2, an Amazon Developer account and a USB data cable. The Fire OS path needs a Fire OS stick or AQC Live Device Interaction. Not adb on the 4K Select. (6) Do not commit family media to a public repo. Use consented sample media, or a private repo shared with the 6 reviewers plus testing@devpost.com (forum 45418, 45420).
- Native overlap: Reviewed sources show no Amazon-native group story-capture or family-archive feature. Amazon Luna GameNight shares only the QR phone-join mechanic. It is games, not an archive, and India is not in its country list (rivals_weartv.md). The Fire TV Alexa+ mobile companion (US from November) is focused on watchlists. Inference: native risk is low, but judges may read the join flow as 'GameNight-like'.
- Crowding: Storytelling theme: 0 of about 37 visible Fire TV repos (github.md §2; a lower bound). The QR phone-companion pattern is common, though: CommonScene, HomeBoard and Cinemastamps per research claim GH-035. The memory/elder theme has about 15 repos across tracks, including Marc-Dvci/Mantel (a Fire TV 'patient memory' for dementia). Fire TV has about 12 visible repos per prize slot.
- Day-one proof: On the VVD (Rosetta installed, Vega SDK 0.24, Node 20 via nvm), a Vega RN app must do four things: receive 50 phone-PWA events over WSS through API Gateway with a median under 500 ms (or work over 1 s polling); show a phone-uploaded JPEG full-screen from a presigned GET; play an S3 audio clip through react-native-w3cmedia with currentTime advancing. Separately, Bedrock Nova Lite in us-east-1 must return a structured claims JSON for one test transcript.
- Redesign to pass: Not a gate fail. To remove the conditions for a solo builder: (a) ship the question relay and episode player first, at about 100 h, and treat the claim ledger as a stretch; (b) drop the adb fallback and stay VVD-only, which is rules-compliant, or use AQC Live Device Interaction with a separate Fire OS build; (c) script the shoot with 3 consenting adults if minors complicate consent.

**Round-2 judge ratings (1–5; base case: solo, India, VVD on Mac, no Bee hardware, no US Ring device). Weighted = 0.25·Tech + 0.20·Design + 0.20·Impact + 0.20·Originality + 0.15·Feasibility.**

| Judge lens | Tech | Design | Impact | Originality | Feasibility | Weighted | Confidence | One-line verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| devrel | 3 | 4 | 2 | 3 | 2 | 2.85 | medium | Charming multimodal design on the right track, but over capacity solo and weak on measured impact. |

| product | 3 | 4 | 2 | 3 | 2 | 2.85 | medium | Warm family idea with a sound ten-foot design but weak, unevidenced impact and over-capacity solo scope; viable only as a duo. |
| **Mean of three** | 3.00 | 4.00 | 2.00 | 3.00 | 2.00 | **2.85** | | Scenario weighted: solo+hw 2.93; duo 3.22; duo+hw 3.30 |

**Why each judge rated it that way (verbatim rationale, one per criterion):**

- *devrel* — Tech: Verified Vega surfaces (TVEventHandler/FocusManager, KeplerA11y, firetv.md §5) and the correct phone-mic/camera workaround for a VVD with no microphone (firetv.md §3). Capped: WebSocket on VVD untested (JDR-11), w3cmedia currentTime unverified, and at 140 h the claim ledger becomes a stretch so judges see less. — Design: Closest of the round-1 set to the Rules' 'voice + D-pad + visual in a single flow' creative example (JDR-03): private phone questions, D-pad host release, shared photo. Not 5: three phones plus TV is fragile for elders and QR-pairing reads GameNight-like. — Impact: Episodic (monthly or less by the concept's own assumption); evidence is vendor-authored story-service copy. Nothing measured shows families want the group-on-TV form. — Originality: Claim ledger with attribution is new, but Remento/Storyworth cover capture, and TV+phone QR hubs are a visible cluster (~8 repos: CommonScene, HomeBoard, family-hub). — Feasibility: 140 h solo against ~104 h capacity (estimates); gate conditional on a 2-person split or cut to ~110 h, which still exceeds capacity.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 3, feasibility 2 (A 4K Select take adds credibility only if the stick is on OS 1.2 (firetv.md §4); hours problem unchanged.); duo tech 4, feasibility 3 (85 h each fits; ledger likely ships, lifting what judges see.); duo+hw tech 4, feasibility 3 (As duo; real-device footage is optional polish.)
- *engineer* — Tech: The Vega surfaces are real and documented: TVEventHandler/FocusManager (firetv.md section 5), KeplerA11ySettings getters (saved .firecrawl/oct4/ftv2-kepler-a11y-readme.md: isScreenReaderEnabled, getCaptionPreferences, getUiScaleSetting return Promises), w3cmedia playback. The backend (API Gateway WSS room with roles, Transcribe word timings, Nova Lite claim JSON, deterministic year/place conflict check) is more than… — Design: Device roles are right: phones do private capture and questions, the TV is the shared stage, the remote holder is host. It is close to the rules' own Fire TV creative example of voice + D-pad + visual in one flow (evt-rules.md L442 area) and never needs the VVD microphone or a TV camera, which do not exist (firetv.md sections 3 and 7). Deductions: onboarding 3-8 phones over HTTPS with mic permission is real friction… — Impact: Episodic and emotional; 'monthly or less' is the concept's own assumption. Evidence is vendor-authored (Remento, Storyworth comparison written by Storyworth) and unmeasured for the group-on-TV form; the concept's own falsification test admits the TV may be dispensable. — Originality: No storytelling project among the 27 '"fire tv" hackathon' repos saved on Oct 4 (.firecrawl/oct4/gh2-firetv-hackathon.json) or the roughly 37 classified on Oct 3 (github.md). The never-merged multi-teller conflict ledger is a fresh mechanism. Offsets: Remento already turns photos into voice prompts; QR phone-join is normalised by Luna GameNight and Jackbox; question generation is a latent LLM capability (forum 45397… — Feasibility: 140 h solo against about 104 h of capacity (6.5 h x 16 net days; estimate). Heaviest realtime choreography in the set (3+ phones plus TV), a consented shoot with an elder and three people, Bedrock/Transcribe quota on a Paid-plan account (two zero-quota reports, forum 45416/45384 per aws.md), and the stated adb fallback does not apply to the India-sold Vega 4K Select (firetv.md section 4).
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 3, feasibility 2 (A 4K Select adds only an optional real-device take. The device spec page lists it on Vega OS 1.1 while SDK 0.24 requires OS 1.2 (firetv.md section 4), so even that take is uncertain. Hours unchanged.); duo tech 4, feasibility 3 (At about 85 h each (estimate) a TV/backend split can land the claim ledger and captions with polish; the shoot, quota and VVD WebSocket risks remain.); duo+hw tech 4, feasibility 3 (Same as duo_no_hw plus an optional real take gated on OS 1.2.)
- *product* — Tech: Vega RN app on the VVD is real and accepted (Rules L344, .firecrawl/oct4/evt-rules.md), uses TVEventHandler, w3cmedia audio and an API Gateway WebSocket relay plus Transcribe and Nova Lite (prior_summary mvp_scope). But the TV only renders photos, cards and replays; capture, speech and questions live on phones and the backend, and WebSocket on the VVD is still a day-2 spike (gate). Platform use is genuine but not de… — Design: Matches the Blueprint pattern of shared display, phones as private input, remote as host control (trends_home.md T6 implementation implication; trd2-firetv-blueprint-pt5-accessibility.md remote-first journeys). Question relay via D-pad host and conflict chips are device-appropriate for a 3-8 person room. Not 5: no VoiceView or caption-setting binding is specified, and the demo depends on staged multi-person choreogr… — Impact: Emotional, episodic value for multigenerational households; no demand evidence beyond the existence of Remento/Storyworth/Willow (rivals_weartv.md not covering it; rival matrix 'Remento, Storyworth, Willow'), and the Indian joint-family segment is an assumption. The removal test admits two people get most value from a phone app (prior_summary). Audience beyond the hackathon is plausible but unevidenced; no severity. — Originality: Synchronous group story circle with D-pad host and multi-teller conflict ledger is not verified in Remento/Storyworth (asynchronous, single teller). But QR-paired phone-to-TV sessions are a common entrant pattern (CommonScene, HomeBoard, Cinemastamps per github.md and the refresh 'ambient hubs' cluster of about 8) and question generation plus transcription is a latent LLM capability (prior judge_rejection_risks). — Feasibility: 140 h solo against about 104 h capacity (estimate), gate is conditional on a 2-person split or a cut to about 110 h, three consenting participants including an elder, a day-2 WSS spike and Bedrock/Transcribe quota on a Paid plan (prior gate conditions). Realtime multi-device demo with live people is the highest execution risk in the Fire TV set.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 4, feasibility 2 (A real 4K Select take (if on Vega OS 1.2, two reports of sticks stuck on 1.1) would show phone-to-TV realtime over real Wi-Fi, closing the main credibility gap (+1 tech); hours and people dependence unchanged.); duo tech 3, feasibility 4 (85 h per person fits capacity (prior estimate); the claim ledger can stay in scope; still needs three participants.); duo+hw tech 4, feasibility 4 (Both effects: real-device realtime take plus split build.)

**Round-1 judge ratings (3 Oct, same model; shown for traceability):** devrel 3/4/2/4/2 = 3.05; engineer 3/4/2/3/2 = 2.85; product 3/4/2/4/2 = 3.05

**Red-team verdicts:** not red-teamed (did not reach the top of either round's ranking); ranking rests on gate and judge scores alone.

---

#### FTV-2: WordGlow TV: adaptive same-language subtitle stories

**Round:** 1. **Track:** Fire TV (primary). Mini challenges: AWS Builder, Open Source.

**Pitch:** A Vega OS kids' channel of short, original, illustrated stories. Each word lights up on the TV at the moment the narrator says it, using Amazon Polly word speech marks. Pressing Play/Pause freezes on the current word, D-pad left and right move between words, and OK replays just that word's slice of audio. The app logs each replay and how long the child stays paused. These signals make up the household's 'tricky words', which drive the next generated episode: the target words recur, sentences get shorter or longer, and the highlight pace changes for each child profile. It builds on Same Language Subtitling (SLS), a TV literacy method with field evidence in India.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Primary user | Early or struggling readers aged about 5–9, watching with a parent or grandparent. First segment: English-medium learners in Indian households. Second: US households. Profiles live on the device. |
| Frequency | Daily viewing slot of about 10 minutes, for example after school. This is the target behavior, an assumption not yet measured. |
| Severity | Medium-high long-term (reading proficiency compounds over time), low acute. The paper cited below says 60% of Indian 'literates' cannot read simple texts. |
| Current workaround | Turning on subtitles for regular shows (the UK 'Turn On The Subtitles' campaign), PBS KIDS Video on Fire TV, phone and tablet reading apps, and parents reading aloud. Subtitles on ordinary content are not adjusted to the child's level, and research suggests engagement drops when the text is too hard. |
| Amazon integration and its indispensable role | Fire TV is the household's daily viewing surface and the place where SLS evidence was gathered. The Vega app renders word-timed text over illustrations, drives a word clock from the W3C Media audio player, handles word-level D-pad navigation (TVEventHandler and FocusManager), respects system caption styles (KeplerA11ySettingsInterfaceTurbo), and labels every word for VoiceView. |
| Removal test (honest) | Fairly strong, with a stated caveat. The evidence base (SLS) concerns subtitles on TV watched habitually, often with family present. Remove the TV and the product becomes a tablet read-along app, a different and crowded category. That move gives up the habitual viewing slot and co-viewing. Caveat: word highlighting itself works on any screen. The bet that the TV drives more exposure than a tablet has not been tested here. |
| Wow moment under 45 s | 0:00 A story plays, and each word glows in sync with the narrator. 0:10 The child presses Play/Pause, and the focus ring lands on 'elephant'. D-pad right moves to 'enormous', OK is pressed, and the narrator says only 'enormous'. 0:20 The parent overlay (Menu) shows 'Tricky words today: enormous, whisper'. 0:30 Pressing 'Next episode' opens a newly generated episode (cached before the demo, and labelled as pre-generated). Its title card reads 'Practice words: enormous ×3', and its sentences are visibly shorter: level 2.0 → 1.5. 0:40 The new episode starts with 'enormous' glowing. |
| Original mechanism | (1) Word clock from speech marks. Polly returns word marks (time, start, end, value) alongside the MP3. The TV binary-searches the audio player's currentTime against the marks to find the active word. Replaying a word means seeking to mark[i].time and pausing at mark[i+1].time, with no extra synthesis. (2) Household-adaptive generation behind a deterministic gate. Each child's replay and dwell signals update a difficulty profile. A Strands agent generates the next episode under explicit constraints (target words, maximum sentence length, share of known words). A readability gate written in plain code, not an LLM, rejects drafts and asks for a rewrite until the constraints pass. Only then are Polly and illustration generation called. Episodes are flagged for human review before children see them. |
| Simulator or real-data route (data route) | The TV sends batches of replay and dwell events (random profile ID, word, count) by HTTPS POST to API Gateway and Lambda, which store them in DynamoDB. A generation job runs as a Lambda-hosted Strands agent: Nova Lite writes a draft, the code gate checks it, and the agent rewrites until it passes. Polly then returns the MP3 and the word-marks JSON, and the illustrations are generated. Everything goes to S3, and CloudFront serves the TV, which downloads and caches the bundle. Playback is fully local: the word clock runs on the device with no network calls during the story. |
| Authentication | No child accounts. A random device or profile ID is created on first launch, and the parent overlay is behind a local 4-digit PIN. Only aggregate counts of replayed words are stored: no voice, images or names of children. The backend accepts writes signed with a per-install secret. Content endpoints are read-only through CloudFront. |
| Preview constraints | Runs entirely on the VVD, with no microphone or camera needed. Timing precision of VVD audio output and currentTime is not documented. The Cinemastamps entrant reports capturing simulator audio, which is anecdotal. All episode content is original and generated, so no copyright issue. HTTPS only. |
| Hardware | Apple Silicon Mac with the VVD only. An optional Fire TV Stick 4K Select for a check on real-device timing. |
| SDK versions | Vega SDK 0.24.x, RN 0.83 early access (0.72 as fallback), Node 20, @amazon-devices/react-native-w3cmedia 2.3.2, @amazon-devices/kepler-a11y-settings-interface-turbo 1.0.0, @amazon-devices/react-native-svg 3.0.9000000001 (fallback illustrations), @strands-agents/sdk 1.19.0, AWS SDK v3. |
| Deployment prerequisites | Rosetta, Vega SDK and the VVD. A Paid-plan AWS account with credits. Bedrock access to Nova Lite (and Nova Canvas if used) in us-east-1 or us-west-2, checked on day 1. Polly neural voice available in the same region. A CloudFront distribution. Keep the content endpoint live through 20 Nov 2026, and ship a bundled offline fallback set in the app package. |
| Firecrawl: research role | Used during ideation to collect SLS evidence (UNESCO, PMC, Chartered College), saved to .firecrawl/idea-ftv-sls-literacy.json, and to verify the Polly speech-marks doc (.firecrawl/idea-ftv-polly-speechmarks.md). |
| Firecrawl: runtime role | none |
| AWS Builder path | A multi-service pipeline where each service has a clear job. A Strands agent orchestrates generation with deterministic tools: readability_check, target_word_coverage, synthesize_with_marks (Polly) and illustrate. Bedrock Nova writes drafts, Polly provides audio and word timing (the core sync mechanism), S3 and CloudFront distribute episode bundles, and DynamoDB holds the adaptation signals. This matches the rules' 'Creative' AWS example of a multi-service pipeline plus agent orchestration. Development uses Claude Code on Bedrock or Kiro Crew. |
| Open Source path | Release 'react-native-word-clock' under MIT: a component and hook that turns Polly speech marks or WebVTT word timestamps into a seek-safe active-word index, slices per-word replay, and renders 10-foot highlighted text with D-pad word focus. It targets RN for Vega, react-native-tvos and web, with vitest unit tests for the binary-search clock and drift correction. Also open a PR adding a speech-mark cue source to the MIT-licensed vega-media-kit. This is a reusable Vega-ready integration pattern, not a README edit. |
| Fallback | If the audio clock jitters on the VVD, run a JS timer started at play() and re-anchor it at each sentence mark. If image generation fails, use simple vector scenes (the react-native-svg Vega port exists). If the generation pipeline is late, ship a hand-reviewed, pre-generated episode set, with adaptation choosing among existing levels instead of generating new ones. If Vega is blocked, use Fire OS on a real stick. |
| Falsification test (≤1 day) | Day 1, technical: build a single-screen Vega prototype that plays one Polly MP3 with word marks and logs the highlight error against the marks over 3 minutes on the VVD. Kill or rework if the median error is above 150 ms. Day 1, user (with parental consent): 3–5 children aged 5–8 watch 2 pre-generated episodes on a TV, word-level highlight versus sentence-level subtitles, while an observer tallies looks at the text and any use of word replay. If fewer than half look at the words or none use replay, or if parents say they would rather use a tablet app, the TV-specific bet fails. |
| Risk profile | Medium. The runtime is a single device with no realtime networking and can play offline from cache, which keeps demo risk low. The core risk is technical: A/V timing precision on Vega is unverified. Reputational and compliance risk around children's content and efficacy claims is high. |
| Estimate (unmeasured) | Solo 125 h; two-person 75 h per person; cloud $15–35 through 20 Nov 2026 (estimate). Assumptions: All estimates. hours_duo is per person when split (TV app vs generation pipeline). Includes 14 h for Vega setup, 16 h for writing and reviewing episodes, 10 h for the video and 8 h for submission. Cloud usage assumes about 60 generated drafts and 12 kept episodes of about 1,500 characters each, about 90k characters for Polly. Polly neural pricing was not verified this session; at an assumed $16 per 1M characters that is about $1.50. Nova Lite text costs under $1 (verified rates). Illustrations:… |

**Demand evidence** (quality labelled by the researcher):

- UNESCO UIL LitBase (updated 23 May 2024): in India's SLS programme, randomly selected viewers were tested before and after at least one year of exposure. Those exposed to SLS showed significant improvement in reading and writing compared with those who had no access. SLS also inspired the UK TOTS campaign. — [https://www.uil.unesco.org/en/litbase/reading-billion-same-language-subtitling-india](https://www.uil.unesco.org/en/litbase/reading-billion-same-language-subtitling-india) — *Medium-high. An intergovernmental case entry summarizing programme evaluations, not an independent RCT write-up.*
- An eye-tracking study of 136 weak readers in rural Rajasthan watching Hindi film clips found that 70% engaged in unprompted reading when SLS was shown. — [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10615567/](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10615567/) — *Medium-high. Peer-reviewed (PMC). Measures engagement, not learning outcomes.*
- A research summary (10 Jul 2020) reports a PlanetRead 2018 eye-tracking finding: 94% of children engaged with subtitles when the story was at a low reading difficulty, and engagement fell as difficulty rose. The Linebarger 2010 RCT (n=70, ages 7–9) found gains mainly among the weakest readers, and overall differences were not always significant. — [https://my.chartered.college/research-hub/can-turning-on-the-subtitles-really-improve-the-literacy-levels-of-millions-of-children/](https://my.chartered.college/research-hub/can-turning-on-the-subtitles-really-improve-the-literacy-levels-of-millions-of-children/) — *Medium. A secondary summary by a professional teachers' body. Its nuance (weak effects for average readers) is why the design adapts to each child's level.*
- PBS KIDS Video on Fire TV has 102,406 ratings (4.4 stars). Amazon's AI summary of its reviews flags 'Functionality' in 542 mentions, 300 of them negative. Kids' learning video on Fire TV is well used but frustrating. — [https://help.pbs.org/support/solutions/articles/12000078117](https://help.pbs.org/support/solutions/articles/12000078117) — *Low to medium. An Amazon-generated summary of reviews, drawn from the rivals_weartv digest.*

**Rivals named at generation** (strongest feature; our difference; evidence status):

| Rival | Strongest feature | Our difference | Evidence status |
|---|---|---|---|
| [PBS KIDS Video (Fire TV)](https://help.pbs.org/support/solutions/articles/12000078117) | Free, trusted brand, 600+ episodes, works with the Fire TV remote, offline downloads. | Interactive word-level reading practice, adapted to the child. PBS KIDS offers passive viewing. Word-level interaction is not verified in reviewed sources. | Verified in the rivals_weartv digest. |
| [Khan Academy Kids](https://khankids.zendesk.com/hc/en-us/articles/360007054791) | Free, deep interactive early-literacy curriculum, built mobile-first for touch screens. | Designed for the TV and for co-viewing with a D-pad. Official support lists only Fire tablets, while an Appstore listing mentions the Fire TV remote, so its Fire TV availability is contradictory. | Digest. Fire TV status unresolved. |
| [Amazon Kids+ / Explore with Alexa](https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/devices/new-alexa-top-features) | First-party, age-appropriate answers, trivia, interactive games and Stories with Alexa. | Word-synced reading practice that adapts to tricky words. Whether Kids+ does this is not verified in reviewed sources. | Digest (feature guide updated 24 Jul 2026). Availability on Fire TV not verified. |
| [vega-media-kit (open-source entrant tooling)](https://github.com/moiz-lakkadkutta/vega-media-kit) | A 10-foot CueOverlay with selectable word focus and a WebVTT cue model. 159 tests. Device-verified on Fire OS. | It is a tool, not a product. Its Vega adapter is 'experimental, not device-verified'. We add speech-mark-driven word timing and audio slicing per word, and could contribute these upstream. | README read (GitHub digest). MIT license. |
| [lingo (visible Fire TV entrant)](https://github.com/moiz-lakkadkutta/lingo) | Dual subtitles on existing TV content, a pause that explains, and phone quizzes the next day, aimed at second-language learners. | Early-literacy readers rather than second-language learners, with original generated stories whose difficulty is controlled. | Repo description only. README not read. |

**Smallest credible MVP:**

- Vega app on the VVD with three screens: profile picker (PIN-gated parent overlay), episode shelf, and player with word clock
- Word-level interaction: pause to focus a word, D-pad to move between words, OK to replay that word's audio slice, with VoiceView labels on each word
- Caption font and colors read from KeplerA11ySettingsInterfaceTurbo, with a large-text default
- Episode bundle format: mp3, speech-marks JSON, 4 illustrations and metadata, cached in AsyncStorage or on the file system for offline play
- Generation pipeline: Strands agent, deterministic readability and target-word gate, Polly MP3 plus word marks, and illustrations, stored in S3 behind CloudFront
- Adaptation loop: replay and dwell events sent in batches to the API; the next episode is generated for that profile
- 8–12 reviewed English episodes across three levels, labelled as AI-generated and human-reviewed

**Deliberately excluded stretch:**

- Read-aloud fluency check on a phone (the VVD has no microphone, and collecting children's voices adds compliance risk)
- Hindi and other Indian languages (Polly voice and word-mark quality for Hindi not verified)
- Subtitling third-party or copyrighted shows (not allowed)
- Live per-child generation during the demo (cached episodes instead)
- Parent dashboard on phone

**APIs, events and actions** (status as assessed by the researcher; 'unverified' means not confirmed in reviewed docs, 'gated' means not available to entrants, 'simulated' means not real in the demo):

| Status | API / event / action | Documentation |
|---|---|---|
| verified | Amazon Polly SynthesizeSpeech with OutputFormat=json and SpeechMarkTypes=[word, sentence] (speech marks on neural, long-form and standard engines) | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/polly/latest/dg/speechmarks.html |
| unverified | @amazon-devices/react-native-w3cmedia AudioPlayer currentTime and seek | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/vega/latest/media-player.html |
| verified | TVEventHandler (playPause, left, right, select) and FocusManager | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/vega/0.24/vega-rn-overview |
| verified | KeplerA11ySettingsInterfaceTurbo (query closed-caption font, color, background) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/vega-api/0.24/README.amazon-devices_kepler-a11y-settings-interface-turbo |
| verified | @amazon-devices/react-native-async-storage__async-storage (profile and cache) | https://www.npmjs.com/package/@amazon-devices/react-native-async-storage__async-storage |
| unverified | Assistive technologies on Vega (VoiceView labels) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/vega/0.22/assistive-technologies-vega |
| verified | Strands Agents TypeScript SDK 1.19.0 (agent loop with custom tools) | https://www.npmjs.com/package/@strands-agents/sdk |
| unverified | Amazon Bedrock Converse with Nova Lite (story drafts) | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/APIReference/API_runtime_Converse.html |
| unverified | Amazon Nova Canvas image generation through Bedrock (illustrations) | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/ |

**Blockers:** Precision of audio currentTime and seek on the VVD and Vega devices is unverified. Highlight drift above about 150 ms would undermine the core interaction.; Safety and accuracy of AI-generated children's content. Amazon pulled its own AI Video Recaps after errors (Engadget, 12 Dec 2025).; Consistency of illustrations across pages if Nova Canvas is used. Its availability and price were not verified.; No real children are tested before submission unless parents consent, so efficacy cannot be claimed and only the SLS literature can be cited.; Rosetta and Vega toolchain setup, plus possible Bedrock quota problems.

**Reasons judges might reject it:** Could be read as 'a video player with subtitles', which is the rules' 'obvious' example.; AI-generated content for children raises safety concerns, and Amazon has a recent public history of pulled AI recaps.; The SLS evidence covers subtitled songs and films, not AI-generated stories. Judges may see the leap as unproven.; Amazon Kids+ and PBS already serve this audience on Fire TV, which may hurt perceived market need.; An India-first literacy pitch with an English-only MVP could look inconsistent.; There is no outcome data from real children.

**Gate result:** overall **conditional** (eligibility pass, feasibility conditional; native overlap medium, visible crowding high).

- Eligibility: A Vega OS app on the VVD meets the Fire TV rule (Rules L152), and Fire TV is exempt from the runtime hook. It is single-device, with no camera or microphone needed. All episode content is original or generated, so there is no third-party copyrighted media (forum 45329). Caveat: everything needed to run must be in the repo (M19). Forum 45418 recommends a private repo shared with reviewers if AI-generated illustrations and audio are not open-licensed.
- Feasibility: (1) The 125 h solo estimate exceeds the roughly 117 h solo capacity (estimate). (2) The core risk is unverified: whether react-native-w3cmedia currentTime and seek on the VVD are precise enough (the concept marks this unverified). Polly word speech marks are verified (.firecrawl/idea-ftv-polly-speechmarks.md). (3) Nova Canvas availability and price are unverified, and Bedrock quota may be 0 on new accounts. (4) Children's content needs a human review pass, and efficacy cannot be shown. (5) The Fire OS 'real stick' fallback has the same problem as FTV-1: the 4K Select is Vega, so it needs a Fire OS stick or AQC Live Device Interaction. Supporting data point: entrant usv240/everyword reports …
- Conditions: (1) Fit solo scope within about 117 h: drop Nova Canvas in favour of react-native-svg scenes, and drop live generation in favour of hand-reviewed pre-generated episodes. (2) On day 1, the VVD word clock must reach a median error of 150 ms or less with no word highlighted more than 150 ms early, or the JS-timer re-anchoring fallback must meet the same bar. (3) Differentiation from usv240/everyword must be visible in the first 30 s of the video: per-word D-pad replay plus the household-adaptive next episode. Otherwise Quality of the Idea is at risk. (4) No efficacy claims about real children. Any child viewing only with parental consent. (5) AI-generated assets must be licensed into the repo, or the repo made private and shared with the reviewers (forum 45418). (6) Bedrock Nova Lite access is confirmed in us-east-1 or us-west-2.
- Native overlap: Fire TV has system captions (line-level). Amazon already ships read-along highlighting for books: Kindle Assistive Reader on Alexa 'features automatic page turns and real-time highlighting' (Amazon help page, undated, retrieved 2026-10-03, .firecrawl/idea-gate-echo-vision-a11y.md), and everyword's README cites Immersion Reading. PBS KIDS Video and Amazon Kids+ serve this audience with passive video. Reviewed sources show no native word-level, adaptive practice on Fire TV.
- Crowding: Direct visible collision with usv240/everyword (created 2026-09-14, pushed 2026-10-03, MIT; README read via Firecrawl 2026-10-03, .firecrawl/idea-gate-everyword.md; all of the following are entrant claims, unverified). It has the same Same Language Subtitling thesis on Fire TV, word-by-word highlighting built from Polly speech marks and Transcribe, 'read-that-line-again' and slow mode, the published npm package karaoke-captions-react (which collides with the proposed react-native-word-clock OSS…
- Day-one proof: A single-screen Vega app on the VVD plays one Polly neural MP3 through w3cmedia. It logs highlighted-word error against the speech marks over 3 minutes and must show a median of 150 ms or less with zero words lit more than 150 ms early. Play/Pause followed by OK must replay exactly one word's slice. The same day, write a 3-line differentiation against everyword that a judge could verify from the video.
- Redesign to pass: This is not a gate fail, but the headline thesis is already visibly built by a strong entrant. Two options. Option (a): drop the SLS/karaoke framing and pitch only the household-adaptive practice loop (replay and dwell signals, then a tricky-word profile, then a constrained next episode behind a deterministic readability gate) plus per-word D-pad replay; also make the Open Source piece a Vega port of an existing MIT React Native caption library, which matches the rules' own example of 'adding V…

**Round-2 judge ratings (1–5; base case: solo, India, VVD on Mac, no Bee hardware, no US Ring device). Weighted = 0.25·Tech + 0.20·Design + 0.20·Impact + 0.20·Originality + 0.15·Feasibility.**

| Judge lens | Tech | Design | Impact | Originality | Feasibility | Weighted | Confidence | One-line verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| devrel | 3 | 3 | 3 | 2 | 2 | 2.65 | medium | Good literacy evidence undone by a verified entrant collision and an over-capacity solo plan. |
| engineer | 4 | 4 | 3 | 2 | 2 | 3.10 | medium | Technically the most Vega-native concept, but a strong visible entrant has already built its headline thesis and it is over solo capacity. |
| product | 3 | 3 | 3 | 2 | 3 | 2.80 | high | Credible literacy need, but the thesis is already built and filmed by a stronger entrant; only the adaptive-practice loop is left to pitch. |
| **Mean of three** | 3.33 | 3.33 | 3.00 | 2.00 | 2.33 | **2.85** | | Scenario weighted: solo+hw 2.93; duo 3.13; duo+hw 3.22 |

**Why each judge rated it that way (verbatim rationale, one per criterion):**

- *devrel* — Tech: Polly speech marks verified; per-word D-pad replay through TVEventHandler is real Fire TV craft. Core timing (w3cmedia currentTime/seek precision on VVD) is undocumented and the day-1 150 ms bar is unproven; 125 h means generation gets cut. — Design: Coherent single-device 10-foot product with VoiceView and caption styles, but on its surface it is the Rules' 'simple video player' obvious example (JDR-03) unless word replay dominates the video. — Impact: Strongest literacy evidence in the set (SLS field evidence, PMC eye-tracking), daily viewing slot plausible; capped because evidence covers subtitled film, not generated stories, and no child outcomes. — Originality: Direct collision with usv240/everyword (same SLS-on-Fire-TV thesis, Polly read-alongs, APK on real Fire TV) and a 5-repo kids/literacy cluster (lingo, ask-along, contextcue). — Feasibility: 125 h solo vs ~104 h; gate's cut target (117 h) was set against an earlier, larger capacity.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 3, feasibility 2 (Real-device timing check helps the 150 ms claim but does not fix hours.); duo tech 4, feasibility 3 (75 h each; adaptive generation survives.); duo+hw tech 4, feasibility 3 (As duo.)
- *engineer* — Tech: The most Vega-native mechanism in the set: Polly word speech marks drive a binary-search word clock off the w3cmedia player's currentTime, with per-word seek-and-pause replay, D-pad word focus, KeplerA11y caption styles and offline bundles; generation sits behind a deterministic readability gate rather than trusting the model. Not higher because the Vega media page documents no timing precision and the call crosses … — Design: Strong 10-foot design: lean-back viewing with optional word-level interaction (Play/Pause freezes, D-pad moves between words, OK replays one word), PIN-gated parent overlay, offline playback, VoiceView labels. Weaknesses: react-native-svg fallback scenes may look thin, and an India-first pitch with an English-only MVP is inconsistent. — Impact: Best-evidenced mechanism in the set (SLS literature, UNESCO evaluations, eye-tracking study cited in the Oct 3 digests), but that evidence covers subtitled songs and films, not AI-generated stories, and no consenting child testing is possible before submission, so efficacy cannot be claimed. — Originality: Direct visible collision: usv240/everyword (MIT, pushed Oct 3, 2026) ships the same Same Language Subtitling thesis on Fire TV with Polly speech-mark timing, read-again, slow mode, a published npm renderer and an MCP server (rival matrix; github.md). What remains is per-word D-pad replay and the household-adaptive next episode, a narrower wedge. — Feasibility: 125 h solo against about 104 h (estimate); even after dropping Nova Canvas and live generation it sits near 110 h. The runtime is single-device and offline-capable, which keeps demo risk low, but the binding risk is the day-1 VVD timing test and children's-content review time.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 4, feasibility 2 (A stick allows a real-device timing check only if the 4K Select is on OS 1.2 (listed 1.1, firetv.md section 4). Hours unchanged.); duo tech 4, feasibility 4 (At about 75 h each (estimate) a TV-app/generation split fits with buffer including episode review; the timing unknown and the everyword collision remain.); duo+hw tech 4, feasibility 4 (As duo_no_hw plus optional real-device timing validation.)
- *product* — Tech: Single-device Vega app driven by Polly speech marks and a word clock (prior mechanism) is a legitimate Vega build, but A/V timing precision on the VVD is unverified (gate day-one proof demands median error under 150 ms) and the generation pipeline is offline/pre-cached. usv240/everyword already shipped the same speech-mark karaoke on a real Fire TV with an MCP server (rival matrix), so the implementation does not st… — Design: Play/Pause word freeze, D-pad word stepping, OK replay and a parent overlay are device-appropriate and child-friendly at 10 feet. But the headline experience is a video player with subtitles, the rules' own Obvious Fire TV example (evt-rules.md L444), and no household co-viewing or accessibility-setting binding is specified. — Impact: Early-reader literacy via Same Language Subtitling has field evidence in India (prior pitch; everyword cites UNESCO/PlanetRead) and the TV viewing slot is habitual; audience is large. Discounted because the SLS evidence concerns subtitled songs/films, not AI-generated stories, there is no outcome data, and Amazon Kids+/PBS KIDS already serve this audience on Fire TV (rivals_weartv.md Matrix B; prior risks). — Originality: Direct visible collision: usv240/everyword (MIT, 66 commits, pushed Oct 3) does word-level SLS captions from Polly speech marks and Transcribe on Fire TV with a published npm renderer and real-device footage (rival matrix; gate crowding 'high'). Only the per-word replay and the household-adaptive next episode remain, and adaptive content is a latent LLM capability. — Feasibility: 125 h solo exceeds the about 104 h capacity; the gate's cut (react-native-svg scenes, pre-generated hand-reviewed episodes) brings it near 117 h with a day-1 timing kill test (prior gate). Single device, offline playback keeps demo risk low.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 4, feasibility 3 (Real-device timing check on a 4K Select addresses the one open technical question (word-clock precision) and reaches everyword's real-hardware parity; scope unchanged.); duo tech 3, feasibility 4 (75 h per person (prior estimate) fits; differentiation from everyword must still be visible in the first 30 s (gate).); duo+hw tech 4, feasibility 4 (Timing verified on hardware plus split build.)

**Round-1 judge ratings (3 Oct, same model; shown for traceability):** devrel 3/3/3/2/3 = 2.80; engineer 4/4/3/2/3 = 3.25; product 3/3/3/2/3 = 2.80

**Red-team verdicts:** not red-teamed (did not reach the top of either round's ranking); ranking rests on gate and judge scores alone.

#### FTV-3: BigPrint: a talking TV magnifier where the remote is the shutter

**Round:** 1. **Track:** Fire TV (primary). Mini challenges: AWS Builder, Open Source.

**Pitch:** For older adults with low vision, a spare phone on a cheap stand becomes the camera and the Fire TV becomes the large display of a video magnifier. They press OK on the remote and the paired phone, running a kiosk page, photographs the letter on the table. Within seconds the TV shows the page reflowed at 60–96 px in the viewer's high-contrast colors and reads it aloud sentence by sentence. It also shows a card answering what this is, who sent it, what they want, by when and how much. Every fact on the card has a 'Show me' action that zooms into the exact region of the photographed page, using Textract geometry, so the user can check it. 'Send to Priya' shares the card with a family helper only after an explicit on-screen confirmation.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Primary user | Adults aged 65 and over with low vision who live independently, plus one remote family helper (often an adult child). |
| Frequency | Several times a week: mail, bills, medicine labels and appointment letters. This is an assumption and has not been measured. |
| Severity | High for the tasks affected: missed due dates, medication confusion and loss of independence or privacy when someone else has to read the mail. NCOA reports that 37% of older adults with blindness or low vision have incomes below $20,000, so expensive aids are often out of reach. |
| Current workaround | Video magnifiers (CCTV): models that plug into a TV cost $400–$1,000, and stand-mounted models with an x-y table cost $1,800–$4,000 (AFB). Desktop units with OCR and speech list at $3,299–$4,998 at one retailer. Other options are handheld magnifiers, phone magnifier or reader apps (not verified in reviewed sources), or asking family to read the document over the phone. |
| Amazon integration and its indispensable role | Fire TV is the large-format reading display and the tactile control surface. The Vega app maps remote keys to camera actions: OK = capture, Up/Down = step through lines, Play = read aloud, Menu = share. It renders reflowed text in the system caption and accessibility styles (KeplerA11ySettingsInterfaceTurbo), zooms into evidence regions, and labels everything for VoiceView. The phone acts only as a fixed camera appliance. |
| Removal test (honest) | Strong. In the existing CCTV magnifier category, a large display is the whole point, and the TV is that display. Remove the TV and the product becomes a phone magnifier showing a few words at a time on a 6-inch screen, which is the pain point of the category. Honest caveat: cheap magnifiers that plug into a TV also use the TV (a Reddit snippet, not read, mentions $60–80 units). Our difference comes from AI reflow, fact cards grounded in the source and the remote-as-shutter design, not from the TV alone. |
| Wow moment under 45 s | 0:00 A phone on a stand points down at an electricity bill on the coffee table. The viewer presses OK on the remote. 0:04 The TV shows 'Electricity bill · Due 12 Oct · ₹2,340' in yellow on black at about 90 px. 0:12 Down then OK on 'Show me': the TV zooms into the photographed bill with a box around the amount. 0:22 Play: the reflowed text is read aloud while the current line is enlarged. 0:35 Menu, 'Send to Priya?', OK: a confirmation appears. Under 45 s. |
| Original mechanism | (1) Remote-as-shutter across devices. A TV key event becomes a WSS command to a paired phone page that keeps a live getUserMedia stream and Wake Lock. The phone grabs a full-resolution still and uploads it, so the older adult never handles the phone. (2) Fact cards grounded in the source. Textract AnalyzeDocument with QUERIES and LAYOUT returns answers with confidence scores and bounding boxes. Nova Lite may only restate facts in plain language using the Textract lines it was given, each with a line ID. Facts below a confidence threshold show as 'Not sure, show me' instead of a value. The TV renders the source crop from the geometry, so every claim can be checked by eye. Account numbers are masked to the last 4 digits on screen. |
| Simulator or real-data route (data route) | TV key OK sends a WSS 'capture' message to API Gateway, and Lambda posts it to the paired phone's connection. The phone takes a still from the live stream and does a presigned PUT to S3 (KMS-encrypted, deleted after 24 h). An S3 event triggers Lambda, which calls Textract AnalyzeDocument with queries such as 'Who is this from?', 'What is the amount due?', 'What is the due date?' and 'What action is requested?', plus LAYOUT. Lambda then calls Nova Lite with only the returned lines and IDs to produce the plain-language card, including line IDs. Polly synthesizes the sentence audio. The result goes to DynamoDB and is pushed to the TV over WSS. The TV fetches crops and audio through short-lived presigned GETs. Nothing is stored after 24 h unless shared. |
| Authentication | One-time pairing: the TV shows a QR code and the phone kiosk page claims a device-pair token (HMAC, rotatable). The helper is invited once from the TV with an explicit consent screen, and the helper link is scoped to shared cards only. No passwords. In-app copy states that documents go to AWS us-east-1 for processing. Account numbers are masked on the TV. |
| Preview constraints | The VVD has no camera, which is by design here, so a real phone is used. The VVD has no microphone, which is not needed. The demo runs on the VVD plus one real phone over HTTPS/WSS. VoiceView on the VVD is supported only by an entrant's report (Cinemastamps captured VoiceView speech on the simulator), which is anecdotal. Demo documents must be the team's own, redacted, or clearly synthetic sample bills labelled as such. |
| Hardware | Apple Silicon Mac with the VVD, one spare phone (Android or iPhone), a $10–15 phone stand and a desk lamp. Optional: a Fire TV Stick 4K Select for a real-device take with the new tactile remote (that remote ships with the US 3rd-gen stick; India availability not verified). |
| SDK versions | Vega SDK 0.24.x, RN 0.83 (0.72 as fallback), Node 20, @amazon-devices/kepler-a11y-settings-interface-turbo 1.0.0, @amazon-devices/react-native-qrcode-svg 3.0.0, @amazon-devices/react-native-w3cmedia 2.3.2, AWS SDK v3 (Textract, Polly, Bedrock Runtime). |
| Deployment prerequisites | Rosetta, Vega SDK and the VVD. A Paid-plan AWS account. Textract, Polly and Bedrock Nova Lite enabled in us-east-1, with quota checked on day 1. A KMS key and an S3 lifecycle rule. A budget alarm. Keep the endpoint live through 20 Nov 2026 with a judge-test phone page. |
| Firecrawl: research role | Used during ideation to establish category pricing and rivals (.firecrawl/idea-ftv-lowvision-magnifier.json), prevalence (.firecrawl/idea-ftv-lowvision-prevalence.json) and to verify the Textract AnalyzeDocument contract (.firecrawl/idea-ftv-textract-analyzedocument.md). |
| Firecrawl: runtime role | none |
| AWS Builder path | Textract AnalyzeDocument QUERIES and LAYOUT provide geometry and confidence that make each fact checkable. That is the core safety feature, not decoration. Bedrock Nova Lite is limited to restating Textract lines with citations. Polly reads aloud. API Gateway WebSocket carries the TV-to-phone shutter command. S3 with KMS and a 24 h lifecycle supports the privacy promise, and DynamoDB holds cards. A published evaluation over 30 documents of field accuracy and the 'not sure' rate gives judges a measured technical claim. Development uses Claude Code on Bedrock or Kiro Crew. |
| Open Source path | Release 'react-native-tv-reader' under MIT: a 10-foot reflow reader for RN for Vega, react-native-tvos and web. It includes D-pad line-stepping, a focus-safe zoom-to-bounding-box viewer that takes normalized geometry (Textract-compatible), VoiceView-friendly labels, caption-style binding through KeplerA11ySettingsInterfaceTurbo with a non-Vega fallback, and jest tests for the geometry math and focus order. This fills a visible gap: no reusable 10-foot document reader exists in the @amazon-devices port list (103 packages checked on npm). |
| Fallback | If the remote-triggered channel fails on Vega, have the phone capture automatically when the page is still for 1.5 s and push results to the TV by polling. If Textract QUERIES accuracy is low, use DetectDocumentText plus Nova Lite with line-ID citations, still showing evidence crops. If Vega is blocked, use Fire OS on a real stick through adb. |
| Falsification test (≤1 day) | One day. Photograph 30 of the team's own documents (bills, letters, medicine labels; redacted) with a phone on a stand under ordinary living-room light. Run Textract AnalyzeDocument QUERIES for sender, amount, due date and requested action. Kill or rescope if fewer than 90% of returned fields are correct or more than 20% fall below the confidence threshold. In parallel, ask 2–3 older adults (or teammates wearing low-vision simulation glasses if none are available, labelled as a simulation) to read the same letter on TV reflow and on a phone magnifier app. If the TV is not faster or preferred, the TV premise fails. |
| Risk profile | Low-medium technical risk: synchronous Textract plus a simple two-device command channel with a fixed phone. High sensitivity to trust, privacy and accuracy, because the documents are financial and medical. High crowding and collision risk among accessibility entries on Fire TV. Demo risk is low, since one person can record it. |
| Estimate (unmeasured) | Solo 115 h; two-person 70 h per person; cloud $15–30 through 20 Nov 2026 (estimate). Assumptions: All estimates. hours_duo is per person (TV app vs backend, phone kiosk and evaluation). Includes 14 h for Vega setup, 10 h for the 30-document evaluation, 10 h for the video and 8 h for submission. Cloud usage assumes about 500 pages analyzed in dev, evaluation and demo. Textract QUERIES pricing was not verified this session; an assumed ≤$0.02 per page gives ≤$10. Polly sentence audio about $1–2 (assumed rate). Nova Lite under $1 (verified rates). WebSocket, Lambda, S3, KMS and DynamoDB a few d… |

**Demand evidence** (quality labelled by the researcher):

- About 7 million people in the US have vision impairment, including 1 million who are blind. As of 2012, 4.2 million Americans aged 40 or older had uncorrectable vision impairment (CDC Fast Facts, published 15 May 2024). — [https://www.cdc.gov/vision-health/data-research/vision-loss-facts/index.html](https://www.cdc.gov/vision-health/data-research/vision-loss-facts/index.html) — *High. Government source, although some figures are older.*
- 7.3% of Americans aged 65 and over report being blind or having serious difficulty seeing even with glasses, rising to 9.8% at age 80 and over. 37% of this group have annual incomes below $20,000, and 33.3% also have a hearing impairment (NCOA, 1 May 2025, using BRFSS and ACS data). — [https://www.ncoa.org/article/understanding-older-adults-with-vision-loss-and-how-to-help/](https://www.ncoa.org/article/understanding-older-adults-with-vision-loss-and-how-to-help/) — *Medium-high. A nonprofit summary of federal survey data.*
- About 60% of people with vision impairment or blindness in the US are aged 65 and over, at least 8 million people according to prior studies. — [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9857701/](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9857701/) — *High. Peer-reviewed.*
- Video magnifiers are an established assistive-technology category that already uses a TV as the display. Models that plug into a TV cost $400–$1,000, and stand-mounted models cost $1,800–$4,000. Some include OCR and speech with word highlighting (ClearView+ Speech HD). — [https://afb.org/blindness-and-low-vision/using-technology/assistive-technology-products/video-magnifiers](https://afb.org/blindness-and-low-vision/using-technology/assistive-technology-products/video-magnifiers) — *Medium-high for category and price ranges (American Foundation for the Blind). The page is undated.*
- Amazon's Fire TV accessibility guidance (23 Jul 2026) makes remote-only journeys and VoiceView baseline expectations. The new tactile Fire TV remote (30 Sep 2026) has buttons designed to be identified without looking. — [https://developer.amazon.com/apps-and-games/blogs/2026/07/blueprint-series-pt5-accessibility](https://developer.amazon.com/apps-and-games/blogs/2026/07/blueprint-series-pt5-accessibility) — *First-party guidance. Supply direction, not user demand. The blog was read through a WebFetch summary.*

**Rivals named at generation** (strongest feature; our difference; evidence status):

| Rival | Strongest feature | Our difference | Evidence status |
|---|---|---|---|
| [ClearView+ Speech HD (Optelec/Vispero) via AFB listing](https://afb.org/blindness-and-low-vision/using-technology/assistive-technology-products/video-magnifiers) | Dedicated hardware: integrated OCR and text-to-speech that highlights each word as it is spoken, with optical-quality magnification. | Uses the TV and a spare phone the household already owns. Adds fact cards with 'show me' evidence and sharing with a helper. Their structured-fact features are not verified in reviewed sources. | Feature verified on the AFB listing. Price not on that page. |
| [DaVinci Pro HD OCR / Reveal 16 desktop magnifiers](https://nelowvision.com/product-category/low-vision-products/desktop-cctvs/) | Full HD camera with optical zoom (the Reveal 16 has up to 10x optical and 45x digital), purpose-built for reading mail and documents. | Costs nothing beyond a TV and a phone, compared with $3,299–$4,998 at retail. Remote-only control. AI summary with grounding. | Prices verified on the retailer page (2026-10-03). Reveal 16 features verified on the Humanware store page. |
| [E-Bot Pro (via AFB)](https://afb.org/blindness-and-low-vision/using-technology/assistive-technology-products/video-magnifiers) | Full-page OCR, and connects to a TV over HDMI or to an iPad over Wi-Fi. | No dedicated camera hardware. A native Fire TV app with D-pad and VoiceView. Grounded fact cards. | Verified on the AFB listing. Price not captured. |
| [ai-tv-vision-assistant (visible Fire TV entrant)](https://github.com/vikrant-29/ai-tv-vision-assistant) | Described as 'A D-pad-first AI vision assistant designed for Fire TV' (Flutter, Fire OS). | Its scope is unknown. Possible overlap. Ours targets document reading for low vision, with remote-triggered phone capture and evidence-region zoom. | Repo description only. README not read. |
| [Alexa+ on Fire TV](https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/devices/alexa-plus-fire-tv-free-ai) | Free conversational assistant on compatible US Fire TVs, giving answers on screen. | Reading physical documents through a paired camera is not verified in reviewed sources. Third-party apps cannot plug into Alexa+. | Digest (19 Aug 2026). |

**Smallest credible MVP:**

- Vega app with five screens: pair phone (QR), reading view (reflow with line-stepping), fact card, 'Show me' zoom, share confirmation
- Phone kiosk page: camera preview, Wake Lock, capture on WSS command, upload confirmation tone
- Backend: API Gateway WebSocket and HTTPS, Lambda, S3 (SSE-KMS, 24 h lifecycle delete), Textract AnalyzeDocument (QUERIES and LAYOUT, synchronous, ≤10 MB JPEG), Nova Lite restatement restricted to cited line IDs
- Polly sentence-level read-aloud of the reflowed text (no per-word highlight; a hardware rival already does that)
- Confidence threshold logic with 'Not sure, show me' states, and masking of long digit strings
- Helper share: card plus crop link sent to a pre-invited helper page after confirmation on the TV
- An evaluation script over 30 of the team's own redacted English documents, with field accuracy published in the README

**Deliberately excluded stretch:**

- Hindi and Indian-language documents (Textract language coverage for Hindi not verified)
- Handwriting
- Live video magnification streaming (stills only in the MVP)
- Any payment, form filling or action on the document (read-only by design)
- Medication interaction advice (out of scope, and a medical-device risk)

| Status | API / event / action | Documentation |

| verified | Amazon Polly SynthesizeSpeech plus sentence speech marks (read-aloud and line sync) | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/polly/latest/dg/speechmarks.html |
| verified | TVEventHandler key mapping (select, up, down, playPause, menu) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/vega/0.24/vega-rn-overview |
| verified | KeplerA11ySettingsInterfaceTurbo (caption font, color, background used for reflow styling) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/vega-api/0.24/README.amazon-devices_kepler-a11y-settings-interface-turbo |
| unverified | Assistive technologies on Vega (VoiceView announcements and labels) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/vega/0.22/assistive-technologies-vega |
| unverified | WebSocket in React Native for Vega 0.83 (capture command channel) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/react-native-vega/0.83/network.html |
| unverified | API Gateway WebSocket API with @connections | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/apigateway/latest/developerguide/apigateway-websocket-api.html |
