The Fire TV Vega app polls GET /board every 5 s over HTTPS, and Accept, Decline and Confirm actions POST back. When an item is accepted and owned by the wearer, the daemon runs `bee todos create --text ... --alarm-at ...`. Todo completion is detected through `bee changed` and closes the loop on the board only after counterparty confirmation. |
| Authentication | Bee: `bee login` after Developer Mode is unlocked on the wearer's iPhone. The token stays on the Mac, and the Bee proxy and MCP HTTP endpoints stay on 127.0.0.1 as the docs require. Backend: a per-household secret (32 or more random bytes) is created on first run. The Fire TV pairs by entering a 6-digit code shown on the laptop dashboard, exchanged for a scoped device token that a Lambda authorizer checks. There is no Login with Amazon in the MVP. AWS: a Lambda IAM role grants least-privilege access to Bedrock and DynamoDB. |
| Preview constraints | Bee's Help Center describes the developer API as still being built. The stream is at-most-once, and one entrant reports it dies after about 43 h with no replay. Todo events are documented but reported as never delivered; ticks show up via the changefeed about 35 s later (entrant report). `bee now` duplicates utterances (issue #3). Recordings under about 50 words are deleted (Amazon staff, forum 45111). Processing latency is undocumented. Speaker labels are anonymous (speaker_1) unless named in the app. Since 23 Sep 2026 new customers sign in with an Amazon account, and `bee login` behaviour for them is not documented. Bulk `bee sync` returns 500s (issue #14). Vega SDK 0.24: the VVD has no microphone, experimental APIs are being deprecated, macOS 27 needs Rosetta, Vega tooling wants Node 18-20, and the VVD can crash under memory pressure. |
| Hardware | Required: a Bee Pioneer ($49.99, ships only to the US) paired with an iPhone running the Bee iOS app (iOS 17.6+), or an iPhone plus an Apple Watch (watchOS 10+) running Bee. Watch ambient-capture parity with Pioneer is unverified, though a forum participant reports the Watch works as a recording source. Android is not viable from India (the app is US-only and a pairing block is reported). An Apple-silicon Mac runs the Bee CLI and the Vega Virtual Device (Vega tools need about 20 GB of disk; 48 GB is free). Optional: a Fire TV Stick 4K Select (Rs 5,499, Vega OS) for real-device footage. People: a second consenting adult on camera. |
| SDK versions | @beeai/cli 0.7.3: pin it, because main has an unreleased 'external-access' commit from 2026-08-17. Use Node 20 LTS via nvm (Vega pins Node 18-20; the system has Node 25.2.1). Vega SDK 0.24.x (RN 0.72 or RN 0.83 early-access variants) with a manifest targeting os.version 1.2. AWS SDK for JavaScript v3. Bedrock model IDs: Nova Lite and Claude Haiku 4.5 (us-east-1 or us-west-2). Optional: strands-agents 1.57.2 (Python) for the revision-linking agent. |
| Deployment prerequisites | Bee account, device data, and Developer Mode (tap the app version 5 times; at least one iOS user failed); `bee login` and `bee status` OK. Rosetta installed (`softwareupdate --install-rosetta --agree-to-license`), then the Vega SDK 0.24 tools and the VVD; register the VVD at amazon.com/code if Amazon services are needed. An AWS account on the Paid plan with Bedrock model access validated on day 1, given forum reports of zero quota or suspension on new accounts. API endpoint live through 20 Nov 2026. Repo public with an OSI licence, or private and shared with the six reviewers plus testing@devpost.com. |
| Firecrawl: research role | Pre-build and weekly: re-check rival features (Hearth, Caring Village, Skylight, Bee app release notes) and Bee docs pages for changes to stream or todo semantics, and scan new public Bee entrants for overlap. Evidence goes into the README's rival table with dates. |
| Firecrawl: runtime role | none |
| AWS Builder path | Real value: the Bee CLI must run on the laptop, but the TV needs a reachable, always-on endpoint. API Gateway HTTP API plus Lambda gives that endpoint, and DynamoDB conditional writes make the two-key transitions idempotent and race-free (no double accept from TV and laptop). DynamoDB TTL enforces the 72 h quote expiry server-side as data minimization rather than a promise. Bedrock runs Nova Lite for cheap extraction and Claude Haiku 4.5 only for revision linking. Build with Kiro Crew or Claude Code on Bedrock to satisfy AWS Builder, and describe each service in the Product Feedback. |
| Open Source path | An MIT-licensed repo with CI. Extract a small reusable package, `bee-cursor`: an exactly-once changefeed processor (persist the cursor after processing), utterance dedupe for issue #3, and a reconnecting wrapper for `bee stream`, all with tests and recorded fixtures labelled SIMULATED. Also publish a D-pad focus-safe kanban board component for Vega. Candidate upstream contribution: a docs PR to bee-cli about todo events not arriving on the stream, once reproduced. |
| Fallback | If the Vega app is not stable by about day 12, ship the board as a responsive web page on phone and laptop and submit to Bee only; the Bee rules are still satisfied. If attribution is poor, the wearer assigns the owner with one tap before the card reaches the TV, which keeps two-key acceptance. If the stream is unreliable, run changefeed-only polling at about 60 s. If no Bee hardware or Watch path is working by about day 5, this concept is ineligible: pivot to a non-Bee track. |
| Falsification test (≤1 day) | One day, with a Bee and a consenting partner. Wear Bee through one evening at home, run the extractor over that evening's real conversations from `bee changed`, and hand-label every promise made between the two people. Kill or pivot if any of these hold: (a) fewer than 3 real two-person promises occur (the frequency assumption is wrong); (b) owner and counterparty attribution from Bee speaker labels is right for fewer than about 70% of labelled promises even after naming speakers in the Bee app (most items would need manual assignment, and the capture advantage collapses); (c) the partner, shown 5 real proposal cards on a laptop mock of the TV board, says they would not bother to accept or decline them. These thresholds are judgment calls, fixed before running. |
| Risk profile | Integration-heavy and people-dependent: two platforms (Bee and Vega) and two humans. Highest build scope of the three (about 122 h solo, above solo capacity). Consent risk medium-high (non-wearer partner). Idea risk medium, because the wedge rests on rival features being unverified rather than proven absent. Technical risk medium: speaker attribution and the lossy stream. |
| Estimate (unmeasured) | Solo 122 h; two-person 70 h per person; cloud Estimate: about $5-15 through 20 Nov 2026. Bedrock Nova Lite extraction: 2,000 windows x (3k in + 400 out), about $0.55. Claude Haiku 4.5 revision and owner linking: 600 calls x (5k in + 400 out), about $4.20. Lambda, API Gateway, DynamoDB and CloudWatch at demo volume: about $0-5, mostly free tier. Prices come from the aws.amazon.com/bedrock/pricing Ohio table; us-east-1 is assumed equal. Not cloud: Bee Pioneer $49.99 plus US forwarding; optional Fire TV Stick 4K Select Rs 5,499.. Assumptions: Estimates, not measurements. Solo means one developer fluent in TypeScript and React Native working about 6.5 focused h/day for 18 working days, which gives about 117 h. At about 122 h, BEE-1 does not fit solo unless revision linking or the Fire TV app is cut. Breakdown in hours: setup (Bee Developer Mode and CLI, Rosetta, Vega SDK and VVD, Bedrock quota check) 10; ingestion daemon 10; extraction and speaker mapping 14; state machine, API, DynamoDB and tests 16; revision linking 8; completion c… |

**Demand evidence** (quality labelled by the researcher):

- Amazon frames Bee's job as catching 'the commitments you make on Mondays that disappear by Fridays' and says customers ask Bee what commitments they have lost track of. This shows the problem exists, but it is also native supply that we must not duplicate. — [https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/devices/bee-amazon-wearable-ai-device-new-features](https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/devices/bee-amazon-wearable-ai-device-new-features) — *OFFICIAL vendor statement (5 Jan 2026). Problem framing, not independent demand measurement.*
- A hands-on reviewer found that Bee produces dozens of suggested to-dos a day, many from TV or podcasts. Approving each to-do and fact one by one became unmanageable after a missed day, and speaker attribution was poor. — [https://www.forbes.com/sites/forbes-personal-shopper/article/best-ai-wearables/](https://www.forbes.com/sites/forbes-personal-shopper/article/best-ai-wearables/) — *HANDS-ON, single reviewer, affiliate links (byline 24 Jul 2026, updated 16 Sep 2026).*
- Romper's feature on 'administrative parenting' cites a 2026 systematic review of 50+ studies. Parents value digital tools but report overcommunication and multi-platform burden. — [https://www.romper.com/parenting/the-hidden-back-to-school-supply-parents-cant-buy-more-bandwidth-the-rise-of-administrative-parenting](https://www.romper.com/parenting/the-hidden-back-to-school-supply-parents-cant-buy-more-bandwidth-the-rise-of-administrative-parenting) — *PRESS citing peer-reviewed research (3 Aug 2026). The review itself was not read.*
- Parents are heavy AI users (84% use AI, 40% daily), and managing to-do lists is among the top admin uses of AI. — [https://menlovc.com/perspective/2026-the-state-of-consumer-ai/](https://menlovc.com/perspective/2026-the-state-of-consumer-ai/) — *SURVEY, n=5,067 US adults, published by a VC firm (16 Sep 2026). Methodology stated but not audited.*
- A reviewer wearing AI recorders concluded that consent 'should be the cost of entry'. This supports making the non-wearer's explicit acceptance part of the product. — [https://www.nbcnews.com/tech/tech-news/wore-bunch-ai-devices-was-probably-overkill-rcna263705](https://www.nbcnews.com/tech/tech-news/wore-bunch-ai-devices-was-probably-overkill-rcna263705) — *HANDS-ON/PRESS (16 Mar 2026). Evidence about consent norms, not demand for this product.*

**Rivals named at generation** (strongest feature; our difference; evidence status):

| Rival | Strongest feature | Our difference | Evidence status |
|---|---|---|---|
| [Bee native app (commitments, suggested to-dos, Actions)](https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/devices/bee-amazon-wearable-ai-device-new-features) | Automatic commitment and to-do extraction from ambient conversation, plus Actions that draft emails and calendar invites, all first-party and free with the device. | Acceptance by the counterparty, revisions that must be re-accepted, counterparty-confirmed completion, and a shared TV surface for the non-wearer. | Multi-party acceptance, revision chains and shared commitments are not verified in reviewed sources. Absence from Amazon pages is not proof that Bee lacks them. |
| [Otter (My Action Items)](https://otter.ai/) | Action items are auto-assigned to participants and linked to the moment they were said, with a weekly digest of incomplete items (blog, 22 Aug 2024). | Household rather than meeting context, ambient wearable capture rather than a meeting bot, and explicit counterparty acceptance and close-out. | Whether Otter has an acceptance or decline state or completion confirmation is not verified in reviewed sources. |
| [Hearth Display / Hearth Helper](https://hearthdisplay.com/pages/features) | A family wall display with assignable to-dos, marketed as 'delegate it, own it, and follow through'. Hearth Helper lets you text in events, with approval before upload. | Items originate from spoken conversation with a verbatim quote, rather than being typed or texted in. | Whether Hearth to-dos have an accept or decline state is not verified in reviewed sources. |
| [Caring Village](https://caringvillage.com/app/) | Care-coordination to-dos 'with a named owner' that people claim, plus a shared calendar and messaging. | Spoken capture, evidence pointers and revision linking. Claiming in Caring Village overlaps with our acceptance step, so acceptance alone is not novel. | Capture from conversation is not verified in reviewed sources. |
| [Alexa+ household features](https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/devices/new-alexa-top-features) | Family calendar, email or flyer to calendar event, 'when everyone is free' scheduling, and person-targeted reminders via Visual ID. | Commitments come from ambient Bee capture rather than being set by voice or app, and they need two-party acceptance. | No Bee-to-Alexa integration is verified in reviewed sources. |

**Smallest credible MVP:**

- Local ingestion daemon on the Mac: `bee stream --json` for liveness plus `bee changed --cursor` every 60 s as the completeness backstop, with the cursor persisted only after processing, utterance dedupe (issue #3), and fixture replay for tests labelled SIMULATED.
- One-time household setup: map Bee speaker labels to 2 named members. Unmapped speakers are dropped before any model call.
- Commitment extraction (Bedrock) producing an evidence pointer: conversation_uuid, a quote of 25 words or fewer, start_time, proposer and owner.
- Two-key state machine (proposed, accepted or declined, revision-proposed, re-accepted, done-claimed, done-confirmed) behind API Gateway, Lambda and DynamoDB conditional writes, with unit tests on every transition.
- Fire TV Vega app: a four-column board (Proposed / Accepted / Changed / Done), D-pad focus handling, Accept, Decline and Confirm-done, and pairing by a 6-digit code.
- Bee write-back: a wearer-owned accepted item becomes `bee todos create --alarm-at`; completion is detected via `bee changed`.
- Revision linking: a later utterance by the same counterpart that semantically matches an open item becomes a 'Changed' card that needs re-acceptance.
- Consent and purge: a first-run household consent screen on the TV, a 72 h TTL on unaccepted quotes, and decline deletes the quote.

**Deliberately excluded stretch:**

- Echo / Alexa voice acceptance (Vega voice is limited to the Content Launcher, and the VVD has no microphone)
- Location-based automatic completion evidence via `bee locations recent` (stretch only; needs multi-day data)
- A phone companion app (the TV plus a laptop dashboard is enough for the MVP)
- Households with more than one wearer, guests, or children's items
- Calendar sync, payments, Ring or Fire TV picture-in-picture
- Multi-household or public multi-user hosting

**APIs, events and actions** (status as assessed by the researcher; 'unverified' means not confirmed in reviewed docs, 'gated' means not available to entrants, 'simulated' means not real in the demo):

| Status | API / event / action | Documentation |
|---|---|---|
| verified | bee stream --types new-utterance,new-conversation,update-conversation --json (at-most-once SSE; no top-level event field) | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/realtime |
| verified | bee changed --cursor <meta.next_cursor> --json (agentic changefeed) | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/agentic-sync |
| verified | bee conversations transcript <id> --since <epochMs> --json | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/cli |
| verified | bee todos create --text ... --alarm-at <ISO8601> / bee todos update <id> --completed true | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/cli |
| unverified | todo-created / todo-updated stream events (documented, but an entrant reports they are never delivered; completions are read via bee changed instead) | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/realtime |
| verified | bee locations recent --from --to (completion evidence, stretch) | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/cli |
| verified | Vega SDK 0.24 install and Vega Virtual Device run (aarch64 vpkg on Apple silicon) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/vega/0.24/run-apps |
| verified | Vega WebView (only if used; cleartext HTTP blocked by default) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/vega/0.24/overview-of-webview |
| unverified | Amazon Bedrock on-demand inference (Nova Lite, Claude Haiku 4.5) via the Converse API | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/conversation-inference.html |
| unverified | DynamoDB condition expressions plus TTL for two-key transitions and quote expiry | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/Expressions.ConditionExpressions.html |
| simulated | Fixture replay of recorded `bee stream --json` lines for CI tests | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/realtime |

**Blockers:** Eligibility: without a Bee Pioneer (US shipping only) or an iPhone plus Apple Watch running Bee, real data cannot be recorded and the project fails the Bee rules. The forum's 'no device / generated audio' answer conflicts with the Official Rules, which prevail.; Developer Mode unlock failed for at least one iOS user, and `bee login` with the post-23-Sep-2026 Amazon-account sign-in is undocumented.; Speaker attribution is reported as poor (TechCrunch, Forbes), which directly threatens owner and counterparty mapping.; The Vega toolchain on macOS 27 needs Rosetta and Node 18-20, and the VVD can crash under memory pressure. This adds setup risk to a second platform.; The demo needs a second consenting adult. About a dozen US states require all-party consent, and EU AI Act Art. 50 transparency has applied since 2 Aug 2026.; Bee processing latency is undocumented, so the live 'card appears' moment may need a cut with a visible clock.

**Reasons judges might reject it:** It may read as 'a to-do list with extra steps' that overlaps Bee's native commitments and Actions.; Household coordination is a saturated theme (Alexa+ family calendar and Visual ID reminders, Skylight Assistant, Hearth, Cozi, Fambot).; Fire TV may look bolted on, since the board could be a phone page, which weakens the cross-device story.; Capturing a non-wearing partner's speech raises consent concerns that judges may weigh against the idea.; Live-data proof is fragile on camera: processing delay, speaker mislabels and the 50-word threshold.; Scope is broad for a solo builder, so polish (the Design criterion) may suffer.

**Gate result:** overall **conditional** (eligibility conditional, feasibility conditional; native overlap medium, visible crowding medium).

- Eligibility: Rules §4 (L156) require data 'recorded and processed through an actual Bee device or an Apple Watch running the Bee software', shown live in both the video and the code. The base-case user has neither. Bee Pioneer ships only to the US (Help Center), and the Android app is US-only with a reported pairing block (forum 45111). The iOS app is listed on the India App Store, but whether the service works for an India-based account is unverified (bee.md §7). The FAQ and forum 'no device' answers conflict with the rules, and the rules prevail (§11.4, D2). Eligible only if a real capture path exists.
- Feasibility: The 122 h solo estimate exceeds the roughly 117 h capacity; the concept itself says so. It spans two platforms (Bee CLI and the Vega app) and needs two people. Speaker attribution is reported as poor (TechCrunch 2026-05-24). The stream is at-most-once and reportedly dies after about 43 h. Todo events are reportedly never delivered on the stream; ringfence reports this, and it is a single-source anecdote. Processing latency is undocumented, and recordings under about 50 words are deleted (forum 45111).
- Conditions: (1) Hardware path A: an iPhone (iOS 17.6+) plus an Apple Watch (watchOS 10+) running the Bee app, whose recordings reach the CLI. Forum 45111 has a participant report that the Watch works. A Reddit snippet (not read) reports about 1 h recordings on a Watch Ultra. Ambient parity with Pioneer is unverified. (2) Or hardware path B: a Bee Pioneer ($49.99) shipped to a US address and forwarded to India. The address cannot be changed once processing starts. Forwarding lead time is unverified. (3) The account works from India: the new Amazon-account sign-in (since 2026-09-23), Developer Mode unlocked by tapping the app version 5 times, and `bee login` plus `bee status` both OK. Login with an Amazon-account sign-in is undocumented. (4) Hardware and a working CLI are in hand by about day 5, or the concept is abandoned (it is ineligible without real data). (5) A second consenting adult is on camera, and all recordings exceed 50 words. (6) Scope fits solo: drop the Vega board and use the concept's web-board fallback, or work as a 2-person team. (7) Optional: a written clarification request under Rule 11.6 if any part relies on a Watch-only account.
- Native overlap: Bee natively extracts the wearer's commitments and suggested to-dos and offers Actions (aboutamazon, 2026-01-05). The capture and extraction half is native. The two-key acceptance, revision chain and counterparty-confirmed completion were not found in Bee, Otter, Hearth or Caring Village, but that is absence from pages, not proof (rivals_home). Caring Village's claimable to-dos overlap the acceptance step.
- Crowding: The Bee track is the least crowded, at about 7–10 visible repos (github.md). johnwils/kept ('Bee-powered commitment tracker for developers') and carrerasdarren-cell/commitment-brief overlap the commitment theme. Household coordination is saturated across tracks, with at least 10 Alexa+ household replanning or handoff repos.
- Day-one proof: With hardware: Developer Mode is on and `bee login` plus `bee status` succeed. One consented, over-50-word, two-person exchange containing a promise is recorded. `bee changed --cursor --json` returns it with two distinct speaker labels and a conversation_uuid. The extractor emits one proposal with the correct owner and counterparty. `bee todos create --alarm-at` writes back and the todo appears in the Bee app.
- Redesign to pass: If no Bee or Watch capture path works by about day 5, it is ineligible and should not be built. If hardware exists, cut the Vega app (submit to Bee only) to fit solo hours, and keep the two-key ledger and the counterparty-confirmed close-out as the visible wedge.

**Round-2 judge ratings (1–5; base case: solo, India, VVD on Mac, no Bee hardware, no US Ring device). Weighted = 0.25·Tech + 0.20·Design + 0.20·Impact + 0.20·Originality + 0.15·Feasibility.**

| Judge lens | Tech | Design | Impact | Originality | Feasibility | Weighted | Confidence | One-line verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| devrel | 1 | 3 | 2 | 3 | 1 | 2.00 | low | Ineligible without a Watch; even with one it is over capacity and depends on poor speaker attribution. |
| engineer | 1 | 3 | 2 | 3 | 1 | 2.00 | medium | Ineligible in the base case; with hardware and a partner it is a reasonable cross-device Bee entry whose wedge rests on rival features being unverified rather than absent. |
| product | 1 | 3 | 2 | 3 | 1 | 2.00 | medium | Ineligible without a Watch; even then a saturated household-coordination theme where Bee already extracts commitments. |
| **Mean of three** | 1.00 | 3.00 | 2.00 | 3.00 | 1.00 | **2.00** | | Scenario weighted: solo+hw 2.73; duo 2.00; duo+hw 2.97 |

**Why each judge rated it that way (verbatim rationale, one per criterion):**

- *devrel* — Tech: Ineligible in the base scenario: Rules §4 requires data recorded through an actual Bee device or Apple Watch (JDR-02); the user has neither, Pioneer ships US-only (bee.md §7). — Design: Bee + Fire TV orchestration is a named creative example (JDR-03) and reaches the non-wearer with no install, but the TV is admitted to be a surface choice and capturing a non-wearer's speech raises consent issues. — Impact: Frequency inferred, severity medium (friction, late fees); evidence is Amazon vendor framing and one hands-on review. — Originality: Two-key acceptance with verbatim evidence not found in Bee, Otter, Hearth or Caring Village, but Bee natively extracts commitments and kept/commitment-brief overlap the theme. — Feasibility: No capture path; cannot be completed eligibly.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 3, feasibility 2 (iPhone+Watch makes it eligible but Watch ambient parity is unverified (bee.md §8) and 122 h exceeds ~104 h.); duo tech 1, feasibility 1 (Still no capture path.); duo+hw tech 4, feasibility 3 (70 h each; speaker labelling (TechCrunch) remains the risk.)
- *engineer* — Tech: Base case has no Bee Pioneer and no Apple Watch, so no data can be 'recorded and processed through an actual Bee device or an Apple Watch running the Bee software' (Oct 4 live rules copy, evt-rules.md L348) and the runtime hook (L342) cannot be met with real data; Stage 1 fails. Design, impact and originality below are scored on concept merit. — Design: The two-key board reaches the non-wearer through the shared TV with no install, matching the organizers' cross-device example, but Fire TV reads as bolted on (the board could be a phone page) and poor speaker attribution (TechCrunch 2026-05-24; App Store reviews) threatens owner/counterparty mapping. — Impact: Medium-severity household friction; evidence is Amazon's own framing of Bee commitments, one hands-on reviewer and a VC survey; the frequency of two-person promises is unmeasured. — Originality: The second key (counterparty acceptance, re-acceptance of revisions, confirmed close-out) is a real twist on Bee's native commitment extraction, but Caring Village's claimable to-dos overlap acceptance, household coordination is saturated across tracks, and kept/commitment-brief share the theme. — Feasibility: Ineligible without hardware (Pioneer ships US-only per the Bee Help Center; Android blocks at pairing per forum 45111; Watch-only path unverified from India, bee.md sections 7-8). At 122 h it also exceeds about 104 h across two platforms.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 4, feasibility 2 (With iPhone plus Watch the build (stream and changefeed cursor processing, DynamoDB conditional two-key transitions with TTL, todo write-back, Vega board) is real, but 122 h exceeds about 104 h across two platforms, Wat…); duo tech 1, feasibility 1 (A second person does not create Bee device data; still ineligible.); duo+hw tech 4, feasibility 3 (At about 70 h each (estimate) it fits; attribution, processing latency and the 50-word threshold (forum 45111) remain.)
- *product* — Tech: Base scenario has no Bee device or Apple Watch; Rules L348 require data 'recorded and processed through an actual Bee device or an Apple Watch running the Bee software' shown live (evt-rules.md). Without hardware the project is ineligible, matching the prior judges' base tech 1. — Design: Two-key promise board for the non-wearer on a Fire TV surface with verbatim quotes and revision chips is coherent, but the TV is a bolted-on surface (removal test says a phone page works), consent for the non-wearing partner is a design burden, and live-data proof on camera is fragile (50-word threshold, speaker labels). — Impact: Household coordination is saturated (Alexa+ family features, Skylight, Hearth, Cozi, Google CC; rivals_home.md finding 4 and heat map) and Bee natively extracts commitments (rivals_weartv.md finding 1). Evidence that the second adult fails to accept is vendor claims (trends_home.md T8). Two-adult Bee households are a small audience. — Originality: Counterparty acceptance, re-acceptance on revision and confirmed close-out are a hypothesis gap not verified in Bee, Otter, Granola or Hearth (rivals_weartv.md implication C1), but Caring Village's claiming overlaps acceptance and johnwils/kept is a visible Bee commitment tracker (gate crowding). — Feasibility: Ineligible in the base scenario; even with hardware the 122 h estimate exceeds solo capacity and needs a second consenting adult on camera (prior gate).
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 3, feasibility 2 (iPhone plus Watch running Bee makes it eligible; Watch ambient-capture parity with Pioneer is unverified (bee.md section 8), so tech capped at 3. 122 h solo forces cutting the Vega board.); duo tech 1, feasibility 1 (Still no real Bee data; ineligible.); duo+hw tech 3, feasibility 3 (70 h per person with hardware; people and consent dependence remain.)

**Round-1 judge ratings (3 Oct, same model; shown for traceability):** devrel 1/3/2/3/1 = 2.00; engineer 1/3/2/3/1 = 2.00; product 1/3/2/3/1 = 2.00

**Red-team verdicts:** not red-teamed (did not reach the top of either round's ranking); ranking rests on gate and judge scores alone.

---

#### BEE-2: Explain-Back: on-wrist retrieval coaching from your own tutoring sessions

**Round:** 1. **Track:** Bee (primary). Education priority use case; matches the organizers' 'on-wrist coaching for learning' and 'Agent Skills' examples.

**Pitch:** After a tutoring session or consented lecture captured by Bee, Explain-Back schedules spaced 'explain it back in a minute' prompts as Bee todos. The student answers aloud with the Bee button, and an Agent Skill grades the spoken explanation against the tutor's exact words. It shows what was covered, what was missed and any misconception, then writes mastery back to Bee as facts and books the next targeted prompt.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Primary user | University or college students, and self-learners preparing for exams, who attend 1:1 or small-group tutoring, office hours, or lectures where the instructor consents to recording. The student is the Bee wearer. Answers are given alone, so answer capture has no bystanders. |
| Frequency | Inference: several sessions per week, with review moments daily during term and exam periods. Not measured. |
| Severity | Medium to high in exam periods (inference). Re-reading notes is the default habit, while practice testing has a moderate average effect in research syntheses (weighted mean 0.49 across 5 meta-analyses). |
| Current workaround | Re-reading notes or Bee summaries. Bee Templates turn a lecture into a study plan. AI flashcard apps (RemNote, Studr, StudyFetch) generate cards from a phone recording or PDF, and students tap or type answers. Spoken explanations graded against the source session were not verified in any reviewed rival. |
| Amazon integration and its indispensable role | Bee is both input and output. It captures the source session (tutor plus student), carries the student's spoken answers (button voice notes or short conversations), delivers prompts as Bee todos with alarms, and stores mastery as Bee facts that Bee's own chat and insights can use. The logic ships as an Agent Skill that runs on the Bee CLI or MCP, as the track rules allow. |
| Removal test (honest) | Without Bee, a student could record with a phone into RemNote, Studr or StudyFetch and type answers. The 'session to questions' half survives unchanged; that part is commodity. What dies is the Bee-dependent loop: hands-free spoken explain-back answers in the same data store as the source session, the context gate (no prompts during a live conversation), and mastery written back into Bee facts. So Bee is indispensable to the explain-back loop but not to question generation. We deliberately cut any flashcard UI so the product is the Bee-dependent loop. Flagged weakness: if voice-note answers cannot be captured reliably (the 50-word threshold, latency, the Watch path), the product degrades into a clone of the rivals. |
| Wow moment under 45 s | The student's phone (and wrist, if mirroring works) shows the Bee todo 'Explain-Back #4: why doesn't a catalyst change the equilibrium constant? Answer aloud, about 1 minute.' The student presses the Bee button and explains for about 70 words, including one plausible misconception. Seconds to minutes later (the real delay is shown with a clock), the dashboard shows 2 key ideas in green, 1 missing idea in grey, and the misconception in red, next to the tutor's exact quote and timestamp from yesterday's session. In the Bee app, the todo is completed, a targeted follow-up todo appears for tomorrow 8:00, and a new fact appears: 'Can explain: catalysts and equilibrium (mastered 2026-10-14)'. |
| Original mechanism | An evidence-anchored explain-back loop. (1) A speaker allow-list (tutor plus student, chosen once per session) drops everyone else's utterances before any model call. (2) A rubric of key ideas is built for each session, each idea anchored to verbatim tutor utterances with timestamps. (3) Prompts always ask for a roughly one-minute explanation, so answers clear Bee's ~50-word processing threshold; the platform constraint becomes pedagogy (elaborative retrieval). (4) Prompts are released as Bee todos with alarm_at by a context gate: no active conversation in `bee now` or the stream in the last 5 minutes, and the student is preferably at a 'study' location cluster. (5) The answer arrives as a Bee voice note (`journal-created` / `journal-text`) and is linked to the open prompt. A grader returns covered, missing and misconception, each with side-by-side quotes from the tutor and the student.… |
| Simulator or real-data route (data route) | Bee Pioneer or Watch > Bee cloud > Bee CLI on the Mac. `bee stream` and `bee changed` detect a processed session, and `bee conversations transcript` fetches it. The speaker allow-list filter is applied locally, and only the filtered tutor and student utterances go to Bedrock for rubric and prompt generation. Rubric, prompts and anchors are stored locally (SQLite). The scheduler runs `bee todos create --alarm-at` when the context gate passes, and the alarm reaches the phone (wrist unverified). The student presses the Bee button and answers, producing `journal-created` / `journal-text` events (or `bee changed`); the answer is fetched with `bee journals get`. The answer plus the anchored rubric goes to the Bedrock grader. Results feed `bee todos complete`, a follow-up `bee todos create --alarm-at`, `bee facts create` on mastery, and a local dashboard update. Raw transcripts stay on the Mac… |
| Authentication | Bee: `bee login` with Developer Mode, token on the Mac; the Skill uses the stored CLI login (Bee docs say the Skill can complete its own auth flow). Bedrock: a local AWS profile (IAM user or SSO) with least-privilege bedrock:InvokeModel. The dashboard is localhost only in the MVP. If hosted for judges, it shows a redacted demo dataset behind Cognito. |
| Preview constraints | Recordings under about 50 words are deleted (forum 45111); whether the threshold also applies to voice notes is unverified. The journal (push-to-talk) path is what a Bee contributor recommends for command-like use (issue #4), but Apple Watch parity is unverified. An entrant observed todo alarms buzzing the phone but not the watch. The stream is at-most-once. Processing latency is undocumented. Transcripts 'can be a bit of a mess' (TechCrunch), which matters for technical terms. Bee MCP answers MCP 2024-11-05. Developer Mode is required, and Amazon-account sign-in is undocumented for the CLI. |
| Hardware | Preferred: a Bee Pioneer ($49.99, US shipping only), because its button gives push-to-talk voice notes, paired with an iPhone running the Bee iOS app. Alternative: an iPhone plus an Apple Watch (watchOS 10+) running Bee; the voice-note answer path and wrist alarms are unverified. A Mac runs the CLI, the Skill and the dashboard. People: a consenting teammate acting as tutor for demo sessions. No TV or Echo. |
| SDK versions | @beeai/cli 0.7.3 (pinned). bee-skill repo at main (no releases; GitHub detects no LICENSE file, so do not vendor it, reference it). strands-agents 1.57.2 (Python) or @strands-agents/sdk 1.19.0 (TS). PyPI mcp 2.3.0 if an own MCP wrapper is needed. Bedrock Claude Haiku 4.5 for grading and Nova Lite for prompt drafting, in us-east-1 or us-west-2. |
| Deployment prerequisites | Bee device or Watch recording, Developer Mode, `bee login` OK. Real sessions must start by about day 5, so that 1/3/7-day spacing shows up in real data before the video. AWS account with Bedrock model access validated on day 1. A written consent note from the demo tutor. The repo must show the Bee CLI or MCP actually called at runtime (Skill scripts invoking `bee`), not just named. |
| Firecrawl: research role | Pre-build: learning-science evidence (practice testing, self-explanation) and rival feature checks (RemNote, Studr, StudyFetch, Bee Templates). Already done: three searches saved as .firecrawl/idea-bee-retrieval-practice.json, idea-bee-lecture-flashcard-rivals.json and idea-bee-lecture-recording-policy.json. During the build: monitor Bee docs for journal and todo event changes. |
| Firecrawl: runtime role | none |
| AWS Builder path | Strands Agents runs the Explain-Back Skill with Bedrock models: Nova Lite drafts prompts cheaply, and Claude Haiku 4.5 grades with structured output. Bedrock batch inference (50% off for select models) re-grades the labelled evaluation set whenever the rubric prompt changes, giving a reproducible agreement number in the README. Built with Kiro Crew or Claude Code on Bedrock. Honest limit: the Bee CLI must run locally, so AgentCore Runtime adds little here, and the AWS story is mostly model hosting plus evaluation. Judges may rate the AWS depth as modest. |
| Open Source path | Publish `explain-back` as an installable Agent Skill (`npx skills add <repo>`), MIT licensed. It includes the anchored-rubric grader prompt, the Leitner scheduler and the context-gate logic, with unit tests on fixture transcripts labelled SIMULATED. Real transcripts are never committed; only aggregate agreement numbers from consented real sessions are reported. This is a reusable pattern for any Bee-based coaching skill. |
| Fallback | If voice-note answers fail, accept answers as button-started short Bee conversations (60 words or more) linked by time window. If wrist delivery fails, deliver prompts on the phone via the Bee todo alarm and keep answering on the wrist, stating this honestly in the video. If lecture consent is unavailable, use 1:1 tutoring or office-hours sessions with a consenting tutor. If grader agreement is low, show coverage hints rather than scores and add a one-tap self-assessment. |
| Falsification test (≤1 day) | At most one day. With a consenting teammate as tutor, record two 8-minute mini-tutorials through Bee, then give 8 spoken explain-back answers of about 60-90 words each by Bee voice note. Kill or pivot if: (a) answers do not reach the CLI as journal events or conversations within about 10 minutes, or short answers are dropped; (b) the grader agrees with a human rubric on fewer than 6 of 8 answers; (c) fewer than 8 of 10 generated prompts are answerable from the anchored tutor quotes (the transcript is too messy for technical terms); (d) the todo alarm reaches neither wrist nor phone. If (a) or (b) fails, the product collapses into a flashcard app that rivals already ship. |
| Risk profile | Data-path and efficacy risk. Moderate build scope (about 109 h solo) on a single platform with no TV, Echo or second app. The highest uncertainty is in Bee-specific paths: voice-note answers, the 50-word threshold and wrist delivery. Idea crowding is high in the consumer study-app market but low among visible Bee entrants (no education entrant found among the roughly 10 visible Bee repos). Consent risk medium for the tutor or lecturer; low for answers, which are given alone. |
| Estimate (unmeasured) | Solo 109 h; two-person 62 h per person; cloud Estimate: about $5-20. Claude Haiku 4.5 grading: 500 answers x (4k in + 500 out), about $3.25. Prompt and rubric generation: 40 sessions x (10k in + 2k out), about $0.80. Repeated evaluation runs, about $2-10. Optional static dashboard on S3 and CloudFront, about $1, or $0 if local. Prices are from the Bedrock pricing page (Ohio table, us-east-1 assumed equal). Not cloud: Bee Pioneer $49.99 plus forwarding, if no Apple Watch path.. Assumptions: Estimates, not measurements. Solo is about 6.5 focused h/day over 18 working days (about 117 h available), so it fits with about 8 h buffer. Breakdown in hours: setup 6; ingestion and speaker allow-list 8; anchored rubric and prompt generation 12; scheduler, context gate and todo alarms 8; answer capture via journals with fallback 10; grader and calibration on the labelled set 16; fact write-back 3; Skill packaging and runner 8; dashboard 10; tests 8; real-data sessions across at least 7 calend… |

**Demand evidence** (quality labelled by the researcher):

- Practice testing (retrieval practice) across 5 meta-analyses, 744 studies and 222,708 students has a weighted mean effect size of 0.49. — [https://www.visiblelearningmetax.com/influences/view/practice_testing](https://www.visiblelearningmetax.com/influences/view/practice_testing) — *Research aggregator (Visible Learning MetaX), undated page. Secondary synthesis of meta-analyses.*
- In a 2025 peer-reviewed study, retrieval practice improved performance on never-before-seen application questions compared with other conditions, which supports 'explain and apply' prompts over rote cards. — [https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S0959475225001434](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S0959475225001434) — *Peer-reviewed article on ScienceDirect (2025). Lab experiments, not a field trial.*
- Counter-evidence: two preliminary online (Prolific) experiments in 2026 found no significant testing effect despite delayed tests and feedback. The benefit is not guaranteed in every setting. — [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12894256/](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12894256/) — *Peer-reviewed open access (2026), preliminary, online sample. Contrarian evidence.*
- Amazon says many customers first used Bee for 'work and school life' to record 'meetings, lectures', and Bee Templates organize 'lecture content into study plans'. Students do use Bee for this, and Bee covers the summary step natively. — [https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/devices/bee-amazon-wearable-ai-device-new-features](https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/devices/bee-amazon-wearable-ai-device-new-features) — *OFFICIAL vendor statement (5 Jan 2026).*
- The organizers' guidance says that for Bee, 'on wrist coaching for learning' is what they are hoping to see. — [https://amazonappdev2026.devpost.com/updates/46456-got-an-idea](https://amazonappdev2026.devpost.com/updates/46456-got-an-idea) — *Organizer guidance (about 18 Sep 2026). Judge signal, not user demand.*
- Constraint evidence: Illinois State University prohibits student recording in instructional settings without explicit advance written consent from the instructor and the written agreement of other students who may be captured. — [https://policy.illinoisstate.edu/academic/4-1-22/](https://policy.illinoisstate.edu/academic/4-1-22/) — *OFFICIAL institutional policy (one university). Shows the consent design is necessary, not that all institutions are the same.*

**Rivals named at generation** (strongest feature; our difference; evidence status):

| Rival | Strongest feature | Our difference | Evidence status |
|---|---|---|---|
| [RemNote](https://www.remnote.com/) | AI flashcards and practice quizzes generated from notes, PDFs or lecture transcripts, with spaced repetition and per-topic mastery tracking; claims more than 1M students. | Answers are spoken explanations captured hands-free on the wrist and graded against the source session's verbatim tutor quotes, timed by Bee's conversation and location context, with results written back into Bee. | Whether RemNote grades spoken free-form explanations against the source is not verified in reviewed sources. |
| [Studr](https://studr.app/blog/ai-note-taking-app-with-flashcards/) | Lecture audio, PDF or YouTube in; summary, flashcards and quiz out, with FSRS-style spaced repetition (vendor blog, 20 May 2026). | No phone recording step, a spoken explain-back answer channel, and a context-gated prompt schedule. | Vendor blog with a conflict of interest. Spoken-answer grading is not verified in reviewed sources. |
| [StudyFetch (Live Lecture)](https://www.studyfetch.com/section/live-ai-note-taker-for-lectures) | Real-time lecture transcription into notes, plus flashcards with spaced repetition, quizzes and a 24/7 AI tutor. | Wearable capture, wrist answers, and anchored-rubric grading with mastery stored in Bee. | Whether StudyFetch's tutor grades spoken explanations against lecture quotes is not verified in reviewed sources. |
| [Bee Templates (native)](https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/devices/bee-amazon-wearable-ai-device-new-features) | Organizes lecture content into study plans automatically, first-party. | Turns the session into active retrieval with graded spoken answers and spaced follow-ups, rather than a study plan. | Bee retrieval practice or answer grading is not verified in reviewed sources. |
| [Otter Education agent](https://otter.ai/) | Automatically captures lectures, discussions and study sessions as searchable notes and summaries. | A retrieval-coaching loop rather than notes. | Otter quizzing or grading is not verified in reviewed sources. |

**Smallest credible MVP:**

- Session ingestion on `update-conversation` / `bee changed`: fetch the transcript, apply the tutor-plus-student speaker allow-list, and drop all other utterances before any LLM call.
- A key-idea rubric plus 5-10 explain-prompts per session, each anchored to tutor utterance quotes and timestamps.
- Leitner scheduler (1/3/7 days) emitting `bee todos create --text --alarm-at`, with a context gate that defers a prompt if Bee shows an active conversation in the last 5 minutes.
- Answer capture via Bee voice notes (journal events, then `bee journals get`), linked to the most recent open prompt. Fallback: a button-started short conversation of 60 words or more, linked by time window.
- Grader: covered, missing and misconception with side-by-side quotes. Results complete the todo, create a targeted follow-up todo, and write a Bee fact on mastery.
- Packaged as an Agent Skill (`explain-back/SKILL.md` plus scripts, agentskills.io format) that runs in Claude Code, Kiro or a Strands agent, using the Bee CLI and MCP.
- A minimal web dashboard: a concept mastery map with links to the exact source moments.
- An evaluation: at least 30 of our own consented answers, human-labelled, with grader agreement reported in the README.

**Deliberately excluded stretch:**

- A flashcard or tap-to-reveal UI (deliberately cut; it is the rivals' strength)
- Classroom-wide or teacher dashboards and LMS integration
- FSRS scheduling (Leitner is enough for the MVP)
- Echo or Fire TV study sessions
- Non-English sessions (Hindi is supported by Bee, but grading quality is untested)
- Recording real university lectures without written instructor consent

**APIs, events and actions** (status as assessed by the researcher; 'unverified' means not confirmed in reviewed docs, 'gated' means not available to entrants, 'simulated' means not real in the demo):

| Status | API / event / action | Documentation |
|---|---|---|
| verified | bee stream --types update-conversation,journal-created,journal-text --json | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/realtime |
| unverified | journal-created / journal-text emitted for answers recorded on Apple Watch (rather than the Pioneer button) | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/realtime |
| verified | bee changed --cursor (completeness backstop for conversations, journals and todos) | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/agentic-sync |
| verified | bee conversations transcript <id> --json / bee journals get <id> --json | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/cli |
| verified | bee todos create --text ... --alarm-at <ISO8601> / bee todos complete <id> | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/cli |
| unverified | Todo alarm delivered to the wrist (an entrant observed phone-only delivery) | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/cli |
| verified | bee facts create --text ... / bee facts update <id> --confirmed true | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/cli |
| verified | bee now --json (context gate) / bee locations current, bee locations clusters (study-place gate) | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/cli |
| verified | Bee Agent Skill (npx skills add bee-computer/bee-skill) as the base the Explain-Back skill builds on | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/skill |
| verified | Bee MCP tools bee_create_todo, bee_create_fact, bee_get_conversation_transcript, bee_get_voice_note (stdio; negotiates 2024-11-05 per entrant report) | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/mcp |
| verified | Agent Skills format (SKILL.md, progressive disclosure) | https://agentskills.io/home |
| verified | Strands Agents MCP client (stdio) running the Skill with Bedrock models | https://strandsagents.com/docs/user-guide/sdk/tools/mcp-tools/ |

**Blockers:** Eligibility: no Bee Pioneer (US-only shipping) and no iPhone plus Apple Watch path means no real data and no valid Bee submission.; The voice-note answer path is unverified on Apple Watch, and whether the ~50-word deletion threshold applies to voice notes is unknown.; On-wrist prompt delivery is unverified: an entrant saw Bee todo alarms reach the phone, not the watch.; Recording real lectures needs instructor (and sometimes classmate) written consent under some institutional policies, so demo sessions need a consenting tutor.; Spaced repetition needs several calendar days of real data before the video.; LLM grading errors could mislead a student; the agreement rate must be measured and shown.

**Reasons judges might reject it:** The AI study-app category is crowded (RemNote, Studr, StudyFetch, NoteHive, Otter, Bee Templates), so judges may file it as 'transcription plus quiz'.; The learning benefit of LLM-graded spoken explanations is unproven, and the testing effect does not replicate in every setting (2026 Prolific study).; Consent around lecture recording may concern judges if real classes appear.; The 'on-wrist' claim weakens if prompts visibly arrive on the phone.; A teammate acting as tutor may look staged unless the Bee app and CLI timestamps are shown as live.; The AWS Builder angle is modest (mostly Bedrock).

**Gate result:** overall **conditional** (eligibility conditional, feasibility conditional; native overlap low, visible crowding low).

- Eligibility: It has the same hard gate as all Bee concepts: the rules require real data captured by a Bee device or an Apple Watch running Bee (L156), and the base-case user has neither. The preferred answer channel, push-to-talk voice notes, is a Pioneer button feature. On a Watch, the journal-created and journal-text events are marked unverified. It is eligible only with a real capture path and a consenting human tutor whose sessions are recorded.
- Feasibility: The 109 h solo estimate fits (estimate). The binding constraint is the calendar: the 1/3/7-day Leitner spacing needs real sessions starting by about day 5, which depends on when hardware arrives. Whether voice-note answers reach the CLI from a Watch, whether the roughly 50-word deletion threshold applies to voice notes, and whether todo alarms reach the wrist (one entrant saw phone-only delivery) are all unverified. The Bee contributor's guidance to use journal-text for command-like use comes from issue #4 (bee.md §3). Grader agreement must be measured.
- Conditions: (1) Hardware path A (iPhone plus Apple Watch running Bee, with recordings reaching the CLI) or path B (a US-shipped and forwarded Pioneer), and the account works from India with Developer Mode and `bee login`, as for BEE-1. (2) Real tutoring sessions start by about day 5 so that the spaced intervals appear in real data before the video. (3) Written consent from the demo tutor. No real lectures recorded without instructor (and, where the policy requires, classmate) consent. (4) If the Watch voice-note path fails, answers fall back to button- or Watch-started short conversations of 60 words or more, linked by time window. The video must state this. (5) If prompts arrive on the phone rather than the wrist, the video says so (the 'on-wrist' claim is limited).
- Native overlap: Bee Templates natively turn lectures into study plans, and voice notes are native (aboutamazon, 2026-01-05). Spaced explain-back prompts graded against the tutor's verbatim words were not found in Bee, RemNote, Studr, StudyFetch or Otter in reviewed sources; that is absence from pages, not proof.
- Crowding: No education entrant among the roughly 7–10 visible Bee repos. Cross-track, the mechanism exists in Alexa+ entrants: khalidbench1-collab/Recall_Amazon ('Grades what you said aloud'), senih25/recall-alexa-mcp (hands-free spaced repetition) and corepilotlabs/yasmin-voice-tutor. Novelty within Bee rests on wearable capture of the source session.
- Day-one proof: Record one consented 8-minute mini-tutorial through Bee, then give 3 spoken explain-back answers of 60–90 words. Each answer must reach the CLI (journal event or `bee changed`) within about 10 minutes and not be dropped. A `bee todos create --alarm-at` prompt must fire on the wrist or the phone. The grader must agree with a hand rubric on at least 2 of 3 answers.
- Redesign to pass: If no capture path is working by about day 5, it is ineligible; pivot to another track. If only a Watch is available, design answers around short conversations from the start rather than voice notes, and frame prompts as 'on your phone and wrist'.

**Round-2 judge ratings (1–5; base case: solo, India, VVD on Mac, no Bee hardware, no US Ring device). Weighted = 0.25·Tech + 0.20·Design + 0.20·Impact + 0.20·Originality + 0.15·Feasibility.**

| Judge lens | Tech | Design | Impact | Originality | Feasibility | Weighted | Confidence | One-line verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| devrel | 1 | 3 | 3 | 4 | 1 | 2.40 | low | The most Bee-literate idea in the set, blocked by hardware and a tight calendar. |
| engineer | 1 | 4 | 3 | 4 | 1 | 2.60 | medium | Best Bee idea on merit and closest to organizer guidance, but ineligible without hardware and calendar-bound by its spaced intervals. |
| product | 1 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 1 | 2.20 | medium | The best-evidenced Bee learning loop, but ineligible without a Watch and crowded by phone study apps. |
| **Mean of three** | 1.00 | 3.33 | 3.00 | 3.67 | 1.00 | **2.40** | | Scenario weighted: solo+hw 3.13; duo 2.40; duo+hw 3.37 |

**Why each judge rated it that way (verbatim rationale, one per criterion):**

- *devrel* — Tech: Ineligible in base scenario (JDR-02); generated or fixture data fails the live-data test. — Design: Uses Bee's own surfaces (todo alarms as prompts, button voice notes) and matches 'on-wrist coaching for learning' (JDR-03); capped because wrist alarm delivery and the Watch voice-note path are unverified, so 'on-wrist' may become 'on-phone'. — Impact: Retrieval practice has a meta-analytic base and Amazon cites students using Bee for lectures; recording policies and unproven LLM grading cap it. — Originality: Turns the ~50-word deletion threshold into pedagogy, anchors grading to the tutor's verbatim utterances, writes mastery back as facts ('advance use of facts'); discounted because it is the organizers' own suggested example. — Feasibility: No capture path in the base scenario.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 3, feasibility 2 (Eligible with Watch, but 109 h exceeds capacity and real sessions must start by day 5.); duo tech 1, feasibility 1 (Still ineligible.); duo+hw tech 4, feasibility 3 (62 h each; Pioneer button preferred over Watch for answers.)
- *engineer* — Tech: No Bee device or Watch in the base case, so it cannot use real Bee-recorded data and fails the Bee rule (evt-rules.md L348) at Stage 1. Merit scores below assume it is built. — Design: Best wearable fit of the Bee concepts and the closest match to the organizers' 'on-wrist coaching for learning' example: prompts as Bee todos with alarms, spoken explain-back answers, mastery written back as facts, and the 50-word deletion threshold turned into one-minute elaborative answers. Risks: push-to-talk voice notes are a Pioneer button feature and wrist delivery of todo alarms is unverified (an entrant saw … — Impact: Retrieval practice has a moderate meta-analytic effect (Oct 3 digests) and the education priority use case is named by the rules; LLM-graded spoken explanations are unproven and grader agreement must be measured. — Originality: No education entrant among the six saved Bee repos (gh2-bee-wearable.json); grading a spoken explain-back against the tutor's verbatim timestamped utterances behind a context gate is distinct from RemNote, Studr, StudyFetch and Bee Templates. Cross-track Alexa+ entrants (Recall_Amazon, recall-alexa-mcp) already grade spoken recall. — Feasibility: Ineligible without a Pioneer or a working Watch path; the 1/3/7-day spacing also needs real sessions by about day 5.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 4, feasibility 2 (109 h exceeds about 104 h (estimates), the Watch voice-note answer path is unverified, and the spaced-interval calendar must start by about day 5; a consenting tutor is needed.); duo tech 1, feasibility 1 (Still ineligible without Bee hardware.); duo+hw tech 4, feasibility 3 (At about 62 h each (estimate) grader calibration and the dashboard fit; the spacing calendar remains the binding constraint.)
- *product* — Tech: No Bee hardware in the base scenario; ineligible under Rules L348 (evt-rules.md). — Design: Explain-back prompts sized to clear Bee's 50-word threshold, a context gate so prompts never fire mid-conversation, and side-by-side tutor/student quotes are thoughtful wearable-context design. 'On-wrist' may degrade to phone delivery and the Watch voice-note path is unverified (gate conditions). — Impact: Students are a large audience and retrieval practice has an evidence base (prior pitch), though a 2026 replication caveat exists (prior risks). The crowded study-app market (RemNote 1M+ students, Studr, StudyFetch, Hekko; rival matrix) and Otter's Education agent (bee2-otter-education.md: syllabus-based study questions) show demand but also strong substitutes. — Originality: Graded spoken recall exists in visible Alexa+ entrants (Recall_Amazon, recall-alexa-mcp; gate crowding) and the session-to-questions half is commodity; the wearable-captured source session plus hands-free answers written back as Bee facts is the novelty. No education entrant among visible Bee repos (gh2-bee-wearable.json). — Feasibility: Ineligible without hardware; with hardware 109 h exceeds solo capacity and sessions must begin by about day 5 (prior gate).
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 3, feasibility 2 (Eligible with iPhone plus Watch; Pioneer button voice notes preferred and unavailable, so answers become short Watch conversations; 109 h over capacity.); duo tech 1, feasibility 1 (Ineligible without real Bee data.); duo+hw tech 3, feasibility 3 (62 h per person; the spaced-interval calendar still constrains the demo.)

**Round-1 judge ratings (3 Oct, same model; shown for traceability):** devrel 1/3/3/4/1 = 2.40; engineer 1/4/3/4/1 = 2.60; product 1/3/3/3/1 = 2.20

**Red-team verdicts:** not red-teamed (did not reach the top of either round's ranking); ranking rests on gate and judge scores alone.

---

#### BEE-3: TriedThat: spoken debugging memory that stops coding agents repeating your dead ends

**Round:** 1. **Track:** Bee (primary). Developer-experience priority use case; candidate for AWS Builder (Claude Code on Bedrock) and Open Source mini-challenges.

**Pitch:** While you debug aloud or pair, Bee hears 'I bumped the timeout to 30 seconds and it still flakes'. TriedThat turns that narration into symbol-linked attempt records. When you hand the bug to Claude Code, hooks inject what you already ruled out, with your exact words. If the agent starts editing toward a ruled-out fix, TriedThat stops and asks. The pull request gets a cited 'ruled out during debugging' section only after you tick a Bee todo.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Primary user | Individual developers and pairs who debug aloud or pair-program and then hand work to coding agents (Claude Code, Kiro). Solo narration mode has no bystanders. Pair mode requires the partner's opt-in. |
| Frequency | Inference: daily for developers who use coding agents. Agents start each session without the human's spoken reasoning. Not measured; the falsification test measures repeat attempts directly. |
| Severity | Medium: wasted agent turns and tokens, wrong fixes merged, review time. The top AI frustration in the 2025 Stack Overflow survey was solutions that are 'almost right, but not quite' (66%), followed by debugging AI code taking more time (45.2%). |
| Current workaround | Typing context into the prompt or CLAUDE.md (rarely done mid-debug), re-explaining after the agent repeats a failed fix, or connecting Bee's own MCP to Claude Code (`bee mcp connect claude-code`) and asking the agent to search transcripts manually. That baseline is the main substitute to beat. |
| Amazon integration and its indispensable role | Bee is the sole input: spoken debugging narration, processed into speaker-labelled utterances and read via CLI, stream and changefeed. It is also the consent channel: a Bee todo tick approves quoting in a PR, read back through `bee changed`. Optionally, AWS hosts a team copy of derived attempt records (no transcripts) on AgentCore Runtime, and Claude Code runs on Bedrock. |
| Removal test (honest) | Without Bee, developers would have to type what they tried mid-debug, which in practice they rarely do (assumption), so the product has no input. Bee is indispensable as the source. The harder test is removing our layer instead: Bee MCP plus Claude Code already lets an agent search transcripts. If that baseline avoids repeated dead ends as well as TriedThat, our product is a thin wrapper and Bee alone delivers the value. The falsification test is built around exactly this baseline. |
| Wow moment under 45 s | Split screen. Left: the developer, wearing Bee, debugging a flaky checkout test aloud: 'I bumped the timeout to 30 seconds and it still fails, so it's not timing. I also flushed Redis, no change...' (60+ words). Right: the terminal runs `claude "fix the flaky checkout test"` twice on the same commit. Run 1 (no TriedThat): the agent's first edit raises TIMEOUT_MS. Run 2 (TriedThat): the first message shows 'Ruled out by you at 14:02 (Bee conversation 6531525): timeout to 30s, Redis flush', with quotes, and the agent investigates clock skew instead. When it later tries to edit TIMEOUT_MS, a PreToolUse prompt appears: 'Matches a ruled-out attempt (quote). Proceed?' |
| Original mechanism | A symbol-grounded attempt ledger with a deterministic collision guard. (1) A spoken-identifier aligner maps transcribed tokens to the repo's symbol table (tree-sitter or ctags index; camel/snake splitting; phonetic plus edit-distance match), so 'retry with back off' resolves to retryWithBackoff() in src/net/retry.ts. (2) An extractor turns narration windows into attempt records: hypothesis, action, outcome, verdict (ruled-out, confirmed or unknown), symbols, quotes, and Bee conversation id and timestamps. (3) Claude Code hooks: UserPromptSubmit adds matching ruled-out attempts as additionalContext. PreToolUse on Edit, Write and Bash scores the proposed change against ruled-out attempts (symbol overlap plus text similarity) and returns permissionDecision 'ask' with the quote as the reason. (4) The PR provenance section is appended only after the developer ticks a Bee todo ('Share 2 debug… |
| Simulator or real-data route (data route) | Bee > Bee cloud > Bee CLI on the developer's Mac. `bee stream` provides the live timeline; `bee changed` plus `bee conversations transcript --since` close gaps. The speaker allow-list filter is applied, and filtered windows go to Bedrock (Claude Haiku 4.5) for attempt extraction. The aligner resolves symbols against the local repo index. Attempt records (derived text, quotes of 25 words or fewer, ids, symbols) are stored in local SQLite and served by our MCP server over stdio. Claude Code hooks call a local CLI that queries the store. On PR creation, a Bee todo asks for consent; when `bee changed` shows the tick, `gh pr edit` appends the section. Raw transcripts never leave the Mac except filtered windows sent to Bedrock. In the stretch version, only derived records are synced to AgentCore. |
| Authentication | Bee: `bee login` with Developer Mode, token on the Mac; Bee MCP and proxy stay on localhost. Own MCP server: stdio (no network auth); the optional local HTTP mode needs a bearer token of 32 or more characters and binds to 127.0.0.1. GitHub: `gh auth` (the user's existing login). Bedrock: an AWS profile with least privilege. The stretch AgentCore Runtime uses AgentCore Identity (IAM or OAuth inbound). |
| Preview constraints | Bee's built-in MCP negotiates 2024-11-05 (entrant report), below the hackathon's 2025-11-25 floor for Alexa+, so our own MCP server targets 2025-11-25 and calls Bee via CLI. The stream is at-most-once and reportedly dies after about 43 h. Todo events are reportedly never delivered on the stream. Recordings under about 50 words are deleted, so short asides are lost. Speaker labels are anonymous by default. Claude Code hook schemas evolve; pin the Claude Code version used in the demo. AgentCore is unavailable on the AWS Free plan. |
| Hardware | A Bee Pioneer ($49.99, US shipping only) or an iPhone plus Apple Watch (watchOS 10+) running Bee, with an iPhone running the Bee iOS app. A Mac with the target repo, the Bee CLI and Claude Code. No TV, Echo or second person needed in solo mode. |
| SDK versions | @beeai/cli 0.7.3 (pinned). @modelcontextprotocol/sdk 1.32.0 (TS v1 line; main-branch LATEST_PROTOCOL_VERSION is 2025-11-25, so test negotiation explicitly). Claude Code: the version current at build time, pinned in the README (hooks per code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks). Tree-sitter or universal-ctags: versions to be pinned at build time. Optional strands-agents 1.57.2. Bedrock Claude Haiku 4.5 (extraction) and Claude Sonnet 5.5 (agent runs in the A/B). |
| Deployment prerequisites | Bee recording, Developer Mode, `bee login` OK. A sample repo with 5 seeded, reproducible bugs. Claude Code configured (optionally on Bedrock for the AWS Builder claim, per forum guidance). Bedrock model access validated on day 1. For the stretch: a Paid AWS plan for AgentCore. A README section stating the MCP protocol version actually negotiated. |
| Firecrawl: research role | Pre-build: verify hook semantics (done: .firecrawl/idea-bee-claude-code-hooks.json), developer-frustration evidence (done: .firecrawl/idea-bee-dev-context-demand.json) and ADR practice (done: .firecrawl/idea-bee-design-rationale.json). Weekly: re-scan new public Bee entrants (kept, ringfence and others) to keep the differentiation honest. |
| Firecrawl: runtime role | none |
| AWS Builder path | Run Claude Code on Bedrock (the forum says this qualifies) and use Bedrock for extraction. The value-adding AWS piece is a stretch goal: host the derived attempt store as an MCP server on AgentCore Runtime with AgentCore Identity, so a teammate's agent on another machine can consult 'what has been ruled out on this bug' without any transcript leaving the wearer's Mac. AgentCore Observability traces show which attempts were injected or blocked. Bedrock model costs for the A/B are reported in the README. |
| Open Source path | Publish `spoken-symbols`, a small library that aligns transcribed speech to code identifiers (identifier splitting, phonetic and edit-distance matching against a tree-sitter or ctags index), with a test corpus of spoken identifiers. It is reusable by any voice-coding or meeting-to-code tool. Also publish the Claude Code hook pack and Agent Skill under MIT. Candidate upstream: reproduce and document the stream todo-event gap for bee-cli, if confirmed. |
| Fallback | If the A/B shows the Bee MCP baseline is as good, narrow the product to the consent-gated PR provenance plus the deterministic PreToolUse guard (still Bee-dependent) or drop the concept. If identifier alignment is weak, prompt the developer to say file names (easier fuzzy matching) and retrieve by time window around commit timestamps. If hook APIs change, fall back to an Agent Skill plus MCP tool the agent calls voluntarily, which is less deterministic and must be stated as such. |
| Falsification test (≤1 day) | At most one day. Seed 5 reproducible bugs in a sample repo. For each, debug aloud with Bee for 3-5 minutes (50 words or more), ruling out 2 hypotheses. Then run Claude Code three ways on the same commit: (A) no Bee; (B) Bee MCP connected via `bee mcp connect claude-code` plus the instruction 'check what I already tried'; (C) TriedThat hooks. Count ruled-out approaches repeated in the agent's first 3 edits, and turns and tokens to a passing test. Kill if C does not clearly beat B (for example, B already avoids repeats in 4 or more of 5 bugs), because then the value is Bee MCP itself. Also kill if the aligner maps fewer than about 80% of 30 spoken identifiers correctly. |
| Risk profile | Adoption and crowding risk. Lowest hardware and consent risk of the three (solo narration, laptop only, no second platform). Moderate scope (about 108 h solo). Technical risk is concentrated in identifier alignment and collision scoring. Highest originality risk among visible Bee entrants. Impact must be proven with an A/B against the native Bee MCP baseline rather than asserted. |
| Estimate (unmeasured) | Solo 108 h; two-person 62 h per person; cloud Estimate: about $30-60. Extraction with Claude Haiku 4.5: 1,500 windows x (3k in + 400 out), about $7.50. A/B harness: 45 coding-agent runs (5 bugs x 3 conditions x 3 repeats) at an assumed 250k input + 15k output tokens each on Claude Sonnet 5.5 via Bedrock ($2/$10 per M), about $29; prompt caching would lower this, and run size is an assumption. Stretch AgentCore Runtime at about $0.0067 per session (vendor example), under $5. Prices are from the Bedrock and AgentCore pricing pages.. Assumptions: Estimates, not measurements. Solo is about 6.5 focused h/day over 18 working days (about 117 h available), so it fits with about 9 h buffer. Breakdown in hours: setup 5; ingestion 8; symbol index, aligner and tests 16; attempt extraction and labelled evaluation 14; MCP server and Skill 10; Claude Code hooks 8; collision scoring 8; PR provenance and Bee todo consent 6; timeline view 6; A/B harness 10; real narration sessions 5; video, README and friction log 12. hours_duo is hours per person whe… |

**Demand evidence** (quality labelled by the researcher):

- The 2025 Stack Overflow survey (AI section) found that the biggest single AI-tool frustration, cited by 66%, is 'AI solutions that are almost right, but not quite'. The second is 'Debugging AI-generated code is more time-consuming' at 45.2%. — [https://survey.stackoverflow.co/2025/ai](https://survey.stackoverflow.co/2025/ai) — *SURVEY (2025, large developer sample). The vendor blog below swaps the 66% and 45% labels, a contradiction; the survey page figures are used.*
- 75% of developers would still ask another person for help when they don't trust AI's answers, and trust in AI accuracy fell to 29%. Human context still matters. — [https://stackoverflow.blog/2025/12/29/developers-remain-willing-but-reluctant-to-use-ai-the-2025-developer-survey-results-are-here/](https://stackoverflow.blog/2025/12/29/developers-remain-willing-but-reluctant-to-use-ai-the-2025-developer-survey-results-are-here/) — *VENDOR-BLOG summarizing its own survey. Its 66/45 framing conflicts with the survey page.*
- A practitioner review of ADRs lists 'The context is underspecified' as the first common mistake and stresses recording why an option was chosen. Rationale is routinely lost between discussion and writing. — [https://ozimmer.ch/practices/2026/09/12/ADRMistakes.html](https://ozimmer.ch/practices/2026/09/12/ADRMistakes.html) — *PRACTITIONER essay (12 Sep 2026), experience-based, not measured.*
- A Bee owner piped `bee stream` to a server to drive agent actions and found the transcript stream 'too lossy for reliable action automation'. Developers are wiring Bee into agents, and reliability (changefeed backstop) is a real requirement. — [https://github.com/bee-computer/bee-cli/issues/4](https://github.com/bee-computer/bee-cli/issues/4) — *COMMUNITY, single user report plus one Bee contributor reply (May-Jun 2026).*
- Builder interest signal: at least 3 visible hackathon Bee repos target developer workflows (kept, ringfence, Mental-Model-Drift). This shows perceived demand but is also the main crowding risk. — [https://github.com/harshpuri84/ringfence](https://github.com/harshpuri84/ringfence) — *Supply-side signal from GitHub repos (Sep 2026). Not user demand.*

**Rivals named at generation** (strongest feature; our difference; evidence status):

| Rival | Strongest feature | Our difference | Evidence status |
|---|---|---|---|
| [kept (hackathon entrant, johnwils)](https://github.com/johnwils/kept) | Spoken commitments and decisions become GitHub issues and ADRs with transcript evidence, plus a Bee todo, with confidence thresholds (designed on Strands, Bedrock and AgentCore). | Debugging attempts and outcomes rather than decisions or commitments; injected into the coding agent at handoff, with a collision guard rather than filed as documents. | README read: status 'Phase 0 - scaffold' (13 Sep 2026); the stream daemon was not built at that date. |
| [ringfence (hackathon entrant, harshpuri84)](https://github.com/harshpuri84/ringfence) | Spoken tasks start a sandboxed coding agent on its own branch. Questions, results and PR approvals travel as Bee todos. Policy 'rings' bound what a wrong guess costs, and the README reports measured cost per run and a shadow-day replay. | A pull model: the agent is started by the developer, and TriedThat supplies negative knowledge (what was ruled out) plus a deterministic edit-time guard, rather than starting agents from speech. | README read (pushed 29 Sep 2026). Mature and evidence-heavy; a strong competitor in the same track. |
| [Mental-Model-Drift (hackathon entrant)](https://github.com/Marc-Dvci/Mental-Model-Drift) | Per its description, 'Bee hears the claim; AWS AppConfig, Sentry and the repository settle' whether an engineer's belief about a system still matches reality. | Checks agent actions against the developer's ruled-out attempts, not beliefs against production state. | Repo description only; README not read. |
| [Bee MCP connected to Claude Code (native baseline)](https://docs.bee.computer/docs/mcp) | `bee mcp connect claude-code` exposes 34 Bee tools (search, transcripts, facts, todos) to the coding agent with no extra code. | Structured, symbol-linked attempt records and hooks that inject context and block repeats deterministically, rather than relying on the agent choosing to search. | Documented. Whether the baseline already prevents repeats is untested; this is the falsification test. |
| [Granola (MCP connector)](https://www.granola.ai/) | Meeting notes and 'what did I promise' chat across meetings, exposed to other agents through an MCP connector. | Ambient capture of informal debugging talk (not scheduled meetings) and code-symbol grounding. | Granola debugging or attempt extraction is not verified in reviewed sources. |

**Smallest credible MVP:**

- Capture daemon: `bee stream --json` plus `bee changed --cursor`, restricted to sessions the developer tags as debug. Speaker allow-list: the wearer, plus a pair partner only with opt-in.
- Repo symbol index (tree-sitter or ctags) and a spoken-identifier aligner with unit tests on at least 30 spoken identifiers.
- Attempt extractor producing records with verdicts and evidence pointers, plus a small human-labelled evaluation.
- Own MCP server (spec 2025-11-25, stdio and local Streamable HTTP) with tools find_attempts, get_attempt and mark_attempt, plus an Agent Skill describing when to call them.
- Claude Code hooks: UserPromptSubmit injects context; PreToolUse on Edit\|Write\|Bash returns 'ask' with a quote on collision.
- PR provenance: append a cited section on `gh pr create` only after a Bee todo tick (read via `bee changed`).
- A/B harness: 5 seeded bugs x 3 conditions (no Bee / Bee MCP baseline / TriedThat) x 3 repeats, with logs committed.

**Deliberately excluded stretch:**

- ADR or issue generation (kept's territory)
- Starting agents from speech (ringfence's territory)
- Team-shared attempt store on AgentCore Runtime (stretch)
- IDE plugins (VS Code, JetBrains) and Kiro hooks parity
- Fire TV or Echo surfaces
- Multi-repo or monorepo symbol federation

**APIs, events and actions** (status as assessed by the researcher; 'unverified' means not confirmed in reviewed docs, 'gated' means not available to entrants, 'simulated' means not real in the demo):

| Status | API / event / action | Documentation |
|---|---|---|
| verified | bee stream --types new-utterance,update-conversation --json | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/realtime |
| verified | bee changed --cursor (completeness, plus todo-tick consent detection) | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/agentic-sync |
| verified | bee conversations transcript <id> --since <epochMs> --json / bee search --query ... --neural --since --until --json | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/cli |
| verified | bee todos create --text 'Share 2 debugging notes in PR #42?' / completion read via bee changed | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/cli |
| verified | Bee MCP `bee mcp serve` (stdio, 34 tools; answers protocol 2024-11-05 per entrant report), used as the A/B baseline | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/mcp |
| verified | Claude Code hooks: UserPromptSubmit (additionalContext) and PreToolUse (hookSpecificOutput.permissionDecision ask/deny with permissionDecisionReason) | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks |
| verified | Own MCP server on spec 2025-11-25 (tools, Streamable HTTP transport) | https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25/basic/transports |
| gated | AgentCore Runtime MCP hosting for team-shared attempt records (Paid plan only) | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/runtime-mcp.html |
| unverified | GitHub CLI gh pr create / pr edit (append provenance section) | https://cli.github.com/manual/gh_pr_create |
| simulated | Seeded-bug repo and replayed Bee fixtures for CI (labelled SIMULATED) | https://docs.bee.computer/docs/realtime |

**Blockers:** Eligibility: without a Bee Pioneer (US-only) or an iPhone plus Apple Watch running Bee, there is no real data and no valid submission.; Behavioural adoption: many developers do not talk aloud while debugging, so the demo must not look staged and the target user must be framed narrowly.; Transcription of code identifiers may be poor, which is assumed and is why the aligner exists; if alignment fails, the guard is noisy.; Workplace recording policies and teammates' consent for pair mode.; Crowding: kept, ringfence and Mental-Model-Drift already target Bee plus developer workflow.; Bee MCP protocol version (2024-11-05) and stream quirks (43 h drop, missing todo events) need workarounds.

**Reasons judges might reject it:** Originality discount: at least 3 visible Bee entrants already target developer workflows, and ringfence is mature and evidence-heavy.; May be judged niche: debugging aloud is uncommon behaviour.; A terminal-heavy demo scores weaker on the Design criterion.; If the A/B delta over `bee mcp connect claude-code` is small, judges will see a wrapper.; Workplace privacy concerns about always-on recording at work.

**Gate result:** overall **conditional** (eligibility conditional, feasibility pass; native overlap medium, visible crowding high).

- Eligibility: Same hardware gate: the rules require real data captured by a Bee device or an Apple Watch running Bee (L156), and the base-case user has neither. Once real capture exists, it satisfies the runtime hook directly (`bee stream`, `bee changed`, transcripts and todos are called at runtime) and needs no second person in solo mode.
