The concept correctly avoids relying on Bee's built-in MCP for protocol compliance, because entrants report it negotiates 2024-11-05.
- Feasibility: Assuming the hardware condition holds: 108 h solo fits the roughly 117 h capacity (estimate). It is a single platform on a laptop. Claude Code hooks (UserPromptSubmit, PreToolUse with permissionDecision) are verified (.firecrawl/idea-bee-claude-code-hooks.json). The risk sits in identifier alignment and in the A/B result against the native `bee mcp connect claude-code` baseline. Cost note: the $30–60 A/B estimate uses Claude on Bedrock. Whether the promotional credits cover Anthropic models is unverified (the AWS Marketplace is excluded; event.md §4), so this may be out-of-pocket unless Nova is used for extraction and the number of runs is reduced. The AgentCore stretch needs a Paid plan.
- Conditions: (1) Hardware path A or B, and the account works from India with Developer Mode and `bee login`, as for BEE-1. Hardware must be in hand by about day 5. (2) Narration sessions exceed 50 words. Pair mode only with the partner's opt-in. (3) The Claude Code version is pinned in the README, because the hook schemas evolve. (4) The A/B budget is confirmed: either credits cover the Bedrock model chosen, or runs are capped or switched to Nova.
- Native overlap: `bee mcp connect claude-code` natively exposes 34 Bee tools (search, transcripts, facts, todos) to the coding agent with no extra code (docs.bee.computer/docs/mcp). That is the main substitute. The deterministic PreToolUse guard and the symbol-grounded attempt records are not native. The concept rightly makes beating this baseline its kill test.
- Crowding: Within the small Bee field, 3 of roughly 7–10 visible repos target developer workflows: johnwils/kept (decisions to issues and ADRs; a scaffold as of 09-13), harshpuri84/ringfence (mature and evidence-heavy) and Marc-Dvci/Mental-Model-Drift (github.md). It has the highest originality risk among the Bee concepts.
- Day-one proof: Debug one seeded bug aloud with Bee for 3–5 minutes, ruling out 2 hypotheses with over 50 words. The narration reaches the CLI via `bee changed` or a transcript. The aligner maps at least 80% of 10 spoken identifiers to repo symbols. On that bug, run Claude Code with and without the hook. The TriedThat run must avoid both ruled-out edits that the `bee mcp connect claude-code` baseline repeats or risks repeating.
- Redesign to pass: If no capture path works, it is ineligible; pivot. If hardware exists, keep the scope narrowed to what the native Bee MCP baseline cannot do: the deterministic edit-time guard and the consent-gated PR provenance. Publish the A/B logs.

**Round-2 judge ratings (1–5; base case: solo, India, VVD on Mac, no Bee hardware, no US Ring device). Weighted = 0.25·Tech + 0.20·Design + 0.20·Impact + 0.20·Originality + 0.15·Feasibility.**

| Judge lens | Tech | Design | Impact | Originality | Feasibility | Weighted | Confidence | One-line verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| devrel | 1 | 3 | 2 | 2 | 1 | 1.80 | low | Clever mechanism in a crowded Bee sub-theme; ineligible without hardware. |
| engineer | 1 | 2 | 2 | 3 | 1 | 1.80 | medium | Technically rigorous with an honest kill test, but ineligible without hardware, terminal-bound on Design, and in the most crowded Bee niche. |
| product | 1 | 2 | 2 | 2 | 1 | 1.60 | medium | Lowest-scoring concept: niche developer behaviour, crowded Bee developer corner, weak design surface, ineligible without hardware. |
| **Mean of three** | 1.00 | 2.33 | 2.00 | 2.33 | 1.00 | **1.73** | | Scenario weighted: solo+hw 2.60; duo 1.73; duo+hw 2.75 |

**Why each judge rated it that way (verbatim rationale, one per criterion):**

- *devrel* — Tech: Ineligible in base scenario (JDR-02). — Design: Developer experience is a Bee priority use case and the hook design (UserPromptSubmit context, PreToolUse 'ask' with the quote) is well considered, but the demo is terminal-heavy and depends on developers narrating while debugging. — Impact: The frustration is documented (Stack Overflow 2025) but the target behaviour is niche and the impact claim rests on an unrun A/B test. — Originality: Three of the ~8-9 visible Bee repos are developer-workflow tools (ringfence, kept, Mental-Model-Drift) and native bee mcp connect claude-code is the baseline to beat. — Feasibility: No capture path in the base scenario.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 4, feasibility 2 (Solo-narration mode needs no second person and hooks are verified, but 108 h slightly exceeds ~104 h.); duo tech 1, feasibility 1 (Still ineligible.); duo+hw tech 4, feasibility 3 (62 h each.)
- *engineer* — Tech: No Bee device or Watch in the base case, so no real narration can be captured and the Bee rule (evt-rules.md L348) fails. Merit scores below assume it is built. — Design: Claude Code hooks are the right surface for developers, but the demo is terminal-heavy (a known Design weakness), debugging aloud is uncommon behaviour, and the wearable adds nothing visible beyond being the microphone. — Impact: Stack Overflow 2025 supports the general 'almost right' AI pain, not this fix; value must be proven by an A/B against the native bee mcp connect claude-code baseline and the target behaviour is narrow. — Originality: Negative-knowledge injection with a deterministic PreToolUse guard and spoken-identifier alignment is distinct, but 3 of the roughly 8-9 visible Bee repos target developer workflows (kept, ringfence, Mental-Model-Drift) and the native Bee MCP already exposes 34 tools to Claude Code. — Feasibility: Ineligible without a Pioneer or working Watch path from India (bee.md sections 7-8).
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 4, feasibility 3 (108 h sits about 4 h over about 104 h (estimates) but the PR-provenance piece is a clean cut; single platform, no second person. The $30-60 A/B may be out of pocket if credits do not cover Anthropic models on Bedrock (a…); duo tech 1, feasibility 1 (Still ineligible without Bee hardware.); duo+hw tech 4, feasibility 4 (At about 62 h each (estimate) the aligner/evaluation and hooks/MCP/PR work split cleanly and the A/B can be run with enough repeats.)
- *product* — Tech: No Bee hardware in the base scenario; ineligible under Rules L348. — Design: Terminal-and-hooks experience for a coding agent; the rules' Bee track is consumer-flavoured (education, developer experience, productivity) but the demo is split-screen terminal output, weak on the Design criterion (prior risks). Solo narration avoids bystanders, a plus. — Impact: Debugging aloud is uncommon behaviour (prior risk, assumption), the benefit over the native Bee MCP plus Claude Code baseline is unproven until an A/B is run, and the audience is a slice of developers who own a Bee. Workplace recording raises consent issues (trends_agents.md T5). — Originality: Three visible Bee developer-workflow entrants (kept, ringfence, Mental-Model-Drift; rival matrix) and the developer-tools-in-consumer-tracks cluster (refresh) make this the most crowded corner of the smallest track; the edit-time guard is the only distinct piece. — Feasibility: Ineligible without hardware; 108 h with hardware is over solo capacity and the A/B harness budget is uncertain (prior gate).
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 3, feasibility 2 (Eligible with Watch; solo narration needs no second person, but 108 h and the A/B run cost exceed solo capacity.); duo tech 1, feasibility 1 (Ineligible without real Bee data.); duo+hw tech 3, feasibility 3 (62 h per person; A/B logs become affordable.)

**Round-1 judge ratings (3 Oct, same model; shown for traceability):** devrel 1/3/2/2/1 = 1.80; engineer 1/3/2/3/1 = 2.00; product 1/2/2/2/1 = 1.60

**Red-team verdicts:** not red-teamed (did not reach the top of either round's ranking); ranking rests on gate and judge scores alone.

---


### Track: Ring

#### RNG-1: CurbClock: a curb witness and 5-minute wait clock for paratransit riders

**Round:** 1. **Track:** Ring (primary). Can also enter the AWS Builder and Open Source mini challenges.

**Pitch:** A Ring camera that can see the curb acts as the rider's own witness. When a vehicle stops during the paratransit pickup window, CurbClock starts the agency's 5-minute wait clock on the rider's accessible screen and pages a named helper, who has to accept. If the vehicle leaves early, it builds a dated dispute packet that quotes the agency's own no-show rule. It records observations only. It never gives a verdict, and it never claims who was in the vehicle.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Primary user | ADA paratransit riders with mobility or vision disabilities who are picked up curb-to-curb from a house or duplex where a Ring doorbell or camera can see the curb. Secondary users are one or two named helpers (a family caregiver or personal care attendant) who help the rider reach the vehicle. In practice this is US-only, because both ADA paratransit and the Ring partner path are US. |
| Frequency | Every home pickup. Each trip has a fixed ready window: scheduled time to +30 min at Union City, a 20-minute window at Access Services LA, a 30-minute window at Erie LIFT. Each policy read also has a 5-minute driver wait. Access Services alone reports more than 12,000 weekday trips. How many trips a typical rider takes per week was not verified in the reviewed sources. |
| Severity | High for the rider. Each recorded no-show earns penalty points that escalate to suspension: warning, then 7-, 14- and 21-day suspensions at Union City, and suspension at 6 points in a calendar month at Erie LIFT. A suspension cuts off trips to medical and work appointments. Dispute windows are short (3 business days at Union City; 2 business days after month end at Erie). The rider has no record of their own. DREDF cites the National Council on Disability: riders feel penalized despite their efforts. |
| Current workaround | Riders wait at the window or curb for the whole window. They phone dispatch; DREDF lists being on hold while checking ride status as a cause of missed pickups. They use agency apps that show vehicle location or send an imminent-arrival alert before the scheduled time (Access 'Where's My Ride', myRTCpara, Pace TripCheck). Disputes are made by phone from memory, while the agency relies on its own AVL and driver logs. |
| Amazon integration and its indispensable role | Ring is the independent sensor and the source of evidence. Inputs are motion events with sub_type=vehicle, WHEP live-view frames analysed for vehicle presence and dwell inside a curb zone the rider draws, Event History, and (on real devices) MP4 clip download for the window. The mandatory Ring watermark (logo, device ID, timestamp) stays on evidence frames, because it corroborates time and device. It is masked only for inference. AWS hosts the HMAC webhook intake, durable wait-clock timers and dispute-packet storage. No Alexa or Fire TV integration, to keep scope narrow. |
| Removal test (honest) | Without Ring, what is left is a countdown timer plus the agency's own tracking app, which is the status quo. Both the 'a vehicle is at your curb now' trigger and the rider's own arrival/departure record disappear, and those are the product. Ring is load-bearing. Honest caveat: any curb-facing camera with a partner API could play this role. The indispensability comes from Ring being the camera already mounted at the door, not from a capability only Ring has. |
| Wow moment under 45 s | 0-8 s: The rider's tablet shows today's trip: dialysis, pickup 2:00-2:30, driver waits 5 min, per the Union City policy (clause quoted on tap). 8-22 s, REAL: Ring Playground live view of the simulated Vehicle event, with the CC BY attribution on screen. The in-browser detector boxes a vehicle inside the curb zone. The rider's screen flips to a large 'Vehicle at your curb 2:07 - 5:00' countdown with sound, and the helper's phone shows 'Asha needs help to the curb - Accept?'. The helper taps Accept. 22-40 s, SIMULATED (ring-sandbox emulator, labelled on screen, team-recorded curb footage): the vehicle leaves at 2:09:10. CurbClock shows that the vehicle left after 2 min 10 s, while the agency's policy lists departing without the 5-minute wait as an agency error. One tap produces a dispute packet with timeline, two watermarked frames (people blurred), the quoted clause and 'dispute by Fri 5… |
| Original mechanism | Policy-as-code dwell evaluation. Three inputs are fused into a vehicle-present interval with explicit uncertainty: (1) the rider's scheduled pickup; (2) a machine-readable version of that agency's published no-show policy (window anchor and length, wait minutes, early-arrival rule, dispute deadline, agency-error clauses, each with its quoted source text); (3) Ring observations. Those observations are a vehicle event that wakes the pipeline, then WHEP frames sampled through chained 60 s sessions, classified by an object detector (car/bus/truck classes) inside a curb polygon. The watermark band and privacy zones are masked, and an enter/exit hysteresis is applied. The interval drives an accessible countdown and a named-helper accept ladder (primary, then backup, with timeouts). On a missed trip, a pure evaluator checks the interval against the agency's own error clauses and emits 'candida… |
| Simulator or real-data route (data route) | Live view: the backend POSTs the SDP offer to api.amazonvision.com (server-to-server) and returns the answer to the curb-watcher tab. WebRTC media goes straight to the browser, MediaPipe runs there, and only derived observations go to the backend (timestamp, class, confidence, zone, optional one thumbnail). Events: Ring webhook (real) or ring-sandbox (simulated) goes to API Gateway, then Lambda (HMAC check on raw bytes, ack under 5 s), SQS, the trip engine and DynamoDB. EventBridge Scheduler fires clock and timeout transitions. Web Push goes to the rider and helper PWAs. Dispute packets are assembled in S3 (SSE, lifecycle expiry). Policy onboarding: a public agency URL goes through Firecrawl JSON extraction, then the rider confirms each field. No street footage is stored except at most 3 blurred frames per missed trip. |
| Authentication | Ring: the Playground bearer token (30 min) lives server-side only; the docs say all calls must be server-to-server. Production would use one-way account linking with a nonce and refresh tokens (about 4 h access, about 30 d refresh). Webhooks use HMAC-SHA256 X-Signature verification with constant-time compare and dedupe. Riders and helpers sign in with passwordless magic links. Helpers see frames only if the rider turns that on. No face recognition, no plate reading. |
| Preview constraints | Playground provides one simulated Doorbell Pro (assumed line-powered, 60 s cap; to be confirmed from capabilities). Live view plays pre-recorded CC BY footage that needs on-screen attribution, and what the Vehicle clip shows was not inspected. History holds on_demand events only. There are no motion webhooks or sub_type in the Playground (forum 45309). Snapshot and clip availability is inconsistent across entrants. Tokens last 30 min and are regenerated by hand. Field of view, curb distance and night performance cannot be tested on Playground footage. TAKE-encrypted real devices return encrypted media, which would disable the CV path for those households. |
| Hardware | Development on the MacBook M4 Pro (24 GB); headless Chromium for WHEP capture and tests. Two phones of any make for the rider and helper PWAs. A smartphone to record consented curb footage for simulated scenarios (team's own street; people blurred, no plates kept). No Ring hardware is needed for the Playground path. Optional: a US-located Ring camera that sees the curb, with a Ring plan (conditional real path). |
| SDK versions | There is no official Ring SDK (stated in Ring docs). Reference for the WHEP flow: AmazonAppDev/ring-api-helloworld (MIT; Next.js 14 plus Python 3.8+ scripts; last main commit 2026-07-01). Emulator: ring-sandbox 0.5.0 (PyPI, 2026-10-02, Python >=3.11, MIT, third-party, single maintainer who is also an entrant), pinned. MediaPipe Tasks object detector (version to pin; Apache-2.0 licence to be verified). Playwright Chromium (version to pin). Node 20/22 LTS through nvm for the starter; the machine has Node v25.2.1 and Next 14 compatibility with it is unverified. boto3 for AWS. |
| Deployment prerequisites | Amazon Developer sign-in for Playground tokens. AWS account on a Paid plan in us-east-1; check Bedrock quota on day 1, since the forum reports quota 0 and suspensions on new accounts. Firecrawl API key for policy onboarding. Public HTTPS (API Gateway) for the webhook route. Judge-facing endpoints stay live through 20 Nov 2026. CC BY attribution for Playground footage in the video and README. For a real event path: an identity-verified Ring developer account, a registered app with Token Exchange and Account Link URLs, a US-located device with a Ring plan, and a staging user. |
| Firecrawl: research role | Used to find and read primary no-show policies (Union City, Erie LIFT, Vacaville City Coach, Access Services LA), DREDF's guide, FTA's ADA FAQ and the agency rider apps (myRTCpara, Pace TripCheck). This showed that windows (20 vs 30 min, anchors), early-arrival rules and dispute deadlines vary by agency, which is why the policy-as-code schema exists. |
| Firecrawl: runtime role | Optional onboarding helper only. The rider pastes their agency's public no-show policy URL. A Firecrawl scrape with a JSON schema extracts window rule, wait minutes, early-arrival rule, dispute deadline and agency-error clauses, each with its quoted text, and the rider confirms or edits every field. Only a public URL is sent. It is never in the live event path. Fallback is manual entry. |
| AWS Builder path | API Gateway HTTP API and a Python Lambda verify Ring's HMAC on raw bytes and ack within Ring's 5 s limit, then SQS, a worker Lambda and DynamoDB (trips, observations, policies). EventBridge Scheduler one-time schedules hold the 5-minute clock, helper timeouts and dispute-deadline reminders, so timers survive restarts; the pricing page lists 14M Scheduler invocations/month free. S3 with lifecycle rules holds packets that expire. An optional Bedrock Nova Lite call rewrites the fixed letter template in plain language; all decisions stay deterministic. AWS Budgets alarm. Built with Kiro or Claude Code on Bedrock (forum 45321 says this counts). The value is durable timers and a reliable public webhook endpoint, not a logo. |
| Open Source path | (1) Publish 'paratransit-noshow-policies' (MIT code, CC BY data): a JSON schema plus a dataset of agency policies with source URLs, quoted clauses and retrieval dates, and a pure evaluate_pickup(observations, policy) function with table-driven tests. (2) Upstream PR to ring-sandbox adding vehicle-dwell scenarios (early_departure, before_window, no_vehicle) and a --media-dir example; its README names scenario files as the most valuable contribution. (3) Publish the headless-Chrome WHEP frame-capture script with its failure modes. One entrant reports a werift offer got HTTP 500 and only headless Chrome worked. |
| Fallback | If WHEP fails: run the CV on ring-sandbox snapshots of team footage (labelled SIMULATED), keep real Playground device/status/history reads as the live Ring calls, and document the failure in the friction log. If vehicle CV is unreliable on watermarked frames: drive dwell from simulated motion.vehicle start/end plus one-tap rider or helper confirmation. If the dispute angle is falsified: ship the readiness and helper loop alone, and present the packet as a personal log. |
| Falsification test (≤1 day) | In one day. (1) Technical: capture 30 frames from the Playground Vehicle simulation over WHEP and run the detector with the watermark band masked. Fewer than 3 successful WHEP sessions in 10 attempts, or vehicle recall under 80% on clearly visible vehicles, means the real-Ring CV claim fails. (2) Value: read the dispute sections of 10 more agency no-show policies and call 2 agency customer-service lines, asking whether a rider may submit their own timestamped photos or video in a no-show dispute. If none of the 10 offers a written dispute path and both calls say only AVL or driver logs are considered, the evidence-packet thesis is wrong and the product shrinks to a readiness prompt. |
| Risk profile | Medium-high technical and simulation risk (vehicle CV on pre-recorded watermarked footage; event layer simulated). High problem severity backed by primary-source policies. Medium adoption risk (agency acceptance of rider evidence unverified). Low platform-policy risk as long as there is no OCR, face or plate analysis. |
| Estimate (unmeasured) | Solo 105 h; two-person 60 h per person; cloud Estimate: about $3-15 total through 20 Nov 2026. Lambda, SQS, DynamoDB on-demand and EventBridge Scheduler stay inside listed free allowances at demo volume (Lambda 1M requests and 400k GB-s/month; Scheduler 14M invocations/month), if the account's plan includes them. CloudWatch logs and S3 cost a few dollars. Optional Nova Lite at $0.06/$0.24 per 1M input/output tokens (Ohio rates; us-east-1 assumed equal) comes to under $1 for about 1,000 calls. Vision runs in the browser, so inference costs nothing. Firecrawl API credits for a few dozen extractions are not priced here.. Assumptions: These are estimates, not measurements. One engineer comfortable with Python/TypeScript and basic WebRTC. Hours include day-1 validation spikes (8h), WHEP capture (8h), detector and dwell tracker (10h), emulator scenarios and footage (8h), policy model and evaluator (8h), Firecrawl onboarding (5h), wait clock and helper ladder (8h), accessible PWAs (14h), dispute packet (7h), AWS deployment (8h), tests (6h), and video, README, friction log and feedback (12h). hours_duo is elapsed hours per perso… |

**Demand evidence** (quality labelled by the researcher):

- Union City Paratransit policy (updated 21 Jul 2021): the pickup window runs from the scheduled time to +30 min and the driver waits at most 5 min. Arrivals before the window, and departures within the window without the 5-minute wait, are agency errors and are not counted as no-shows. Disputes must be made within 3 business days. Repeat violations lead to 7-, 14- and 21-day suspensions. — [https://www.unioncityca.gov/DocumentCenter/View/111/Union-City-Paratransits-No-Show-Policy-PDF?bidId=](https://www.unioncityca.gov/DocumentCenter/View/111/Union-City-Paratransits-No-Show-Policy-PDF?bidId=) — *Primary source (official agency policy, read in full). Shows the rules and what is at stake. Does not show how often wrongful no-shows happen.*
- Access Services (Los Angeles) rider guide: 20-minute pickup window, curb-to-curb service, driver waits only 5 min. If the driver is early, the 5 minutes start at the scheduled time. Listed grounds include a driver departing before the scheduled time and a driver failing to wait 5 minutes. Riders may appeal or dispute any no-show. More than 1,000 vehicles make more than 12,000 weekday trips. — [https://accessla.org/riders-guide-text-page.html](https://accessla.org/riders-guide-text-page.html) — *Primary source (agency rider guide, undated page, relevant sections read).*
- Erie MTA LIFT (effective July 2023): no-show if the rider does not board within 5 minutes of a vehicle arriving in the 30-minute window. Suspension at 6 points in a calendar month. Disputes within 2 business days after month end. — [https://ride-the-e.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/07/PARATRANSIT-No-Show-Late-Policy-updated-7-14-23-r.g..pdf](https://ride-the-e.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/07/PARATRANSIT-No-Show-Late-Policy-updated-7-14-23-r.g..pdf) — *Primary source (official agency policy, 2023).*
- DREDF Topic Guide 7 lists agency-error cases that must not count against riders: vehicle early, vehicle late, vehicle never arrived, wrong location, driver did not follow procedures to locate the rider. It recommends that dispatchers check vehicle arrival against the window using AVL. It cites the National Council on Disability on riders penalized despite their efforts. — [https://dredf.org/ADAtg/noshow.shtml](https://dredf.org/ADAtg/noshow.shtml) — *Disability-rights advocacy guidance. Undated; citations run to about 2010, so it is old. The NCD point is second-hand.*
- FTA ADA FAQ: before suspending service, a provider must notify the rider in writing and offer an appeal. Trips missed for reasons beyond the passenger's control are not grounds for sanctions. — [https://www.transit.dot.gov/regulations-and-guidance/civil-rights-ada/frequently-asked-questions](https://www.transit.dot.gov/regulations-and-guidance/civil-rights-ada/frequently-asked-questions) — *Federal regulator FAQ (undated page). Sets the legal frame for disputes.*
- AARP/NAC (1 Jul 2026): 51M caregivers of adults 50+, 74% living with or within 20 minutes of the person they care for. This supports a nearby named helper being reachable at pickup time. — [https://www.aarp.org/pri/topics/ltss/family-caregiving/family-caregiving-in-us-2025/](https://www.aarp.org/pri/topics/ltss/family-caregiving/family-caregiving-in-us-2025/) — *Credible non-vendor research (via trends_home digest). Indirect support for the helper feature only.*

**Rivals named at generation** (strongest feature; our difference; evidence status):

| Rival | Strongest feature | Our difference | Evidence status |
|---|---|---|---|
| [Access Services 'Where's My Ride' app (Los Angeles)](https://accessla.org/riders-guide-text-page.html) | Official agency data: book trips and see the vehicle's location from 15 minutes before scheduled pickup, including over SMS on flip phones. | CurbClock is rider-held and independent of the agency. It observes the vehicle at this curb, runs the 5-minute clock against the agency's own rule, pages a helper, and produces a dispute packet. Whether 'Where's My Ride' exposes arrival or wait logs to riders is not verified in reviewed sources. | Agency rider guide read 2026-10-03; app not tested. |
| [myRTCpara (RTC Southern Nevada)](https://www.rtcsnv.com/news/paratransit-riders-and-caretakers-can-now-manage-rides-with-the-touch-of-a-button/) | Riders and caretakers can book and cancel trips, track the vehicle and see estimated pickup times in one app. | Agency apps show where the vehicle is supposed to be. CurbClock records when a vehicle was actually at this curb and for how long, and checks that against the policy. Rider-side arrival or dwell records in myRTCpara are not verified in reviewed sources. | Agency news post (10 Nov 2021) read; app not tested. |
| [Pace Paratransit TripCheck](https://www.pacebus.com/tripcheck) | Automatic email/text/phone alerts, including an imminent-arrival alert 20 minutes before scheduled pickup and a next-day itinerary. | TripCheck's alert is tied to the schedule. CurbClock's trigger is an observed vehicle at the curb, followed by a wait clock and evidence. Arrival-at-curb alerts in TripCheck are not verified in reviewed sources. | Official page read 2026-10-03. |
| [Ring Smart Alerts (vehicle detection)](https://ring.com/plans) | Native person, package and vehicle alerts on paid Ring plans, with no setup. | A vehicle alert carries no pickup window, no wait clock, no helper acceptance and no policy-based dispute packet. Dwell measurement in native alerts is not verified in reviewed sources. | Plans page via rivals_home digest; features not tested. |
| [School Bus Home (Ring Appstore)](https://ring.com/appstore/school-bus-home) | Same pattern for a different vehicle: watches for a scheduled vehicle during an expected window and sends a heads-up if no bus is seen. Up to 4 cameras. $4.99/mo. | Built for paratransit rules (window anchors, 5-minute wait, agency-error clauses, dispute deadlines), accessible rider output and helper acceptance. Shows the expected-window pattern is already shipped for one niche. | Listing blurb read; app not tested. |

**Smallest credible MVP:**

- Playground token flow and device/status/capabilities reads. Every screen shows a REAL badge for live Ring calls and a SIMULATED badge for emulator events.
- Curb-watcher browser tab. A server-proxied WHEP session (SDP exchange server-side, since CORS blocks browser calls to Ring) is re-negotiated within the 60 s cap. MediaPipe object detector runs in the browser on frames, inside a rider-drawn curb polygon, with the watermark band masked. Output is vehicle-present intervals with hysteresis. Raw frames never leave the browser.
- ring-sandbox 0.5.0 emulator sends HMAC-signed motion_detected sub_type=vehicle webhooks. Four scenario YAMLs run on team-recorded curb footage served as snapshots: boarded on time, left before 5 min, arrived before window, no vehicle in window.
- Trip and policy model seeded with Union City, Access Services LA and Erie LIFT parameters. Each field links to its quoted source clause.
- Wait clock and named-helper accept ladder (primary, then backup, 60 s timeout), on an accessible rider PWA: large type, high contrast, screen-reader labels, audible cue, WCAG 2.2 AA target.
- Closure: the rider or helper taps Boarded or Missed. On Missed, the evaluator builds a dispute packet (PDF): timeline, at most 3 watermarked frames with people blurred, quoted clause, deadline, free-text rider statement. Packets auto-delete 30 days after the dispute deadline.
- AWS deployment in us-east-1, plus unit tests for the evaluator, HMAC verification over raw bytes and dedupe on meta.request_id. README includes a verified-vs-simulated table and a friction log.

**Deliberately excluded stretch:**

- Real US device path with real vehicle webhooks and window-clip download. This depends on someone with a US device, Ring plan and identity-verified developer account; it stays a labelled conditional.
- Return trips from appointments (away from the home camera).
- Door-to-door service variants where the driver must come to the door.
- Integration with agency scheduling systems or rider APIs.
- Any OCR of vehicle numbers or plates. Excluded on purpose: plate-reading apps are not allowed per Ring's CEO (TechCrunch via rivals_home), and fleet-number OCR is too close to that.
- Echo/Alexa announcements and Fire TV surfaces.
- Spanish UI and automatic monitoring of policy changes.

**APIs, events and actions** (status as assessed by the researcher; 'unverified' means not confirmed in reviewed docs, 'gated' means not available to entrants, 'simulated' means not real in the demo):

| Status | API / event / action | Documentation |
|---|---|---|
| verified | Developer Playground one-click OAuth token (30 min, no app or account linking) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/release-notes.html#developers-playground |
| verified | GET /v1/devices?include=status,capabilities,configurations (device discovery; capabilities confirm whether the 60 s line-powered cap applies) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#device-discovery |
| verified | POST /v1/devices/{id}/media/streaming/whep/sessions + DELETE session (receive-only, 60 s line-powered cap). An entrant verified frames on 2026-09-23 using a headless-Chrome offer; another entrant reported a WHEP failure on 2026-09-30. | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#whep-live-video-streaming |
| unverified | Playground live-view event simulation, Vehicle type (pre-recorded CC BY footage; what the vehicle footage shows was not inspected) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/release-notes.html#developers-playground |
| verified | GET /v1/history/devices/{id}/events (Playground returns on_demand only; used to log sessions) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#event-history |
| gated | motion_detected webhook with attributes.sub_type=vehicle (needs a real US device, Ring plan and linked account) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#motion-detection |
| simulated | motion_detected sub_type=vehicle delivered by the ring-sandbox 0.5.0 emulator with HMAC X-Signature (scenario YAMLs) | https://github.com/josepha-mayo/ring-sandbox |
| simulated | Webhook verification: X-Signature HMAC-SHA256 over raw body, 2xx within 5 s, dedupe on meta.request_id (exercised against the emulator) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#webhook-authentication--verification |
| gated | GET /v1/history/devices/{id}/events?event_types=motion.vehicle (start/end per event) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#event-history |
| gated | POST /v1/devices/{id}/media/video/download (MP4 for the pickup window, for post-hoc dwell) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#media-clips |
| unverified | POST /v1/devices/{id}/media/image/download (latest_in_range). Playground results conflict: 416 for one entrant, a real 68 KB JPEG for another. | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#image-snapshots |
| unverified | Amazon EventBridge Scheduler one-time schedules (wait clock, helper timeout, dispute-deadline reminder) | https://aws.amazon.com/eventbridge/pricing/ |
| unverified | Amazon Bedrock with Nova Lite (optional; only rephrases a fixed dispute-letter template, makes no decisions) | https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/pricing/ |

**Blockers:** WHEP from the Playground has conflicting reports (frames captured 2026-09-23 with headless Chrome only; a failure reported 2026-09-30). Reproduce on day 1.; Content of the Playground Vehicle footage is unknown. It may not show a curb or a van.; The Playground has no vehicle webhooks or sub_type, so the event layer for an India team must be simulated and labelled.; The real path needs a US device, Ring plan, identity-verified developer account and account linking, none of which the team has confirmed.; Bedrock quota may be 0 on a new account (forum reports). The core does not depend on Bedrock.; Node v25 on the machine versus the starter's Next.js 14 (use nvm).

**Reasons judges might reject it:** For an India-based team, most of the event layer is simulated. Only device reads, history and Playground live view are real.; Many doorbells cannot see the curb, and apartment riders are excluded, so the addressable share of riders is unproven.; A camera cannot prove the vehicle was the paratransit van, that the driver tried to locate the rider, or that the rider was ready. Judges may see the evidence as weak.; Whether agencies accept rider-held evidence was not verified.; It may be read as a 'vehicle alert' (the Obvious category) if the policy and dispute mechanics are not shown clearly in the video.; Narrow, US-only population.; Recording a public street captures drivers and passers-by. Mitigated by blurring people and keeping no plates, but still sensitive.

**Gate result:** overall **conditional** (eligibility pass, feasibility conditional; native overlap medium, visible crowding low).

- Eligibility: The Ring Developer Playground is the Ring-documented simulator the FAQ accepts, and forum 45309 calls it 'the only documented route' without a US device. The demo's real runtime Ring calls are the Playground token, device, status and capabilities reads, and WHEP live view of the Playground Vehicle simulation with in-browser vehicle detection. Gated motion_detected sub_type=vehicle webhooks are delivered by the ring-sandbox emulator and labelled SIMULATED, not claimed live. There is no plate or face analysis, and CC BY attribution is planned. The real US-device path is correctly excluded.
- Feasibility: The 105 h solo estimate fits (estimate). The core real-Ring feature depends on two unverified facts. (1) What the Playground 'Vehicle' live-view footage shows: the console is behind sign-in and was not inspected (ring.md gaps #1), and it may not show a vehicle stopping at a curb. (2) WHEP reliability. One entrant (doorstep-ring) got 201 and 1280x720 frames on 2026-09-23 only with browser-built SDP offers; a werift offer got 500. The concept's 'WHEP failure 2026-09-30' and '68 KB JPEG' anecdotes could not be traced in the research digests. Playground regional gating for an India account is unverified. The machine runs Node v25 while the starter uses Next 14, so use nvm. If the vehicle live v…
- Conditions: (1) The Playground opens for the user's Amazon Developer account from India (regional gating unverified). (2) The Playground Vehicle simulation shows a vehicle that MediaPipe detects inside a drawn zone, with at least 80% recall on clearly visible frames and the watermark band masked. (3) WHEP sessions succeed on at least 3 of 10 attempts using a browser or headless-Chrome SDP offer through a server-proxied exchange. (4) Every emulator-driven event is labelled SIMULATED on screen and in the README. CC BY attribution for Playground footage appears in the video. (5) Team-recorded curb footage is consented, with people blurred and no plates kept. No OCR of plates or fleet numbers. (6) No claim that agencies accept rider-held evidence; this is unverified.
- Native overlap: Ring Smart Alerts include native vehicle alerts on paid plans. The partner app School Bus Home already ships the 'expected window plus no-vehicle heads-up' pattern ($4.99/mo). Agency apps (Access 'Where's My Ride', myRTCpara, Pace TripCheck) give vehicle ETA. A policy-based wait clock, a helper accept ladder and a dispute packet were not found natively.
- Crowding: No paratransit or curb entrant among the roughly 36 visible Ring repos (github.md). An analogous evidence pattern exists: christin09110-stack/doorstep-receipt reconciles carrier tracking claims against what a Ring doorbell recorded. The elder-care cluster (about 15 repos) is adjacent but different. Note an internal collision: ALX-2 uses the same paratransit domain.
- Day-one proof: With a Playground token: list the device and capabilities; open 10 WHEP sessions on the Vehicle simulation through a server-proxied SDP exchange with a browser-built offer, and get at least 3 successes; capture 30 frames; run the MediaPipe detector with the watermark band masked and get at least 80% vehicle recall inside a drawn curb polygon. Verify a ring-sandbox HMAC-signed vehicle webhook against API Gateway, with raw-byte HMAC and a 2xx in under 5 s.
- Redesign to pass: Not a gate fail. If the Vehicle footage is unusable, keep a load-bearing real Ring interaction: the rider or helper starts a Playground live-view check from the app ('look at my curb now'), and the WHEP frame plus the on_demand history entry become part of the dispute timeline. Then the core still visibly calls documented Ring surfaces, and the dwell CV runs on labelled team footage.

**Round-2 judge ratings (1–5; base case: solo, India, VVD on Mac, no Bee hardware, no US Ring device). Weighted = 0.25·Tech + 0.20·Design + 0.20·Impact + 0.20·Originality + 0.15·Feasibility.**

| Judge lens | Tech | Design | Impact | Originality | Feasibility | Weighted | Confidence | One-line verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| devrel | 3 | 3 | 2 | 3 | 3 | 2.80 | medium | Survives narrowed, but the key moment leans on contested WHEP and a simulated trigger. |
| engineer | 3 | 3 | 2 | 3 | 3 | 2.80 | medium | An honest redesign that makes Playground calls load-bearing, but its decisive beats are still simulated, WHEP is contested, and ProofClock already ships most of the mechanism. |
| product | 3 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 3.00 | low | Honest redesign of a real disability need, but the decisive beats stay simulated for an India team and the mechanism overlaps shipping Ring Appstore apps. |
| **Mean of three** | 3.00 | 3.00 | 2.33 | 3.00 | 3.00 | **2.87** | | Scenario weighted: solo+hw 2.87; duo 3.02; duo+hw 3.02 |

**Why each judge rated it that way (verbatim rationale, one per criterion):**

- *devrel* — Tech: Scored as the CurbClock redesign (prior_redteam RNG-1.redesign): Playground WHEP live view opened for a human 'is this your ride?' confirmation, on_demand history read, EventBridge wait clock, HMAC webhook intake. Capped: WHEP on the Playground is contested (forum 45309 reports 500s; JDR-07), the vehicle trigger is emulator-SIMULATED, and TAKE encryption degrades the real path. — Design: WCAG rider PWA, helper accept ladder and an 'AVL review request' letter are coherent, but about half the beats are labelled SIMULATED, which strains forum 45058's 'looks functional' bar (JDR-06). — Impact: High severity for a defined US population with primary-source policies, but ProofClock and School Bus Home already ship ~70% of the mechanism (red team), agencies' acceptance of rider-side records is unverified, and many doorbells cannot see the curb. — Originality: Red team revised to 3: policy-anchored wait clock plus human confirmation is a new combination; components (vehicle alerting, attendance verdicts) exist natively or on the Ring Appstore. — Feasibility: <=80 h target fits, but three day-1 kill switches (Playground from India, WHEP 3/10, agency dispute paths) can end it.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 3, feasibility 3 (Ring stays Playground; no change.); duo tech 3, feasibility 4 (60 h each; WHEP risk unchanged.); duo+hw tech 3, feasibility 4 (As duo.)
- *engineer* — Tech: Scored as the red-team redesign 'CurbClock human-confirmed policy-anchored pickup log' (prior_redteam.json RNG-1.redesign). Real Ring calls at runtime: Playground token, device/status/capabilities reads, server-proxied WHEP live view with a browser-built offer, and on_demand history entries for each session (doorstep-ring read 7 on_demand events on 2026-09-24; forum 45309). Around them sit a fully modelled agency po… — Design: The redesign replaces unverifiable CV with a human 'Is this your ride?' confirmation backed by a watermarked frame and the on_demand record, keeps a large-type spoken countdown (WCAG 2.2 AA) and a helper accept ladder, and reframes the output as an AVL review request that never claims proof. Device fit stays doubtful: many doorbells cannot see the curb, apartment riders are excluded, and the decisive beats (vehicle … — Impact: Severity rests on primary agency no-show rules (Union City, Access Services LA) but ProofClock and School Bus Home already ship window/dwell verdicts and a 'no bus by expected time' heads-up on the Ring Appstore (red team), agency acceptance of rider-side records is unverified, and the addressable share (US, curb-visible Ring owner) is unproven; red team revised impact to 2.5. — Originality: No paratransit or curb entrant among about 36 visible Ring repos, and policy-as-code dwell against quoted agency clauses with a rider countdown is not in any reviewed rival. But ProofClock plus School Bus Home cover roughly 70% of the mechanism (red team estimate), and Ring Smart Alerts plus 'Alexa, tell me when the pizza is delivered' cover vehicle arrival natively. — Feasibility: Red-team trimmed scope of about 80 h fits about 104 h (estimates), but the meaningful version hinges on day-1 unknowns: Playground access from India (unverified for every Ring concept), WHEP succeeding on at least 3 of 10 attempts, and the Vehicle footage content (never published by an entrant). The redesign's own kill switches can end it on day 1.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 3, feasibility 3 (Ring remains Playground-only in this scenario; the listed hardware does not help. No change.); duo tech 3, feasibility 4 (At about 60 h each (estimate) the build fits easily, but the Playground unknowns cap the tech ceiling regardless of headcount.); duo+hw tech 3, feasibility 4 (Same as duo_no_hw.)
- *product* — Tech: Scored as the red-team redesign 'CurbClock human-confirmed, policy-anchored pickup log' (prior_redteam.json RNG-1.redesign): Playground live view on the helper's phone (REAL, WHEP contested: one entrant frame on 23 Sep, another's sweep failed 30 Sep), the on_demand history record Ring logs for that session (REAL), EventBridge wait clock, one fully modelled agency policy, AVL review request. The vehicle event and dep… — Design: Accessible large-type spoken countdown for the rider, 'Is this your ride? Yes/No/Can't tell' helper confirmation, helper accept ladder and a one-page AVL review letter quoting the agency clause are coherent and honest (redesign steps 1-4). The demo mixes real and labelled simulated beats across three surfaces, which weakens the ten-second read. — Impact: ADA paratransit riders face documented 5-minute wait and no-show rules (prior policy sources) and a missed dialysis ride is severe; the dispute path is quoted from agency text. But the addressable share (curb-visible camera, house or duplex, US only) is unproven, agency acceptance of rider records is unverified, and some agencies already push arrival notifications (Valley Metro snippet; red team demo_attack). — Originality: ProofClock and School Bus Home (same developer) already ship window, vehicle-at-entrance, dwell, verdict and dispute export, about 70% of the original mechanism (red-team estimate; rival matrix). The paratransit workflow layer with human confirmation and an agency-specific review request is not verified in any rival; prior originality 4 was overstated per red team. — Feasibility: Trimmed to about 80 h (red-team estimate) but gated on the Playground opening from India (unverified), WHEP success on at least 3 of 10 attempts with a browser-built offer, and a read of agency dispute paths; multiple kill switches.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 3, feasibility 3 (Ring stays on the Playground in every scenario; no change.); duo tech 3, feasibility 4 (About 40-50 h per person after the trim leaves buffer for WHEP troubleshooting.); duo+hw tech 3, feasibility 4 (Same as duo without hardware.)
