#### RNG-2: QuietBell: a booking-aware silent doorbell for solo appointment pros

**Round:** 1. **Track:** Ring (primary). Can also enter the AWS Builder and Open Source mini challenges.

**Pitch:** For practitioners who have no front desk and are mid-service behind a closed door. A press on the shared Ring doorbell is matched to the right booking without identifying anyone. It is routed silently to that one practitioner's wrist or phone, and their one-tap acknowledgement appears on the client's booking link. If the client gives up and walks away before being seen, QuietBell flags it.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Primary user | Solo or small-suite appointment professionals who work behind a closed door with no receptionist and no staffed waiting room: salon-suite stylists, estheticians, massage therapists, tattoo artists, tutors and music teachers in a suite or home studio with a Ring doorbell at the entrance. The secondary user is the arriving client. Clinical practices covered by HIPAA are excluded from the MVP. |
| Frequency | Every appointment, so several times a day for a full book. The number of appointments per day was not verified in reviewed sources. |
| Severity | Moderate. Each arrival either interrupts a session (a knock, a doorbell heard in every room, a text that pulls the practitioner to their phone) or leaves the client waiting without knowing if anyone noticed. A client who leaves unnoticed costs revenue: one salon-suite landlord blog puts a missed appointment at $50-$300, a vendor-adjacent figure with no source. This is not a safety problem. |
| Current workaround | Clients knock, ring a doorbell that sounds in every office, or text the practitioner. Some practices buy wall button and light boards (I'm Here!, Lobbywizard alert lights) or iPad check-in with silent watch notifications and message-back acknowledgement (TapIn, The Receptionist / Sign In App). Others use SMS queue tools (Waitwhile). Late-arrival policies are written into salon booking rules. |
| Amazon integration and its indispensable role | Ring is the check-in device and the presence sensor. The doorbell press (button_press) is the arrival signal for clients who never open a link. A WHEP frame gives a party-size count with no identity. The end times of motion.human events in history, plus one confirmatory frame, detect a walk-away. Configurations provide privacy zones, which are excluded from analysis. AWS hosts routing, escalation timers and the live booking-link page. |
| Removal test (honest) | This partly fails. Without Ring, a booking-link 'I'm here' button gives most of the routing and acknowledgement value, as TapIn and The Receptionist already show with iPads. Ring adds three things a link cannot: the press itself as check-in for clients who ignore links, the party size, and the walk-away signal. That is a real but narrow wedge, and it is the concept's main weakness. It fits best where there is no indoor waiting room and the door is the only touchpoint. |
| Wow moment under 45 s | 0-8 s: One door, two practitioners (Maya, Ravi), two bookings 3 minutes apart, both on a split screen of their phones. 8-20 s, REAL: Ring Playground live view (CC BY attribution on screen) feeds the in-browser person counter: 1 person in the door zone. SIMULATED (ring-sandbox, labelled): doorbell press at 2:57. Within about 2 s, only Maya's screen lights up with a silent 'Likely your 3:00, Jordan, party of 1, 3 min early'; Ravi's stays dark. 20-30 s: Maya taps 'Seen, about 5 min'. Jordan's booking link, open on a second phone, updates to say Maya knows they are here and will be about 5 minutes. 30-42 s, SIMULATED: no motion for 4 min, and a confirmatory frame of team footage counts 0 people. Maya gets 'Your 3:00 may have left (last seen 3:01), text them?' with a pre-filled message. |
| Original mechanism | Booking-aware routing of an arrival without identifying anyone. An assignment step scores every open ICS booking across the practitioners who share one door. The inputs are time offset from the start (an early/late band set per practice), whether that practitioner is in session now (from the calendar), party size from the CV person count compared with the booking's party size, and whether the booking is already matched. Clear wins go silently to one practitioner. Ambiguous ones go to both as 'maybe yours', and the first acknowledgement claims it. The practitioner's acknowledgement is pushed to the client's booking-link page (SSE), closing the loop the doorbell lacks. A walk-away check fires only on events: last motion.human end older than N minutes, then exactly one short confirmatory WHEP frame. That avoids continuous streaming and cluttering the owner's history. Output is a day log of… |
| Simulator or real-data route (data route) | Ring webhook (real) or emulator (simulated) goes to API Gateway, then Lambda (HMAC check, ack under 5 s), then the matcher Lambda, with booking state in DynamoDB (ICS fetched server-side every 5 min). Web Push goes to the matched practitioner. The acknowledgement goes to DynamoDB and then the client's booking-link page over SSE. Frames are fetched only through the server-proxied WHEP or snapshot path and analysed in the browser. Only the count and timestamp are kept, never images. Clients are shown to practitioners by first name or initials only. Day logs expire after 30 days. |
| Authentication | Ring: a Playground bearer token server-side only; production would use one-way account linking and refresh tokens. Webhook HMAC verification and dedupe on meta.request_id. Practitioners use magic-link sign-in. Clients reach their booking page through an unguessable per-booking token that expires 2 h after the appointment. A door sign gives clients notice (no face recognition, no stored images). |
| Preview constraints | Playground provides one simulated Doorbell Pro and pre-recorded CC BY footage (Package/Vehicle/Motion), which needs attribution and is not a salon doorway. There are no ding or motion webhooks, and history has on_demand only, so routing and walk-away timing must be simulated. WHEP sessions are capped at 60 s on line-powered devices and 30 s on battery devices. Each live view also creates an on_demand entry in the owner's history and may trigger recording, so sampling is kept to one confirmatory session. Tokens expire every 30 min. On real devices, TAKE encryption returns encrypted media and disables the CV features. |
| Hardware | MacBook M4 Pro for development. Three phones or browsers for two practitioners and one client in the demo. A smartphone to record consented doorway footage for simulated scenarios. No Ring hardware for the Playground path. Optional US-located Ring doorbell with a plan for the conditional real path. |
| SDK versions | No official Ring SDK. ring-api-helloworld (MIT, Next.js 14, Python 3.8+) as the WHEP reference. ring-sandbox 0.5.0 (Python >=3.11, MIT, third-party), pinned. MediaPipe Tasks object detector (pin the version; verify the Apache-2.0 licence). Python icalendar library (version to pin). Node 20/22 LTS through nvm (the machine has Node v25.2.1). |
| Deployment prerequisites | Amazon Developer sign-in for the Playground. AWS Paid-plan account in us-east-1. Public HTTPS for the webhook and booking pages, kept live through 20 Nov 2026. VAPID keys for Web Push. iOS Web Push needs the PWA added to the home screen (assumption to verify). CC BY attribution. For the real path: an identity-verified Ring developer account, app registration, a US device with a plan, and account linking. |
| Firecrawl: research role | Used to read rivals and the evidence for the job (TapIn, The Receptionist, Lobbywizard, I'm Here!, salon-suite landlord policy blogs, Vagaro policy guide). The result was that the acknowledgement loop and silent notification already exist for practices with an indoor lobby, which narrowed QuietBell to door-only setups with walk-away detection. |
| Firecrawl: runtime role | None. Bookings come from ICS feeds the practitioner controls, and scraping booking pages would add fragility and consent problems without real value. |
| AWS Builder path | API Gateway and a Lambda webhook receiver (HMAC on raw bytes, ack under 5 s), an SQS-buffered matcher Lambda, and DynamoDB (bookings, arrivals, claims, with TTL expiry). EventBridge Scheduler one-time schedules handle acknowledgement escalation and walk-away re-checks, so no long-running process is needed. Lambda serves the booking-link page and SSE stream (or API Gateway WebSocket; that pricing is not verified). Budgets alarm. Developed with Kiro or Claude Code on Bedrock. No model calls are needed in the decision path. |
| Open Source path | (1) Publish 'ics-arrival-matcher': a library that matches arrivals with no identity to ICS bookings, with explicit confidence, an ambiguity band and the first-acknowledgement-claims protocol, plus property-based tests. (2) Upstream ring-sandbox PR adding shared_suite_rush and walk_away scenarios. (3) A small 'whep-frame-sampler' utility for one-shot confirmatory frames from a Ring WHEP session, with documented failure modes. |
| Fallback | If confirmatory frames are unreliable: drop CV-based walk-away and send the client's booking page a 'still here?' check, using only motion end times. If judges' feedback or the falsification test shows the removal-test weakness is fatal: reposition as the door layer for shared suites, where one Ring doorbell replaces a doorbell that rings in every room. If WHEP fails: run the CV on emulator snapshots and keep real device and configuration reads. |
| Falsification test (≤1 day) | In one day: show a 60-second clickable prototype to 5 solo appointment pros (stylists, massage therapists, tutors), remotely and in any country, since the job does not depend on location. Ask how clients announce arrival today, and whether they would put a camera doorbell at their door for this. If 4 or more say they have a lobby or check-in method they are happy with, or would refuse a door camera for client-privacy reasons, the demand thesis is falsified. In parallel, run the person counter on 30 Playground Motion frames. If count accuracy is below 80% with the watermark masked, party size and walk-away are cut. |
| Risk profile | Low-medium technical risk (standard webhook, scheduling and CV pieces). Highest competition and substitution risk of the three (strong check-in rivals, partial removal test). Medium privacy risk (client footage at a business door). Low simulation risk relative to RNG-1, because routing logic is deterministic and testable. |
| Estimate (unmeasured) | Solo 90 h; two-person 52 h per person; cloud Estimate: about $3-12 total through 20 Nov 2026. Lambda, SQS, DynamoDB and Scheduler stay within the listed free allowances at demo volume. CloudWatch and API Gateway cost a few dollars (API Gateway free-tier applicability on post-July-2025 accounts is unverified). No Bedrock or Rekognition calls; detection runs in the browser.. Assumptions: Estimates, not measurements. One engineer. Hours include day-1 spikes (6h), Ring client and configuration handling (5h), WHEP sampler and person counter (12h), ICS import and matcher with tests (12h), emulator scenarios and webhook intake (7h), practitioner surface (9h), client booking page (7h), walk-away logic (6h), AWS deployment (8h), tests (5h), and docs, video and friction log (12h). hours_duo is elapsed hours per person with about 15% coordination overhead. |

**Demand evidence** (quality labelled by the researcher):

- A therapy check-in vendor says most private and small group practices have no receptionist. It says the fallback is a knock, a text, or a doorbell ringing in every office, and that a client's text pulls the clinician to their phone mid-session. — [https://tapinforclinics.com/for/mental-health](https://tapinforclinics.com/for/mental-health) — *Vendor marketing (undated). Describes the job; no data.*
- A visitor-management vendor sells instant practitioner notification plus a message back acknowledging the client's arrival, so clients do not wait without knowing whether anyone noticed them. — [https://thereceptionist.com/industries/behavioral-health-practice/](https://thereceptionist.com/industries/behavioral-health-practice/) — *Vendor page (modified 10 Sep 2026). Supply evidence that the acknowledgement loop is paid for.*
- Dedicated hardware exists so clients can silently tell a therapist they have arrived when there is no receptionist: wall button boards with in-office lights, and wireless alert lights plus SMS. — [https://www.im-here.biz/support.html](https://www.im-here.biz/support.html) — *Vendor FAQ (undated). Supply evidence; demand is inferred.*
- Salon-suite owners run their businesses without a manager or front desk, and late arrivals throw off the rest of the day. — [https://mallorcasalonstudios.com/blog/salon-client-policies-san-antonio](https://mallorcasalonstudios.com/blog/salon-client-policies-san-antonio) — *Vendor-adjacent (suite landlord blog, 26 Feb 2026).*
- A missed appointment can cost a suite owner $50-$300. — [https://qualitystylzsuites.com/blog/salon-no-show-policy-baltimore-client-mgmt](https://qualitystylzsuites.com/blog/salon-no-show-policy-baltimore-client-mgmt) — *Vendor-adjacent (8 May 2026). Figure has no source.*
- Small-business arrival management is a mature SaaS category: Waitwhile has 63 reviews, 78% from small businesses, and offers SMS and self check-in. The trends digest found no evidence that Ring doorbells are used as a check-in trigger, so this is a hypothesis. — [https://www.capterra.com/p/152494/Waitwhile/](https://www.capterra.com/p/152494/Waitwhile/) — *Review-site summary (Sep 2026), via trends_home. Supply evidence; demand for a Ring-based version is untested.*

**Rivals named at generation** (strongest feature; our difference; evidence status):

| Rival | Strongest feature | Our difference | Evidence status |
|---|---|---|---|
| [TapIn](https://tapinforclinics.com/for/mental-health) | Silent iPad check-in with phone or Apple Watch notification, an optional fully anonymous check-in, and one iPad for a whole suite. | Needs no iPad or waiting room: the doorbell press is the check-in and works for clients who never open a link. Adds a party-size count and walk-away detection. Whether TapIn routes by booking time is not verified in reviewed sources. | Vendor page read 2026-10-03; not tested. |
| [The Receptionist / Sign In App](https://thereceptionist.com/industries/behavioral-health-practice/) | iPad check-in that notifies the specific practitioner and lets them message back an acknowledgement and next steps. | Same acknowledgement loop, but triggered at a door with no iPad, matched to bookings, and with walk-away detection. Assumes an indoor lobby; QuietBell targets door-only setups. | Vendor page read (modified 10 Sep 2026); not tested. |
| [Lobbywizard / I'm Here! notification systems](https://lobbywizard.com/?page_id=47514) | Dedicated hardware: wireless alert lights in each office, SMS to the provider, and a reply text to the client (Lobbywizard). Hardwired button boards with lights (I'm Here!). | No new hardware beyond an existing Ring doorbell. Matches to bookings rather than a name button, and detects walk-aways. The hardware rivals are simpler and fully offline. | Vendor pages read; not tested. |
| [Front Desk (hackathon entrant, banksythequantLab/front-desk)](https://github.com/banksythequantLab/front-desk) | Ring events go through a local vision model that only reports observables, then deterministic rules, for a law office. Measured 18/20 on real doorstep frames, with an MCP server and a Fire TV lobby screen. | Front Desk classifies what is at the door (for example possible service of process). QuietBell matches the arrival to a booking, routes it to one practitioner, closes the acknowledgement loop to the client and detects walk-aways. | README read via the GitHub API 2026-10-03; not run. |
| [Lumeo (Ring Appstore)](https://ring.com/appstore/category/all-apps) | Customer wait-time, demographics and loitering analytics on Ring cameras. | Analytics after the fact versus a per-booking routing and acknowledgement workflow. No demographics, deliberately. | Listing blurb only (all-apps page); detail page not read. |

**Smallest credible MVP:**

- Playground token; device, status and configuration reads; privacy zones excluded from every frame analysis, and their coordinates never shown.
- ICS import for 1-3 practitioners (Google Calendar ICS in the demo), with a booking-link page per appointment (accessible, live updates over SSE).
- Matcher with scoring, an ambiguity threshold and the first-acknowledgement-claims protocol, with table-driven tests (early, late, overlapping bookings, party-size mismatch).
- In-browser MediaPipe person counter on WHEP frames from the Playground Motion simulation (real), plus ring-sandbox snapshots of team footage (simulated).
- ring-sandbox scenarios: shared_suite_rush, early_client, walk_away, link_only_client. button_press and motion_detected sub_type=human are delivered with HMAC.
- Practitioner surface: a silent web-push card with Seen / N min / Not mine buttons. If unacknowledged, it escalates or sends an automatic estimate based on the calendar session end.
- Walk-away check: last motion end plus one confirmatory frame. If the client left, a 'text them' button opens a pre-filled SMS from the practitioner's own phone, so no messaging infrastructure is needed.
- AWS deployment, plus a README with a verified-vs-simulated table, a privacy notice template for the door sign, and a friction log.

**Deliberately excluded stretch:**

- Clinical or HIPAA-covered practices.
- Automating no-show fees or payments.
- SMS sent by the platform (US A2P registration needed; not verified).
- Native APIs for Square, Vagaro or Acuity beyond ICS.
- Fire TV lobby display and Alexa+ MCP.
- Chime announcements (Early Access, not in the Playground).
- Buildings with several doors.
- Multilingual client pages.

**APIs, events and actions** (status as assessed by the researcher; 'unverified' means not confirmed in reviewed docs, 'gated' means not available to entrants, 'simulated' means not real in the demo):

| Status | API / event / action | Documentation |
|---|---|---|
| verified | Developer Playground one-click OAuth token (30 min) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/release-notes.html#developers-playground |
| verified | GET /v1/devices?include=status,configurations | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#device-discovery |
| verified | GET /v1/devices/{id}/configurations (motion zones, privacy_zones, used to exclude areas from analysis) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#configurations |
| verified | POST /v1/devices/{id}/media/streaming/whep/sessions + DELETE (one short confirmatory session per check). An entrant verified frames on 2026-09-23; another reported a failure on 2026-09-30. | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#whep-live-video-streaming |
| unverified | Playground live-view event simulation, Motion type (what the footage shows was not inspected) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/release-notes.html#developers-playground |
| gated | button_press webhook (doorbell ding; needs a real US device, plan and linked account) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#button-press |
| simulated | button_press and motion_detected sub_type=human via the ring-sandbox emulator with HMAC X-Signature | https://github.com/josepha-mayo/ring-sandbox |
| gated | GET /v1/history/devices/{id}/events?event_types=ding,motion.human (start/end used for walk-away; the Playground only has on_demand) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#event-history |
| unverified | POST /v1/devices/{id}/media/image/download at_timestamp (party size at the event time). Playground results conflict. | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#image-snapshots |
| unverified | Amazon EventBridge Scheduler one-time schedules (acknowledgement escalation, walk-away re-check) | https://aws.amazon.com/eventbridge/pricing/ |

**Blockers:** WHEP reliability on the Playground is contested between entrants.; No ding or human-motion events on the Playground; routing timing must be simulated and labelled.; Playground footage is not a practice doorway, so the CV demo is on whatever people appear in it plus team footage.; ICS export availability varies across booking tools (unverified).; iOS Web Push needs an installed PWA.

**Reasons judges might reject it:** The removal test is only partial: a link check-in like TapIn or The Receptionist delivers much of the value without Ring.; Demand evidence is mostly vendor marketing, and a Ring doorbell as a business check-in device is untested.; Close to the 'motion alert' Obvious category if the matching and acknowledgement loop are not front and centre.; Privacy of clients filmed at a business door, especially for wellness services. HIPAA practices are excluded, which narrows the market.; Most of the event layer is simulated for an India team.; Live-view sampling adds clutter to the owner's history and uses battery on battery-powered doorbells.

**Gate result:** overall **conditional** (eligibility conditional, feasibility pass; native overlap medium, visible crowding medium).

- Eligibility: The product's core trigger is the doorbell press (button_press), and walk-away detection uses motion.human history. Neither exists on the Playground: forum 45309 says it has no motion or doorbell webhooks, and history holds only on_demand. Both are delivered only by ring-sandbox, a third-party entrant tool that is not a simulator listed in Ring's developer documentation, which is the rules' test (L158). As planned, the only real Ring calls are device and configuration reads and a WHEP party-size frame, so Ring is peripheral to the demonstrated value. That is a Stage 1 'reasonably applies the required APIs' risk. It is eligible if a real Playground interaction is made load-bearing.
- Feasibility: The 90 h solo estimate fits (estimate). The matcher, scheduler and webhook pieces are standard and deterministic, with no model in the decision path. WHEP is contested (works only with browser-built offers per doorstep-ring). The Playground's Motion footage is CC BY and not a practice doorway, so person-count accuracy must be measured. iOS Web Push needs an installed PWA (assumption). ICS export availability varies across booking tools (unverified).
- Conditions: (1) Redesign the demo so the arrival signal shown live comes from the Playground: a Motion-simulation live view with a person detected in the door zone acts as the check-in. button_press from ring-sandbox is shown only as a labelled SIMULATED secondary path. (2) The Playground opens for the user's account from India. WHEP sessions succeed using a browser-built SDP offer. (3) The person counter reaches at least 80% accuracy on 30 Playground Motion frames with the watermark band masked, or party-size and walk-away features are cut. (4) HIPAA practices are excluded. Clients get a door-sign notice. Privacy zones are excluded from analysis, and their coordinates are never shown. (5) CC BY attribution, and SIMULATED labels on all emulator events.
- Native overlap: Ring natively notifies all shared users of a doorbell press. Ring Quick Replies auto-message visitors, and Alexa+ Greetings (US/CA) answers the door and takes messages (IDEATION_CONTEXT native list). Silent check-in with acknowledgement already exists in TapIn and The Receptionist (iPad). Booking-matched routing to one practitioner and walk-away detection were not found natively. The concept's own removal test is only partial.
- Crowding: Visible Ring and business-door entrants include banksythequantLab/front-desk (law-office door, Ring events plus MCP plus a Fire TV lobby, with a reported 18/20 on real frames), ANUOLUWA-1/doorstep-concierge, and iot-forge/Stoop ('expected visitors matched'). That is out of roughly 36 visible Ring repos.
- Day-one proof: With a Playground token: open a WHEP session on the Motion simulation (browser-built offer), sample 30 frames, and get at least 80% person-count accuracy in a door polygon with the watermark masked. Drive the ICS matcher from that real detection and get the correct practitioner for a 2-booking, 3-minute-apart table test. Also verify a ring-sandbox button_press webhook end to end, with raw-byte HMAC and a 2xx in under 5 s.
- Redesign to pass: Make the real Playground live view the trigger rather than an add-on: person-in-door-zone detection on the Motion simulation starts matching, and a second confirmatory WHEP frame drives walk-away. Present ring-sandbox button_press as the labelled production path for US devices. Alternatively, narrow to shared suites where one doorbell rings every room, and lead with the booking-matched silent routing.

**Round-2 judge ratings (1–5; base case: solo, India, VVD on Mac, no Bee hardware, no US Ring device). Weighted = 0.25·Tech + 0.20·Design + 0.20·Impact + 0.20·Originality + 0.15·Feasibility.**

| Judge lens | Tech | Design | Impact | Originality | Feasibility | Weighted | Confidence | One-line verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| devrel | 2 | 3 | 2 | 2 | 3 | 2.35 | medium | Ring is not load-bearing here; weakest Ring concept. |
| engineer | 2 | 3 | 2 | 2 | 3 | 2.35 | medium | Weakest Ring option: the decisive Ring signals are simulated, the removal test is partial, and check-in rivals already ship the loop. |
| product | 2 | 3 | 2 | 2 | 3 | 2.35 | medium | A doorbell-as-check-in hypothesis with no demand evidence, strong non-Ring substitutes and a simulated core trigger. |
| **Mean of three** | 2.00 | 3.00 | 2.00 | 2.00 | 3.00 | **2.35** | | Scenario weighted: solo+hw 2.35; duo 2.67; duo+hw 2.67 |

**Why each judge rated it that way (verbatim rationale, one per criterion):**

- *devrel* — Tech: Neither button_press nor motion.human exists on the Playground (JDR-07); both come from the third-party ring-sandbox, so the Ring calls are device reads plus one WHEP frame and Ring is peripheral (Stage 1 risk). — Design: Silent routing to one practitioner with acknowledgement to the client's booking page is good small-business UX; filming clients at a wellness door is sensitive and most beats are simulated. — Impact: Evidence is almost entirely vendor marketing (TapIn, The Receptionist); the removal test partly fails since a booking-link 'I'm here' button delivers most value. — Originality: Ring natively notifies shared users of presses and offers Quick Replies; TapIn/The Receptionist do silent check-in; banksythequantLab/front-desk already pairs a Ring door with office rules. — Feasibility: 90 h fits, but eligibility is conditional on redesigning the live trigger around Playground motion frames.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 2, feasibility 3 (No change; a US doorbell is not in any scenario.); duo tech 3, feasibility 4 (52 h each allows the person-in-zone redesign.); duo+hw tech 3, feasibility 4 (As duo.)
- *engineer* — Tech: As planned, the core trigger (button_press) and the walk-away signal (motion.human history) do not exist on the Playground (forum 45309; history holds only on_demand) and come only from ring-sandbox, a third-party emulator not listed in Ring's docs, which is the rules' test (evt-rules.md L350). The real calls are peripheral (device and configuration reads plus a contested WHEP frame for party size). The gate's redes… — Design: Silent routing to one practitioner, first-acknowledgement-claims and an acknowledgement pushed to the client's booking page close a real loop, but iPad check-in products already deliver routing and acknowledgement, and walk-away sampling clutters the owner's history because every live view creates an on_demand record. — Impact: Moderate, non-safety pain; evidence is vendor marketing (TapIn, The Receptionist, Lobbywizard) and an unsourced no-show figure; using a Ring doorbell as a check-in device is untested. — Originality: Partial removal test (a booking-link 'I'm here' button gives most of the value), visible business-door entrants (front-desk, doorstep-concierge, Stoop) and native Alexa+ Greetings and Quick Replies overlap the door loop. — Feasibility: 90 h fits about 104 h (estimates), but passing Stage 1 needs the Playground-triggered redesign with WHEP and at least 80% person-count accuracy on watermarked CC BY footage, both unverified.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 2, feasibility 3 (Ring remains Playground-only. No change.); duo tech 3, feasibility 4 (A second person can absorb the Playground-triggered redesign (WHEP sampler plus person counter), lifting the share of real Ring calls.); duo+hw tech 3, feasibility 4 (Same as duo_no_hw.)
- *product* — Tech: The trigger that matters (doorbell press) exists only as a ring-sandbox emulator event in the base scenario; the gate's redesign makes a Playground Motion live-view person count the check-in, which is a stretch on watermarked pre-recorded footage, and WHEP is contested (ring.md section 0; gate conditions). Ring is load-bearing in name but thin and simulated at the key beat. — Design: Silent routing to one practitioner's phone, a booking-link acknowledgement for the client and a walk-away flag are sensible for door-only suites; generic across devices and the removal test admits a link 'I'm here' button delivers most value (prior removal_test). — Impact: Demand evidence is vendor marketing (TapIn, The Receptionist, Lobbywizard; rival matrix) and no reviewed source shows Ring doorbells used as business check-in (trends_home.md T4 note: untested hypothesis). HIPAA practices excluded narrows the market; client privacy at a wellness door is a concern. — Originality: Strong check-in rivals already close the acknowledgement loop; visible entrant banksythequantLab/front-desk classifies Ring events for a law office with an MCP server and Fire TV lobby screen, plus Ring Appstore business apps (Lumeo, QueueFlow Sentinel; rival matrix). Booking-matched routing is the only unverified-in-rivals piece. — Feasibility: 90 h solo (prior estimate) with standard webhook, scheduling and browser CV, but gated on Playground access from India, WHEP success and 80% person-count accuracy on 30 frames (gate conditions).
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 2, feasibility 3 (Playground only; no change.); duo tech 2, feasibility 4 (52 h per person.); duo+hw tech 2, feasibility 4 (Same as duo without hardware.)

**Round-1 judge ratings (3 Oct, same model; shown for traceability):** devrel 2/3/2/2/3 = 2.35; engineer 2/3/2/2/3 = 2.35; product 2/3/2/2/3 = 2.35

**Red-team verdicts:** not red-teamed (did not reach the top of either round's ranking); ranking rests on gate and judge scores alone.

---

#### RNG-3: OnAir: two-sided camera-use agreements for in-home workers

**Round:** 1. **Track:** Ring (primary). Can also enter the AWS Builder and Open Source mini challenges.

**Pitch:** A camera-use agreement that nannies, home-care aides or cleaners and the household that employs them both sign. It names which Ring cameras exist, when live view may be used and for what, and who sees what. During each shift the worker is told within moments when a Ring camera's live view is opened, whether that matched the agreement, and when camera privacy settings change. Both sides get a per-shift receipt. OnAir never stores or analyses video.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Primary user | Nannies, home-care aides and house cleaners working in homes that have Ring cameras; they are the people being filmed. Also the household employer who owns the Ring account, and optionally the agency that placed the worker. US focus, because of the Ring partner path. |
| Frequency | Every shift, which is daily for full-time nannies and aides. How often households open live view during shifts was not measured in reviewed sources. |
| Severity | Moderate-high for workers: loss of privacy and trust, legal exposure around audio recording, and fear of retaliation if they raise concerns. Fi2W reports that 63% of over 100 nannies surveyed by Matahari did not know if or how their employers record their voices. Some workers say they welcome video without audio as backup proof against false accusations. For employers the stakes are moderate: retaining workers, and notice duties that Ring's own content policy places on employers who use workplace monitoring. |
| Current workaround | Workers hunt for cameras themselves and often stay silent to keep the job (Fi2W). Some households disclose verbally or in a written work agreement; per Fi2W, Massachusetts requires written terms for entering a live-in worker's space and Virginia bans cameras in live-in workers' private quarters. The Ring account holder can see live-view history in their own app. The worker sees nothing. |
| Amazon integration and its indispensable role | Ring is both the subject and the data source. The agreement is checked against Ring data: the device list with user-assigned names, device status, configurations (privacy-zone count and motion detection on/off; coordinates never shown) and Event History on_demand records, which the API docs define as user- or partner-initiated live-view sessions. One WHEP frame per camera at signing shows the worker exactly what each camera covers. is_third_party_reviewed lets OnAir show that it never pulled media itself. AWS runs the shift-window poller and the shared ledger. |
| Removal test (honest) | Without Ring nothing meaningful is left. The product is a transparency layer over Ring's own live-view and settings data. Ring is fully load-bearing. Caveat: the same pattern could later be extended to other camera vendors that expose access-log APIs, so the agreement schema is vendor-neutral. |
| Wow moment under 45 s | 0-10 s: The aide's phone, with no Ring account, shows today's agreement: Front Door camera, live view allowed for arrival checks, private hour 13:00-14:00, audio pledge, and a frame of what the camera sees (captured at signing). 10-25 s, REAL: on the household phone Dana taps 'Check the front door' in OnAir, which opens a real Playground WHEP session. The aide's phone shows 'Front Door live view opened 14:02 by Dana via OnAir, for an arrival check, which your agreement allows', then '47 s, within agreement' when it ends. The latency is measured on day 1 and shown on screen. 25-40 s, REAL event with a shifted demo clock that is labelled: a Playground console simulation creates an on_demand record not opened through OnAir. Both phones show 'Live view opened from the Ring app or another app during your private hour', and Dana is prompted to add a reason, which goes into the shared ledger. … |
| Original mechanism | An inverted access log. During agreed shift windows only, a poller reads Event History for on_demand records and diffs device configurations and status against a versioned, two-party agreement. Sessions OnAir itself opened are attributed to the named viewer. All others are labelled 'Ring app or another app' (the API does not say who, and OnAir does not guess). A deterministic rule engine classifies each session as within agreement, outside agreement (time or purpose), or unexplained, and settings drift is detected the same way. Results go to the worker as low-key notices and to both parties as a per-shift receipt they each acknowledge. Changes to the agreement need both parties to accept again. The only media used is one field-of-view frame per camera at signing, shown and never stored (only its hash). OnAir's own media access can be checked through is_third_party_reviewed on every even… |
| Simulator or real-data route (data route) | EventBridge Scheduler starts a Lambda poller only inside agreed shift windows. It makes server-to-server calls to api.amazonvision.com for history, configurations and status, and writes session rows (device, start, end, attribution, rule result) and config snapshots (counts and on/off flags only) to DynamoDB. The rule engine then sends Web Push notices to the worker and household PWAs. Receipts are stored in DynamoDB and both parties acknowledge them. No video, audio or images are stored. The field-of-view frame is streamed to the signing screen and only its SHA-256 is kept. Retention defaults to 90 days, and either party can delete their data. |
| Authentication | Ring: the household's Playground bearer token (production: one-way account linking plus refresh tokens) is held server-side and never shown. The worker never touches Ring credentials and needs no Ring account. Both parties sign in with magic links. Signing and amending the agreement requires an affirmative tap from both parties, as Ring's policy requires consent by affirmative action. Revoking consent stops polling immediately. |
| Preview constraints | The Playground has no Ring mobile app and no owner account, so live views opened from the Ring app cannot be shown; only Playground console simulations and OnAir's own WHEP sessions create on_demand records. The single sandbox Doorbell Pro probably has empty privacy zones (unverified), so the drift demo uses a fixture. The Playground has no device_online/offline webhooks, so status is polled. Tokens last 30 min, which interrupts long shift demos. In production, E2EE devices do not appear in the Partner API at all. Whether TAKE affects history metadata is not verified. |
| Hardware | MacBook M4 Pro for development. Two phones (household and worker). No Ring hardware for the Playground path. Optional US Ring Indoor Cam with a plan for the real path. |
| SDK versions | No official Ring SDK; plain HTTPS client (httpx or fetch). ring-sandbox 0.5.0 (Python >=3.11, MIT) pinned, for fixtures and tests. ring-api-helloworld (MIT) as the WHEP reference. Node 20/22 LTS through nvm. web-push library (version to pin). |
| Deployment prerequisites | Amazon Developer sign-in for the Playground. AWS Paid-plan account in us-east-1. VAPID keys. Public HTTPS PWA kept live through 20 Nov 2026. A clear 'not legal advice' notice and privacy policy. For the real path: an identity-verified Ring developer account, app registration with Event History scope, US devices with a plan, and account linking. |
| Firecrawl: research role | Found and read the Fi2W investigation (worker survey, state rules) and checked the angle against the nursing-home camera discussion. Ring's Content Policy was read from the saved research capture. Together they shaped the agreement fields (audio pledge, private windows, private-quarters exclusion). |
| Firecrawl: runtime role | None. The product reads only Ring data and data the two parties enter. Scraping law summaries at runtime would amount to unreliable legal advice. |
| AWS Builder path | EventBridge Scheduler (14M invocations/month free per the pricing page) triggers a 128 MB Lambda once a minute during shift windows; each invocation polls 4 times at 15 s intervals. DynamoDB stores agreements, sessions and receipts with TTL. Lambda sends Web Push. Optional KMS key to encrypt token material. Budgets alarm. Built with Kiro or Claude Code on Bedrock. Value: polling runs only while a worker is present (data minimisation) and costs close to nothing. No model is in the decision path. |
| Open Source path | (1) Publish a vendor-neutral 'camera-use-agreement' JSON schema with a reference rule engine and tests: windows, purposes, attribution classes, drift rules. It is useful to worker-advocacy groups and to other camera APIs. (2) Publish a 'ring-liveview-audit' library that turns on_demand history plus configuration diffs into a per-shift log, including the measured on_demand latency and the client-side event_types filtering workaround. (3) Upstream ring-sandbox PR adding a fidelity note or option for on_demand delay, based on measured Playground data. |
| Fallback | If the delay is over 60 s or records only appear after a session ends: change the promise from 'live now' to 'notice within N minutes plus a per-shift receipt', and keep real-time notices for sessions opened through OnAir, which it knows about immediately. If Ring-app sessions do not appear: make the agreement require household live checks to go through OnAir's viewer (weaker, and stated honestly). If the employer-incentive test fails: reposition for agencies that place nannies and aides and would require it as a condition of placement. |
| Falsification test (≤1 day) | In one day. (1) Technical: 20 trials measuring the time from starting a live view (an OnAir WHEP session and a Playground console simulation) to its on_demand record appearing in GET history. A median over 60 s, or records appearing only after the session ends, falsifies the live-notice claim. (2) Demand: show the two-screen prototype to 3 domestic workers or caregivers and 3 household employers. If fewer than 2 of the 3 employers would opt in voluntarily, the self-serve buyer thesis is falsified. |
| Risk profile | Lowest technical and simulation risk of the three: the core loop runs on real Playground calls (devices, status, configurations, on_demand history, WHEP). Highest buyer-incentive risk. Medium legal and reputational sensitivity, which counts in its favour only if handled carefully. Lowest cloud cost. |
| Estimate (unmeasured) | Solo 70 h; two-person 40 h per person; cloud Estimate: about $2-8 total through 20 Nov 2026. Poller: 60 invocations/h over 8 h/day for 30 days is about 14,400 invocations; at about 50 s and 0.125 GB that is about 90,000 GB-s, roughly $1.50 at $0.0000166667/GB-s if outside the free allowance. DynamoDB and Scheduler cost cents, plus a few dollars of CloudWatch logs. No Bedrock, Rekognition or media storage.. Assumptions: Estimates, not measurements. One engineer. Hours include day-1 spikes, including the latency measurement (6h), Ring client, poller and attribution (8h), agreement model with two-party acceptance and versioning (10h), rule engine and drift detection (7h), worker and household PWAs (14h), field-of-view preview (5h), AWS deployment (6h), tests on fixtures (5h), and docs, video and friction log (10h). hours_duo is elapsed hours per person with about 15% coordination overhead. |

**Demand evidence** (quality labelled by the researcher):

- Investigation of immigrant domestic workers being recorded at work (published 7 Apr 2026). Cameras, including Ring, are pervasive in employers' homes. A Matahari survey of over 100 nannies found 63% did not know if or how their voices were recorded. Some workers welcome video without audio as protection against false accusations. Massachusetts bars monitoring workers' private communications, and Virginia bars cameras in live-in workers' private quarters. NDWA estimates about 2.2M domestic workers. — [https://www.fi2w.org/no-privacy-in-private-immigrant-domestic-workers-are-being-recorded-on-the-job/](https://www.fi2w.org/no-privacy-in-private-immigrant-domestic-workers-are-being-recorded-on-the-job/) — *Investigative journalism (Fi2W, 7 Apr 2026, read in full) citing an NGO survey whose year and method are not stated. Moderate.*
- Ring's Content Policy forbids apps for secretly surveilling roommates or employees, requires apps to give customers easy access to monitoring activity or access logs, and makes employers who use workplace-monitoring apps responsible for notice to employees and any required consents. — [https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/content-policy.html#122-covert-third-party-surveillance](https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/content-policy.html#122-covert-third-party-surveillance) — *Official platform policy (undated). This is a norm on the supply side, not user demand, but it shows the concept matches Ring's stated direction.*
- Ring's Search Party ad (Feb 2026) drew 'creepy' and 'dystopian' reactions, the Flock partnership was cancelled days later, and the CEO was still addressing privacy fears in March 2026. — [https://techcrunch.com/2026/03/08/rings-jamie-siminoff-has-been-trying-to-calm-privacy-fears-since-the-super-bowl-but-his-answers-may-not-help/](https://techcrunch.com/2026/03/08/rings-jamie-siminoff-has-been-trying-to-calm-privacy-fears-since-the-super-bowl-but-his-answers-may-not-help/) — *Repeated across news outlets (GeekWire, TechCrunch; via trends_home). Demand-side sentiment.*

**Rivals named at generation** (strongest feature; our difference; evidence status):

| Rival | Strongest feature | Our difference | Evidence status |
|---|---|---|---|
| [Attest (hackathon entrant, josepha-mayo/attest)](https://github.com/josepha-mayo/attest) | Tamper-evident, offline-verifiable visit records that keep Ring observations, the worker's own statements and coordinator conclusions separate. It also logs live-view sessions it brokers into signed coverage. | Attest answers 'did the visit happen?' for disputes. OnAir answers 'how is the camera being used on me right now, and is that what we agreed?' for the person being filmed. Whether Attest notifies workers of live views opened in the Ring app is not verified in reviewed sources. | README read in full via the saved GitHub API capture; not run. |
| [Ring app Event History (owner-side, native)](https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#event-history) | Native, free, complete record of live-view sessions for the account holder. The Partner API defines on_demand as user- or partner-initiated live view. | Faces the worker and checks against the agreement. The filmed worker has no Ring account and sees nothing today. | Partner API doc read. The consumer app's history UI was not read. |
| [Beside Care (Ring Appstore)](https://ring.com/appstore/beside-care) | Privacy-first caregiver digests from motion events, with 0-day video retention. $19.99/mo per device. | Beside Care watches the person receiving care on the family's behalf. OnAir gives notice and an agreement to the worker who is being filmed. Worker-facing notices in Beside Care are not verified in reviewed sources. | Listing read (via rivals_home). |
| [Written nanny or agency work agreements (substitute)](https://www.fi2w.org/no-privacy-in-private-immigrant-domestic-workers-are-being-recorded-on-the-job/) | Zero tech, legally familiar, and required in some forms (Massachusetts written terms for live-in workers, per Fi2W). | A paper agreement cannot tell the worker when a camera is actually viewed or when its settings change. OnAir makes the agreement observable. | Described in the Fi2W article; no template product reviewed. |

**Smallest credible MVP:**

- Agreement builder, filled from GET /v1/devices (user-assigned names, never IDs). Fields: allowed purposes, allowed and private windows, audio pledge, retention pledge, notice preferences. Both parties accept through magic links, and every version is kept.
- Field-of-view preview at signing: one WHEP frame per camera, shown to both parties and then discarded, keeping only its hash.
- Shift-window poller: on_demand history every 15 s per camera, filtered client-side (one entrant reports the event_types filter is ignored), with OnAir-opened sessions attributed to their named viewer.
- Rule engine and configuration-drift detection (privacy-zone count, motion detection on/off, device offline), with unit tests on ring-sandbox fixtures.
- Worker PWA: a status line ('camera live now' or 'last viewed'), low-key notices and the shift receipt. Household dashboard: notices delivered, breach reasons, and receipts both sides acknowledge.
- Self-audit panel showing is_third_party_reviewed=false for every event, as proof OnAir never downloaded media.
- Data deletion for either party, a privacy policy, and an explicit 'not legal advice' notice. AWS deployment.

**Deliberately excluded stretch:**

- Indoor-camera coverage tested on real devices (the Playground only has a doorbell).
- Detecting audio use, which the API does not expose.
- Integration with Ring Modes (not exposed).
- Agency dashboard across many households.
- Cryptographically signed receipts (Attest already covers this well).
- Spanish and other UI languages (high value for this user group; first stretch item).
- Fire TV or Echo indicators in the home.

**APIs, events and actions** (status as assessed by the researcher; 'unverified' means not confirmed in reviewed docs, 'gated' means not available to entrants, 'simulated' means not real in the demo):

| Status | API / event / action | Documentation |
|---|---|---|
| verified | Developer Playground one-click OAuth token (30 min) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/release-notes.html#developers-playground |
| verified | GET /v1/devices (user-assigned names for the agreement) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#device-discovery |
| verified | GET /v1/devices/{id}/status (online/offline, reported_at) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#status |
| verified | GET /v1/devices/{id}/configurations (privacy_zones count and motion_detection.enabled; coordinates never displayed) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#configurations |
| verified | GET /v1/history/devices/{id}/events (on_demand records appear on the Playground from simulations and media requests; how fast they appear is unverified) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#event-history |
| unverified | on_demand records created by the owner's own Ring-app live views (the doc says 'user or partner initiated'; cannot be shown on the Playground) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#event-history |
| unverified | history attribute is_third_party_reviewed (self-audit that OnAir never pulled media) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#is_third_party_reviewed |
| verified | POST /v1/devices/{id}/media/streaming/whep/sessions (one field-of-view frame at signing, plus OnAir-brokered checks) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#whep-live-video-streaming |
| gated | device_online / device_offline webhooks (real devices; the Playground path polls status instead) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#device-offline |
| gated | app_integration_removed webhook (consent revocation ends monitoring; real path) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#webhook-events |
| simulated | Configuration drift (privacy zones 2 to 0) from a ring-sandbox recorded fixture | https://github.com/josepha-mayo/ring-sandbox |

**Blockers:** Unknown delay between a live view starting and its on_demand record appearing in history. Records may appear only after the session ends.; It is unverified whether live views opened in the owner's Ring app reach the partner history in practice; the doc says they do.; Reports conflict on the history event_types filter (an entrant says it is ignored; the forum says use the plural). Filter client-side.; The 30-min Playground token interrupts long demos.; Clear 'not legal advice' messaging is needed, so the product does not imply legal compliance.

**Reasons judges might reject it:** Unclear buyer: why would an employer install a tool that reports on themselves? Adoption may depend on agencies or advocacy groups.; Can be read as a compliance dashboard with no 'wow' and no AI or CV. Tech Implementation may be judged thin unless the attribution, drift and latency engineering is shown.; It cannot say who opened a Ring-app live view, only that one happened.; Owner-app sessions are documented but cannot be demonstrated on the Playground.; Legal sensitivity: it must not suggest it makes a household compliant with recording or monitoring laws.; Niche user group in a hackathon whose Ring examples stress accessibility, caretaking and business.

**Gate result:** overall **pass** (eligibility pass, feasibility pass; native overlap low, visible crowding medium).

- Eligibility: The core loop runs on surfaces the Playground supports, all called at runtime: device list, status and configurations; Event History on_demand records (live views), which entrant doorstep-ring reports are the only events the Playground history holds; and WHEP. The drift beat uses a labelled ring-sandbox fixture and is not claimed live. Ring-app owner sessions are stated as documented but not demonstrable. No media is stored and there is no face or plate analysis. Its anti-covert design matches Ring Content Policy §12.2 as quoted in the concept (policy page read via saved capture).
- Feasibility: The 70 h solo estimate (estimate) is the lowest in the set and leaves a large buffer. The unknown is the delay between a live view and its on_demand record appearing in history. The fallback ('notice within N minutes' plus a per-shift receipt, with real-time notice for sessions OnAir opens itself) is honest and rules-safe. is_third_party_reviewed and the 'event_types filter is ignored' anecdote are unverified, so filter client-side and do not depend on that attribute. The 30-minute token interrupts long takes.
- Conditions: (1) OnAir-opened WHEP sessions produce on_demand records that GET history returns. Latency is measured over 20 trials and reported in the README. (2) Whether Playground console simulations also create on_demand records is verified. If not, a second labelled script stands in for 'another app'. (3) A 'not legal advice' notice. No claim of legal compliance. CC BY attribution for any Playground footage shown. (4) Both parties' consent screens are shown. The worker never handles Ring credentials.
- Native overlap: The Ring app shows the account owner their own event and live-view history, and the Partner API defines on_demand as user- or partner-initiated live view. No worker-facing notice or two-party agreement check was found in reviewed sources. Beside Care watches the care recipient, not the worker.
- Crowding: josepha-mayo/attest (MIT, CI) logs the live-view sessions it brokers into signed coverage, which is the closest visible overlap; its answer is 'did the visit happen', not worker notice. The privacy, consent and redaction theme has about 19 visible repos across tracks (github.md), and AtchayamG/bee-bystander does consent for Bee.
- Day-one proof: With a Playground token: open 20 OnAir WHEP sessions and poll GET /v1/history/devices/{id}/events every 15 s. Each session must appear as an on_demand record; record the median and p90 latency, and whether records appear only after the session ends. Also read /configurations to confirm the privacy_zones and motion_detection fields are present for the drift diff.
- Redesign to pass: Not required. To lift Tech Implementation, put the measured on_demand latency, the attribution classes and the drift diffing on screen, because the concept has no AI or CV 'wow'.

**Round-2 judge ratings (1–5; base case: solo, India, VVD on Mac, no Bee hardware, no US Ring device). Weighted = 0.25·Tech + 0.20·Design + 0.20·Impact + 0.20·Originality + 0.15·Feasibility.**

| Judge lens | Tech | Design | Impact | Originality | Feasibility | Weighted | Confidence | One-line verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| devrel | 3 | 3 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 2.95 | medium | Honest and cheap, but after the redesign it is a receipt without a wow moment. |
| engineer | 3 | 3 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 2.95 | medium | The most honest metadata-only Ring build with a policy-aligned idea, but technically modest, buyer-incentive-bound, and overlapping both Attest and RNG-4. |
| product | 3 | 3 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 2.95 | medium | Low-risk, honest metadata product for an under-served person (the worker) whose buyer does not want it; modest ceiling. |
| **Mean of three** | 3.00 | 3.00 | 2.00 | 3.00 | 4.00 | **2.95** | | Scenario weighted: solo+hw 2.95; duo 3.05; duo+hw 3.05 |

**Why each judge rated it that way (verbatim rationale, one per criterion):**

- *devrel* — Tech: Scored as the metadata-only OnAir receipt (prior_redteam RNG-3.redesign): real Playground reads of devices, status, configurations (privacy-zone count, motion on/off) and on_demand history matched against household announcements; no media, works on TAKE devices. Thin: no processing beyond matching and diffing, and the 'another app' live view is a labelled stand-in. — Design: Two-party agreement, low-key worker notices and honest 'OnAir cannot see playback' line follow Ring Content Policy and the UX guide (ring.md §8); announce-then-match is a sensible fix but the receipt is modest and the buyer (employer) has weak incentive. — Impact: Red team 2.5: credible harm (Fi2W nanny survey) but unclear buyer, niche US population, and contract camera clauses are the standard substitute. — Originality: Inverting Ring toward the filmed person is a non-security use (creative list), but Attest shares the mechanism and audience and consent/privacy is a ~19-repo theme; red team 3.5 trimmed for the RNG-4 primitive overlap. — Feasibility: ~60-70 h after cuts, deterministic; depends on console/WHEP sessions producing on_demand records (day-1 test) and Playground access from India.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 3, feasibility 4 (No change.); duo tech 3, feasibility 5 (40 h each.); duo+hw tech 3, feasibility 5 (As duo.)
- *engineer* — Tech: Scored as the red-team redesign 'OnAir metadata-only camera-use receipt' (prior_redteam.json RNG-3.redesign). The runtime Ring use is honest and robust: devices (user-assigned names), status, configurations (privacy_zones count only, never coordinates, per the UX guide MUST) and on_demand Event History, all Playground-supported, with announce-then-match against Ring's own record, a deterministic agreement ledger and… — Design: Two-party agreement with versioned re-acceptance, announce-before-check, per-shift receipts, a household access log of OnAir's own calls (Content Policy 1.2.2) and 'OnAir cannot see playback or audio use' are coherent and compliance-grade. Deductions: compliance-style rather than delightful, 30-minute Playground tokens interrupt shift demos, and the real-time beat is a declaration by the household rather than Ring d… — Impact: Credible under-served user (Fi2W 7 Apr 2026: pervasive cameras, nannies unaware of recording; NDWA about 2.2M domestic workers) but capped by buyer incentive (the household must opt in to report on itself), the API's inability to say who opened a Ring-app view, and written agreements with dated acknowledgement as the standard substitute; red team revised impact to 2.5. — Originality: Turning on_demand semantics into a worker-facing receipt aligned with Ring's Content Policy and the 2026 privacy backlash is fresh, but Attest (pushed Oct 3) already shows brokered sessions to the worker as a signed timeline, Routines/Density and Beside Care occupy the caregiver space, and the configuration-drift primitive is shared with RNG-4; red team revised to 3.5, I round down for the Attest adjacency. — Feasibility: About 70 h minus the cut viewer, the lowest build in the original set, against about 104 h (estimates), no model, no hardware. Not 5 because the redesign carries a day-1 kill test that depends on Playground behaviour (a console press and a WHEP stand-in must each yield an on_demand record within 5 minutes), the latency study over 20 trials is required, and Playground access from India is unverified for every Ring co…
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 3, feasibility 4 (Ring remains Playground-only. No change.); duo tech 3, feasibility 5 (At about 40 h each (estimate) there is ample time for the latency study, replay mode and the OSS schema; the tech ceiling stays bounded by Playground surfaces.); duo+hw tech 3, feasibility 5 (Same as duo_no_hw.)
- *product* — Tech: Scored as the red-team redesign 'OnAir metadata-only camera-use receipt' (prior_redteam.json RNG-3.redesign): only devices, status, configurations and on_demand history are called (REAL on the Playground; organizer 45309 confirms console buttons write on_demand records), announce-then-match replaces attribution, no media so it works on TAKE devices (Ring-specific advantage). Thin but genuine; the 'Ring-app stand-in'… — Design: Worker-facing notices without a Ring account, per-shift receipts, agreement classes (announced and confirmed, unannounced, outside window), honest exposure lines ('cannot see playback of recorded clips or audio'), private-quarters checklist (redesign items 2, 4, 5). Coherent for the person being filmed, but it is a compliance dashboard with no wow (red team). — Impact: Filmed in-home workers report real distrust (Fi2W 7 Apr 2026; r/Nanny snippet, rival matrix) and Ring's Content Policy treats secret roommate/employee surveillance as prohibited. But the buyer is the employer who installs a tool that reports on themselves (prior risk; red team: buyer-incentive problem unsolved), agencies as a channel are unverified, and recorded-clip playback is invisible to the API, so the receipt … — Originality: Attest (josepha-mayo/attest, 204 commits, pushed Oct 3) already shows brokered live views to workers post-visit (rival matrix); contract camera clauses with dated acknowledgement are standard practice; Ring Modes natively enforce a private hour but are not in the Partner API (red team differentiation_holds). In-shift notice checked against a two-party agreement plus drift notices is not verified in any rival; shares… — Feasibility: About 60-70 h after cutting the viewer (prior 70 h minus 8-10 h; estimate), deterministic polling and diffing, lowest cloud cost; still gated on the Playground opening from India and day-1 checks that console/WHEP sessions yield on_demand records within 5 minutes.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 3, feasibility 4 (Playground only; no change.); duo tech 3, feasibility 4 (About 40 h per person; buffer for latency instrumentation and household access logs.); duo+hw tech 3, feasibility 4 (Same as duo without hardware.)

**Round-1 judge ratings (3 Oct, same model; shown for traceability):** devrel 3/4/3/4/4 = 3.55; engineer 3/3/3/4/5 = 3.50; product 3/3/3/4/5 = 3.50

| Round 1 (single reviewer) | **survives_with_redesign** | [Attest (josepha-mayo/attest, Ring-track entrant, MIT, CI, also entering AWS Builder and Open Source)](https://github.com/josepha-mayo/attest) | 2.5/3/2.5/3.5/4 | Partly.
