## 3. The 8 round-2 concepts (two per track, generated after the round-1 red teams)


### Track: Fire TV

#### FTV-4: Across the Room: a living-room voice gym for soft speech

**Round:** 2. **Track:** Fire TV (primary, Vega OS on the Vega Virtual Device). Mini challenges: AWS Builder, Open Source.

**Pitch:** Up to 90% of people with Parkinson's develop a soft, monotone voice (APDA). The evidence-based programs (LSVT LOUD, SPEAK OUT!) prescribe 10-15 minutes of loudness practice every day for life, but the home feedback tools are a clinician-licensed PC program (LSVT Companion/Coach), US-daytime live Zoom sessions, or a phone sound-meter app that a person with tremor has to hold at arm's length while trying to project across a room. Across the Room makes the TV the stage: the phone lies on the coffee table as a microphone (a web page, no install, no audio leaves the phone), and the TV shows a huge loudness arc with a target band set from the person's own calibrated baseline, times the sustained 'ahh', draws pitch glides, and runs the daily set with the remote alone (OK = go/next, Back = pause). Phrases are the household's own ('Rohan, dinner is ready'), typed once by a family member. A second phone across the room lets the family tap 'heard it', so intelligibility at real distance is logged next to loudness. Targets and rests adapt deterministically from the last seven days of that person's numbers; no model is in the loop. Not a medical device.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Primary user | Adults with Parkinson's disease (or other soft-voice conditions) who have been told by a speech-language pathologist to practise loudness daily, plus the spouse or adult child in the same room. First segment (assumption): India households with a person with Parkinson's and a Fire TV stick, where LSVT/SPEAK OUT! clinicians and US-daytime sessions are hard to reach. Second: US households after an LSVT or SPEAK OUT! course. |
| Frequency | Daily. LSVT Global says practice continues 'at least once a day for 10-15 minutes' after the 4x4-week course and should become 'a life-long habit' (vendor page, undated). SPEAK OUT! runs home practice sessions Monday to Friday (PVP page, dated 2026-09-22). |
| Severity | High for communication and quality of life, not life-threatening. APDA (2021): 'up to 90% of people with PD will experience reduced vocal loudness, monotone voice and imprecise articulation which can negatively impact communication and quality of life.' |
| Current workaround | Paper homework sheets with no measurement; the LSVT Companion / LSVT Coach Client Edition (FDA-cleared PC program, clinician-mediated); SPEAK OUT! live or recorded Zoom sessions at 10:00 US Central (21:30 IST); a generic phone decibel-meter app or hardware sound level meter held by a caregiver (a Facebook group thread asks 'What app does Speak Out use to measure voice volume?'; snippet only, body not read). |
| Amazon integration and its indispensable role | A React Native for Vega app on the Vega Virtual Device is the only display during practice. It uses TVEventHandler for OK/Back/D-pad, FocusManager/TVFocusGuideView for the few on-screen controls, KeplerA11ySettingsInterface (UI scale, caption preferences, screen-reader state) to size the meter and text, react-native-w3cmedia to play Polly prompts and start tones, fetch/WebSocket to the AWS relay, and AsyncStorage for the on-device profile. The remote is the only input the person with Parkinson's touches; the phone is a microphone they never pick up. |
| Removal test (honest) | Partly strong, with a stated weakness. The exercise is to be heard across a room, so the feedback must be readable from across a room: a 6-inch phone meter at arm's length defeats the task, and PD tremor makes holding a phone while projecting hard. A laptop or tablet on a stand could show the same meter (the LSVT Companion is a PC program), so the TV is not the only possible large display. What the TV adds: it is already in the room at 10 feet, it has a physical remote that needs no fine touch, the family sees the same screen, and the phone needs no install. If the one-day falsification shows users prefer reading the meter from a tablet at arm's length, the TV premise weakens to 'convenient', not 'indispensable'. |
| Wow moment under 45 s | 0:00 TV: 'Say ahh, as long and loud as you can.' A phone lies on the coffee table in frame. 0:03 OK on the remote; a huge arc fills as the voice rises and turns green inside the target band set from yesterday's baseline; a seconds counter runs. 0:12 '14.2 s, your longest this week' with a 7-day bar chart. 0:18 D-pad right: 'Pitch glide'; a line climbs the screen as the voice slides up. 0:30 Phrase card 'Rohan, dinner is ready'; the user says it; a family member's phone in the corner of the frame taps 'heard it' and the TV logs 'Heard across the room: yes'. 0:40 Back: session summary with 'Not a medical device' footer. One unedited take with an on-screen elapsed timer. |
| Original mechanism | (1) A hands-free 10-foot loudness meter with personal calibration: the phone's getUserMedia stream is analysed locally (Web Audio AnalyserNode RMS at about 20 Hz, autoGainControl requested off) and converted to decibels relative to the person's own quiet-room baseline recorded at a marked distance; only numbers travel to the TV over WebSocket, no audio. No absolute dB SPL is claimed. (2) A deterministic adaptive band: the target is the median of the last five sessions' peaks plus 1 dB, capped; rest time follows measured phonation time. No LLM decides anything. (3) A carry-over check across the room: a second phone held by a family member sends 'heard' or 'not heard' per phrase, so the TV records real-distance intelligibility beside loudness, a social loop that meter apps do not offer (not verified in reviewed rivals). (4) Household phrase bank: the family types the ten phrases that matt… |
| Simulator or real-data route (data route) | Phone page (HTTPS on CloudFront) captures the mic, computes RMS/dB-relative and f0 locally, and sends about 20 small JSON messages per second over API Gateway WebSocket (ap-south-1) to a Lambda that fans out to the TV connection in the same room; DynamoDB keeps room membership and per-session summaries (numbers only). The TV app keeps the profile in AsyncStorage and reads the 7-day summary from DynamoDB via a Lambda HTTPS endpoint. Polly prompts are synthesized once (us-east-1), stored in S3 and fetched by the TV. No audio is recorded or stored anywhere. |
| Authentication | No accounts. The TV shows a 6-digit room code and QR; the phone page exchanges it for a signed, 2-hour room token (HMAC in Lambda). Household id is a random UUID on the TV. The optional Nova call uses the Lambda's IAM role. |
| Preview constraints | Runs on the Vega Virtual Device on this Apple-silicon Mac (Rosetta required on macOS 27, 20 GB disk, Node 20 via nvm). The VVD has no microphone, which is fine because the microphone is the phone. The video should show the VVD full-screen on a real TV over HDMI, labelled 'Vega Virtual Device', with the phone and the user in frame. Phone pages need HTTPS, so test against the deployed endpoint, not localhost. The TV app only renders and plays audio; no WebView is needed. |
| Hardware | Apple-silicon Mac (M4 Pro, 24 GB) running the VVD; one Android or iPhone as microphone; a second phone (any) for the family check; optional Fire TV Stick 4K Select (₹5,499, Vega OS, needs vega devmode and an Amazon Developer account) for a real-device take. |
| SDK versions | Vega SDK 0.24 (requires device OS 1.2; RN 0.83 early access, RN 0.72 fallback); @amazon-devices/kepler-a11y-settings-interface-turbo ~1.0.0; @amazon-devices/react-native-w3cmedia (version not captured); @amazon-devices/react-native-async-storage__async-storage 2.1.9000000001; Node 18-20 for Vega builds (machine has v25, use nvm); Amazon Devices Builder Tools MCP 1.0.15 for docs search. |
| Deployment prerequisites | softwareupdate --install-rosetta; Vega SDK install (10-15 min) and VVD boot; a Paid-plan AWS account with promotional credits (Free plan cannot use credits); SAM/CDK stack for API Gateway WebSocket + Lambda + DynamoDB in ap-south-1 and Polly in us-east-1; CloudFront static hosting for the two phone pages; keep the stack live until 20 Nov 2026 (judging per rules). |
| Firecrawl: research role | Used this round to read LSVT Global, APDA, PVP, the JMIR 2025 study and the 2012 LSVT review, and to check that no visible Fire TV entrant does voice practice. Next: scrape 2-3 SLP home-practice handouts to make the daily set faithful, and check Project Relate (Google) and Apple Live Speech pages to confirm they do not do loudness training (not read this session). |
| Firecrawl: runtime role | none |
| AWS Builder path | API Gateway WebSocket, Lambda and DynamoDB carry the phone-to-TV numbers with nothing to keep warm through judging; Polly gives consistent spoken prompts; S3/CloudFront hosts the phone pages and audio; an optional Nova Lite call suggests household phrases behind an accept-with-remote gate. Development with Kiro Crew or Claude Code on Bedrock qualifies for the mini challenge per forum 45321. Honest note: the AI content is light; the judged value is the measurement loop, not the model. |
| Open Source path | Publish 'ten-foot-meter' under MIT: (a) React Native for Vega gauge components (arc meter, sustain timer, 7-day bars) bound to KeplerA11ySettings UI-scale hooks with a non-Vega fallback, and (b) a dependency-free browser module 'phone-mic-meter' (RMS to relative dB, YIN pitch, autoGainControl handling, Wake Lock) with jest tests on recorded fixtures. Candidate PR for the rules' creative example: add Vega (react-native-kepler) support to a popular RN gauge library such as react-native-circular-progress, labelled as an option until its maintainers' stance is checked. |
| Fallback | If WebSocket fails on Vega, use HTTPS long-poll at 250 ms (meter becomes 4 Hz, still usable). If AGC cannot be disabled, recalibrate at the start of every session and score peaks relative to that session's baseline. If Polly is blocked, show text prompts only. If the Vega toolchain blocks, build Fire OS react-native-tvos and record on a real Fire OS stick over adb (rules allow a real device). |
| Falsification test (≤1 day) | One day. (a) With two phones (Android Chrome and iPhone Safari) at a marked 60 cm, play a fixed tone at three known relative levels ten times each and check the page's dB readings are monotonic and repeat within ±2 dB. (b) Clap test on video: phone-to-VVD meter latency p50 under 300 ms over the deployed relay from India to ap-south-1. (c) Two volunteers do the sustained 'ahh' set twice, once reading the meter on the TV from 3 m and once holding the phone; if both prefer the phone or the TV adds no reported value, the TV premise fails and the concept is dropped. |
| Risk profile | Low-to-medium technical risk: simple DSP on the phone, one relay channel, a rendering-only TV app. Medium reputational risk from health framing. Low demo risk: one person can record it. Medium crowding risk by adjacency to Fire TV health entrants, low direct collision (no visible voice-practice project). |
| Distinct from round 1 because | No round-1 concept addresses speech or voice, or people with Parkinson's. Story Night recorded and transcribed voices for an archive; WordGlow had the TV speak and the child listen; BigPrint photographed documents. Here the human performs and the TV measures acoustics in real time with no transcription, no OCR and no model in the loop. It also avoids the round-1 phone-camera/magnifier pattern and the visible camera-based movement-coaching entrants. |
| Estimate (unmeasured) | Solo 95 h; two-person 60 h per person; cloud $5-20 through 20 Nov 2026 (estimate). Assumptions: All figures are estimates. hours_duo is per person when split (TV app vs phone pages and backend) including about 15% coordination. Solo budget: 14 h Vega toolchain, Rosetta, VVD and Node setup; 30 h TV app; 15 h phone pages and DSP; 12 h relay and data; 6 h calibration and unit tests; 10 h video; 8 h submission, friction log and feedback. Assumes a developer fluent in React/TypeScript and new to Vega. Cloud: WebSocket traffic within the free tier (1M messages/month); Lambda and DynamoDB near z… |

**Demand evidence** (quality labelled by the researcher):

- Up to 90% of people with Parkinson's experience reduced vocal loudness, monotone voice and imprecise articulation. — [https://www.apdaparkinson.org/article/lsvt-and-parkinsons-disease/](https://www.apdaparkinson.org/article/lsvt-and-parkinsons-disease/) — *Patient-association article quoting LSVT founders, published 2021-02-16; medium (advocacy source, figure not independently checked).*
- LSVT LOUD is 16 one-hour sessions over 4 weeks plus 'daily homework and carryover exercises'; afterwards 'you'll continue to practice at least once a day for 10-15 minutes'. — [https://www.lsvtglobal.com/LSVTLOUD](https://www.lsvtglobal.com/LSVTLOUD) — *Vendor page, undated; verifies the daily home-practice regimen, not demand volume. Medium.*
- A self-delivered smartphone voice-training app for PD (20 sessions, 4 days/week): 25 of 28 completed; adherence above 90% in 20 of 25; vocal intensity rose from 71.59 to 73.81 dB SPL (P<.001); maximum phonation time 11.15 to 14.01 s (P=.003); satisfaction 75%. — [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11888063/](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11888063/) — *Peer-reviewed single-arm feasibility study (J Med Internet Res, published 2025-02-13, n=25); shows people with PD will practise with app feedback at home. Does not test a TV. High for feasibility, low for effect size (no control arm).*
- LSVT principles include 'repetition, increased repetition of tasks (minimum 15 repetitions) within treatment sessions and home practice' and 'saliency ... individualized hierarchy and carryover assignments' tailored to each person. — [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3316992/](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3316992/) — *Peer-reviewed review by LSVT developers (2012); supports the rep-counting and household-phrase design. Medium (author conflict of interest).*
- Parkinson Voice Project offers 'SPEAK OUT! Home Practice Sessions Monday - Friday, 10:00 AM U.S. CT. Recordings are available 24/7.' — [https://parkinsonvoiceproject.org/](https://parkinsonvoiceproject.org/) — *Nonprofit program page (metadata date 2026-09-22); shows an existing daily home-practice offering and its US-time constraint. Medium.*

**Rivals named at generation** (strongest feature; our difference; evidence status):

| Rival | Strongest feature | Our difference | Evidence status |
|---|---|---|---|
| [LSVT Companion / LSVT Coach, Client Edition (LSVT Global)](https://www.lsvtglobal.com/LSVTLOUD) | FDA-cleared speech-exercise program that 'leads you through LSVT LOUD exercises, measures results and provides instant feedback via a virtual coach'; a study found 7 of 16 sessions on the Companion gave outcomes comparable at 6 months to all-in-person. | Free, runs on the household TV at 10 feet with the remote alone, phone is only a microphone, adds a family 'heard it' loop. We have no clinical validation and are not a therapy program. India availability and pricing of the Companion are not verified in reviewed sources. | Vendor page read 2026-10-04 (undated). |
| [SPEAK OUT! (Parkinson Voice Project)](https://parkinsonvoiceproject.org/program/speak-out/) | Evidence-based program with certified providers, classes and daily home practice sessions (Mon-Fri 10:00 US CT, recordings 24/7). | On-demand, measured feedback at any hour in any time zone; no live clinician. Measured loudness feedback inside SPEAK OUT! sessions is not verified in reviewed sources. | Program page (metadata 2026-08-13) and homepage (2026-09-22) read 2026-10-04. |
| [Smartphone voice-training app, Seoul National University (research prototype)](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11888063/) | Five-stage program (breathing, oral motor, loudness, prosody, functional speech) with published adherence and acoustic outcomes. | TV-sized hands-free meter, remote-only control, family intelligibility check. Public availability of their app is not verified in reviewed sources. | Open-access paper read 2026-10-04 (published 2025-02-13). |
| [Generic phone sound-level meter apps](https://www.facebook.com/groups/160217455195/posts/10161995944525196/) | Free and instant; already used informally by SPEAK OUT! participants (thread title only). | Must be held and read while speaking; no program, no baseline, no family loop. Facebook body not read (search snippet only), so treat as anecdote. | Snippet only; unverified. |

**Smallest credible MVP:**

- Vega RN 0.83 app (0.72 fallback) on the VVD with five screens: pair phone (room code + QR), daily set (sustained 'ahh', high/low glides, 10 household phrases), live meter, session summary with 7-day chart, settings (baseline recalibration, UI scale from KeplerA11ySettings)
- Phone microphone page (HTTPS, no install): getUserMedia with autoGainControl off where supported, Web Audio RMS and YIN pitch at about 20 Hz, Wake Lock, sends numbers only over WebSocket; shows 'Mic live' and nothing else
- Family 'heard it' page: joins the same room, one big Yes/No per phrase
- Relay: API Gateway WebSocket + Lambda + DynamoDB in ap-south-1 (Mumbai) for latency; room tokens expire after 2 hours
- Deterministic adaptation: baseline calibration, target band from the last 5 sessions, rest from measured phonation time; unit tests on recorded number streams
- Polly MP3 prompts cached in S3 and played through react-native-w3cmedia; text prompts shown at the same time for the hard of hearing
- README with a verified-vs-simulated table, latency measurements, 'not a medical device' wording, and a friction log

**Deliberately excluded stretch:**

- Clinician dashboard or export to an SLP
- Articulation scoring with Amazon Transcribe
- Hindi or other non-English prompts (voice set is language-neutral, phrase cards are typed by the family in any script the TV font supports)
- Multiple household profiles
- Alexa or Alexa+ integration
- Swallowing exercises or any clinical claims
- Real Fire TV Stick 4K Select take (optional if time allows)

**APIs, events and actions** (status as assessed by the researcher; 'unverified' means not confirmed in reviewed docs, 'gated' means not available to entrants, 'simulated' means not real in the demo):

**Blockers:** WebSocket on React Native for Vega is documented (mirrored RN docs) but untested on the VVD; needs a day-1 spike, with HTTPS long-poll ready.; Phone microphone automatic gain control can flatten loudness differences; autoGainControl:false support varies by browser and is unverified here, so the one-day test must confirm monotonic, repeatable readings.; iOS Safari needs HTTPS and a user gesture for getUserMedia, and the mic stops if the screen locks; Wake Lock support on Safari is not verified in reviewed sources.; Medical-adjacent framing: the product must say 'not a medical device', avoid the LSVT and SPEAK OUT! trademarks in the UI, and make no outcome claims.; Rosetta missing on macOS 27 blocks the Vega tools until installed; Bedrock quota-0 reports on new accounts matter only if Nova is used.

**Reasons judges might reject it:** An FDA-cleared rival exists, so judges may ask why a hackathon app should touch Parkinson's at all; the answer must be 'practice support, not therapy'.; It can read as 'a decibel meter on a TV' if the baseline, adaptation and family loop are not shown clearly.; No real people with Parkinson's in the demo (the developer simulates), which weakens Impact and raises authenticity questions.; Phone microphones are uncalibrated; a technical judge may dismiss relative dB as unscientific.; Fire TV health and movement entries are visible (vita-heart, spot, kinetiq-v, everybody-moves), so it may be lumped with 'TV health coach' projects even though none is about voice.; Little generative AI; some judges equate 'AI-enhanced' with an LLM.; Two phones for the family check may look fiddly in a 3-minute video.

**Gate result:** overall **pass** (eligibility pass, feasibility pass; native overlap low, visible crowding low).

- Eligibility: Meets the Fire TV rule: a React Native for Vega app shown on the Vega Virtual Device, which the Official Rules (Oct 4 live copy, §4 Submission Requirements, Fire TV bullet) name as 'the Fire TV/Vega simulator'; Fire TV is exempt from the runtime-hook rule. The TV only renders, takes remote keys (TVEventHandler/FocusManager, verified in the RN-for-Vega overview) and plays Polly audio through react-native-w3cmedia; it needs no TV microphone (run-apps: 'The Vega Virtual Device doesn't use the microphone'; the phone browser is the mic, outside Amazon's platform) and does not touch the privileged AudioRecordStreamBuilder API. It depends on no gated tool, no Android TV emulator, no unsupported de…
- Feasibility: The 95 h solo estimate fits the roughly 104 h solo capacity (estimate: 6.5 focused h/day x 16 net build days, Oct 5-22 minus 2 reserve days; the Oct 3 gate used 117 h with one more day available), leaving about 9 h of buffer. The TV app is render-only and single-screen-heavy; the DSP is small and runs on the phone. Unverified pieces each have a rules-safe fallback: RN-for-Vega WebSocket is now documented from raw page text (platform re-check) but untested on the VVD (fallback: 250 ms HTTPS long-poll); phone autoGainControl behaviour (fallback: per-session recalibration); Polly SynthesizeSpeech was not read as an API page, but Polly service availability in us-east-1 and ap-south-1 is verifie…
- Conditions: (1) Day-2 spike passes: phone-to-VVD numbers arrive over WSS through API Gateway (or 250 ms long-poll) with p50 under 300 ms from India; otherwise ship the long-poll path and say so. (2) Phone microphone readings are monotonic and repeat within +/-2 dB on Android Chrome and iOS Safari at a fixed distance, or the design switches to per-session baseline calibration and drops cross-day comparisons. (3) No LSVT LOUD or SPEAK OUT! trademarks in the UI or video (Rules: no third-party trademarks); 'Not a medical device' footer on every screen; no outcome or therapy claims; English prompts only. (4) Rosetta installed (macOS 27 removed it), Node 20 via nvm, 20 GB disk free before the Vega SDK install. (5) Polly synthesized in us-east-1 or ap-south-1 (both verified Regions); Nova Lite phrase suggestions stay optional and are not demoed if the account's Bedrock quota is 0. (6) If a real Fire TV Stick 4K Select take is attempted: confirm the stick is on Vega OS 1.2 (SDK 0.24 apps require it) via Settings > My Fire TV > About before buying time for it; the VVD path is the deliverable.
- Native overlap: Reviewed sources show no Amazon-native voice or speech-practice feature on Fire TV, Alexa or Echo. The Fire TV Appstore has a large Health & Fitness category (534 apps per IDEATION_CONTEXT) but no voice-practice app was verified in reviewed sources. The incumbents are non-Amazon: LSVT Companion/Coach (vendor page read, undated), SPEAK OUT! sessions (PVP pages, metadata Aug-Sep 2026) and phone sound-meter apps (Facebook thread title only). Inference: judges may lump it with 'TV health coach' ent…
- Crowding: No visible voice, speech or Parkinson's project among the roughly 33-45 visible Fire TV entrant repos (saved Oct 3/Oct 4 result sets, keyword scan) and GitHub searches on 2026-10-04 (gh api): 'loudness "fire tv" created:>2026-08-31' = 0; 'parkinson voice created:>2026-08-31' = 49, all ML screening/diagnosis repos (e.g. premsuggu/neurotone, Baffxy/PD-Voice-Screening), none hackathon-labelled or TV. Adjacent clusters: TV fitness and movement coaching with the phone as camera (about 7: Couch-Coach…
- Day-one proof: On the VVD (Rosetta installed, Vega SDK 0.24, Node 20 via nvm): a Vega RN app receives 20 JSON messages per second from a phone page over WSS through API Gateway (ap-south-1 if available, else us-east-1) and the on-camera clap test shows p50 latency under 300 ms (or the 250 ms long-poll meets 4 Hz); the arc meter redraws at 20 Hz without visible stutter; KeplerA11ySettingsInterface.getUiScaleSetting() resolves and the meter scales with useScaledSize; react-native-w3cmedia plays a Polly MP3 prompt with currentTime advancing; OK and Back from TVEventHandler start and pause the set. On two phones (Android Chrome, iOS Safari, HTTPS) a fixed tone at three known relative levels, ten times each, gives monotonic readings within +/-2 dB with autoGainControl requested off.
- Redesign to pass: Not required. Recommended: make the single-phone path the default and the family 'heard it' second phone optional, so the 3-minute video is not fiddly; put the personal baseline, the deterministic target band and the 7-day chart on screen in the first 20 s so it does not read as 'a decibel meter on a TV'; state the TV premise honestly (10 feet, remote-only, shared screen) and keep the one-day falsification test (tablet vs TV preference) as a documented result.

**Round-2 judge ratings (1–5; base case: solo, India, VVD on Mac, no Bee hardware, no US Ring device). Weighted = 0.25·Tech + 0.20·Design + 0.20·Impact + 0.20·Originality + 0.15·Feasibility.**

| Judge lens | Tech | Design | Impact | Originality | Feasibility | Weighted | Confidence | One-line verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| devrel | 4 | 4 | 3 | 4 | 4 | 3.80 | medium | My top pick: a fully real, uncrowded Fire TV entry whose video can prove it works; keep health claims out. |
| engineer | 3 | 3 | 3 | 4 | 3 | 3.20 | medium | An original, daily-use idea with an honest platform design, but technically light (render-only TV, uncalibrated phone mic) and now known to lose AGC control on iPhones. |
| product | 4 | 4 | 4 | 4 | 3 | 3.85 | medium | Strongest new Fire TV concept: a daily, evidence-backed need, a design that only makes sense at ten feet with a remote, and no TV rival; must avoid reading as a decibel meter and must keep health claims out. |
| **Mean of three** | 3.67 | 3.67 | 3.33 | 4.00 | 3.33 | **3.62** | | Scenario weighted: solo+hw 3.62; duo 3.77; duo+hw 3.77 |

**Why each judge rated it that way (verbatim rationale, one per criterion):**

- *devrel* — Tech: Fully real on the VVD: Vega RN with TVEventHandler/FocusManager, KeplerA11y UI-scale hooks (JDR-10), react-native-w3cmedia for Polly prompts, AsyncStorage, phone-as-microphone correctly routing around the VVD's missing mic (firetv.md §3). Not 5: WebSocket on VVD is documented but untested (JDR-11; long-poll fallback), the TV side is render-only while the DSP lives on the phone, and relative-dB from an uncalibrated p… — Design: Remote-only for the person with tremor, 10-foot arc meter sized by system UI scale, personal baseline band, text alongside Polly, 'not a medical device' footer: a complete device-appropriate design that lands on 'voice + D-pad + visual in a single flow' (JDR-03). Not 5: the second 'heard it' phone is fiddly and the health framing needs discipline. — Impact: Daily, prescribed practice for a defined population (APDA: up to 90% of PD develop reduced loudness, JDR-19) with peer-reviewed support for app-based home practice (JMIR 2025 feasibility study in the concept's evidence). Capped: no PD users in the demo, no absolute dB, an FDA-cleared rival exists, India segment is an assumption. — Originality: No voice, speech or Parkinson's project in the saved Fire TV repo sets (my keyword scan, JDR-25) and no native Amazon voice-practice feature in reviewed sources; mechanism (self-calibrated band, deterministic adaptation, cross-room intelligibility loop) is not a latent-LLM idea. Not 5: a decibel meter is an old tool and TV fitness-coach adjacency (~7 repos). — Feasibility: 95 h vs ~104 h with ~9 h buffer; each unverified piece (WSS, AGC, Polly) has a rules-safe fallback that does not kill the concept; one person can record the take.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 4, feasibility 4 (A 4K Select take on OS 1.2 strengthens 'runs on real devices' (JDR-05); cap it at ~4 h and keep the VVD as the deliverable.); duo tech 4, feasibility 5 (60 h each; the family loop and 7-day chart ship comfortably.); duo+hw tech 4, feasibility 5 (As duo plus real-device footage.)
- *engineer* — Tech: Honest use of the platform (TVEventHandler, FocusManager, KeplerA11ySettings UI-scale hooks verified in ftv2-kepler-a11y-readme.md, w3cmedia for Polly prompts, AsyncStorage) and a correct reading of the VVD's limits (no microphone, so the phone is the mic). But the TV is render-only and the DSP is a Web Audio RMS meter plus YIN pitch at 20 Hz; the relay is a standard API Gateway WebSocket fan-out. A verified platfor… — Design: The core design is coherent and device-appropriate: a huge arc meter readable at 10 feet, remote-only control (OK go/next, Back pause), personal baseline and deterministic target band, 7-day chart, text prompts alongside Polly for the hard of hearing, UI scale from accessibility settings. Held at 3 because the two-phone family 'heard it' loop is fiddly for a 3-minute video (the gate recommends making it optional), t… — Impact: Daily need with a prescribed regimen: LSVT Global says practice continues 'at least once a day for 10-15 minutes' (vendor page, undated), APDA cites up to 90% of people with PD with reduced loudness (2021), and a JMIR 2025 single-arm study (n=25) shows people with PD will practise with app feedback at home. Capped at 3: no people with Parkinson's in the demo, an FDA-cleared rival exists (LSVT Companion), the TV-vers… — Originality: No voice, speech or Parkinson's project among the 27 saved Fire TV repos (gh2-firetv-hackathon.json) or in the gate's GitHub probes ('loudness "fire tv"' = 0). The TV-as-stage for soft speech, a personal-baseline relative-dB band with deterministic adaptation, and the across-the-room 'heard it' intelligibility log are mechanisms not verified in any reviewed rival. Discount: adjacency to the phone-as-camera TV coachi… — Feasibility: 95 h solo against about 104 h (estimates) leaves about 9 h. The TV app is render-only, the DSP runs on the phone, Bedrock is optional, and one person can record the demo. The iPhone in this scenario is the worse microphone phone because of the Safari AGC gap; use the Android phone as mic. Hours unchanged.); duo tech 3, feasibility 4 (At about 60 h each (estimate) the TV app and the phone/DSP/relay split cleanly and the two-browser calibration study can be done properly; the tech ceiling (render-only TV, simple DSP) does not move.); duo+hw tech 3, feasibility 4 (As duo_no_hw plus an optional real take.)
- *product* — Tech: Vega RN app on the VVD is load-bearing throughout practice: 20 Hz arc meter redraw, TVEventHandler OK/Back control, KeplerA11ySettings UI-scale and caption hooks (verified README, gate), react-native-w3cmedia Polly prompts, WebSocket or 250 ms long-poll relay via API Gateway in ap-south-1, DynamoDB summaries. The phone is only a microphone (VVD has no mic; run-apps doc). Family 'heard it' uses the shared-screen property. My live search (pj2-voice-loudness-apps.json, snippets only) found phone apps that already pair phrases with a volume meter (Speak Up For Parkinson's per Tactus Therapy; Loud and Clear on the App St… — Feasibility: 95 h solo against about 104 h capacity leaves about 9 h (gate; estimate), plus 14 h of first-time Vega toolchain (Rosetta on macOS 27, Node 20, 20 GB). Day-2 spike on WSS latency from India and the phone AGC monotonicity test gate the design; each has a rules-safe fallback (long-poll, per-session calibration). One person can record the demo.

Round-2 red-team summary: **survives_with_redesign** (0 kill verdicts of 3 lenses; a concept is killed at two or more). Revised means T 3.33 / D 3.50 / I 3.00 / O 3.33 / F 3.33 → revised weighted **3.30**.

#### FTV-5: Measure by Measure: the TV is the music stand that listens

**Round:** 2. **Track:** Fire TV (primary, Vega OS on the Vega Virtual Device, WebView-based rendering). Mini challenges: Open Source, AWS Builder.

**Pitch:** Anyone practising an instrument or singing at home reads music from paper or from a phone or tablet propped on a stand, and turns pages with a hand they need for playing; forScore users buy Bluetooth pedals or use AirPods head gestures to get around it. Measure by Measure is a Vega OS app that fills a verified ecosystem gap (Vega has 'virtually no presence' in utilities, AFTVnews 2025) by rendering open-licence MusicXML (OpenScore Lieder, CC0; Mutopia, public domain/CC) at 10-foot size in the Vega WebView with OpenSheetMusicDisplay, and by making the remote the practice controller: D-pad moves the cursor measure by measure, OK twice sets a loop, Up/Down sets the tempo, Play starts a metronome and sweeping cursor, Menu toggles half-page scrolling. In 'listen' mode a phone on the stand is the microphone: monophonic pitch detection runs on the phone and the TV follows the score, highlighting the measure actually being played, turning pages by itself, and marking 'stumble' measures where the detected notes or tempo departed from the score. Tomorrow's loops are proposed from each household member's own stumbles, deterministically. Only CC0/public-domain scores and the user's own MusicXML are used, so no copyrighted content appears in the demo.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Primary user | Beginner-to-intermediate learners of a monophonic line (violin, flute, recorder, clarinet, trumpet, voice, single-line keyboard melodies), roughly ages 8-60, practising 15-45 minutes most days at home, with a parent or partner on the couch. First segment (assumption): Indian households with children in Trinity/ABRSM-style lessons or school choirs who own a Fire TV stick; second: US households with the same habit. |
| Frequency | Near-daily practice sessions (assumption from the practice regimes of the learning apps reviewed; no independent frequency survey was read). |
| Severity | Medium. Hobby and education friction (page turns, unreadable small screens, no feedback between lessons), not health. Learners drop out when practice feels unrewarding (assumption; not quantified in reviewed sources). |
| Current workaround | Paper and pencil; an iPad on a stand with forScore plus a Bluetooth pedal, AirPods head gestures or Pro face gestures; Yousician or Simply Piano on a phone or computer with their own content; mirroring a phone app to the TV (a JustinGuitar forum reply: AirPlay to Apple TV or Roku works, 'but not to Chromecast or Google TV'); YouTube play-along videos on the TV. |
| Amazon integration and its indispensable role | A React Native for Vega app whose score view is an @amazon-devices/webview 4.0.2 (Chromium 144) page bundled at file:///pkg/assets, rendering MusicXML with OpenSheetMusicDisplay; remote keys arrive via TVEventHandler in the RN shell and are forwarded over the WebView Messaging Bridge; the metronome click uses the Web Audio API inside WebView (release notes list Web Audio support) with manifest audio services; KeplerA11ySettings UI scale sets staff size and cursor thickness; fetch pulls score bundles from S3/CloudFront; WebSocket receives note events from the phone; the on-screen keyboard service supports score search. The TV is the display at the music stand; the remote is the only control a player needs. |
| Removal test (honest) | Honest and moderate. With both hands on an instrument you cannot touch a screen, and a phone on a stand is unreadable from a piano bench or standing position; a tablet works but needs a pedal and is the player's personal device. The TV is the largest display already in the practice room, the remote needs one thumb, and the parent on the couch sees the same score and the stumble marks. Weakness: an iPad with forScore and a $30 pedal is an established answer; for a household that owns one, the TV adds 'shared and accessory-free', not a new capability. If the one-day test shows learners prefer tablet-plus-pedal, this is a utility, not a must-have. |
| Wow moment under 45 s | 0:00 TV shows a CC0 Schubert song melody line at 10-foot size with a 'OpenScore Lieder, CC0' credit. 0:04 OK on measure 5 and OK on measure 6: a loop bracket appears with the tempo '♩ = 60'. 0:08 Play: a metronome clicks and the cursor sweeps the loop. 0:15 The presenter sings (or plays a recorder) into a phone on the stand, visible in frame; the cursor jumps to the measure actually being sung and the page turns by itself. 0:28 A deliberate wrong bar: measure 6 turns amber, 'stumble'. 0:36 Menu: 'Tomorrow: loop bars 5-6 at ♩ = 52' beside a 7-day practice streak for 'Anya'. 0:42 Back to the shelf. One unedited take with an on-screen timer. |
| Original mechanism | (1) Remote-first notation: MusicXML rendered by OSMD inside the Vega WebView, with a measure-indexed cursor driven by D-pad messages over the bridge, A/B loop points, tempo control and half-page scrolling, plus a Web Audio metronome synchronized to the cursor sweep. (2) Phone-as-microphone score following: the phone computes monophonic f0 with YIN/autocorrelation and emits note-onset events (MIDI number, timestamp) over WebSocket; the TV aligns the event stream against the expected note sequence (extracted from OSMD's parsed model) within a ±2-measure window using an online edit-distance step, which moves the cursor and turns pages; everything is deterministic and runs on the TV. (3) Stumble detection and next-day loops: a measure is a stumble when alignment cost exceeds a threshold or local tempo drops more than 25%; the next session proposes loops from each household member's own stum… |
| Simulator or real-data route (data route) | Build script (Node, OSMD headless) converts each CC0/PD score to .mxl plus an expected-note JSON and uploads to S3 behind CloudFront. The TV app fetches the bundle and loads it into the WebView page via the bridge. The phone page computes pitch locally and sends note events over API Gateway WebSocket (ap-south-1) to a Lambda that forwards to the TV connection in the same room; DynamoDB keeps rooms, profiles, sessions and stumble lists; the TV also caches profiles in AsyncStorage. A user's own MusicXML is uploaded from the phone page to S3 via a presigned PUT and appears on the TV shelf. No audio is stored. |
| Authentication | No accounts. Room code and QR on the TV; signed 2-hour room token from Lambda; household UUID on the TV; presigned S3 PUT for uploads limited to 2 MB .xml/.mxl. |
| Preview constraints | WebView on the VVD works only on Apple-silicon Macs (this Mac qualifies); not on Intel Mac or Ubuntu. Cleartext HTTP is blocked, so all endpoints are HTTPS. File uploads, multiline inputs and window.open are unsupported in WebView, so uploads happen on the phone page. OSMD rendering speed on a 1 GB Vega stick is unverified; the VVD on an M4 Pro is a best case. An entrant repo (vega-webview-bridge) exists specifically to fix Menu/Back key relay in Vega WebView, which signals bridge friction. Show the VVD full-screen on a real TV over HDMI in the video. |
| Hardware | Apple-silicon Mac (M4 Pro, 24 GB) running the VVD; one phone as microphone; an instrument or the presenter's voice (a recorder or singing is enough for a monophonic demo); optional Fire TV Stick 4K Select (₹5,499) for a real-device render test. |
| SDK versions | Vega SDK 0.24 (OS 1.2), RN for Vega 0.83 with @amazon-devices/webview ~4.0.2 (RN 0.72 track uses WebView 3.5.7 and is being deprecated); @amazon-devices/kepler-a11y-settings-interface-turbo ~1.0.0; opensheetmusicdisplay latest npm (version not captured); Node 18-20 for Vega builds via nvm; Amazon Devices Builder Tools MCP 1.0.15. |
| Deployment prerequisites | Rosetta on macOS 27; Vega SDK and VVD; a Paid-plan AWS account; S3 + CloudFront with HTTPS; API Gateway WebSocket + Lambda + DynamoDB in ap-south-1; a licence table for every bundled score (CC0 or public domain only) committed to the repo; backend live until 20 Nov 2026. |
| Firecrawl: research role | Used this round to read forScore, Yousician, Crescendo, OSMD, OpenScore and Mutopia pages and a JustinGuitar thread. Next: read OSMD's licence and cursor API docs, Simply Piano and flowkey device pages, and check OSMD performance reports on low-end Chromium devices. |
| Firecrawl: runtime role | none |
| AWS Builder path | S3 and CloudFront serve score bundles and phone pages; API Gateway WebSocket, Lambda and DynamoDB relay note events and store practice history; a Lambda build step runs OSMD headless to produce expected-note JSON; optional Nova Lite writes a labelled 'practice note' from the deterministic stumble list (under $1). Development with Kiro Crew or Claude Code on Bedrock qualifies per forum 45321. Honest note: AWS is plumbing here, not the core idea; the Open Source mini challenge is the stronger pairing. |
| Open Source path | Publish 'vega-score-view' under MIT: a React Native for Vega component wrapping the WebView with OSMD, a typed bridge protocol for D-pad measure navigation, loop and tempo, a measure-cursor hook, the expected-note extractor script, and the deterministic score follower with jest tests on fixtures. This is a new Vega integration pattern (D-pad-driven interactive web content) that the rules' creative example favours, and it fills a gap: no @amazon-devices port or visible entrant covers notation on Fire TV (lower bound from reviewed GitHub searches). Also candidate: a PR adding a Vega build target or key-relay example to OSMD's demo, subject to maintainer interest. |
| Fallback | If WebView rendering is too slow or key relay is unreliable, render each system to PNG with OSMD headless at build time and show images in RN with a cursor overlay from OSMD's measure positions (OSMD's README confirms headless PNG output). If score following is unreliable, fall back to 'tap-follow' (the player taps the phone or presses OK at the start of each measure) and metronome-only loops. If WebSocket fails, long-poll at 250 ms. If Vega blocks, build Fire OS react-native-tvos with react-native-webview on a real stick. |
| Falsification test (≤1 day) | One day. (a) Load five CC0 scores into OSMD inside the Vega WebView on the VVD: time to first render under 3 s and staff legible at 1080p from 3 m; D-pad measure cursor and loop work through the bridge, including Menu and Back. (b) On a phone in a quiet room, sing or play a one-octave scale and a 16-bar melody ten times: at least 90% of notes detected within 150 ms of onset, and the follower stays within one measure in 8 of 10 runs. (c) Two learners practise one piece twice, once with the TV and remote, once with a tablet and pedal; if both prefer the tablet, the concept is a utility and should be dropped or re-scoped to the image-render stand. |
| Risk profile | Medium technical risk (WebView bridge, OSMD performance, pitch detection and alignment) with a credible image-render fallback. Low reputational risk (no health or children claims; open-licence content). Low demo risk (the developer can perform). Low direct collision (no music-practice project among about 45 visible Fire TV repos; lower bound). Medium scoring risk on Impact. |
| Distinct from round 1 because | WordGlow had the TV speak Polly audio while a child listened and replayed words; here the human performs and the TV listens and follows notation, with a WebView-rendered MusicXML view, a monophonic score follower and an Open Source component as the core, none of which appear in round 1. Story Night (family recordings), BigPrint (document OCR) and the Alexa+, Bee and Ring concepts share no user group, workflow or mechanism. It also fills direction (a), a Vega utility that uses the remote as a practice controller, which no round-1 concept did. |
| Estimate (unmeasured) | Solo 115 h; two-person 70 h per person; cloud $5-15 through 20 Nov 2026 (estimate). Assumptions: All figures are estimates. hours_duo is per person when split (RN shell plus WebView page vs phone DSP, follower and backend) including about 15% coordination. Solo budget: 14 h Vega toolchain and VVD; 12 h WebView bridge and key relay (risk-weighted); 20 h OSMD page, cursor, loop, metronome; 12 h score curation and bundle script; 15 h phone pitch detection and follower with tests; 10 h backend; 6 h accessibility and polish; 10 h video; 8 h submission, friction log and feedback; 8 h buffer. Ass… |

**Demand evidence** (quality labelled by the researcher):

- forScore documents a market of page-turning accessories (foot pedals, handheld remotes, MIDI controllers, keyboards) and turns pages by head movement with AirPods Pro/Max (forScore 12, iOS 14) or Apple Pencil double-tap: hands-free page turning is a paid-for need. — [https://forscore.co/page-turners/](https://forscore.co/page-turners/) — *Vendor help page, undated; verifies the need and the accessory workaround, not volume. Medium.*
- A 2024 practitioner article lists five ways to turn pages in forScore (tap, half-page turns, AirPods Pro, Pro face gestures, Bluetooth pedal), with the author favouring a pedal. — [https://lifefromtheviolasection.com/forscore-page-turns-2024/](https://lifefromtheviolasection.com/forscore-page-turns-2024/) — *Third-party blog with affiliate links, published 2024-06-02; anecdotal but shows the friction is lived. Low-medium.*
- A JustinGuitar community member asked 'if the apps play on your smart tv or computer' (Nov 2023, 1.3k views, 7 users); replies describe mirroring/AirPlay workarounds to Apple TV and Roku but not Chromecast/Google TV. — [https://community.justinguitar.com/t/can-apps-be-played-on-a-smart-tv/263155](https://community.justinguitar.com/t/can-apps-be-played-on-a-smart-tv/263155) — *Community forum thread (2023-11-25 to Oct 2024); anecdotal demand for TV-based practice. Low.*
- Yousician markets 'Our award-winning technology listens to you play and gives instant feedback on your accuracy and timing' and 'No extra accessories like cables or mics needed'; its supported devices are iOS, Android, Mac and PC only. — [https://support.yousician.com/hc/en-us/articles/201590502-How-to-access-Yousician-on-different-devices](https://support.yousician.com/hc/en-us/articles/201590502-How-to-access-Yousician-on-different-devices) — *Vendor marketing and vendor help page, undated; verifies listening feedback as a mainstream expectation and the absence of a TV client. Medium.*
- Vega OS launch catalogue: games and utilities had 'virtually no presence'; nearly half the catalogue was Movies & TV. — [https://www.aftvnews.com/amazon-vega-os-app-report-11-year-old-fire-tvs-support-more-top-apps-than-the-new-fire-tv-stick-4k-select/](https://www.aftvnews.com/amazon-vega-os-app-report-11-year-old-fire-tvs-support-more-top-apps-than-the-new-fire-tv-stick-4k-select/) — *Independent enthusiast analysis, 2025-10-17, one year old (digest trends_home.md T6; not re-read this session). Medium for the supply gap.*

**Rivals named at generation** (strongest feature; our difference; evidence status):

| Rival | Strongest feature | Our difference | Evidence status |
|---|---|---|---|
| [forScore (iPad/iPhone sheet-music reader)](https://forscore.co/page-turners/) | Mature reader with half-page turns and hands-free page turning via Bluetooth pedals, MIDI, AirPods Pro head movement, Apple Pencil double-tap and (Pro) face gestures. | TV-sized score with a remote already in every Fire TV home, no accessories, and listening score-follow with stumble marks. forScore's listening or score-following features are not verified in reviewed sources; a TV version is not verified in reviewed sources. | Vendor page read 2026-10-04 (undated) plus a 2024-06-02 third-party article. |
| [Yousician](https://yousician.com/) | Machine-learning audio recognition that 'listens to you play and gives instant feedback on your accuracy and timing' with no accessories, lesson paths by teachers, and a family plan with four accounts. | Runs on the TV with the remote and open MusicXML scores (including the learner's own files), rather than Yousician's licensed catalogue on iOS/Android/Mac/PC. Our recognition is monophonic and far simpler; polyphonic instruments are out of scope. | Homepage and two support pages read 2026-10-04 (undated). |
| [Crescendo Piano (phone app)](https://crescendopiano.app/support/where-to-find-sheet-music) | Practice mode with note checking from MusicXML files; its support page points users to MuseScore.com and OpenScore for MusicXML. | TV display and remote control; shared household view. Crescendo's device list and listening method are not verified in reviewed sources (only its sheet-music support page was read). | Vendor support page read 2026-10-04 (undated). |
| [Simply Piano / flowkey / MuseScore app](https://support.yousician.com/hc/en-us/articles/204738362-Get-started-with-Yousician-guitar) | Not read this session; listed only as known phone/tablet practice apps. | TV-native. Their TV availability and features are not verified in reviewed sources. | Not verified in reviewed sources. |

**Smallest credible MVP:**

- Vega RN 0.83 app (0.72 fallback) with four screens: household shelf (profiles, streaks), score view (WebView), loop/tempo overlay, session summary with stumble list and 'tomorrow' loops
- WebView page bundled at file:///pkg/assets: OpenSheetMusicDisplay rendering of compressed MusicXML (.mxl), measure cursor, A/B loop, half-page scroll, Web Audio metronome; D-pad/OK/Play/Menu arrive as bridge messages from TVEventHandler
- Score bundles: 12-20 CC0 OpenScore Lieder melody lines and public-domain Mutopia pieces converted to MusicXML, each with an expected-note JSON produced by a build script, stored in S3 behind CloudFront with licence credits shown on the TV
- Phone microphone page (HTTPS): YIN pitch detection, onset events over API Gateway WebSocket (ap-south-1), Wake Lock, nothing stored; an 'upload my MusicXML' form on the phone page (WebView cannot upload files)
- Deterministic score follower (±2-measure window), stumble detection and next-day loop proposal, with jest tests on recorded note-event fixtures
- Accessibility: UI scale from KeplerA11ySettings applied to staff size; VoiceView labels on RN controls; visible focus everywhere
- README with verified-vs-simulated table, render and follow accuracy numbers from the day-1 test, licence table for every score, friction log

**Deliberately excluded stretch:**

- Polyphonic (chordal) following for piano or guitar
- Audio playback of the score (OSMD's player is sponsor-only; a synthesized demo track is a stretch)
- Annotations and fingering marks
- Jianpu/sargam notation for Indian classical learners
- Remote teacher view or sharing
- Alexa or Alexa+ integration
- Real Fire TV Stick 4K Select performance test (optional if time allows)

**APIs, events and actions** (status as assessed by the researcher; 'unverified' means not confirmed in reviewed docs, 'gated' means not available to entrants, 'simulated' means not real in the demo):

| Status | API / event / action | Documentation |
|---|---|---|
| verified | @amazon-devices/webview 4.0.2 WebView component with Messaging Bridge, hasTVPreferredFocus, file:///pkg/assets source, manifest audio services com.amazon.audio.stream and com.amazon.audio.control | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/vega/0.24/overview-of-webview |
| verified | WebView 4.0.x supports the Web Audio API and JavaScript alerts; default cleartext-HTTP block (Vega 0.24 release notes) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/vega/0.24/vega-release-notes |
| unverified | Vega WebView component reference (props and bridge API details) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/vega-api/latest/webview-component-reference.html |
| verified | TVEventHandler / useTVEventHandler and FocusManager / TVFocusGuideView (RN for Vega) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/vega/0.24/vega-rn-overview |
| verified | @amazon-devices/kepler-a11y-settings-interface-turbo: getUiScaleSetting, useUiScaleSetting, isScreenReaderEnabled | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/vega-api/0.24/README.amazon-devices_kepler-a11y-settings-interface-turbo |
| verified | @amazon-devices/react-native-w3cmedia (fallback audio for the metronome outside WebView) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/vega/0.24/media-player |
| unverified | fetch (score bundles) and WebSocket client (phone note events), RN for Vega 0.83 networking; WebSocket runtime on VVD untested | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/react-native-vega/0.83/network |
| verified | On-screen keyboard service com.amazon.inputmethod.service for score search | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/vega/0.24/enable-keyboard.html |
| verified | Vega Virtual Device run-app and inputd-cli (scripted remote presses for the demo); WebView on VVD is supported on Apple Silicon only | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/vega/0.24/kvd-issues |
| verified | OpenSheetMusicDisplay (MusicXML to SVG/PNG in browser or headless Node; npm module; playback not included) | https://github.com/opensheetmusicdisplay/opensheetmusicdisplay |
