**Blockers:** OSMD rendering performance and legibility inside Vega WebView on the VVD and on a 1 GB stick are unverified; the day-1 test gates the whole concept.; Remote key relay into WebView: focus must be set with hasTVPreferredFocus and Menu/Back may need relay work (an entrant repo exists to fix exactly this).; Monophonic pitch detection in a living room (TV speaker, reverberation) may misfire; polyphonic instruments are out of scope and must be stated.; WebSocket on Vega untested at runtime; long-poll fallback needed.; Licensing discipline: only CC0 or public-domain scores; Mutopia files are LilyPond and need conversion; each piece's licence must be checked and credited.; Rosetta missing on macOS 27 blocks the Vega tools until installed.

**Reasons judges might reject it:** Can be read as 'a PDF reader on the TV' or a web page in a wrapper if the remote-driven loop and listening follow are not front and centre.; Potential Impact is modest (hobby learning) next to health or accessibility entries.; Yousician and Simply Piano already listen and teach on phones; judges may see the TV as a nice-to-have.; Score-following demos look staged; it must be one unedited take with a visible deliberate error.; CC0 repertoire is 19th-century art song and classical melodies; judges may find the content narrow.; Little generative AI, and WebView-heavy architecture may score lower on 'Tech' than a native RN app.; Possible 'remote-controlled game' reading if the stumble marks look like a scoring game.

**Gate result:** overall **conditional** (eligibility pass, feasibility conditional; native overlap low, visible crowding low).

- Eligibility: A Vega OS app on the VVD meets the Fire TV rule, and the rules accept any framework including web technologies, so a WebView-rendered score view is in scope. The VVD supports WebView on Apple-silicon Macs only (kvd-issues: 'VVD doesn't support Webview on Ubuntu or Intel-based Mac platforms... Use Apple Silicon Mac (M1, M2, M3, M4) or Fire TV 4K Select'); the user's M4 Pro qualifies. No TV microphone is used (phone), no gated tool, no camera, no Android TV emulator. Content licensing is clean if enforced: OpenScore Lieder is CC0-1.0 (GitHub-detected licence; README: 'released under Creative Commons Zero (CC0)... We kindly ask that you credit OpenScore Lieder'), Mutopia pieces are 'all in the…
- Feasibility: (1) The 115 h solo estimate exceeds the roughly 104 h solo capacity (estimate; same 6.5 h/day method as the Oct 3 gate, which judged FTV-2 at 125 h conditional). About 18 h of the budget is video and submission work that overlaps the two reserve days, and the concept carries an 8 h buffer, so the build portion is close to capacity, but by the same standard this needs a scope cut or a two-person split (70 h each). (2) The WebView bridge, the concept's largest technical risk, is now verified from live Amazon docs (Firecrawl, 2026-10-04): 'React Native for Vega -> Web: injectJavaScript; Web -> React Native for Vega: onMessage' via window.ReactNativeWebView.postMessage (string only); file:// UR…
- Conditions: (1) Fit solo scope to about 104 h or less: for example ship the remote-driven reader (OSMD view or image-render stand), A/B loop, tempo and metronome first, and make phone score-following a stretch or replace it with tap-follow; or split across two people (about 70 h each). (2) Day-1 WebView test passes on the VVD: five CC0 .mxl files each render in under 3 s via OSMD at file:///pkg/assets with allowFileAccess, staff legible at 1080p from 3 m, Arrow/Enter keydown events reach page JS, Back reaches it with allowSystemKeyEvents, and postMessage/injectJavaScript round-trips work; otherwise switch to the image-render fallback (OSMD headless PNG per system plus an RN cursor overlay). (3) Menu-key behaviour is handled in the RN shell via TVEventHandler (Menu is not in the WebView supported-key table) or remapped to a listed key. (4) Every bundled score is CC0 or public domain with its licence and source URL recorded in a committed licence table; Mutopia pieces under CC BY or CC BY-SA variants are excluded or credited exactly as their licence requires; the OSMD BSD-3-Clause notice is retained. (5) All backend endpoints are HTTPS (WebView 4.0.2 blocks cleartext without a manifest policy); phone pages handle uploads (WebView does not support file upload). (6) Pitch detection reaches at least 90% note detection within 150 ms on a sung or recorder scale in a quiet room and the follower stays within one measure in 8 of 10 runs, or listen-mode degrades to tap-follow and the video says so. (7) Optional real-device take only on a 4K Select confirmed on Vega OS 1.2; WebView testing on the Fire TV 4K Select is explicitly supported per kvd-issues.
- Native overlap: No Amazon-native sheet-music, notation or practice-stand feature on Fire TV or Vega was found in reviewed sources (absence, not proof). The rules list 'remote-controlled game' as an Obvious Fire TV idea, so the stumble marks and streaks must not read as a game. Non-Amazon incumbents are strong: forScore (hands-free page turns via pedals, AirPods head gestures), Yousician (listens and gives feedback; iOS/Android/Mac/PC only per its device page) and Crescendo Piano (MusicXML note checking); none …
- Crowding: No music-practice, notation or metronome project among the roughly 33-45 visible Fire TV repos (saved Oct 3/Oct 4 sets; keyword scan). GitHub searches on 2026-10-04: '"sheet music" tv created:>2026-08-31' = 0; 'musicxml created:>2026-08-31' = 102, none Fire TV or hackathon-labelled (e.g. buicongnguyen/Piano_tutor, Efllux/Concentavo, mark216tw android MusicXML player); 'metronome "fire tv" OR vega' returned unrelated desktop metronome repos. The TV-plus-phone-companion pattern itself is common (…
- Day-one proof: On the VVD: a Vega RN app with @amazon-devices/webview ~4.0.2 loads file:///pkg/assets/index.html (allowFileAccess true, hasTVPreferredFocus true), OSMD renders five CC0 OpenScore Lieder .mxl files with time-to-first-render logged (target under 3 s each) and a photo from 3 m confirms legibility; page JS logs keydown for ArrowLeft/Right/Up/Down and Enter, and GoBack (27) with allowSystemKeyEvents; a postMessage from the page reaches onMessage and injectJavaScript sets a loop bracket, round trip under 50 ms; a Web Audio click plays with the manifest audio services declared. Produce one headless-OSMD PNG of a score as the fallback artefact. On a phone (HTTPS), YIN detects at least 90% of notes of a sung C-major scale within 150 ms of onset.
- Redesign to pass: Not a gate fail. To make feasibility unconditional for a solo builder: build the image-render stand first (OSMD headless PNG at build time, measure positions exported to JSON, RN cursor overlay, remote-driven loop, tempo and Web Audio or w3cmedia metronome) at roughly 85-90 h, keep the OSMD-in-WebView view only if the day-1 render test is clean, and make listening score-follow the stretch goal with tap-follow as the demoed default. Alternatively work as a two-person team. Keep the Open Source p…

**Round-2 judge ratings (1–5; base case: solo, India, VVD on Mac, no Bee hardware, no US Ring device). Weighted = 0.25·Tech + 0.20·Design + 0.20·Impact + 0.20·Originality + 0.15·Feasibility.**

| Judge lens | Tech | Design | Impact | Originality | Feasibility | Weighted | Confidence | One-line verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| devrel | 3 | 3 | 2 | 3 | 2 | 2.65 | medium | Technically interesting Vega utility that only works as a duo; solo it collapses to a score viewer. |
| engineer | 4 | 4 | 2 | 3 | 2 | 3.10 | medium | The most technically ambitious Fire TV concept, with its WebView bridge now verified from Amazon docs, but over solo capacity and resting on an unverified OSMD-in-WebView render and a modest hobby impact. |
| product | 3 | 4 | 2 | 3 | 2 | 2.85 | medium | Clever Vega utility with a strong Open Source angle but modest hobby impact and over-capacity solo scope; a duo project at best. |
| **Mean of three** | 3.33 | 3.67 | 2.00 | 3.00 | 2.00 | **2.87** | | Scenario weighted: solo+hw 2.87; duo 3.28; duo+hw 3.28 |

**Why each judge rated it that way (verbatim rationale, one per criterion):**

- *devrel* — Tech: Broad Vega surface (WebView 4.0.2 bridge with injectJavaScript/onMessage, key table, allowFileAccess, cleartext policy all verified, JDR-09; KeplerA11y; w3cmedia) but the core is OpenSheetMusicDisplay in a WebView, whose VVD performance is unverified, and solo the listening follower (the impressive part) likely degrades to tap-follow, leaving a score reader in a wrapper. — Design: Remote-first loop/tempo/metronome is device-appropriate and the Menu-key gap is handled in the RN shell, but a tablet-plus-pedal is an established answer, stumble marks risk a 'remote-controlled game' reading (obvious list, JDR-03), and the shipped solo design is less complete than pitched. — Impact: Hobby friction; frequency is an assumption and demand evidence is forum anecdotes and accessory pages (JDR-20); Yousician already listens on phones. — Originality: No music or notation project in the saved Fire TV sets (JDR-25) and it fills the Vega utilities gap; the D-pad-driven interactive web content pattern is a good Open Source contribution; incumbents on other devices are strong (forScore, Yousician, Crescendo). — Feasibility: 115 h vs ~104 h; gate conditional; day-1 WebView render test gates the whole concept and score licensing costs curation hours.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 3, feasibility 2 (WebView on the 4K Select is supported (kvd-issues) but a 1 GB stick makes OSMD performance riskier.); duo tech 4, feasibility 4 (70 h each; listening score-follow can ship.); duo+hw tech 4, feasibility 4 (As duo.)
- *engineer* — Tech: The deepest Fire TV architecture in the set and the one whose riskiest piece was verified today from Amazon's own pages: the Vega WebView bridge is injectJavaScript (RN to web) and onMessage via window.ReactNativeWebView.postMessage with string-only payloads; GoBack (27) needs allowSystemKeyEvents while Enter (13), arrows (37-40), MediaPlayPause (179), FF (228) and RW (227) reach page JS without a prop; spatial navi… — Design: Remote-first notation at 10-foot size with D-pad measure cursor, A/B loop, tempo, metronome and half-page scroll is a coherent practice controller, and the phone-as-microphone follower with stumble marks adds a feedback loop forScore does not have (not verified in reviewed sources). UI scale from KeplerA11ySettings and VoiceView labels on RN controls are planned. Deductions: default WebView spatial navigation and th… — Impact: Hobby and education friction, not health. Demand evidence is a forScore accessory page, a 2024 affiliate blog, one JustinGuitar forum thread (1.3k views) and Yousician marketing; an iPad plus a $30 pedal is an established answer, and the concept's own removal test concedes the TV adds 'shared and accessory-free', not a new capability. — Originality: No music-practice, notation or metronome project among the 27 saved Fire TV repos or the gate's searches ('"sheet music" tv' = 0), and D-pad-driven interactive web content is a genuinely new Vega integration pattern that suits the Open Source example ('adding Vega support to a popular React Native library' is the rules' own phrasing). Offsets: the TV-plus-phone companion pattern is common (CommonScene, HomeBoard), Y… — Feasibility: 115 h solo exceeds about 104 h (estimates); the gate rated feasibility conditional and requires either the image-render-first scope cut (about 85-90 h) or a two-person split. Gating unknowns stack: OSMD performance in the WebView on the VVD and on a 1 GB stick, pitch detection and follower accuracy (tap-follow fallback), per-piece licence checks for Mutopia variants, and WebSocket on the VVD.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 4, feasibility 2 (WebView testing on the Fire TV 4K Select is explicitly supported per kvd-issues (firetv.md section 3), so a stick on OS 1.2 would answer the 1 GB render-performance question; the OS 1.1 listing makes that uncertain. Hou…); duo tech 4, feasibility 4 (At about 70 h each (estimate) the RN shell plus WebView page and the phone DSP plus follower plus backend split cleanly, and the image-render fallback can be built in parallel with the OSMD view.); duo+hw tech 4, feasibility 4 (As duo_no_hw plus a real-stick render test, which would settle the main technical unknown.)
- *product* — Tech: Vega WebView 4.0.2 bridge (injectJavaScript/onMessage), remote key table, Web Audio metronome and OSMD rendering are a genuinely new Vega integration pattern, verified in docs by the gate, and the phone pitch WebSocket adds a second channel. But OSMD performance and legibility in the Vega WebView are unverified, Menu key relay needs shell work, and the gate's own expectation for a solo build is the image-render fall… — Design: Remote as the practice controller (D-pad measure cursor, OK-OK loop, Up/Down tempo, Play metronome, half-page scroll), 10-foot staff size bound to UI scale, licence credit on screen, stumble marks and next-day loops per household member (concept mvp_scope). Complete and device-appropriate; risk of reading as a 'remote-controlled game' (Obvious example) if stumbles look like scoring. — Impact: Hobby friction, not health or safety: page-turning accessories show a paid-for need (forScore pedals, head gestures; ftv2-forscore.json) and Yousician proves listening feedback is mainstream on phone/tablet/computer (ftv2-yousician-listen.json), but tablet-plus-pedal is a mature answer and the concept concedes the TV adds 'shared and accessory-free' only (removal test). No frequency survey; monophonic beginners and … — Originality: No sheet-music, notation or practice-stand app on Fire TV or Vega was found (gate; my live search pj2-sheetmusic-tv.json returned only phone metronome apps, a weak negative). Yousician/Simply Piano listen on phones and forScore solves page turns with accessories, so the parts exist; the TV-plus-remote-plus-listening-follow combination and the vega-score-view OSS component are the new elements. Fills the Vega utiliti… — Feasibility: 115 h solo exceeds the about 104 h capacity; gate feasibility is conditional on cutting to the image-render stand with tap-follow or a duo split. Day-1 WebView render/legibility test gates the whole concept; pitch tracking in a living room and the +/-2-measure follower are unproven; per-piece licence checks cost hours.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 3, feasibility 2 (WebView testing on a 4K Select is explicitly supported (kvd-issues), but a 1 GB stick raises the OSMD performance risk; no tech gain, no hours gain.); duo tech 4, feasibility 3 (70 h per person makes the OSMD-in-WebView path and listening follow likely to ship, lifting platform depth; still gated by the day-1 render test.); duo+hw tech 4, feasibility 3 (Split build plus real-device render check.)

**Red-team verdicts:** not red-teamed (did not reach the top of either round's ranking); ranking rests on gate and judge scores alone.

---


### Track: Alexa+

#### ALX-4: ClearPlate: the household's verified safe-food ledger, caregiver handoff and timed reaction protocol for food-allergy families (working name)

**Round:** 2. **Track:** Alexa+ (primary). Mini challenges: Open Source (addon functional-requirements conformance harness + care-plan engine), AWS Builder (DynamoDB conditional writes, Lambda/AgentCore MCP host, scheduler timers).

**Pitch:** For households living with a severe food allergy, ClearPlate keeps the family's own verified safe/unsafe product ledger with provenance (who verified which product from which label, and when the verification expires), briefs any non-household caregiver through the kitchen Echo with a two-way acknowledged handoff (sitter confirms by voice where the epinephrine is and what the plan is), answers 'can Aarav have this?' only from that ledger (it never infers safety from an LLM or a label photo), pages a parent for anything unverified, and if a reaction starts it runs the family's allergist-signed emergency care plan as timed, logged steps with an exportable timeline for the ER. It also tracks epinephrine location and expiry.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Primary user | Households managing a diagnosed, potentially anaphylactic food allergy: in the demo, two parents of an 8-year-old with peanut and tree-nut allergy, plus the rotating non-household adults who feed him (grandparent, babysitter, playdate host). Secondary: adults with their own allergies who share a home. US-first (FARE evidence); India plausible as a second market (assumption, not verified). |
| Frequency | Daily (every snack and meal decision) for the household; weekly to monthly third-party caregiving episodes (sitters, grandparents, playdates, sleepovers). FARE reports more than 10 percent of surveyed parents avoid child care settings or playdates at friends' houses and more than one-quarter skip camp or sleepovers because of the allergy, which shows the handoff moment is the one families currently avoid rather than solve (FARE facts page, undated, retrieved 2026-10-04). |
| Severity | High. FARE: about 33 million Americans have a food allergy, 5.6 million children (1 in 13); 3.4 million food-allergy ER visits a year; more than 40 percent of allergic children have had a severe reaction; reports suggest most fatal reactions involve food eaten outside the home; delay in epinephrine beyond a few minutes is a fatality risk factor; more than one dose may be needed. Advisory 'may contain' labels are unregulated (peanut protein found in 7.3 percent of such products), so label inference is unsafe by design. All from the FARE facts page (undated; retrieved 2026-10-04). |
| Current workaround | A printed Food Allergy and Anaphylaxis Emergency Care Plan on the fridge; verbal briefings to sitters; texting label photos to a parent; 'safe snacks' lists in Notes or WhatsApp; parent-only apps such as Spokin (community-reviewed safe foods and places) and Fig (barcode and ingredient matching to the parent's own profile); generic reminders for EpiPen expiry. None of these gives the person actually feeding the child an app-less, in-kitchen answer with a record, or a timed protocol with a log (inference from the rival pages read). |
| Amazon integration and its indispensable role | Alexa+ on an Echo Show in the kitchen is the shared, app-less interface for whoever is feeding the child; Voice ID (simulated) separates household members from a time-boxed guest caregiver session; MCP Apps ui:// resources render the safe-list carousel, the handoff card and the live reaction timeline; elicitation inside the active tool call pages the parent for an unverified product; the parent answers on the Alexa app (simulated phone surface); Alexa notifications and follow-up recall ('what did the sitter give him today?') use the structured data pattern from Amazon's components doc. Everything Alexa-side is a labelled simulation built on the public add-on docs. |
| Removal test (honest) | Honest: the ledger, care-session and protocol engine are host-agnostic and the parent-facing part could be a phone app. What disappears without Alexa+ is the point-of-feeding moment: the grandparent or sitter has no app, has food in one hand and a child in the other, and asks the kitchen device; the same device logs who asked, what was served, and pages the parent. Without it the caregiver texts a label photo and the parent guesses. So the Echo as shared household terminal is indispensable for the third-party gate; Alexa+'s LLM is a convenience (natural phrasing), not the value. The MCP server could serve ChatGPT, but no sitter opens ChatGPT in your kitchen. Weakness acknowledged: judges may still read the ledger as a web app with a voice trigger. |
| Wow moment under 45 s | Two simulated surfaces side by side: kitchen Echo Show and Priya's phone (Alexa app). 0-10 s: sitter (guest voice) says 'Alexa, can Aarav have these Oreos?' Card: 'Oreo Original: VERIFIED SAFE for Aarav by Priya, 12 Sep, label photo attached. OK to serve. Logged.' 10-22 s: 'What about this granola bar?' Card: 'Not on Aarav's verified list. I won't guess. Asking Priya.' Priya's phone shows the question with Approve / Decline; she taps Decline with reason 'contains almonds'. Kitchen card updates: 'Priya says no: tree nuts. Safe alternatives on the shelf: Oreos, Goldfish.' 22-30 s: sitter repeats the Oreo question; the server returns the existing log entry (no duplicate) and the audit strip shows one write with its idempotency key. 30-45 s: sitter says 'His lips are swelling.' The reaction-plan card opens: Step 1 'Give epinephrine NOW. EpiPen is in the red bag by the door (expires Mar 2027… |
| Original mechanism | (1) Provenance-gated allow-list: each product record per person holds verdict (safe / unsafe / unknown), verified_by, verified_at, evidence pointer (label photo or typed ingredient line), and a verification expiry (default 180 days, because formulations change); answers are pure lookups, 'unknown' always escalates, and no model ever decides safety. (2) Time-boxed care session with two-key acknowledgment: a parent opens a session (caregiver name, window, allowed foods, epinephrine location, plan); the caregiver must acknowledge each critical item by voice; the session auto-expires and is the only context in which guest questions are answered. (3) Deterministic reaction-protocol engine encoding the family's allergist-signed plan as a timer state machine (symptom tiers, epinephrine now, 911, second-dose window), producing a timestamped timeline exportable as a card and text for the ER. (4)… |
| Simulator or real-data route (data route) | All data is user-entered or synthetic: per-person allergen profile, product verdicts with evidence pointers, care sessions, protocol timelines, epinephrine inventory. Stored in DynamoDB (single table, conditional writes) with S3 only for optional label photos (SSE, lifecycle delete). No third-party data feeds in the MVP; Open Food Facts product names are a stretch. Treated as personal health data: encrypted at rest, export and delete tools, retention setting, no model training. Demo households and labels are fictional and labelled. |
| Authentication | Local demo runs without auth bound to 127.0.0.1 with Origin validation; hosted endpoint uses a bearer token (or AgentCore CUSTOM_JWT with Cognito) and documents the MCP 2025-11-25 OAuth 2.1 resource-server path as the production design. Parents authenticate in the simulated Alexa app surface; the caregiver never authenticates: a parent-opened, time-boxed care session is the authority for guest questions, with the guest attribution simulated in place of Voice ID. Alexa account linking is gated and not claimed. |
| Preview constraints | Alexa+ Category SDK, MCP Toolkit, CLI and Web Simulator are partner-only; no deployment to real Alexa+ is possible, so the whole Alexa layer is a labelled simulation. Amazon docs say Alexa+ supports MCP 2025-11-25 but the lifecycle sample sends 2025-03-26, so the server accepts both. Exact hydrated payload shapes for native Card/Carousel, Alexa+ support for elicitation at runtime, and notification delivery are documented but not verifiable by entrants. MCP 2026-07-28 is current but not confirmed acceptable by organizers; target 2025-11-25 (dual-era SDK serves both). Hosted MCP endpoint must stay live through 20 Nov 12:00 PT if referenced. |
| Hardware | Apple-silicon Mac only. Optional: a phone to show the parent surface in the video. No Echo required (classic-skill path excluded from MVP). |
| SDK versions | MCP TypeScript SDK v2 2.3.0 (@modelcontextprotocol/server, client, node/express adapters; dual-era) or TS v1 1.32.0; Python mcp 2.3.0 (MCPServer, streamable-http) as alternative; @modelcontextprotocol/ext-apps 2.0.3 with examples/basic-host; @modelcontextprotocol/inspector 2.9.0; Node 20+; optional strands-agents 1.57.2 / @strands-agents/sdk 1.19.0 for the simulated host agent. Versions as of 2026-10-03 (github.md). |
| Deployment prerequisites | AWS account on the Paid plan (promotional credits and AgentCore are not available on the Free plan); request the $150 credit by 21 Oct 12:00 PT using the Devpost account email and naming the track; validate Bedrock quota on day 1 only if the optional Nova Lite path is used (two forum reports of zero quota or suspension with no escalation); Lambda+API Gateway or a small ARM64 container for the MCP endpoint, kept live to 20 Nov with a budget alarm; GitHub repo with detectable MIT licence or private repo shared with the six reviewers plus testing@devpost.com; YouTube/Vimeo video under 3 minutes with no third-party IP; product feedback and friction log sections completed. |
| Firecrawl: research role | Used this round to verify demand (FARE facts page) and the two named rivals (Spokin, Fig). Before build: scrape the FARE Emergency Care Plan page and the FDA FALCPA/FASTER allergen pages to encode the plan schema and the nine major allergens; optionally search for Spokin/Fig feature pages to confirm absence of caregiver flows. |
| Firecrawl: runtime role | none |
| AWS Builder path | DynamoDB conditional writes are the idempotency layer and the two-key acknowledgment guard (a sitter cannot acknowledge twice, a parent decision cannot be overwritten); Lambda or AgentCore Runtime hosts the MCP server (AgentCore stateful mode if elicitation is kept); EventBridge Scheduler or Step Functions Wait states drive protocol timers and care-session expiry; S3 with SSE for evidence photos; CloudWatch per-tool-call logs as Amazon's FR section 13 best practice; Nova Lite only for splitting compound utterances (optional, under $1); Claude Code on Bedrock or Kiro as the development tool also qualifies per forum 45321. Depth is moderate; the honest story is 'serverless state machine with audited writes', not a Bedrock showcase. |
| Open Source path | MIT repo. Two extractable contributions: (a) 'addon-fr-checks', the unofficial conformance harness salvaged from ALX-2 (connects to any Streamable HTTP MCP server, negotiates 2025-03-26/06-18/11-25, replays create/modify/duplicate/cancel-without-confirmation/invalid-input flows, asserts about 12 machine-checkable FR section 8 and 13 rules, emits its own PASS/WARN/FAIL JSON with the banner 'unofficial; not Amazon certification'), run against ClearPlate in CI; (b) 'care-plan-engine', a dependency-free package with the care-plan JSON schema, deterministic timer state machine and scenario tests. Both are reusable by other Alexa+ entrants, which is a concrete integration pattern rather than a dump of the app. |
| Fallback | Drop server-initiated elicitation in favour of a polled parent-decision queue; drop MCP Apps webviews to hydrated data-only cards; keep the provenance ledger, care-session acknowledgment and timed protocol with tests; if hosting fails, document the local-run path (FAQ says hosting is not required) and record the demo locally. |
| Falsification test (≤1 day) | One day. Technical half: MCP Inspector negotiates 2025-11-25 against the deployed endpoint and also accepts a 2025-03-26 initialize; a ui:// card renders in the ext-apps basic-host; a repeated add_verified_product with the same event ID does not create a second record. Human half: run a 10-item tabletop with 3 acquaintances acting as sitters using the simulator (6 verified products, 4 unverified): the test fails if any sitter is told a verdict for an unverified item, or if fewer than 8 of 10 verified answers are retrieved correctly; separately ask 3 parents of food-allergic children (personal network or a FARE community group) 'would you open a care session like this before leaving a sitter?' and kill the concept if fewer than 2 of 3 say yes or all three say they would rather text a photo. |
| Risk profile | Technical risk LOW-MEDIUM (deterministic ledger, timers, MCP Apps, simulated host; no CV, no real-time multi-device choreography). Domain and liability risk HIGH-ish (health, children). Crowding risk LOW (no food-allergy entrant among 363 visible hackathon repos as of 3 Oct; the live GitHub search on 4 Oct was inconclusive due to name-collision noise; lower bound only). Platform risk LOW (fully simulated Alexa layer; FAQ allows local-run). Impact-evidence quality HIGH (FARE statistics), effectiveness unproven. |
| Distinct from round 1 because | User group: food-allergy households and the non-household adults who feed them, not carpool parents (ALX-1), paratransit riders (ALX-2, RNG-1), insurance claimants (ALX-3), multi-generation story circles (FTV-1), early readers (FTV-2), low-vision elders (FTV-3), two-adult promise households (BEE-1), students (BEE-2), developers (BEE-3), salon pros (RNG-2) or filmed in-home workers (RNG-3). Workflow: a point-of-feeding safety gate asked by a third party, a time-boxed acknowledged handoff, and a timed emergency protocol, rather than booking, replanning, promise-keeping, claims reconciliation or… |
| Estimate (unmeasured) | Solo 96 h; two-person 64 h per person; cloud 3-15 (estimate). Assumptions: Estimates, not measurements. About 6 productive hours a day over 16 build days leaves ~96 h solo; duo assumes one person on MCP server/engine/AWS and one on the simulated host/MCP Apps/video with ~30 percent coordination overhead. Breakdown (solo): MCP server and tools 18 h, ledger/care-plan engine and tests 16 h, MCP Apps resources 12 h, simulated two-surface host 18 h, AWS deploy and hosting 8 h, harness 12 h (reuse of ALX-2 design), video/README/friction log 12 h. No LLM at runtime by defaul… |

**Demand evidence** (quality labelled by the researcher):

- Approximately 33 million people in the United States have at least one food allergy; 5.6 million children (nearly 8 percent, one in 13); about 40 percent of allergic children have multiple food allergies. — [https://www.foodallergy.org/resources/facts-and-statistics](https://www.foodallergy.org/resources/facts-and-statistics) — *Advocacy organisation summarising peer-reviewed studies (citations listed); page undated; retrieved 2026-10-04. High for prevalence.*
- Each year 3.4 million patients have a food-allergy-related ER visit (one every 10 seconds); more than 40 percent of children with food allergies have experienced a severe reaction such as anaphylaxis; emergency treatment for food anaphylaxis rose 377 percent 2007-2016. — [https://www.foodallergy.org/resources/facts-and-statistics](https://www.foodallergy.org/resources/facts-and-statistics) — *Advocacy organisation citing studies; undated; retrieved 2026-10-04. High-medium.*
- More than 10 percent of surveyed parents avoid child care settings or playdates at friends' houses, and more than one-quarter say their children skip camp or sleepovers because of food allergy; most fatal reactions are triggered by food consumed outside the home; delayed epinephrine is a fatality risk factor. — [https://www.foodallergy.org/resources/facts-and-statistics](https://www.foodallergy.org/resources/facts-and-statistics) — *Survey results reported by FARE with citations (sample sizes not on page); undated; retrieved 2026-10-04. Medium-high; directly supports the caregiver-handoff problem.*
- Advisory/precautionary labeling ('may contain') is unregulated and voluntary; random testing found allergen levels from undetectable to substantial; peanut protein detected in 7.3 percent of products with peanut advisory labels. — [https://www.foodallergy.org/resources/facts-and-statistics](https://www.foodallergy.org/resources/facts-and-statistics) — *FARE citing published studies; undated; retrieved 2026-10-04. High. Supports the design rule 'never infer safety from a label'.*
- Organizer guidance: a project that is a latent capability of modern LLMs or an existing Alexa+ feature 'would likely score poorly on Design' and Quality of the Idea; Alexa+ is consumer-focused, voice-first not voice-only. — [https://amazonappdev2026.devpost.com/forum_topics/45397-what-exactly-are-you-looking-for-in-the-alexa-track](https://amazonappdev2026.devpost.com/forum_topics/45397-what-exactly-are-you-looking-for-in-the-alexa-track) — *Official DevRel forum answer (Emerson Sklar), ~30 Sep 2026; saved copy retrieved 2026-10-03, re-read 2026-10-04. High. Used to shape the 'deterministic ledger, no inference' stance.*

**Rivals named at generation** (strongest feature; our difference; evidence status):

| Rival | Strongest feature | Our difference | Evidence status |
|---|---|---|---|
| [Spokin](https://www.spokin.com/) | Large community of allergy-managing users reviewing safe products, restaurants, bakeries and travel destinations (founder cites 1,914 personal reviews; user testimonials on eating out and travel). | ClearPlate is the household's private, provenance-stamped list answered to a third-party caregiver at the moment of feeding, plus an acknowledged handoff and a timed reaction protocol. Caregiver briefing, acknowledgment or emergency timeline features: not verified in reviewed sources (homepage only). | Homepage read live 2026-10-04 (saved .firecrawl/oct4/alx2-rival-spokin.md); feature pages and app store listing not read. |
| [Fig: Food Scanner & Guide](https://foodisgood.com/) | Barcode scanning and ingredient matching against the user's own dietary profile ('your Fig'), with 9,514 matching products across 50+ stores shown on the homepage. | Fig answers the allergic person or parent on their own phone; ClearPlate answers whoever is feeding the child, with a logged question, parent escalation and an emergency protocol. Multi-caregiver sessions or acknowledgment flows: not verified in reviewed sources. | Homepage read live 2026-10-04 (saved .firecrawl/oct4/alx2-rival-fig.md); app not used. |
| [Alexa+ native memory and photo/document understanding](https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/devices/new-alexa-top-features) | Remembers personal facts and understands uploaded documents and photos; voice-created routines; per-person reminders (vendor feature guide, updated 24 Jul 2026). | Native memory is a single free-form household memory with no provenance, expiry, caregiver session, acknowledgment or timed protocol; and ClearPlate deliberately refuses label inference that a general model would attempt. Whether Alexa+ offers any allergy-specific feature: not verified in reviewed sources. | Vendor marketing page read in the Oct 3 digest (saved .firecrawl/competitor-alexa-search.json); describes offering, not measured behaviour. |
| [FARE Food Allergy and Anaphylaxis Emergency Care Plan (paper standard)](https://www.foodallergy.org/living-food-allergy/food-allergy-essentials/food-allergy-anaphylaxis-emergency-care-plan) | The clinician-signed one-page plan most US families already have; it is the authoritative source ClearPlate encodes rather than replaces. | ClearPlate turns the static plan into a timed, logged, voice-guided protocol for a caregiver who may be seeing a reaction for the first time, and exports the timeline. | Link seen on the FARE facts page (read 2026-10-04); the plan page itself was not read this round. Encoding must be checked against the actual document before build. |

**Smallest credible MVP:**

- MCP server (spec 2025-11-25 over Streamable HTTP, sessionful, also accepting 2025-03-26 and 2025-06-18 clients) with tools: get_safe_list, check_food (readOnlyHint), add_verified_product, request_parent_decision (elicitation inside the call, polling fallback), open_care_session, acknowledge_item, start_reaction_protocol, log_protocol_step, get_epinephrine_status, export_timeline; all writes idempotent via client event IDs and DynamoDB conditional writes; isError for invalid input; stable IDs chain session -> question -> decision
- MCP Apps ui:// resources: safe-list carousel (3-5 items, strongest first), care-session handoff card with acknowledgment chips, live reaction-timeline card; hydrated data-only fallback for every tool
- Labelled Alexa+-style simulated host built on the ext-apps basic-host: kitchen Echo Show surface, parent phone surface, guest-vs-household speaker selector standing in for Voice ID; deterministic utterance parser by default (forum 45058 allows this), optional Bedrock Nova Lite for compound utterances
- Deterministic care-plan engine (JSON schema modelled on the FARE plan structure, timers for second dose, 911 prompt) with unit tests on 6 scripted scenarios; epinephrine inventory with expiry
- Synthetic households and products only, clearly labelled; export and delete controls; encryption at rest for evidence photos (S3 SSE) if photos are included
- Open-source 'addon-fr-checks' harness (salvaged from ALX-2) run against this server in CI and shipped as the Open Source mini-challenge contribution
- Hosted endpoint on Lambda+API Gateway (stateless path) or a small container/AgentCore Runtime (stateful path for elicitation) kept live through 20 Nov, plus a documented local-run path; README with verified-vs-simulated table and friction log

**Deliberately excluded stretch:**

- Phone-camera barcode scanning and Open Food Facts product lookup (stretch; API not verified this round)
- Textract OCR of label text (would only populate the evidence field, never a verdict; stretch)
- Real Voice ID, real Alexa notifications, real Echo deployment (all gated or unavailable; simulated)
- Epinephrine reorder or purchasing flows
- School or camp integrations; multi-language; adult-only self-management mode
- Classic Alexa Skill on a physical Echo (optional only if a non-Alexa+ Echo on the same developer account is confirmed working; forum anecdote says dev skills break under Alexa+ Early Access)

**APIs, events and actions** (status as assessed by the researcher; 'unverified' means not confirmed in reviewed docs, 'gated' means not available to entrants, 'simulated' means not real in the demo):

| Status | API / event / action | Documentation |
|---|---|---|
| verified | MCP 2025-11-25 Streamable HTTP transport (single endpoint, Origin validation, MCP-Session-Id, MCP-Protocol-Version) | https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25/basic/transports |
| verified | MCP 2025-11-25 tools: inputSchema/outputSchema, structuredContent, isError | https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25/server/tools |
| verified | MCP ToolAnnotations (readOnlyHint, destructiveHint, idempotentHint, openWorldHint) | https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25/schema#toolannotations |
| verified | MCP elicitation (form mode; server-to-client during an active request) | https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25/client/elicitation |
| verified | MCP Apps extension (ui:// resources, _meta.ui.resourceUri, @modelcontextprotocol/ext-apps 2.0.3, basic-host example) | https://apps.extensions.modelcontextprotocol.io/api/ |

| verified | Alexa+ components and patterns: List, Carousel (3-5 inline, first item within 500 ms), Card, Map; notification structured data; follow-up recall | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/alexaplus/add-ons/mcp-addon-components-and-patterns.html |
| verified | Alexa+ Functional Requirements sections 1, 2, 8, 9, 13 (context continuity, no tool names in customer text, duplicate prevention, explicit confirmation, voice-only max 5 options, tools/list all invocable, stable IDs) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/alexaplus/add-ons/functional-requirements.html |

| verified | Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Runtime hosting MCP (0.0.0.0:8000/mcp, ARM64, stateful mode for elicitation) | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/runtime-mcp.html |
| verified | AWS Lambda pricing and free tier | https://aws.amazon.com/lambda/pricing/ |
| verified | Amazon DynamoDB on-demand pricing | https://aws.amazon.com/dynamodb/pricing/on-demand/ |
| unverified | DynamoDB conditional writes (idempotency and two-key acknowledgments) | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/WorkingWithItems.html#WorkingWithItems.ConditionalUpdate |
| unverified | Amazon EventBridge Scheduler (protocol timers, care-session expiry) | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/scheduler/latest/UserGuide/what-is-scheduler.html |

| verified | MCP Inspector 2.9.0 (tools/list verification, 2025-11-25 negotiation check) | https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/inspector |
| unverified | FARE Emergency Care Plan structure (source for the care-plan schema) | https://www.foodallergy.org/living-food-allergy/food-allergy-essentials/food-allergy-anaphylaxis-emergency-care-plan |

**Blockers:** Medical-liability framing: anything that reads as dosing or diagnostic advice invites judge discomfort; the design must only replay the family's clinician-signed plan and say so on every card; Native/latent overlap perception: Alexa+ already understands photos and remembers facts; the 'we refuse to infer' stance must be explicit in the first 20 seconds of the video or the idea reads as a wrapper; Sessionful Streamable HTTP with server-initiated elicitation fits Lambda+API Gateway poorly (SSE); either run a small always-on container (AgentCore is Paid-plan only) or replace elicitation with a parent-decision queue the simulator polls; No real families or clinicians can be involved in 20 days; all households, labels and plans are synthetic; Bedrock quota or suspension on a new account (forum 45416/45384) if the optional Nova Lite parser is used; the deterministic parser is the default

**Reasons judges might reject it:** 'Alexa+ can already read the label and remember his allergies' (latent LLM capability / existing feature) if the no-inference stance is not front and centre; Health and children's data sensitivity (perceived HIPAA/COPPA adjacency) and 'not a doctor' liability concerns; Demo relies entirely on synthetic households, a simulated Voice ID switch and a simulated phone surface, so 'looks functional' depends on production quality; Episodic third-party caregiving may read as low frequency even though daily household use is the base; A sceptical judge may say a sitter would simply text a photo, making the Echo a trigger rather than the product; Timed emergency guidance on a consumer device could be judged as overreach even when it only replays the family's own plan

**Gate result:** overall **pass** (eligibility pass, feasibility pass; native overlap medium, visible crowding medium).

- Eligibility: Uses the forum-accepted path: a self-hosted MCP server on spec 2025-11-25 over Streamable HTTP plus a labelled simulated Alexa+-style host (Rules §4 Alexa+ bullet; forum 45058 and 45403). The gated Alexa+ Toolkit, Web Simulator, Voice ID and Alexa notifications are correctly marked gated or simulated and not claimed. No partner OAuth or account linking is claimed. All households, products and plans are synthetic and labelled. Not a native Alexa+ feature in reviewed sources: Alexa+ remembers facts and understands photos, but a provenance-stamped allow-list with verification expiry, a time-boxed caregiver session with two-key acknowledgement, and a timed reaction-protocol engine were not foun…
- Feasibility: The 96 h solo estimate fits the roughly 104 h capacity (estimate), leaving about 8 h. All dependencies are public and current: MCP TS SDK v2 2.3.0 or v1 1.32.0, @modelcontextprotocol/ext-apps 2.0.3 with examples/basic-host, Inspector 2.9.0, DynamoDB conditional writes (standard, not re-verified), Lambda/API Gateway. No LLM is needed at runtime by default (forum 45058 accepts deterministic simulations), so the new-account Bedrock quota reports (45416/45384) only affect the optional Nova Lite parser. The one architectural tension is server-initiated elicitation inside an active tool call, which needs a stateful SSE-capable host; Lambda plus API Gateway fits that poorly and AgentCore Runtime n…
- Conditions: (1) The host is labelled a simulation built on public Alexa+ add-on docs; no claim of live Alexa+ deployment, Voice ID or real notifications. (2) The server negotiates 2025-11-25 and also accepts 2025-03-26 and 2025-06-18 initialize requests (Amazon's lifecycle sample sends 2025-03-26). (3) Every protocol card states that it replays the family's clinician-signed plan and is not medical advice; no dose computation or symptom diagnosis beyond the plan's own tiers; the FARE plan is encoded as a schema and cited, not copied. (4) Either run a small always-on container for elicitation or replace elicitation with a polled parent-decision tool call so the MCP server can live on Lambda; decide on day 1. (5) Hosted MCP endpoint stays live through 20 Nov 12:00 PT with a budget alarm, and a local-run path is documented. (6) Bedrock Nova Lite is used only if the account's quota is verified non-zero on day 1; the deterministic utterance parser is the default. (7) Synthetic households and labels only; S3 evidence photos (if any) encrypted with export and delete tools.
- Native overlap: Alexa+ natively remembers personal facts and understands uploaded documents and photos (aboutamazon top-features page, updated 24 Jul 2026), so 'can Aarav have this?' answered from a label photo is a latent capability, and forum 45397 warns such ideas score poorly on Design and Quality of the Idea. ClearPlate's wedge is the deliberate refusal to infer, the provenance ledger with expiry, the acknowledged care session and the timer-driven protocol with an exportable timeline; none was found nativ…
- Crowding: The Alexa+ track is the most crowded visible track (about 158+ of 363 hackathon-labelled repos on Oct 3; 188 '"alexa" mcp' repos on Oct 4; about 53 per prize). The theme itself is uncrowded: no food-allergy entrant in the saved result sets, and GitHub searches on 2026-10-04 returned 'allergy alexa created:>2026-08-31' = 0 and 'allergen mcp' = 1 unrelated restaurant knowledge server. Adjacent clusters: elder-care and caregiver check-ins (about 18), scam guardians (about 7), household handoffs wi…
- Day-one proof: The deployed endpoint negotiates 2025-11-25 in MCP Inspector 2.9.0 (tools/list passes) and also answers a 2025-03-26 initialize; check_food returns VERIFIED or UNKNOWN strictly from a DynamoDB lookup and a unit test proves UNKNOWN never yields a verdict; add_verified_product repeated with the same client event ID creates exactly one record (conditional write); a ui:// safe-list card renders in the ext-apps basic-host; start_reaction_protocol schedules a timer (EventBridge Scheduler or Step Functions Wait) that fires the 5-minute second-dose prompt in a test run; export_timeline returns the ordered, timestamped steps.
- Redesign to pass: Not required. Hygiene: open the video with the refusal-to-infer stance ('I won't guess; asking Priya') before any card; drop in-call elicitation from the MVP in favour of parent-decision tool calls (as ALX-5 does) so Lambda hosting is clean; keep the care-plan engine and the FR-checks harness as the two Open Source artefacts.

**Round-2 judge ratings (1–5; base case: solo, India, VVD on Mac, no Bee hardware, no US Ring device). Weighted = 0.25·Tech + 0.20·Design + 0.20·Impact + 0.20·Originality + 0.15·Feasibility.**

| Judge lens | Tech | Design | Impact | Originality | Feasibility | Weighted | Confidence | One-line verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| devrel | 4 | 3 | 4 | 4 | 3 | 3.65 | medium | Best Alexa+ concept: strong evidence and contract literacy, but it must open by refusing to guess or it reads as native memory. |
| engineer | 3 | 3 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 3.20 | medium | The best-evidenced impact in the set and a disciplined no-inference design, but the MCP core is a lookup and the elicitation-on-Lambda tension is unresolved in the MVP. |
| product | 4 | 4 | 5 | 4 | 3 | 4.05 | high | Top of my board: the most credible, severe and frequent customer need in the set, with a disciplined no-inference design; its risks are medical framing and the crowded Alexa+ track, not the idea. |
| **Mean of three** | 3.67 | 3.33 | 4.33 | 3.67 | 3.00 | **3.63** | | Scenario weighted: solo+hw 3.63; duo 3.87; duo+hw 3.87 |

**Why each judge rated it that way (verbatim rationale, one per criterion):**

- *devrel* — Tech: Exercises more of the documented Alexa+ client contract than any other new concept: MCP 2025-11-25 Streamable HTTP with 2025-03-26 acceptance, ToolAnnotations, elicitation, MCP Apps ui:// cards, notification structured data (mcp.md §2), DynamoDB conditional writes, EventBridge timers, and the FR conformance harness in CI. Not 5: the Alexa layer is necessarily simulated and the elicitation-on-Lambda tension must be r… — Design: Two-surface, voice-first-not-voice-only flow with a refusal-to-infer stance, acknowledged care session and timed protocol is thoughtful, but 'can Aarav have this?' from a label is a latent capability and Alexa+ natively remembers facts and reads photos (mcp.md §11), so forum 45397's Design penalty (JDR-04) applies unless the opening 20 s make the wedge explicit. — Impact: Primary advocacy statistics (FARE: 33M, 5.6M children, 3.4M ER visits, >40% severe reactions, parents avoiding playdates, 7.3% advisory-label contamination, JDR-16); daily household decisions plus high-stakes handoffs. Not 5: synthetic demo, no clinicians, effectiveness unproven. — Originality: No food-allergy entrant visible; provenance ledger with expiry, two-key acknowledgement and a deterministic protocol timer were not found natively or in Spokin/Fig homepages (read); medical-safety gate pattern has one adjacent entrant (Raksha). — Feasibility: 96 h vs ~104 h; three subsystems plus the harness means one will thin; elicitation hosting decision and medical-content review add risk.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 4, feasibility 3 (No hardware helps; an Echo is optional and risky under Alexa+ Early Access.); duo tech 4, feasibility 4 (64 h each; MCP Apps and elicitation both land.); duo+hw tech 4, feasibility 4 (As duo.)
- *engineer* — Tech: The MCP work is correct on paper: 2025-11-25 Streamable HTTP accepting 2025-03-26/06-18, ToolAnnotations (readOnlyHint on check_food), structuredContent, MCP Apps ui:// cards via ext-apps 2.0.3, DynamoDB conditional writes as the idempotency and two-key acknowledgement guard, and a deterministic timer state machine on EventBridge Scheduler or Step Functions. Two things keep it at 3. First, the MVP keeps server-initi… — Design: Two simulated surfaces (kitchen Echo Show, parent phone) with a guest-vs-household selector standing in for Voice ID, the refusal-to-infer card, the acknowledged handoff and a live reaction timeline are coherent and the medical framing is handled (plan replay only, not advice). Held at 3 because the obvious alternative, a sitter texting a label photo, is cheap and judges will ask it; the Voice ID and notifications a… — Impact: The only impact 4 I award. Primary-source severity and specificity: FARE reports about 33M Americans with food allergy, 5.6M children, 3.4M ER visits a year, over 40% of allergic children with a severe reaction, most fatal reactions from food eaten outside the home, delayed epinephrine as a fatality risk factor, and over 10% of parents avoiding playdates (saved .firecrawl/oct4/alx2-fare-facts.md, page undated, retri… — Originality: Uncrowded theme (gate: 'allergy alexa' = 0 on Oct 4) and a clear stance (provenance ledger with expiry that refuses inference, two-key care session, timed protocol). Offsets: native Alexa+ memory and photo understanding make the entry question a latent capability, Spokin and Fig cover the parent side, and the FARE care plan is the authoritative artefact being encoded. — Feasibility: 96 h solo against about 104 h (estimates) leaves about 8 h; all dependencies public; no LLM by default. The elicitation-vs-polled-queue decision and the two-surface host (18 h) are the schedule risks; the human half of the falsification test (three parents) is a recruiting sensitivity, not a blocker.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 3, feasibility 3 (Hardware is irrelevant to a simulated Alexa+ host. No change.); duo tech 4, feasibility 4 (At about 64 h each (estimate) one person can run the stateful container path for elicitation properly while the other builds the host and cards, resolving the Lambda tension.); duo+hw tech 4, feasibility 4 (Same as duo_no_hw.)
- *product* — Tech: The richest MCP implementation in the set: 2025-11-25 Streamable HTTP server accepting 2025-03-26/06-18, ten tools with readOnlyHint/destructiveHint annotations, structuredContent, idempotent writes via DynamoDB conditional writes, EventBridge/Step Functions protocol timers, MCP Apps ui:// carousel, handoff and live timeline cards with hydrated fallbacks, and the addon-fr-checks harness in CI (concept mvp_scope; gat… — Design: Voice-first point-of-feeding question on a kitchen Echo Show, cards that refuse to infer ('Not on Aarav's verified list. I won't guess. Asking Priya'), two-key acknowledged care session, timed reaction protocol with countdown and 911 chip mirrored to the parent's phone, 'not medical advice' on every card (concept wow_moment; gate conditions). Multimodal and appropriate for a hands-full caregiver. Not 5: three simula… — Impact: The only concept with research-grade severity, frequency and reach together: about 33 million Americans have a food allergy, 5.6 million children (1 in 13), 3.4 million ER visits a year, more than 40% of allergic children have had a severe reaction, most fatal reactions involve food eaten outside the home, delayed epinephrine is a fatality risk factor, and more than 10% of parents avoid child care or playdates while… — Originality: No food-allergy entrant among visible hackathon repos (gate crowding; GitHub searches 2026-10-04 returned 0 and 1 unrelated). Alexa+ natively remembers facts and understands label photos (rival matrix row 1), so 'can he have this?' is a latent capability and forum 45397 warns such ideas score poorly; ClearPlate's deliberate refusal to infer, provenance ledger with verification expiry, two-key care session and determ… — Feasibility: 96 h solo vs about 104 h capacity leaves about 8 h (gate; estimate). No LLM at runtime by default, all dependencies public; the elicitation-vs-Lambda architecture decision is due day 1; medical framing needs care on every card; the human half of the falsification test (three allergy parents) is a recruiting sensitivity, not a build blocker.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 4, feasibility 3 (Mac-only concept; the hardware set does not apply.); duo tech 4, feasibility 4 (64 h per person (server/engine vs host/MCP Apps/video) restores buffer; harness and care-plan engine both ship.); duo+hw tech 4, feasibility 4 (Same as duo without hardware.)

#### ALX-5: OutPass: the phone-free kid's permission, cross-household handshake and check-in ledger on the home Echo (working name)

**Round:** 2. **Track:** Alexa+ (primary). Mini challenges: AWS Builder (Step Functions or EventBridge Scheduler escalation ladder, DynamoDB conditional writes, Lambda MCP host), Open Source (addon functional-requirements conformance harness; pass-state-machine package).

**Pitch:** Families who delay smartphones (Wait Until 8th: more than 155,000 parents pledged; average first smartphone at age ten) still need a way for a 9-year-old to ask 'can I bike to Rohan's till six?' and for parents to know they got there and came back. OutPass turns the kitchen Echo into the child's only terminal: the child asks by voice, the parent approves on their phone with conditions (homework first, back by six or by sunset), the host family's parent confirms with a one-tap link or their own Echo, the child checks out and is checked in on arrival, and a deterministic escalation ladder pages parents if the return check-in is missed. No GPS, no device on the child, and a pass history that earns agreed privileges over time.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Primary user | Children roughly 8-13 without smartphones (the acting user), their parents (approvers), and the parents of the households they visit (confirmers). US-first because the smartphone-delay movement and Wait Until 8th are US; gated-community households in India are a plausible second segment (assumption, not verified). |
| Frequency | Daily after school and most weekend days for families with children in this age band; each outing is a request, a confirmation and two check-ins. Frequency is inferred from the use case; no reviewed source measures it. |
| Severity | Moderate to high: the trade-off families face is autonomy versus safety, and the current answers are either give the child a smartphone (which Wait Until 8th parents explicitly refuse: 'Ten years old is the average age children get their first smartphone. You can change this!'), a GPS tracker, or no information at all until the child is late. Severity evidence is movement-level (pledge counts and stated motivations), not incident statistics; labelled as such. |
| Current workaround | Landline or a parent's old phone at home; basic phones and kid smartwatches (Wait Until 8th points pledgers to 'basic phones and smartwatches' that call and text only; specific devices not verified this round); Life360-style location sharing, which requires the child to carry a phone or Tile; Apple Check In, which requires iOS 17 on both ends; texting the other parent; 'be home when the streetlights come on'. |
| Amazon integration and its indispensable role | The home Echo (Show) is the child's ambient terminal: Voice ID (simulated) attributes the request to the child's profile, Alexa+ turns child speech into a structured pass request, MCP Apps ui:// cards show the pass with countdown, conditions and host-confirmation status on the kitchen screen and on the parents' Alexa app surface; a second household's Echo (optional) answers 'Is Aarav still there?' during escalation; Alexa notifications carry structured pass data (per Amazon's components doc) so follow-ups like 'when is Aarav due back?' work. Everything Alexa-side is a labelled simulation on the public add-on docs; host families without Alexa confirm via a signed SMS or email link. |
| Removal test (honest) | Honest in both directions. Without an ambient household voice device there is no product: a phone-free child has no other interface, so the Echo is not a trigger but the only possible terminal, which is the strongest indispensability of the two concepts. However, what is indispensable is the Echo plus speaker attribution, not Alexa+'s LLM: a classic skill with fixed phrases could run the same state machine, and Alexa+ adds natural child phrasing and multimodal cards. The MCP server is host-agnostic in principle, yet no other consumer host reaches a child without a phone. The cross-household confirmation deliberately works without Alexa (signed link), so Alexa is indispensable only on the requesting side. |
| Wow moment under 45 s | Three simulated surfaces: kitchen Echo Show, Mom's phone (Alexa app), and the Mehta family's phone. 0-8 s: Aarav (child voice) says 'Alexa, can I bike to Rohan's? I'll be back by six.' Card: 'Out-pass request: Aarav to Rohan's (Mehta family), back by 6:00. Homework: 2 of 3 done.' 8-16 s: Mom's phone shows the request; she taps 'Approve with conditions: finish math first, home by 6:00.' 16-22 s: the Mehtas' phone receives 'Aarav is coming at about 4:15, OK?' and Rohan's mum taps 'Yes, we're home'; the kitchen card flips to 'APPROVED, host confirmed, leave after math.' 22-28 s: Aarav says 'Alexa, I'm leaving.' Card: 'Pass started 4:12. Back by 6:00.' The audit strip shows each transition with its event ID; a repeated 'I'm leaving' returns the same record. 28-40 s: the demo clock jumps to 6:12 with no check-in: 6:10 reminder card on Mom's phone, 6:15 'Call the Mehtas' one-tap plus a card o… |
| Original mechanism | (1) Pass state machine: requested -> parent_decision (approve / approve_with_conditions / decline) -> host_confirmation (optional per destination) -> active (voice check-out) -> arrived (host check-in) -> overdue -> closed or closed_late; every transition is an idempotent write keyed by a client event ID with DynamoDB conditional writes, so repeated utterances and retried links never double-apply. (2) Deterministic standing-rule engine: parent-entered checklist gates (homework first), curfew = earlier of a fixed time and sunset plus a buffer computed from the home's lat/long by the NOAA solar formula, allowed destinations limited to consented households, weekday/weekend variants; no model judgement anywhere in the decision. (3) Escalation ladder as Step Functions Wait states or EventBridge Scheduler: T+10 reminder to the approving parent, T+15 host query plus one-tap call card, T+25 bot… |
| Simulator or real-data route (data route) | Synthetic families only. Child profile is minimal (first name, age band, no photo, no location); parents' contact and the home's lat/long for sunset; consented host households (name, contact, confirmation preference); passes and transitions; rules; history. Single DynamoDB table with conditional writes; Step Functions execution per active pass; no third-party data feeds. Deletion and export tools; retention setting; privacy statement in README. No location data is collected or inferred anywhere. |
| Authentication | Parents authenticate in the simulated Alexa app surface (simple login); host families act through HMAC-signed one-time links with expiry (no account); the child never authenticates: the household device plus simulated Voice ID attribution is the authority, with parent approval required for every pass. MCP endpoint: localhost without auth for development, bearer token on the hosted endpoint, MCP 2025-11-25 OAuth 2.1 resource-server design documented for production. Alexa account linking is gated and not claimed. |
| Preview constraints | Alexa+ partner tools are unavailable, so the Alexa layer is a labelled simulation. Children's experiences on real Alexa require Amazon Kids and kids-skill certification with parental consent (COPPA), which cannot be demonstrated; the README must say the real deployment path would need that review. Amazon's docs show Alexa+ supporting MCP 2025-11-25 while the lifecycle sample sends 2025-03-26, so both are accepted. Hydrated native card payload shapes and notification delivery are documented but unverifiable. MCP 2026-07-28 not confirmed acceptable; target 2025-11-25. |
| Hardware | Apple-silicon Mac only. Optional: one or two phones to show parent and host surfaces in the video. No Echo required; a second Echo for the host household is optional set dressing, not a dependency. |
| SDK versions | MCP TypeScript SDK v2 2.3.0 (dual-era) or v1 1.32.0; Python mcp 2.3.0 alternative; @modelcontextprotocol/ext-apps 2.0.3 with examples/basic-host; @modelcontextprotocol/inspector 2.9.0; Node 20+; AWS CDK or SAM for Step Functions/DynamoDB/Lambda (version not checked); optional strands-agents 1.57.2 for the simulated host agent. Versions as of 2026-10-03 (github.md). |
| Deployment prerequisites | AWS Paid-plan account (credits not usable on Free plan); credit request by 21 Oct 12:00 PT with Devpost email and track named; Step Functions and DynamoDB in us-east-1 or us-west-2; Lambda+API Gateway MCP endpoint live through 20 Nov with a budget alarm; Bedrock quota check only if Nova Lite is used; GitHub repo with detectable MIT licence or private repo shared with the six reviewers plus testing@devpost.com; video under 3 minutes using actors' voices with consent (no third-party IP); product feedback, friction log and an explicit privacy/no-location statement. |
| Firecrawl: research role | Used this round to verify Wait Until 8th, Life360 and Apple Check In. Before build: scrape the Wait Until 8th devices page and one kid-smartwatch vendor page (Gabb or Apple Watch Family Setup) to complete the substitute table; scrape the Amazon Kids parent-dashboard help page to confirm or deny native permission/check-in features; optionally one Pew Research page on children's phone ownership for a research-grade demand figure. |
| Firecrawl: runtime role | none |
| AWS Builder path | Step Functions Wait states are the escalation ladder itself (reminder, host query, both parents) with a task-token cancellation on check-in, which gives a visible, traceable AWS mechanism in the demo; DynamoDB conditional writes make pass transitions idempotent; Lambda hosts the MCP server and the signed-link handlers; CloudWatch per-tool-call logs; Nova Lite optional for parsing child utterances with slang (default deterministic). Claude Code on Bedrock or Kiro as the dev tool also qualifies (forum 45321). Depth is moderate and honest: an audited serverless workflow, not a model showcase. |
| Open Source path | MIT repo. Contributions: (a) the shared 'addon-fr-checks' unofficial conformance harness (Streamable HTTP, multi-version negotiation, FR section 8 and 13 checks, PASS/WARN/FAIL JSON, 'not Amazon certification' banner) run against OutPass in CI; (b) 'pass-ladder', a small package with the pass state machine, idempotent transition helper and a Step Functions definition template, plus tests. If only one is affordable, ship the harness because it serves every Alexa+ entrant. |
| Fallback | Single-household mode: child request, parent approval with conditions, voice check-out/check-in and the escalation ladder, with host confirmation shown as an optional future step; hydrated data-only cards instead of MCP Apps; local-run demo if hosting fails. Keep the rule engine and the Step Functions ladder because they are the demonstrable mechanism. |
| Falsification test (≤1 day) | One day. Technical half: a Step Functions execution started by check_out times out on schedule and is cancelled by check_in; a duplicate check_in with the same event ID is a no-op; MCP Inspector negotiates 2025-11-25 and a ui:// pass card renders in basic-host. Human half: tabletop with 3 families from the personal network who have an 8-13-year-old without a smartphone (or who are delaying one): the child makes 3 requests through the simulator and a parent approves on a phone; ask each parent 'would you use this instead of giving a watch or phone with GPS?' and 'are the 10/15/25-minute escalation steps right for you?'. Kill the concept if 2 of 3 parents prefer a GPS device, or if none of the 3 children can complete a request by voice without coaching. |
| Risk profile | Technical risk LOW-MEDIUM (state machine, timers, signed links, MCP Apps; no CV, no real messaging). Privacy and child-safety sensitivity MEDIUM-HIGH. Crowding risk LOW (no kids-permission, curfew or phone-free entrant among 363 visible hackathon repos as of 3 Oct; lower bound; the acceptance pattern is shared with several adult replanning repos). Platform risk LOW (fully simulated). Impact-evidence quality MEDIUM (pledge counts and vendor pages, no incident statistics). Adoption risk MEDIUM-HIGH (two households). |
| Distinct from round 1 because | User group: the acting user is a phone-free child, with two households' parents as approver and confirmer; no round-1 concept had a child as the acting user (FTV-2's readers are passive viewers), and the counterparties are peer families, not agencies (ALX-2, RNG-1), insurers (ALX-3), carpool drivers (ALX-1), partners (BEE-1), tutors (BEE-2) or in-home workers (RNG-3). Workflow: permission request -> conditional approval -> cross-household confirmation -> voice check-out/check-in -> deadline escalation, rather than booking, replanning, promise-keeping, claims, curb witnessing or camera agreeme… |
| Estimate (unmeasured) | Solo 92 h; two-person 62 h per person; cloud 3-12 (estimate). Assumptions: Estimates, not measurements. ~6 productive hours a day over 16 build days. Breakdown (solo): MCP server and tools 16 h, rule engine and sunset math with tests 10 h, Step Functions ladder and DynamoDB 12 h, MCP Apps resources 10 h, three-surface simulated host 20 h, hosting and deploy 6 h, harness 10 h (reused design), video/README/privacy statement 8 h. Duo splits backend/AWS from host/UI/video with ~30 percent coordination overhead. No LLM at runtime by default; Step Functions state transition… |

**Demand evidence** (quality labelled by the researcher):

- More than 155,000 parents have signed the Wait Until 8th pledge to delay giving children a smartphone until at least the end of 8th grade; ten years old is the average age children get their first smartphone; the pledge activates once 10 families from a grade sign. — [https://www.waituntil8th.org/](https://www.waituntil8th.org/) — *Advocacy organisation's own counter and claims; page undated; retrieved 2026-10-04. Medium: shows a sizeable, organised segment of parents but no incident or usage data.*
- Wait Until 8th tells pledging families who need contact before 8th grade to use basic phones or smartwatches that 'call and text only', confirming the gap OutPass targets (coordination without a smartphone). — [https://www.waituntil8th.org/](https://www.waituntil8th.org/) — *Advocacy page, undated; retrieved 2026-10-04. Medium; the devices page itself was not read.*
- Life360's consumer offering is device location tracking (real-time location, Place Alerts, location history, Tile item finding), with a free tier of 2 Place Alerts and 2 days of history and paid tiers from $9.99/month; it therefore presupposes a tracked phone or Tile. — [https://www.life360.com/](https://www.life360.com/) — *Vendor homepage read live 2026-10-04; pricing as displayed; the inference that a phone-free child cannot be tracked is mine.*
- Apple Check In notifies a friend that your iPhone has arrived or that a timer ended, and shares location, battery and route if the check-in is not completed; it requires iOS 17 or later for both sender and recipient. — [https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/use-check-in-iphc143bb7e9/ios](https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/use-check-in-iphc143bb7e9/ios) — *Official vendor documentation (iOS 27 guide version shown); retrieved 2026-10-04. High for capability; it is iPhone-to-iPhone only.*
- Organizer guidance that voice-first, multimodal, consumer experiences bringing new functionality are the strong fit, and that latent LLM capabilities or existing Alexa+ features score poorly on Design and Quality of the Idea. — [https://amazonappdev2026.devpost.com/forum_topics/45397-what-exactly-are-you-looking-for-in-the-alexa-track](https://amazonappdev2026.devpost.com/forum_topics/45397-what-exactly-are-you-looking-for-in-the-alexa-track) — *Official DevRel forum answer, ~30 Sep 2026; saved copy retrieved 2026-10-03 and re-read 2026-10-04. High.*

| Rival | Strongest feature | Our difference | Evidence status |
|---|---|---|---|
| [Life360](https://www.life360.com/) | Real-time location of family members' phones and Tile-tagged items, Place Alerts, location history, crash detection; free tier plus Silver $9.99 and Gold $16.99 per month. | OutPass needs no device on the child and no location data; it records intent, approval, host confirmation and return instead of position. A phone-free child cannot be a Life360 member (inference from the product being phone/Tile tracking). Any Life360 feature for permission requests or host confirmation: not verified in reviewed sources. | Homepage read live 2026-10-04 (saved .firecrawl/oct4/alx2-rival-life360.md); app not used. |

OutPass is the household-device equivalent for the child plus a two-household protocol. | Official Apple support page read live 2026-10-04 (saved .firecrawl/oct4/alx2-rival-apple-checkin.json). |

Overlap in the general 'acceptance' pattern is real. | Repository description read from the saved GitHub search results (Oct 3); README not read this round. |

**Smallest credible MVP:**

- MCP server (spec 2025-11-25 over Streamable HTTP, accepting 2025-03-26/06-18 clients) with tools: request_pass, decide_pass, confirm_host (signed token), check_out, check_in, extend_pass (parent only), cancel_pass, get_pass_status (readOnlyHint), list_rules, set_rule, list_pass_history; idempotent writes via client event IDs and DynamoDB conditional writes; isError on invalid input; stable IDs chain request -> decision -> pass
- Deterministic rule engine (homework-first checklist, fixed/sunset curfew via NOAA formula, consented destinations, weekday/weekend) with unit tests on 20 synthetic days
- Escalation ladder on Step Functions Wait states (or EventBridge Scheduler) with atomic cancellation on check-in, shown live in the demo by a fast-forwarded clock
- MCP Apps ui:// resources: pass card with countdown and conditions, household rules list, pass-history carousel; hydrated data-only fallback
- Labelled Alexa+-style simulated host on the ext-apps basic-host with three surfaces: kitchen Echo Show (child), parent phone (Alexa app), host family link/phone (and optional host Echo); child/parent speaker selector standing in for Voice ID; deterministic utterance parser by default, optional Nova Lite
- Host-family onboarding and confirmation via signed one-time links delivered to a mock inbox in the simulator (real SMS/email excluded from MVP)
- Open-source 'addon-fr-checks' harness run in CI against this server (Open Source mini-challenge), plus the pass state machine as a small tested package
- Hosted endpoint (Lambda+API Gateway) live through 20 Nov plus documented local-run path; README with verified-vs-simulated table, privacy statement (no location, minimal child data, deletion) and friction log

**Deliberately excluded stretch:**

- Any GPS or location data, ever (design principle, not a stretch)
- Real SMS or email delivery (SNS SMS needs sandbox exit and US registration; use a mock inbox; SES sandbox email is a stretch)
- Real Voice ID, real Alexa notifications, Amazon Kids certification or parental-consent flows (gated/unavailable; simulated and declared)
- Kid smartwatch or phone integrations; school calendar integration
- Privilege-level automation beyond one worked example; multi-child fairness
- Classic Alexa Skill on a physical Echo (optional only if a non-Alexa+ Echo on the developer account is confirmed working)
