| unverified | Amazon Titan Text Embeddings v2 on Bedrock (clustering and notes index) | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/titan-embedding-models.html |
| unverified | Apple Watch Bee recording becomes a COMPLETED conversation with speaker labels | https://amazonappdev2026.devpost.com/forum_topics/45111-bee-developer-connect |

**Blockers:** Eligibility: without an iPhone plus Apple Watch running Bee (or a US-shipped Pioneer) there is no real data; Watch capture parity and India service availability are unverified.; Speaker labels may not separate the tutor from students, breaking role assignment (TechCrunch reports manual naming).; Consent and recording norms for students; the demo must use consenting adults and the README must address classroom policies.; Live mode depends on an at-most-once stream that reportedly dies after about 43 h and has no utterance timestamps.; At least three real sessions with consenting participants are needed before the video; scheduling friends is a calendar risk.; Bedrock quota or suspension on a new AWS account with no escalation path.; English-only demo; the Indian coaching segment often runs in Hindi or Hinglish, where transcription quality is unverified.

**Reasons judges might reject it:** Recording students, even consenting adults, may concern judges on consent and classroom policy grounds.; May be filed as 'transcription plus clustering' despite the role separation, coverage check and live recall.; TeachFX and Piazza exist; the differentiation rests on features absent from their pages, not on product testing.; A two-person demo with friends may look staged unless Bee app and CLI timestamps are shown live.; Live mode may miss or lag on camera because the stream is at-most-once; a visible fallback is needed.; Judges' education mental model is student-side 'on-wrist coaching'; a tutor-side tool may feel off-brief.; Impact evidence is office-hours volume data, not proof that repeated questions are the main cost.

**Gate result:** overall **conditional** (eligibility conditional, feasibility conditional; native overlap medium, visible crowding low).

- Eligibility: Same hard gate as every Bee concept: real data from an actual Bee device or an Apple Watch running Bee is required (Rules §4), and the base-case user has neither. Once capture exists, the runtime hook is satisfied (bee changed, bee conversations get/transcript, bee search --neural, bee stream and bee todos create --alarm-at are called at runtime) and the project fits the Bee track's 'education' priority use case from the tutor's side, with 'real time functionality' as a Creative hook. Additional eligibility-adjacent requirements: recording students needs announced, consented recording (the demo uses two consenting adults, no minors; about a dozen US states require all-party consent per IDEA…
- Feasibility: The 90 h solo estimate fits the roughly 104 h capacity (estimate) but sits at its own stated limit because of live mode (10 h), which the concept rightly cuts first. The binding constraints are people and calendar: at least three real sessions with two consenting friends must start by about Oct 8, which depends on hardware arriving by about day 5. Technical risks: speaker labels may not separate the tutor from students (TechCrunch 2026-05-24 reports manual naming), which would collapse role assignment to heuristics; the stream is at-most-once with no utterance timestamps in the documented payload and an entrant reports it dying after about 43 h (single-source anecdote), so live mode must de…
- Conditions: (1) Hardware path A (iPhone plus Apple Watch running Bee) or B (US-shipped Pioneer) working from India with Developer Mode and bee login by about day 5, or the concept is ineligible. (2) Real doubt sessions with two consenting adults start by about Oct 8, each 15-20 minutes and well over 50 words, with signed releases and a verbal recording announcement; no minors; English only. (3) Day-1 kill test: both sessions reach COMPLETED with at least two distinct speaker labels and role accuracy of at least 70%; otherwise roles fall back to heuristics and the README states the degradation. (4) Live mode is best-effort and the first thing cut if behind schedule; the video shows transcript --since polling as the backstop and real latency clocks. (5) Bedrock quota verified non-zero on day 1; embeddings via a verified Bedrock model or a local fallback; no student names or labels stored. (6) Transcripts are snapshotted on first observation because Watch recordings can later disappear (forum 45111 participant report).
- Native overlap: Bee natively produces per-conversation summaries, suggested to-dos, Templates (for example a lecture into a study plan) and search-and-chat over conversations (aboutamazon 2026-01-05; bee.computer), so 'what did students ask today?' for one session is a latent chat capability. Cross-session clustering of student questions with recurrence counts, a coverage check against the tutor's own notes, and live 'you answered this before' recall were not found in Bee, Piazza (typed Q&A), TeachFX (talk-tim…
- Crowding: No education or tutor-side project among the roughly 8-9 visible Bee repos; 'bee tutor OR "office hours" wearable created:>2026-08-31' returned 6 repos on 2026-10-04, none education-related (bee-cipher, beereflect-ai, ringfence, bee-bystander, ambient-guard, beepvision). Cross-track, education and tutoring is a visible Alexa+ cluster (about 10: yasmin-voice-tutor, k12-stem-tutor-alexa, mentor-ai-alexa-mcp, StudySidekick), all student-facing. Lower bound: public repos only.
- Day-one proof: With hardware: two consented 15-20 minute Watch sessions in which a friend asks 8 scripted questions each (3 repeated across sessions) reach state COMPLETED and bee conversations get --json shows at least two distinct speaker labels; the Nova Lite extractor yields at least 6 of 8 Q/A pairs per session with the right role; at least 2 of the 3 repeats cluster together or match via bee search --neural --since; bee todos create --alarm-at produces a reminder visible on the iPhone; bee stream --types new-utterance --json emits utterances during the second session with measured delay, and bee conversations transcript --since returns the same lines as the backstop.
- Redesign to pass: If no capture path works by about day 5, it is ineligible; pivot. If hardware exists, demo post-session recurrence and coverage as the core (still Bee-dependent via changefeed, search and todos) and present live mode as a measured, best-effort extra; if friends cannot attend three sessions on different days, run three shorter sessions on consecutive days and say so; keep the asked-again Skill and the bee-cli docs PR as the Open Source artefacts.

**Round-2 judge ratings (1–5; base case: solo, India, VVD on Mac, no Bee hardware, no US Ring device). Weighted = 0.25·Tech + 0.20·Design + 0.20·Impact + 0.20·Originality + 0.15·Feasibility.**

| Judge lens | Tech | Design | Impact | Originality | Feasibility | Weighted | Confidence | One-line verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| devrel | 1 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 1 | 2.20 | low | Good education fit for Bee but people-, consent- and calendar-dependent; ineligible without a Watch. |
| engineer | 1 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 1 | 2.20 | medium | The most Bee-surface-rich of the new concepts and on-brief for education, but ineligible without hardware and dependent on speaker labels and friends' calendars. |
| product | 1 | 3 | 2 | 3 | 1 | 2.00 | low | Thoughtful tutor-side tool with indirect demand evidence and heavy people logistics; ineligible without a Watch. |
| **Mean of three** | 1.00 | 3.00 | 2.67 | 3.00 | 1.00 | **2.13** | | Scenario weighted: solo+hw 2.92; duo 2.13; duo+hw 3.15 |

**Why each judge rated it that way (verbatim rationale, one per criterion):**

- *devrel* — Tech: Ineligible in the base scenario (JDR-02). — Design: Tutor-side dashboard plus a live prior-answer card uses changefeed, neural search, stream and todo write-back, with privacy by discarding student labels; capped because judges' education mental model is student-facing on-wrist coaching and live mode depends on an at-most-once stream. — Impact: Office-hours volume and deadline-spike studies are peer-reviewed (UIUC Queue 70,000 questions; SIGCSE) but say nothing about repeated questions; TA time is capped, so the cost is plausible but inferred. — Originality: Tutor-side recurring-question bank not found in Piazza, TeachFX or Otter Education pages (JDR-26) and no education entrant among visible Bee repos (JDR-25); the pipeline itself (extract, embed, cluster) is standard and may be filed as transcription plus clustering. — Feasibility: No capture path.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 3, feasibility 2 (90 h sits at the limit and real consented sessions must start by ~Oct 8 with hardware by day 5 (forum 45111 Watch behaviour, JDR-08); highest calendar risk of the Bee set.); duo tech 1, feasibility 1 (Still ineligible.); duo+hw tech 4, feasibility 3 (52 h each; people logistics unchanged.)
- *engineer* — Tech: No capture hardware in the base case, so no real doubt sessions exist and the Bee rule (evt-rules.md L348) fails at Stage 1. Merit scores below assume it is built. — Design: Session-as-conversation boundary, role separation with student labels discarded at ingestion, a live prior-answer card with honest spoken/processed/fetched clocks, and todo write-back to the tutor's phone form a coherent tutor-side design that uses the wearable's real-time surface. Deductions: consent and classroom-policy framing, English-only, and a live mode that must visibly degrade to polling. — Impact: Named priority use case (education) with real volume context (one university queue facilitated 70,000+ questions, ASEE 2020; deadline spikes per arXiv 2608.06753, both read live 2026-10-04 per the concept); the specific cost of repeated questions is inferred, not measured, and the Indian coaching segment runs in Hindi/Hinglish where transcription quality is unverified. — Originality: No education or tutor-side project among the six saved Bee repos; cross-session clustering of student questions with coverage against the tutor's own notes is not verified in Piazza, TeachFX or Otter pages. Offsets: it can be filed as 'transcription plus clustering', Bee Templates already recap a single session, and the Alexa+ track has about 10 student-facing education entrants. — Feasibility: Ineligible without a capture path; also people- and calendar-bound (three sessions with two consenting adults starting by about Oct 8).
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 4, feasibility 3 (With hardware in hand, 90 h fits about 104 h (estimates) and the build touches changefeed, conversations, neural search, stream and todos write-back with a polling backstop; speaker-label separation and the at-most-once…); duo tech 1, feasibility 1 (Still ineligible without Bee hardware.); duo+hw tech 4, feasibility 4 (At about 52 h each (estimate) the second person can also act as a consenting student, easing the people dependency; extraction/clustering/coverage and live/dashboard/video split cleanly.)
- *product* — Tech: No Bee hardware in the base scenario; ineligible under Rules L348. — Design: Tutor-side dashboard of recurring clusters, coverage gaps against the tutor's own notes, a live 'you answered this before' card with latency clocks, and Bee todo write-back with alarms (concept mechanism). Role separation and discarding student labels is good privacy design; live mode is best-effort on an at-most-once stream and must degrade visibly (gate conditions). — Impact: Office-hours load is real: one university queue facilitated over 70,000 questions across 25 courses since Fall 2017 and demand spikes near deadlines with long waits and overworked staff (bee2-officehours-research.json). But the specific cost of repeated spoken doubts is inferred, not measured (concept problem_frequency), Piazza already gauges confusing topics from typed questions (bee2-piazza-overview.md) and TeachF… — Originality: Cross-session clustering of student questions with coverage checks against materials and live prior-answer recall is not verified in Piazza, TeachFX, Otter's student-side Education agent (bee2-otter-education.md) or Bee Templates (per-conversation). No education entrant among visible Bee repos (gh2-bee-wearable.json); the education cluster of about 10 is Alexa+ and student-facing (refresh). — Feasibility: Ineligible without hardware; with hardware 90 h sits at the stated limit because of live mode and depends on two consenting friends attending at least three sessions starting by about Oct 8 (gate conditions).
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 3, feasibility 2 (Eligible with Watch; speaker-label separation of tutor and students is unverified (TechCrunch manual naming), the stream reportedly dies after about 43 h, and the three-session calendar with friends is the binding const…); duo tech 1, feasibility 1 (Ineligible without real Bee data.); duo+hw tech 3, feasibility 3 (52 h per person; the people and calendar dependence remains.)

**Red-team verdicts:** not red-teamed (did not reach the top of either round's ranking); ranking rests on gate and judge scores alone.

---


### Track: Ring

#### RNG-4: CamCharter: a Ring-verified camera charter and change-request loop for shared homes

**Round:** 2. **Track:** Ring (primary). Mini challenges: AWS Builder, Open Source.

**Pitch:** Shared homes (roommates, live-in or remote landlords, adult children with parents) run on cameras one person owns and everyone else is filmed by. CamCharter turns the Ring account owner's live device data into a plain-language 'camera sheet' per camera: online, motion detection on/off, what the motion zones cover (rendered and described), how many privacy zones exist, night vision, resolution, and the state the camera is in, each fact stamped 'verified by Ring at <time>' or 'owner-stated'. Every resident, with or without a Ring account, gets a link to read, acknowledge, or file a request ('add a privacy zone over the sofa', 'turn motion detection off on the hallway cam'). The owner gets Ring-app step instructions; when the poller sees the matching configuration change, the request closes as 'verified by Ring' and everyone is notified. Unrequested changes reopen the sheet for re-acknowledgement. No video, no identities, no on_demand receipts.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Primary user | Co-residents of shared homes who do not own the Ring account: roommates in shared flats, tenants in rentals with landlord-provided doorbells or cameras, adult children or elderly parents in multigenerational homes. The Ring account owner (housemate, landlord, parent) is the second user who connects the account and acts on requests. US focus for the real Partner API path; the Playground path is location-agnostic. |
| Frequency | Episodic but recurring: at move-in, when a camera is added or moved, when a dispute happens, and whenever settings change silently. Pattnaik et al. (2024) report 44.9% of 149 shared-home participants had a smart camera or doorbell in the home and 52.3% received no details about landlord-provided devices; settings changes by the owner are otherwise invisible to co-residents at any frequency (inference: the API exposes no change feed, so co-residents never learn of them). |
| Severity | Moderate to high for the filmed party: 20.8% of those with a camera in the shared home did not know how or where recordings were kept and 25.4% had recordings under landlord control (Pattnaik et al., n=67 subset). Power asymmetry between the 'smart home driver' and 'passive users' is the central finding of Geeng and Roesner (CHI 2019). Ring's own Content Policy prohibits secretly surveilling roommates (1.2.1) and requires apps to give customers access logs (1.2.2), so the platform treats the issue as real. |
| Current workaround | Verbal agreements, a lease clause or house-rules document, or adding the person as a Ring shared user. Ring User Permissions give Limited and Standard users Live View and Event History but only Owner, Advanced and Installer roles can adjust device settings, and every shared user needs their own Ring account (support page clv68). Residents without an account have no view at all. None of these show what the zones cover or notify anyone when settings change (inference from the permissions table; whether Limited/Standard users can view zone settings is not verified in reviewed sources). |
| Amazon integration and its indispensable role | Ring Partner API (api.amazonvision.com) via a Developer Playground token: device discovery with include=status,capabilities,location,configurations; configurations give motion_detection.enabled, motion_zones polygons (normalized 0-1 vertices) and privacy_zones (count used only), image_enhancements toggles; status gives online; location gives country/state (used for a non-legal note on audio-consent states). Ring UX Design Guide patterns are followed verbatim: configuration guidance messages ('what, why, where in the Ring app'), never implying settings can be changed in the partner app, never exposing privacy-zone coordinates, user-assigned device names, WCAG 2.2 AA. Optional AWS for hosting and scheduled polling. |
| Removal test (honest) | Without Ring, CamCharter is a shared house-rules document with an acknowledgement checkbox, which is what a lease clause or a Google Doc already does. Everything that makes it a product disappears: the 'verified by Ring' stamp on each fact, the rendered motion-zone coverage, the automatic closure of a request when the configuration actually changes, and the detection of unrequested changes. Ring is load-bearing for verification and change detection. Honest caveats: the acknowledgement and request flow itself is generic, and the API cannot verify placement (indoor/outdoor), audio recording state, Modes, or the physical privacy cover, so those facts stay owner-stated and labelled as such. |
| Wow moment under 45 s | 0-10 s: the owner pastes a Playground token; within about 8 s the dashboard shows 'Front Door (DoorbellPro): Online. Motion detection ON. 1 motion zone covering about 70% of the view, full width from one-fifth down (street strip excluded). 1 privacy zone set (kept private). Night vision OFF. Verified by Ring 3 s ago.' with the zone drawn over a neutral grid. 10-25 s: a housemate opens their link on a phone (no Ring account), reads the sheet and taps 'Acknowledge'; the owner dashboard flips to '2 of 3 housemates acknowledged'. 25-45 s: the housemate files 'Please add a privacy zone over the sofa'; the owner sees exact Ring-app steps (Devices > Device Settings > Privacy Settings > Add Privacy Zones). The emulator applies the change (labelled SIMULATED on screen because the Playground device cannot be edited); the poller reports 'privacy zones 1 -> 2; request closed, verified by Ring' and … |
| Original mechanism | A consent ledger keyed to canonical configuration snapshots. (1) Snapshot: each poll normalizes devices+status+capabilities+configurations into a canonical JSON document and hashes it; motion zones are hashed by vertex list and summarized by area fraction and a 3x3 grid coverage; privacy zones contribute only their count (UX guide MUST). (2) Sheet: a deterministic template renders each fact with provenance (verified-by-Ring vs owner-stated) and a version number tied to the snapshot hash. (3) Acknowledgement: residents (magic-link identities, no Ring account) acknowledge a specific sheet version; a snapshot hash change invalidates acknowledgements for the affected camera only. (4) Requests: a small state machine (open -> instructions issued -> verified-closed \| withdrawn \| stale) where closure is triggered only by a snapshot diff that matches the request type (privacy_zones count +1; m… |
| Simulator or real-data route (data route) | Owner browser -> CamCharter API (Lambda behind API Gateway; required because api.amazonvision.com blocks browser calls via CORS per the API overview) -> Ring Partner API over HTTPS -> canonical snapshot (metadata only: names, booleans, zone vertices, counts) stored in DynamoDB with the token encrypted via KMS. Resident pages are static (S3/CloudFront) and fetch sheet JSON via signed magic links. No video, snapshots, clips or media URLs are ever requested or stored. Privacy-zone vertices are discarded at ingest; only the count is kept (UX guide MUST). |
| Authentication | Development and demo: Playground token pasted by the owner (30 min; the UI shows an honest 'token expired' state linking to the console). Real accounts: refresh-token mode (access token about 4 h, refresh about 30 days) per the starter's RING_REFRESH_TOKEN pattern. Production would need one-way account linking, an identity-verified developer account and US devices (gated; not in MVP). Residents authenticate with magic links and never see Ring credentials or tokens. |
| Preview constraints | Playground issues exactly one simulated DoorbellPro; its configuration is assumed static (assumption; no entrant reports editing it), so request closure and drift must be shown on the ring-sandbox emulator and labelled SIMULATED. Only on_demand events exist in Playground history (not used by the MVP). The 30-minute token interrupts long demos. Whether the Playground opens for an India-based Amazon Developer account is not verified. Whether the Playground DoorbellPro has a non-empty motion_zones array is unknown (day-1 check). Multi-device households can only be shown on the emulator. |
| Hardware | MacBook M4 Pro for development; two phones (or browser profiles) for owner and resident roles; no Ring hardware for the Playground path. Optional: a US-located Ring camera with a Ring plan for the conditional real path (not held by the team). |
| SDK versions | No official Ring SDK (curl/JSON:API). Node 20 via nvm for a Next.js or Vite front end (machine has Node 25; the official starter is Next.js 14). Python 3.11+ for the poller and for ring-sandbox 0.5.0 (PyPI 2026-10-02 per github.md). AWS CDK v2 or SAM for deployment. Versions are from the digests, not re-verified today except where stated. |
| Deployment prerequisites | Amazon Developer account that can open the Playground; AWS account on a Paid plan if promotional credits are used (Free plan cannot use them); API Gateway + Lambda + DynamoDB + EventBridge Scheduler + S3/CloudFront + KMS; a custom domain or CloudFront URL for resident links; no public webhook endpoint needed on the Playground path. No Bedrock dependency. |
| Firecrawl: research role | Used in this round to read the two multi-user-home studies, Ring's User Permissions and privacy-feature help pages, the Google Home permissions page, and the Ring blog on User Permissions (saved under .firecrawl/oct4/rng2-*.json). |
| Firecrawl: runtime role | none |
| AWS Builder path | EventBridge Scheduler triggers a Python Lambda every 5 minutes per connected home (well inside the 14M free invocations/month listed for Scheduler per the aws digest); the Lambda reads Ring, writes the snapshot to DynamoDB (on-demand), and emits change records that a second Lambda turns into request closures and resident notices (Web Push). API Gateway HTTP API fronts the owner and resident APIs; S3 + CloudFront host the static pages; KMS encrypts tokens; AWS Budgets alarm at $10. Developed with Kiro or Claude Code on Bedrock (forum says this qualifies). Value: durable polling with zero servers, encrypted tokens, and per-home isolation; no model in the decision path. |
| Open Source path | (1) Publish 'ring-config-snapshot' (MIT, TypeScript + Python ports): canonicalizes Partner API device/configuration payloads, hashes them, and diffs two snapshots into typed change records (privacy_zone_added, motion_detection_disabled, motion_zone_changed with area delta, device_removed), with fixtures taken from the public API doc examples and table-driven tests. Useful to any Ring partner that must detect configuration drift. (2) Upstream PR to josepha-mayo/ring-sandbox adding configuration-change scenarios (privacy zone added/removed, motion toggled, zone edited) so other entrants can test drift. (3) Publish the Ring-app step templates as a small JSON dataset with source URLs and retrieval dates. |
| Fallback | If the Playground configuration has no zones or the console cannot edit settings: keep the real Playground path for sheet generation, acknowledgements and the 'no change since <time>' poller, and run all change scenarios on the emulator with a persistent SIMULATED banner. If informal interviews show co-residents would not use an acknowledgement flow: reposition as a landlord-tenant disclosure sheet for rentals (the Pattnaik study's 52.3% 'no details received' finding), keeping the same engine. If the Playground does not open from India: build entirely on the emulator and the published API doc fixtures, state so plainly, and expect a lower Tech score. |
| Falsification test (≤1 day) | One day. (a) With a Playground token, GET configurations and record whether motion_zones and privacy_zones are present and non-empty, and whether the Playground console exposes any device-setting control; if the fields are missing, the concept has nothing verifiable beyond 'online' and 'motion on/off' and should be dropped. (b) Show a paper mock of the camera sheet and the request flow to five people who currently live in a shared home or rental with a camera; the concept is falsified if four or more say they would rather text the owner and would not acknowledge or file a request through a tool. (c) Confirm in the public docs that no endpoint exposes audio-recording state or Modes, and write the owner-stated labels accordingly. |
| Risk profile | Low technical risk (reads, hashing, state machine, static hosting) and lowest cloud cost. Medium simulation risk: the Playground device is static, so change detection is demonstrated on an emulator. Medium interpersonal and legal sensitivity (landlord-tenant, roommates) handled by consent-first design and 'not legal advice' notices. Medium competition risk from native User Permissions and consent-themed entrants. Buyer-incentive risk is the main adoption weakness. |
| Distinct from round 1 because | User group: co-residents and tenants who are filmed but do not own the account, not in-home workers (RNG-3), paratransit riders (RNG-1) or appointment professionals (RNG-2). Workflow: a per-camera disclosure sheet with acknowledgement and a request-to-verified-closure loop at move-in and on change, not per-shift camera-use receipts, a wait clock, or booking-matched doorbell routing. Mechanism: canonical configuration snapshots, hashing and typed diffs driving a consent ledger; it never reads on_demand history, never opens WHEP, never classifies sessions. Shared primitive acknowledged honestly… |
| Estimate (unmeasured) | Solo 76 h; two-person 46 h per person; cloud Estimate: about $2-8 total through 20 Nov 2026. Scheduler + Lambda polling (288 invocations/day/home, under 1 s each) stays within listed free allowances; DynamoDB on-demand and S3/CloudFront cost cents at demo volume; KMS about $1/key-month; CloudWatch logs a few dollars. No Bedrock, Rekognition or media egress.. Assumptions: Estimates, not measurements. One engineer comfortable with TypeScript/Python and AWS serverless. Hours: day-1 spikes incl. Playground configuration read and console check (6h), Ring client + poller + canonical snapshot/diff with tests (10h), sheet generator and zone render (8h), resident magic links, acknowledgement ledger and request state machine (12h), owner dashboard and Ring-app step templates (8h), emulator scenarios (6h), AWS deployment (6h), end-to-end tests (6h), README/verified-vs-sim… |

**Demand evidence** (quality labelled by the researcher):

- In a survey of 149 shared-home residents, 44.9% (67/149) had a smart camera or doorbell in the shared home; 20.8% (14/67) were unaware of how and where recordings were kept and 25.4% (17/67) had recordings stored under the landlord's control; 52.3% received no details about landlord-provided devices; 30% had recordings collected by the landlord. — [https://arxiv.org/html/2409.09363v2](https://arxiv.org/html/2409.09363v2) — *Peer-reviewed (PACM HCI / CSCW 2024, arXiv v2 dated 19 Sep 2024); small sample (n=149), UK-centred, self-reported; not Ring-specific.*
- Multi-user smart homes show an outsized role for the 'smart home driver' who installs and controls devices; 'smart home drivers tend to have more access to functionality and data than passive users', and designs 'can and should take a role in minimizing the power differences among users in the home'. — [https://dl.acm.org/doi/fullHtml/10.1145/3290605.3300498](https://dl.acm.org/doi/fullHtml/10.1145/3290605.3300498) — *Peer-reviewed (CHI 2019), 18 participants, mostly drivers rather than passive users (the authors flag this), 2019; directional, not quantitative.*
- Ring's Content Policy prohibits apps for 'secretly surveilling roommates or employees' (1.2.1) and requires that 'Apps must provide to Ring customers easy access to monitoring activity or access logs' (1.2.2). — [https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/content-policy.html](https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/content-policy.html) — *Official policy (undated page); shows platform alignment, not end-user demand.*
- Ring User Permissions: only Owners, Advanced users and Professional installers can 'Adjust Ring device settings'; Limited and Standard users get Live View and Event History; shared users must create their own Ring account to accept an invite; no subscription is required to add shared users. — [https://ring.com/support/articles/clv68/managing-permissions-for-users](https://ring.com/support/articles/clv68/managing-permissions-for-users) — *Official help centre (undated; references a February 2026 permission change). Documents the gap for non-account residents and the lack of settings visibility; it is supply-side evidence, not measured demand.*
- 32 privacy-conscious smart home power users asked for better 'data transparency, visibility, and control' and found the general lack of transparency from device providers a core drawback. — [https://pearl.umd.edu/wp-content/uploads/2023/06/Park_etal-2023-SOUPS-smart-homes.pdf](https://pearl.umd.edu/wp-content/uploads/2023/06/Park_etal-2023-SOUPS-smart-homes.pdf) — *Peer-reviewed (SOUPS 2023); power users, not passive co-residents; qualitative.*

**Rivals named at generation** (strongest feature; our difference; evidence status):

| Rival | Strongest feature | Our difference | Evidence status |
|---|---|---|---|
| [Ring User Permissions (native shared access, replaces Shared Users)](https://ring.com/support/articles/clv68/managing-permissions-for-users) | Role-based, location-level access (Limited, Standard, Advanced, Professional installer with 48-hour auto-expiry); changes take effect immediately; no subscription needed. | Covers residents who have no Ring account; shows what the cameras are configured to do and when that changed; adds acknowledgement and a request-to-verified-closure loop. Whether Limited or Standard users can view motion-zone or privacy-zone settings is not verified in reviewed sources. | Official help page read 2026-10-04 (undated; mentions February 2026). Ring blog announcing User Permissions read 2026-10-04 (image paths dated 2025-11). |
| [Google Home people and permissions (Admin / Member)](https://support.google.com/googlehome/answer/9155535?hl=en) | Members can be granted or denied 'Settings' and 'Activity' access separately, including camera history; everyone can see who is Admin or Member. | Ring ecosystem; residents without accounts; a disclosure-plus-acknowledgement record and verified change requests. An acknowledgement or disclosure workflow is not verified in reviewed sources for Google Home. | Official support page read 2026-10-04 (undated). |
| [Attest (josepha-mayo/attest, Ring-track entrant)](https://github.com/josepha-mayo/attest) | Signed, self-verifying evidence packs with an offline verifier, HMAC webhook intake, a live-API sweep report, and a worker-facing timeline. | Attest proves visits happened for in-home work; CamCharter is about configuration disclosure and consent among co-residents, uses no on_demand records, no media and no signature chain. Overlap: both read Event History metadata and both label Playground limits honestly. | README read from saved copy (retrieved 2026-10-03); repo last pushed 2026-10-03 per github.md. |
| [Ring privacy features (Privacy Zones, Audio Streaming and Recording toggle)](https://ring.com/support/articles/g4e2w/using-privacy-features-in-the-ring-app) | Up to two Privacy Zones per device that are blacked out in live and recorded video; an Audio Streaming and Recording toggle (requires a compatible subscription). | These are the controls; CamCharter surfaces their state (count only for privacy zones) to the people affected and routes requests to the owner. Audio recording state is not exposed by the Partner API, so it stays owner-stated. | Official help page read 2026-10-04 ('Last updated 1 month ago'). |

**Smallest credible MVP:**

- Token-paste and refresh-token modes; server-side Ring client with one include= call per poll; canonical snapshot + hash; access log of CamCharter's own reads.
- Camera sheet generator: provenance-labelled facts, motion-zone render (SVG over a neutral grid, no video) with area fraction and 3x3 coverage text, privacy-zone count only, state/country note on audio-consent laws marked 'not legal advice'.
- Resident roster with magic links (no Ring account), per-version acknowledgement, re-acknowledgement on change.
- Request state machine with 5 request types and Ring-app step templates sourced from Ring support articles (URLs and retrieval dates kept in the repo).
- Poller on EventBridge Scheduler (every 5 min; configurable) with diff-to-request matching and 'changed without a request' notices.
- ring-sandbox emulator scenarios for the labelled SIMULATED beats: privacy zone added, motion detection turned off, device removed, zone edited; the UI shows a persistent SIMULATED banner on emulator homes.
- Table tests for the diff engine and state machine; README with a verified-vs-simulated table; friction log; product feedback.

**Deliberately excluded stretch:**

- Live-view counts on the resident sheet from on_demand history (kept out to stay distinct from RNG-3 OnAir; could be added as an equal-visibility 'camera was viewed N times today' line).
- Production one-way account linking (Token Exchange URL, nonce, PATCH completed) and webhook intake for device_added/device_removed; requires a registered, identity-verified app and US devices.
- Landlord-tenant template packs per US state; email/SMS notifications (Web Push and in-page only in the MVP).
- Multi-location homes and Ring Elite per-component zones.
- Any LLM rephrasing of sheets (deterministic templates only in the MVP).

**APIs, events and actions** (status as assessed by the researcher; 'unverified' means not confirmed in reviewed docs, 'gated' means not available to entrants, 'simulated' means not real in the demo):

| Status | API / event / action | Documentation |
|---|---|---|
| verified | GET /v1/devices?include=status,capabilities,location,configurations (device discovery compound document) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#including-related-data |
| verified | GET /v1/devices/{id}/configurations (motion_detection.enabled, motion_zones vertices 0-1, image_enhancements incl. privacy_zones, hdr, color_night_vision, ir_led_night_vision, auto_zoom_track) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#configurations |
| verified | GET /v1/devices/{id}/status (online) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#status |
| verified | GET /v1/devices/{id}/capabilities (video resolution/codecs, motion_detection and image_enhancements configurable keys, components, battery_status.supported) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#capabilities |
| verified | GET /v1/devices/{id}/location (country, state only) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#locations |
| verified | GET /v1/users/me (owner identity for the access log) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#authentication |
| verified | Ring Developer Playground token (about 30 min, one DoorbellPro, no app or account link) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/release-notes.html#developers-playground |
| gated | Webhooks device_added / device_removed / app_integration_removed (production change feed) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#notifications |
| gated | One-way account linking (Token Exchange URL, nonce Account Link URL, POST/PATCH /v1/accounts/me/app-integrations) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#6-account-linking-flow-one-way-account-linking |
| simulated | ring-sandbox emulator (third-party, MIT) for configuration-change scenarios | https://github.com/josepha-mayo/ring-sandbox |
| verified | UX Design Guide rules applied: privacy-zone coordinates never shown; configuration guidance pattern; WCAG 2.2 AA | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/ux-design-guide.html#privacy-zones |

**Blockers:** Playground device configuration is assumed static, so the signature beat (request closed by a real configuration change) is simulated unless the console allows edits (day-1 check).; Whether the Playground DoorbellPro returns a non-empty motion_zones array is unknown; an empty array makes the zone render a fixture-labelled demo.; Partner API exposes no device type, no audio-recording state, no Modes and no privacy-cover state; those facts stay owner-stated, which weakens the 'verified' promise and must be explained in the UI.; A partner token only covers devices the owner chose to share at linking (production), so CamCharter cannot claim the sheet lists every camera in the home.; Playground regional access for an India-based developer account is not verified.; Node 25 on the machine versus the official starter's Next.js 14 (use nvm).

**Reasons judges might reject it:** 'A settings diff with a checkbox' - Tech Implementation may read as thin because there is no CV, no streaming and no model.; The signature closure beat is simulated on the Playground; a judge may discount a demo whose key moment runs on an emulator.; Overlap with RNG-3 OnAir's configuration-drift notices and with Attest's evidence framing; a judge who sees several consent-themed Ring entries (about 19 privacy/consent repos across tracks per github.md) may tire of the theme.; Ring may be uncomfortable with a product that frames cameras as a household privacy risk, despite the Content Policy language.; The verified promise is partial (no device type, audio or Modes), which a careful judge will probe.; Buyer problem: the person who benefits (the filmed resident) is not the person who installs it (the owner).

**Gate result:** overall **pass** (eligibility pass, feasibility pass; native overlap medium, visible crowding medium).

- Eligibility: The runtime Ring surface is real and Ring-documented: GET /v1/devices?include=status,capabilities,location,configurations on the Developer Playground, which the FAQ names as an acceptable simulator and forum 45309 calls 'the only documented route' without a US device. The configurations endpoint is verified read-only and returns motion_detection.motion_zones (polygons with vertices, normalized 0.0-1.0, origin top-left) and image_enhancements.privacy_zones (same structure; count used only), plus color_night_vision, hdr, ir_led_night_vision and auto_zoom_track (saved API reference, undated). No video, snapshots, on_demand records, webhooks, lock actuation, two-way talk, chime TTS or face reco…
- Feasibility: The 76 h solo estimate fits comfortably within the roughly 104 h capacity (estimate). The engine is hashing, typed diffs, a small state machine and static hosting, all deterministic with no model. Unknowns are Playground-side and each has a stated, rules-safe fallback: whether the Playground DoorbellPro returns non-empty motion_zones (fallback: the API-doc example polygon as a labelled fixture), whether the console allows any device-setting edit (assumed static, so closure runs on the emulator with a SIMULATED banner), whether the Playground opens for an India-based Amazon Developer account (unverified for every Ring concept), and the 30-minute token. Node 25 on the machine versus the offic…
- Conditions: (1) The Developer Playground opens for the user's Amazon Developer account from India (regional gating unverified) and GET /v1/devices with include=configurations returns 200 with the motion_detection and image_enhancements fields present; empty arrays are acceptable but then the zone render is a labelled fixture. (2) Every emulator-driven beat (privacy zone added, motion toggled, device removed) carries a persistent SIMULATED banner and is listed in the README's verified-vs-simulated table; the 'verified by Ring' stamp appears only on facts read from the Playground or a real account. (3) Privacy zones are reported by count only; vertices are discarded at ingest (UX guide MUST); no video or media endpoint is called. (4) 'Not legal advice' notice on audio-consent and landlord-tenant text; the sheet states that a partner token covers only the devices the owner shared, so it cannot claim to list every camera; audio recording state, Modes, device type and privacy covers stay 'owner-stated'. (5) CamCharter's own API reads are shown to the owner as an access log (Content Policy 1.2.2). (6) Do not also build RNG-3 OnAir; the configuration-drift primitive is shared.
- Native overlap: Ring User Permissions (help page clv68, undated, references a February 2026 change) already lets owners grant Limited, Standard, Advanced and Professional-installer access: Live View and Event History go to all four roles, while 'Adjust Ring device settings' is reserved for Owners, Advanced and Professional installers, and every invitee 'must create their own Ring account'. Google Home separates Settings and Activity access for Members. Both cover shared access for account holders; a disclosure…
- Crowding: Ring had about 36 visible repos for 2 prizes on Oct 3 (about 18 per prize; not re-measured). The privacy, consent and redaction theme spans about 19 repos across tracks (github.md), including josepha-mayo/attest (signed evidence packs), NovaCorpAI/agentpos-doorstep (consent, no video) and AtchayamG/bee-bystander. No roommate, tenant or camera-charter project appears in the saved sets, and GitHub searches on 2026-10-04 returned 'roommate ring camera created:>2026-08-31' = 0 and 'tenant landlord …
- Day-one proof: With a Playground token obtained from India: GET /v1/devices?include=status,capabilities,location,configurations returns 200; record whether motion_zones and privacy_zones are present and non-empty; canonicalize and hash the response, poll three times over 15 minutes and confirm the hash is stable; inspect the Playground console for any device-setting control and record the answer; run ring-sandbox 0.5.0 locally and diff a fixture where privacy_zones goes from 1 to 2 into a typed change record that closes a test request; render the zone SVG and 3x3 coverage text from the API-doc example polygon.
- Redesign to pass: Not required. To lift Tech Implementation and Stage-1 confidence: show the real Playground reads with timestamps and the canonical hash on screen, poll live during the take, and run the closure beat on the emulator behind a visible banner; consider adding an equal-visibility 'camera was viewed N times today' line from on_demand history only if RNG-3 is not built. If the five-person paper test rejects acknowledgement flows, reposition as a landlord-tenant disclosure sheet with the same engine.

**Round-2 judge ratings (1–5; base case: solo, India, VVD on Mac, no Bee hardware, no US Ring device). Weighted = 0.25·Tech + 0.20·Design + 0.20·Impact + 0.20·Originality + 0.15·Feasibility.**

| Judge lens | Tech | Design | Impact | Originality | Feasibility | Weighted | Confidence | One-line verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| devrel | 3 | 3 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 2.95 | medium | Clean and cheap consent product, but a settings diff with a checkbox whose wow beat is simulated. |
| engineer | 3 | 3 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 2.95 | medium | A clean, low-risk consent ledger over real configuration reads, but the Ring usage is a repeated GET on a static device, the key beat is emulated, and it duplicates RNG-3's primitive. |
| product | 3 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 4 | 3.15 | medium | Principled consent tool with peer-reviewed motivation and low risk, held back by a simulated signature beat and a buyer who is not the beneficiary. |
| **Mean of three** | 3.00 | 3.00 | 2.33 | 3.00 | 4.00 | **3.02** | | Scenario weighted: solo+hw 3.02; duo 3.12; duo+hw 3.12 |

**Red-team verdicts:** not red-teamed (did not reach the top of either round's ranking); ranking rests on gate and judge scores alone.

#### RNG-5: ZoneSpeak: a non-visual reader for Ring motion zones and camera settings, for blind and low-vision owners

**Round:** 2. **Track:** Ring (primary). Mini challenges: AWS Builder, Open Source.

**Pitch:** Ring's most consequential settings are drawn, not typed: motion zones and privacy zones are polygons dragged over a camera image, which a screen reader cannot convey. ZoneSpeak reads the Ring Partner API's normalized zone polygons and settings and turns them into speech a blind owner can act on: 'Front Door watches the lower two-thirds of the view, full width; the top strip with the street is excluded. One privacy zone is set. Motion detection is on. Night vision is off. The camera is online.' It adds an audio sweep that traces the zone boundary, a high-contrast magnifiable rendering for low-vision users, and a plain-language health check following Ring's own configuration-guidance pattern. Because the API is read-only, it produces a precise, speakable change request for a sighted helper and then confirms the change by re-reading Ring, with recovery steps when nothing changed. No video is used, so it also works on TAKE-encrypted devices.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Primary user | Blind and low-vision adults who own or administer a Ring doorbell or camera and use a screen reader (VoiceOver, TalkBack, NVDA, JAWS) or large text and high contrast. Secondary: the sighted helper (family member, friend, or a remote assistance volunteer) who applies the requested change in the Ring app. US focus for real devices; the Playground path is location-agnostic. |
| Frequency | Occasional but high-friction: at setup, whenever alerts are too many or too few (zone tuning is Ring's own remedy for unwanted alerts), after the camera is repositioned, and whenever a blind owner wants to know what the camera watches. Ring says every doorbell and camera supports motion zones and that zones are the way to reduce false alerts, so tuning recurs for every owner; for a blind owner each occurrence requires sighted help today (AppleVis users report needing it). |
| Severity | Moderate for the individual (loss of independent control over a device they paid for and over what it records), with a large population: the CDC counts about 7 million people in the US with vision impairment including 1 million with blindness (page dated 15 May 2024). One AppleVis user explicitly asked for 'an accessible way to adjust the motion zones for each camera using Voiceover' and called it 'highly visual'; another wrote 'You'll need sighted help for some things'. Ring's accessibility statement claims VoiceOver and TalkBack compatibility, which is about labels, not about conveying a polygon. |
| Current workaround | Ask a sighted person to drag the zone points in the Ring app (support article: 'Drag the points to set your boundary and tap Save'), accept the default zone, or leave motion detection as it is. Some users rely on the Rapid Ring companion app for quicker live view (user report, not verified). None of these tell a blind owner what the zone covers or confirm that a helper's change matches what was asked. |
| Amazon integration and its indispensable role | Ring Partner API via Playground token: GET configurations supplies motion_zones as normalized polygons (vertices 0.0-1.0, origin top-left) and image-enhancement toggles; status supplies online; capabilities supply resolution and configurable keys; history supplies the event timeline (on_demand on the Playground; motion sub-types and ding on real devices); WHEP live view with optional recvonly Opus audio is an optional 'listen at the door' beat. Ring's UX guide provides the guidance-message pattern and the WCAG 2.2 AA bar. Optional Amazon Polly for consistent speech. |
| Removal test (honest) | Without the Ring Partner API there is no zone geometry, no settings and no status to describe; ZoneSpeak would be a generic screen-reader tutorial. Ring is fully load-bearing. Honest caveats: the same approach would work for any camera vendor that exposes zone polygons, and the API's read-only nature means the change itself still happens in the Ring app by a sighted helper; ZoneSpeak owns understanding and confirmation, not actuation. |
| Wow moment under 45 s | 0-10 s: VoiceOver is on; the owner pastes a Playground token and presses 'Read my cameras'. Within about 8 s the page speaks: 'Front Door. Online. Motion detection on. One motion zone covering about 70 percent of the view: the full width, from one-fifth of the way down to the bottom. The top strip is excluded. One privacy zone is set; Ring keeps its position private. Night vision off, HDR off.' 10-20 s: 'Hear the zone' plays a two-second stereo sweep whose pitch rises with height and pans left to right as it traces the boundary, then repeats the 3x3 grid in words. 20-35 s: the owner says the street still triggers alerts; ZoneSpeak speaks a helper script: 'Open Ring, Devices, Front Door, Device Settings, Motion Settings, Edit Zones; drag the top edge down to about one-third.' 35-45 s: the helper applies it (on the emulator, labelled SIMULATED, because the Playground device cannot be edit… |
| Original mechanism | Geometry-to-language with verification. (1) Polygon analysis on normalized vertices: area fraction (shoelace), bounding box, 3x3 grid cell coverage, edges touched, top/bottom exclusion bands and convexity; the watermark band is irrelevant because no video is used. (2) A deterministic phrase generator maps those features to ordered sentences (coverage, position, exclusions) in plain language, with a short and a detailed mode; privacy zones are reported by count only (UX guide MUST). (3) Sonification: boundary traced as a pitch-and-pan sweep (Web Audio), plus a grid-cell readout, so the shape is conveyed non-visually in two complementary ways. (4) Health check: status.online, capabilities.battery_status.supported gating any battery claim, motion_detection.enabled, zone presence, night-vision state, each rendered as a UX-guide guidance message (what, why, where in the Ring app). (5) Helper… |
| Simulator or real-data route (data route) | Screen-reader browser -> ZoneSpeak proxy (Lambda) -> Ring Partner API (server-side because browser calls are blocked by CORS) -> configuration and status JSON returned to the browser, where geometry analysis, phrase generation and Web Audio sonification run locally; speech via the browser's speech synthesis or optional Amazon Polly audio from the proxy. Snapshots of zone features (not raw privacy-zone vertices) are stored in DynamoDB to support the verify-by-re-read diff. No media is requested in the MVP; the optional WHEP beat negotiates browser-to-Ring directly after the proxy creates the session. |
| Authentication | Playground token pasted by the owner for development and demo (spoken expiry state). Refresh-token mode for real accounts (4 h access, 30-day refresh). Production linking would need a registered, identity-verified app and one-way account linking (gated). Owner-only product: a passkey or magic link protects the ZoneSpeak account; helpers receive a one-time script link with no Ring access. |
| Preview constraints | One simulated DoorbellPro whose motion_zones content is unknown until read (day-1 check); if empty, the geometry beat uses the API-doc example polygon as a labelled fixture. The device cannot be edited, so the 'changed, verified' beat runs on the emulator (SIMULATED). History holds on_demand only. WHEP is contested (frame captured 2026-09-23 by one entrant; 'needs streaming hardware' failure in another's 2026-09-30 sweep). 30-minute tokens. CC BY attribution if any Playground frame is shown. India access to the Playground is not verified. |
| Hardware | MacBook M4 Pro with VoiceOver (built in) for development and screen-reader testing; an iPhone with VoiceOver for mobile testing; headphones for sonification checks. No Ring hardware needed for the Playground path. Optional US-located Ring camera with a plan for the conditional real path. |
| SDK versions | No official Ring SDK. Node 20 via nvm (machine has Node 25); plain TypeScript + Vite (or the official Next.js 14 starter) with Web Audio and Web Speech APIs; Python 3.11+ for the Lambda proxy and ring-sandbox 0.5.0 (PyPI 2026-10-02 per github.md); axe-core and pa11y for automated WCAG checks; AWS CDK v2 or SAM. Versions from the digests unless stated. |
| Deployment prerequisites | Amazon Developer account with Playground access; AWS Paid plan if credits are used; Lambda + API Gateway + DynamoDB + S3/CloudFront + KMS; optional Amazon Polly (standard IAM permission); no public webhook endpoint needed on the Playground path; no Bedrock dependency. |
| Firecrawl: research role | Used in this round to read the AppleVis threads, Ring's motion-zone and privacy-feature help pages, Ring's accessibility statement, and the CDC vision-loss fact sheet (saved under .firecrawl/oct4/rng2-*.json). |
| Firecrawl: runtime role | none |
| AWS Builder path | Lambda behind API Gateway as the mandatory server-side Ring proxy with the token encrypted by KMS; DynamoDB stores zone-feature snapshots and pending helper requests; EventBridge Scheduler re-reads configurations every minute for up to 30 minutes after a helper script is issued, so confirmation arrives without the owner polling; Amazon Polly (neural voice) renders the descriptions as audio for browsers whose built-in voices are poor, with the text always available to the screen reader; S3 + CloudFront host the static app; AWS Budgets alarm. Developed with Kiro or Claude Code on Bedrock. Value: consistent speech quality and a reliable confirmation loop with no servers; no model in the decision path. |
| Open Source path | (1) Publish 'zone-describe' (MIT): normalized-polygon analysis (area, bounding box, 3x3 coverage, exclusion bands) and a deterministic phrase generator with golden tests, plus a sonification helper; vendor-neutral, usable for any camera API that exposes zone polygons. (2) PR to the official AmazonAppDev/ring-api-helloworld starter adding a scripts/describe_zones.py example that prints a plain-language description of each device's motion zones (PRs need not be merged). (3) Publish the VoiceOver and NVDA test script and axe-core results as a reusable accessibility checklist for Ring partner apps that follow the UX guide's WCAG 2.2 AA requirement. |
| Fallback | If the Playground zones are empty: run the describer on the API-doc example polygon and emulator fixtures, keep the real Playground reads for status, capabilities, settings toggles and history, and say so. If grid-reconstruction testing shows descriptions are not understood: simplify to coverage percentage plus top/bottom/left/right exclusions only, and rely on the sonified sweep plus the large-print render. If no screen-reader testers can be recruited: test with VoiceOver and screen curtain yourself, document it as a limitation, and ask for feedback on AppleVis. If judges see read-only as fatal: lead with the health check and guidance messages (universal value) and present zone description as the accessibility differentiator. |
| Falsification test (≤1 day) | One day. (a) With a Playground token, GET configurations and record whether motion_zones is non-empty; run the describer on whatever is returned, or on the API-doc example polygon if empty (labelled). (b) Blind reconstruction test: give the generated description (text only, no picture) to four people who have not seen the shape and ask them to mark the covered cells on a 3x3 grid; pass if the mean cell agreement is at least 7 of 9 after at most two description iterations; the concept is falsified if agreement stays below 5 of 9, because the core promise (conveying the zone non-visually) would not hold. (c) Check VoiceOver on the Ring iOS app's motion-zone editor on a borrowed iPhone if available, or collect two more user reports; if the editor turns out to be fully operable and self-describing with VoiceOver, the differentiation collapses and the concept should be dropped. |
| Risk profile | Low technical risk and very low cloud cost; the work is geometry, Web Audio and WCAG craft. Medium simulation risk: the Playground device is static and its zone content is unknown, so part of the demo may be fixture-labelled. Medium adoption-evidence risk: user voice is a handful of AppleVis posts, undated. Low policy risk: metadata only, privacy-zone coordinates never shown, no biometrics. Medium judging risk that the native 'VoiceOver-compatible' claim is taken at face value. |
| Distinct from round 1 because | User group: blind and low-vision Ring account holders, not riders (RNG-1), appointment professionals (RNG-2), in-home workers (RNG-3) or any of the nine non-Ring concepts' users; also not the crowded 'describe the visitor' entrants, who serve blind residents through video. Workflow: single-owner read, hear, hand off to a helper, confirm; no multi-party agreement, booking, dispute packet or receipt. Mechanism: polygon analysis to language and sonification plus a verify-by-re-read confirmation loop; it uses no video, no on_demand matching, no CV and no webhooks. Shared primitive with RNG-4: bot… |
| Estimate (unmeasured) | Solo 72 h; two-person 44 h per person; cloud Estimate: about $1-6 total through 20 Nov 2026. Lambda and API Gateway at demo volume are near zero; DynamoDB cents; Scheduler free allowance; Polly neural voices are priced per million characters (a few thousand descriptions cost under $1; rate not re-verified today); KMS about $1/key-month; CloudWatch a few dollars.. Assumptions: Estimates, not measurements. One engineer comfortable with TypeScript, Web Audio and WCAG practice. Hours: day-1 spikes incl. Playground configurations read and grid-reconstruction test (6h), Ring client and proxy (4h), polygon analysis and phrase generator with golden tests (10h), sonification and high-contrast render (6h), screen-reader-first UI and VoiceOver/NVDA testing passes (14h), helper script and verify loop with Scheduler (8h), emulator fixtures (4h), AWS deployment (4h), README/verif… |

**Demand evidence** (quality labelled by the researcher):

- A blind Ring owner wrote: 'it would be great if there were an accessible way to adjust the motion zones for each camera using Voiceover. I know this is highly visual' and asked whether anyone had succeeded in adjusting zones with VoiceOver. — [https://www.applevis.com/forum/ios-ipados/disappointed-accessibility-ring-app](https://www.applevis.com/forum/ios-ipados/disappointed-accessibility-ring-app) — *User voice; single user; thread undated in the scraped markdown (comment id 121354); other comments describe VoiceOver regressions and later fixes.*
- In a separate AppleVis thread a Ring Alarm owner wrote 'You'll need sighted help for some things' and another needed sighted help to find Wi-Fi networks during setup; a later comment reports battery levels and Wi-Fi networks becoming accessible after an update. — [https://www.applevis.com/forum/smart-home-tech/accessibility-ring-products](https://www.applevis.com/forum/smart-home-tech/accessibility-ring-products) — *User voice; several users; undated in scraped markdown; mixed sentiment, so demand is real but not universal.*
- Ring's official method for motion zones is 'Drag the points to set your boundary and tap Save' with up to three zones per device; the article recommends excluding high-traffic areas such as streets to reduce unwanted alerts. — [https://ring.com/support/articles/q55xm/Setting-Up-Motion-Zones-to-Fix-Motion-Alert-Issues-for-Ring-Cameras](https://ring.com/support/articles/q55xm/Setting-Up-Motion-Zones-to-Fix-Motion-Alert-Issues-for-Ring-Cameras) — *Official help centre ('Last updated 1 month ago' when read on 2026-10-04); shows the task is inherently visual.*
- Approximately 7 million people in the United States have vision impairment, including 1 million with blindness. — [https://www.cdc.gov/vision-health/data-research/vision-loss-facts/index.html](https://www.cdc.gov/vision-health/data-research/vision-loss-facts/index.html) — *Government statistics (page dates 15 May 2024); population scale only, not Ring-specific.*
- Ring's partner UX guide requires WCAG 2.2 Level AA and prescribes a configuration guidance pattern (what is wrong, why it matters, where in the Ring app to fix it) while never implying the partner app can change settings. — [https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/ux-design-guide.html](https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/ux-design-guide.html) — *Official design guide (undated; published 2025-04-22 per release notes); supply-side signal that Ring expects partners to guide configuration accessibly.*
- Ring's accessibility statement says 'The Ring Website and Mobile applications are compatible with VoiceOver, and Talkback Technologies' and describes regular accessibility testing. — [https://ring.com/eu/en/accessibility](https://ring.com/eu/en/accessibility) — *Vendor statement (undated); establishes the native baseline a judge will cite.*

**Rivals named at generation** (strongest feature; our difference; evidence status):

| Rival | Strongest feature | Our difference | Evidence status |
|---|---|---|---|
| [Ring app with VoiceOver / TalkBack (native)](https://ring.com/eu/en/accessibility) | It is the only place settings can be changed; Ring states the apps are compatible with VoiceOver and TalkBack and are regularly tested for accessibility. | ZoneSpeak conveys the content of visual-only settings (zone geometry) and verifies a helper's change against the API; it never claims to change settings. A spoken description of motion-zone geometry inside the Ring app is not verified in reviewed sources (one AppleVis user asks for exactly that). | Ring accessibility statement read 2026-10-04 (undated). AppleVis threads read 2026-10-04 (undated). |
| [Doorstep (AtchayamG/doorstep-ring) and hack-amazon4 (Ring-track entrants for blind residents)](https://github.com/AtchayamG/doorstep-ring) | Objective spoken descriptions of doorbell events using Bedrock Nova Pro, a measured accessibility audit, a real Playground WHEP frame, and an honest verified-vs-unverified table. | They describe who is at the door from video; ZoneSpeak describes what the camera is set to watch and whether it is healthy, from metadata only, so it works on TAKE devices and does not touch privacy zones. No overlap in data path; shared audience. | README read from saved copy (retrieved 2026-10-03); GitHub search on 2026-10-04 found both repos and no repo on Ring accessibility of zones or settings. |
| [Home Assistant Ring integration (community)](https://www.home-assistant.io/integrations/ring/) | Battery and Wi-Fi signal sensors, an event entity for rings and motion, live view, volume controls and automations through the unofficial Ring cloud login. | ZoneSpeak uses the official Partner API and is screen-reader-first; exposure of motion-zone geometry in Home Assistant is not verified in reviewed sources. Home Assistant is also outside the hackathon's sanctioned Ring path. | Integration page read from saved copy (retrieved 2026-10-03; undated page). |

**Smallest credible MVP:**

- Token-paste and refresh-token modes with spoken token-expiry recovery; server-side Ring proxy (CORS).
- Polygon analysis library with golden tests on the API-doc example polygon and synthetic shapes; phrase generator with short and detailed modes.
- Screen-reader-first web app (semantic HTML, ARIA live regions, keyboard-only flow, reduced motion, WCAG 2.2 AA contrast, 200% zoom) tested with VoiceOver on macOS and iOS and with NVDA if a Windows machine is available.
- Sonified boundary sweep plus 3x3 grid readout.
- Health check with UX-guide guidance messages, battery claims gated on capabilities.battery_status.supported.
- Helper script generator and verify-by-re-read loop with spoken recovery paths.
- Spoken event timeline from Event History (on_demand on the Playground; labelled; motion sub-types on real devices).
- Emulator fixtures (ring-sandbox) for the labelled SIMULATED change beat; README verified-vs-simulated table; friction log; product feedback.

**Deliberately excluded stretch:**

- WHEP 'listen at the door' with recvonly Opus audio and a spoken countdown (contested on the Playground; one entrant got a frame, another's sweep failed).
- Zone quality lint beyond the top-band heuristic (e.g., comparing zones to typical approach paths), which would need video.
- Multi-camera Ring Elite per-component zones.
- Native iOS app; Alexa skill front end; Fire TV reader.
- LLM-generated descriptions (deterministic templates only, so every sentence is traceable to a geometric feature).

**APIs, events and actions** (status as assessed by the researcher; 'unverified' means not confirmed in reviewed docs, 'gated' means not available to entrants, 'simulated' means not real in the demo):

| Status | API / event / action | Documentation |
|---|---|---|
| verified | GET /v1/devices/{id}/configurations (motion_detection.enabled, motion_zones[].vertices normalized 0-1, image_enhancements toggles, privacy_zones count only) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#configurations |
| verified | Zone coordinate system (normalized 0.0-1.0, origin top-left) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#zone-coordinate-system |
| verified | GET /v1/devices?include=status,capabilities,configurations | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#including-related-data |
| verified | GET /v1/devices/{id}/status (online) and capabilities.battery_status.supported gating | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#status |
| verified | GET /v1/history/devices/{id}/events (attributes event_type, is_third_party_reviewed, start, end; on_demand only on the Playground) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#event-history |
| unverified | POST /v1/devices/{id}/media/streaming/whep/sessions (recvonly video, optional recvonly Opus audio; 30 s battery / 60 s wired) | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#live-video |
| verified | Ring Developer Playground token and console | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/release-notes.html#developers-playground |
| gated | motion_detected webhooks with sub_type (human/vehicle/other_motion) for the real-device timeline | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/api-documentation.html#notifications |
| simulated | ring-sandbox emulator for the zone-changed confirmation beat | https://github.com/josepha-mayo/ring-sandbox |
| verified | UX Design Guide: configuration guidance pattern, privacy-zone coordinate prohibition, WCAG 2.2 AA | https://developer.amazon.com/docs/ring/ux-design-guide.html#accessibility |

**Blockers:** The Playground DoorbellPro's motion_zones array may be empty; the geometry beat would then run on a labelled fixture until a real device is available.; The Playground device cannot be edited, so the 'changed, verified' confirmation is simulated on the emulator.; Partner API is read-only, so actuation stays with a sighted helper in the Ring app; judges may see this as a half-solution.; WHEP on the Playground is contested; the optional audio beat may not run.; No screen-reader users are on the team; recruiting two testers (AppleVis community or local NGO) within the window is a sensitivity.; Playground regional access from India is not verified.

**Reasons judges might reject it:** 'Ring says the app is already VoiceOver-compatible' - judges may not distinguish labelled controls from conveying a drawn polygon.; Read-only API means ZoneSpeak cannot make the change; a judge may call it a description tool rather than an aid.; Tech Implementation may score low: no CV, no streaming, no model; the engineering is geometry, audio and accessibility craft.; Accessibility entries for blind residents already exist on the Ring track (Doorstep, hack-amazon4), so a judge may file this under the same theme despite the different data path.; The confirmation beat is simulated on the Playground.; Low problem frequency (zone tuning is occasional) may cap Potential Impact.

**Gate result:** overall **pass** (eligibility pass, feasibility pass; native overlap medium, visible crowding medium).

- Eligibility: The runtime Ring calls are real, Ring-documented and Playground-supported: GET configurations (motion_zones polygons with normalized vertices; the API reference's Zone Coordinate System section confirms 0.0-1.0 with origin top-left), status (online), capabilities (battery_status.supported gating), and Event History (on_demand only on the Playground, labelled). Accessibility aid is one of the rules' own Creative Ring examples. No video is used in the MVP, so it also works on TAKE devices; the optional WHEP beat is excluded. The product never changes settings and never implies it can (UX guide warning), privacy-zone coordinates are never shown (UX guide MUST), and no face, plate or biometric …
- Feasibility: The 72 h solo estimate is the lowest in the set and leaves about 32 h of buffer against the roughly 104 h capacity (estimate). The work is geometry (shoelace area, bounding box, grid coverage), deterministic phrase templates, Web Audio sonification, WCAG craft and a one-minute re-read loop on EventBridge Scheduler; no model or CV is on the critical path. Unknowns: the Playground DoorbellPro's motion_zones content (fixture fallback from the API-doc example polygon, labelled), the static device (confirmation beat simulated on ring-sandbox), Playground access from India (unverified for every Ring concept), the 30-minute token, and recruiting two screen-reader testers within the window (sensiti…
- Conditions: (1) The Developer Playground opens from India and GET configurations returns the motion_detection block; if motion_zones is empty, the geometry beat runs on the API-doc example polygon and is labelled a fixture on screen and in the README. (2) The emulator confirmation beat is labelled SIMULATED; 'Verified by Ring' appears only on facts read from the Playground or a real account. (3) Privacy zones are reported by count only; the app never claims to change settings; every guidance message follows the UX guide pattern and sends the helper to the Ring app. (4) Automated WCAG 2.2 AA checks (axe-core, pa11y) show no critical violations and a VoiceOver pass on macOS and iOS is recorded; battery claims are gated on capabilities.battery_status.supported. (5) The blind reconstruction test reaches at least 7 of 9 grid cells after at most two description iterations with four people; if agreement stays below 5 of 9, simplify to coverage percentage plus edge exclusions or drop the concept. (6) No Playground footage or frames are shown (none needed in the MVP); if any is added later, CC BY attribution is required.
- Native overlap: The Ring app is the only place settings can be changed and Ring states its apps are VoiceOver and TalkBack compatible (ring.com accessibility statement, undated); if its motion-zone editor turns out to be self-describing under VoiceOver, the differentiation collapses, which the concept's own test (c) covers. AppleVis users report needing sighted help for some tasks and request accessible zone adjustment (undated threads). Home Assistant's Ring integration exposes sensors and events but motion-z…
- Crowding: Accessibility for blind and low-vision users is the most crowded theme across tracks (about 12 repos, mostly Fire TV audio description), and the Ring track already has blind- or Deaf-resident entrants with a different data path: AtchayamG/doorstep-ring and chinesepowered/hack-amazon4 (video descriptions of visitors) and 321abdulfatah/wave (Deaf and hard-of-hearing). No motion-zone or screen-reader Ring project appears in the saved sets; '"motion zones" created:>2026-08-31' returned 1 unrelated …
- Day-one proof: With a Playground token: GET /v1/devices/{id}/configurations and log whether motion_zones is non-empty; run the describer on the returned polygon or the API-doc example (labelled) and confirm VoiceOver on macOS Safari reads the description from an ARIA live region; play the Web Audio boundary sweep; axe-core reports zero critical violations on the page; an EventBridge Scheduler rule re-reads configurations every minute for 30 minutes and then stops; give the text description to four people and record 3x3 grid agreement (target at least 7 of 9 after at most two iterations).
- Redesign to pass: Not required. Lead the video with the health check and guidance messages (universal value, Ring-sanctioned pattern) and present zone description plus sonification as the accessibility differentiator; show the Playground read, the generated sentences and the sweep in one unedited take with VoiceOver audible. If the Ring app's zone editor proves fully accessible with VoiceOver, drop the concept per the stated test.

**Round-2 judge ratings (1–5; base case: solo, India, VVD on Mac, no Bee hardware, no US Ring device). Weighted = 0.25·Tech + 0.20·Design + 0.20·Impact + 0.20·Originality + 0.15·Feasibility.**

| Judge lens | Tech | Design | Impact | Originality | Feasibility | Weighted | Confidence | One-line verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| devrel | 3 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 4 | 3.35 | medium | Best Ring concept for an India-based solo: on-brief accessibility aid, fully deterministic, cheap; its ceiling is the read-only API. |
| engineer | 3 | 4 | 2 | 4 | 5 | 3.50 | medium | The lowest-risk, most complete accessibility design in the set and a creative use of a read-only API, but technically thin on Ring calls and occasional in use. |
| product | 3 | 4 | 3 | 4 | 4 | 3.55 | medium | Best new Ring concept: a specific, user-voiced accessibility gap with a distinct non-visual mechanism and the smallest build; capped by read-only API, occasional frequency and a static Playground device. |
| **Mean of three** | 3.00 | 4.00 | 2.67 | 3.67 | 4.33 | **3.47** | | Scenario weighted: solo+hw 3.47; duo 3.57; duo+hw 3.57 |

**Why each judge rated it that way (verbatim rationale, one per criterion):**

- *devrel* — Tech: Real configurations/status/capabilities/history reads and the normalized zone geometry (JDR-12) drive deterministic description, sonification and a Scheduler-based verify-by-re-read loop; 'accessibility aid' is a creative Ring example (JDR-03). Thin by construction: no media, no CV, no model, read-only API means actuation stays in the Ring app, and the confirmation beat is emulator-simulated. — Design: Screen-reader-first (ARIA live regions, keyboard-only, WCAG 2.2 AA per UX guide), two complementary non-visual encodings (sentences plus pitch/pan sweep), UX-guide guidance messages, spoken recovery paths and explicit confirmation on every action. Not 5: fixture-labelled zones if the Playground returns none, and helpers are still required. — Impact: Explicit user request on AppleVis (JDR-15), a verified visual-only native task (drag the points, JDR-14) and a large population (CDC ~7M VI); capped because zone tuning is occasional and the tool describes rather than changes. — Originality: Geometry-to-language plus sonification for camera zones was not found in Ring, Home Assistant or visible entrants (doorstep-ring/hack-amazon4 use video), but blind/low-vision aids are the most crowded theme across tracks (~12) and Ring's 'VoiceOver-compatible' claim invites a native-overlap reading. — Feasibility: 72 h, the lowest in the set, with ~32 h buffer; risks are Playground access from India, empty zones, and recruiting screen-reader testers.
  - Scenario adjustments: solo+hw tech 3, feasibility 4 (No change; Ring stays Playground.); duo tech 3, feasibility 5 (44 h each; time for two tester rounds.); duo+hw tech 3, feasibility 5 (As duo.)
- *engineer* — Tech: Same verified Ring surface as RNG-4 (configurations with normalized motion_zones polygons; status.online; capabilities.battery_status.supported gating, which the API reference says to read before trusting battery_status.percentage; on_demand history; .firecrawl/technical-ring-api.md) plus an EventBridge re-read loop for verify-by-re-read. Held at 2 because zone tuning is occasional rather than daily, the user voice is a handful of undated posts, and the product describes and confirms but cannot act. — Originality: Polygon-to-language plus sonification over a read-only camera API is a creative use of the required tool that no reviewed rival or entrant does: the Ring track's blind-resident entrants (doorstep-ring, hack-amazon4) describe visitors from video, Home Assistant does not expose zone geometry in reviewed pages, and '"motion zones"' returned one unrelated NVR on Oct 4. ### 4.2 Solo with hardware (Fire TV Stick 4K Select; iPhone + Apple Watch running Bee; Ring still Playground)
